==================================================================
8. WHAT SELECTS THE BRANCH AT --level 1
==================================================================

8a. Terminal state of six traced trajectories (master seed 42).
    Checkpoint count from CREM_SKIP_CENSUS; the last gap is the
    duration of the final checkpoint.

channel  index  t(ps)            checkpts last_gap(ps)  
para     2      147.82445956     27       0.276266      
para     4      147.824656898    27       0.276265      
ortho    2      147.834632144    27       0.276821      
ortho    3      147.828554091    27       0.276469      
ortho    4      147.556519343    26       0.276758      
ortho    6      147.559958349    26       0.276975      

    Branch gap 147.834632 - 147.556519 = 0.278113 ps against a final
    checkpoint of 0.2768 ps: the two branches differ by exactly one.
    All six stop on the retardation limit and all fire 2 photons, so
    neither the stop cause nor the photon count is the discriminator.

8b. Every trajectory ends ON its final photon.  Binding before the last
    checkpoint, binding at the stop, and hbar*omega at the emission
    point (the census value carried down the skip by (1-s)^(-2/3) and
    omega ~ |E|^(3/2)):

    ortho idx2 (long)   26.23 eV -> 170.09 eV   jump 143.86 eV   hbar*omega 143.85 eV   ratio 1.0001
    ortho idx4 (short)  26.23 eV -> 167.92 eV   jump 141.69 eV   hbar*omega 141.68 eV   ratio 1.0001

    One photon carries five times the binding the pair had left, so it
    takes the orbit past the stop in a single step.  Emission is
    resolved only at checkpoint boundaries, so the reported collapse
    time is quantised in units of one final checkpoint.

8c. Consequence: the level-1 collapse time is NOT converged in the
    secular step.  Trace of para index 2, master seed 42, with
    maximumJumpParameter (s_max) varied:

    s_max    t(ps)       photons
    0.30     147.824     2
    0.20     163.715     3
    0.15     172.203     3
    0.10     180.782     3
    0.045    191.427     3

    Monotonic, +29.5% from s=0.30 to s=0.045 and still climbing.  The
    0.17% para/ortho separation measured at s=0.30 is 150x smaller
    than the step error, so it is not a physical channel difference.

8d. The same scan with --level 2 --ground-state-floor, where the
    cascade stops at n=1 and never reaches the depth where one photon
    outweighs the binding:

    s_max    t(ps)       photons
    0.30     6206.463    2
    0.10     6521.521    2

    +5.1%, better behaved but also not flat.  The convergence table
    recorded at maximumJumpParameter's definition (+/-0.6% over a 25x
    range, 124-125 ps) reproduces at neither current configuration and
    predates the present defaults.

==================================================================
9. CLOSING THE STEP CONVERGENCE
==================================================================

9a. The cause, read off CREM_SKIP_CENSUS.  Under the default
    stochasticElectricDipole model the orbit does NOT decay between
    photons -- the semi-major axis is flat to five digits for nine
    consecutive checkpoints, then one photon moves it by a factor of
    four (para index 2, master seed 42, s_max=0.30):

      #    t(ps)        a(m)        L(hbar)
      1     0.0000   1.0584e-10   1.00000
      5    63.4899   1.0583e-10   1.00000
      9   126.9774   1.0583e-10   1.00001
     10   142.8489   2.7439e-11   0.50944   <- photon
     15   144.2317   2.7435e-11   0.50955
     27   147.5482   2.7427e-11   0.50950

    Six sites nevertheless applied the sinking-envelope shape
    u(n)=u0(1-s)^(-2/3) within each skip: the checkpoint's elapsed
    proper time (1-s/2), the hazard's integralFactor, the identity's
    meanInSkipGrowth and its envelope reference, the photon's position
    in the skip and its quantum (energyRatio), and a sixth, duplicated
    copy of the elapsed-time formula in the lab-frame tail.

9b. Effect on the collapse time, one trajectory (para index 2, seed 42):

      s_max    before      after
      0.30     147.824     199.428
      0.20     163.715     198.970
      0.10     180.782     198.944
      0.045    191.427     199.747

    Before: monotonic, +29.5% and still climbing at the finest step.
    After:  flat to 0.4% over a 6.7x range of step size.

9c. The photon events themselves are now step-independent.  Times and
    semi-major axes at the two emissions, s_max=0.30 against 0.10:

      photon 1   t=186.7296 ps vs 186.7084 ps   a: 1.0583e-10 -> 3.528e-11
      photon 2   t=199.1727 ps vs 198.6764 ps   a: 3.527e-11  -> 7.91e-12

    0.011% and 0.25% apart, against 22% before.

9d. The convergence table recorded in the code at maximumJumpParameter
    (124.169 / 124.957 / 125.585 / 124.986 / 124.328 ps for s_max
    0.012..0.30, claimed flat to +/-0.6%) reproduces at no
    configuration this file has today.  It predates the present
    defaults and was carried forward unremeasured, which is how a real
    step dependence stayed hidden.  Replaced by the table in 9b.

9e. Post-fix paired channel comparison, both seed sets.

9f. --level 2 --ground-state-floor after the fix: NOT closed.
==================================================================

    Its value moves onto the new convention, 6206 -> 7368 ps, which
    returns it to the 7368.8 ps cascade median recorded elsewhere in
    the README.  It is still step dependent:

      s_max    t(ps)      (para index 2, master seed 42)
      0.30     7368.276
      0.20     7318.474
      0.10     7071.144

    -4.0% and monotonic.  There the trajectory ends on the
    ground-state floor rather than on a photon, so the residual has a
    different source, which was not investigated.  The configuration
    is experimental and not the default; recorded as open.

==================================================================
10. DOES L REACH THE CONTACT VALUE?
==================================================================

Contact angular momentum for this pair, L_c = sqrt(mu k r_c)/hbar:
  0.04274 hbar at the Compton barrier (193.30 fm)
  0.00516 hbar at the classical electron radius (2.818 fm)

10a. YES, and it takes exactly one photon more than the stop allows.
     L read immediately AFTER each emission (CREM_EMISSION_REACH),
     in units of L_c at the barrier, with the retardation threshold
     lowered from 150 to 5 so the fourth photon can fire:

       trajectory    photons   L/L_c after each photon
       para  idx 2      4      13.51  6.40  2.30  0.50
       para  idx 3      4      13.51  6.40  2.21  0.47
       para  idx 4      4      13.51  6.40  2.28  0.49
       ortho idx 2      4      13.51  6.40  2.22  0.47
       ortho idx 4      4      13.51  6.43  2.23  0.47

     Uniform across trajectories and across both channels.  L crosses
     the contact value between photon 3 and photon 4.

10b. Where the default stop sits.  At threshold 150 (and at 50 and 20,
     which trip the same checkpoint) the trajectory stops with
     P/LC=17.67 at a=1.022e-12 m = 5.29 barrier radii.  At threshold 5
     photon 4 fires and the orbit lands at a=4.783e-14 m = 0.246
     barrier radii, i.e. INSIDE the barrier, where classical
     point-particle electrodynamics does not apply.  That last row is
     a probe of the model's own bookkeeping, not a physical claim.

10c. THE TERMINAL L THE ESTIMATOR REPORTS CANNOT BE USED FOR THIS.
     Between emissions the spin-orbit transport changes L at fixed
     energy, and the result is not a bound Kepler orbit:

       state                          L (hbar)   h^2/(A a)
       right after photon 3            0.0983      1.0002
       reported at the default stop    0.2151      4.7898
       right after photon 4            0.0212      0.9936
       reported at the deep stop       0.4475    445.9

     h^2/(A a) is 1-e^2 for a bound orbit and cannot exceed 1.  The
     post-emission values satisfy that; the reported ones exceed it by
     factors of 4.8 and 446.  osculatingEccentricity clamps e^2 to
     zero, so nothing in the ordinary output shows it.  Recorded now
     as CremCollapseEstimate::terminalKeplerConsistency.

10d. Correction to what this file said in section 3.  The obstacle to
     answering "does L reach contact" was NOT that the retardation
     stop cuts the trajectory short -- it is one photon short, and
     lowering it answers the question.  The obstacle is that L is
     corrupted between emissions, so the quantity the estimator
     reports at the stop is not the pair's orbital angular momentum.

==================================================================
11. CLOSING THE L<->S EXCHANGE
==================================================================

11a. Where the damage was.  CREM_LS_BALANCE prints, per half-step,
     the change in |L| and the energy that change would demand
     (dE = omega dL on the circular family) beside the dipole coupling
     energy that would have to pay it.  Over the 122 half-steps of a
     default trajectory (seed 42, para) the damage is entirely in the
     half-step that FOLLOWS a photon, and scales with how far that
     photon moved the orbit:

       orbit change       dL/L      h^2/(Aa) after
       a x 0.333          0.0003        1.0006
       a x 0.224          0.0185        1.0373
       a x 0.130          1.1886        4.7898

     Every other half-step moves L by 1e-5..1e-4 relative.  At the last
     one the exchange demanded 1673.9 eV against 0.0449 eV of coupling
     energy, a factor of 37000.

11b. The cause is the operator split, not the coupling.  The symmetric
     split gives each half 0.5*checkpointProperTime on the assumption
     that the radiation between them is a perturbation.  One photon
     here takes the semi-major axis down by a factor of eight and the
     orbital period with it by twenty, so a second half sized for the
     OLD orbit runs the new one for twenty times the precession it
     should see.  The second half now gets the proper time that
     actually follows the last photon, which the lab-frame block
     already computes; with no photon it is 0.5*checkpointProperTime
     and the split is unchanged.

11c. Two closures tried first, both worse, both recorded in the code:
     limiting each half-step's change in |L| to couplingEnergy/omega
     (an energy used as a per-step allowance, so its total scales with
     the checkpoint count: 216.73/210.96/204.93 ps for s_max
     0.30/0.20/0.10), and clamping |L| to the circular value (one-sided,
     so it ratchets: 216.69/210.92/204.90 ps, and the first photon's
     time moves 6.5% where it had been steady to 0.011%).

11d. Result.

       quantity                        before        after
       max h^2/(A a) on the trajectory  1.0616        0.99990
       h^2/(A a) at the stop            4.7898        1.0000
       max |dL/L| per half-step         1.1886        8.34e-04
       terminal L                       0.2151 hbar   0.09500 hbar
       terminal L / L_contact           5.03          2.22
       terminal periapsis / barrier    25.34          3.90
       collapse time                  199.428 ps    199.442 ps

     Step convergence is preserved: 199.442 / 198.984 / 198.943 ps for
     s_max 0.30 / 0.20 / 0.10, against 199.428 / 198.970 / 198.944
     before this change.

11e. The documented one-sidedness survives.  Between photons, ortho's
     orbital L still ramps deterministically and para's does not:

       ortho  mean dL = +2.196e-04 hbar per checkpoint, positive 33/33
       para   mean dL = +1.048e-05 hbar per checkpoint, positive 34/60

     So bounding the exchange removed the unphysical part without
     removing the spin-reservoir asymmetry the channel difference is
     attributed to.

11f. The closure resolved the level-1 channel comparison.
     Paired, 24 pairs per set, both samples uncensored:

complete pairs:        24 / 24
mean t_para:           199.442186 ps
mean t_ortho:          199.467581 ps
DIFFERENCE ortho-para: 0.0253952354 +/- 0.00226334397 ps   (11.220228 sigma)
GEOMETRIC RATIO:       1.00012733,  95% CI [1.00010509, 1.00014957]
SIGN TEST:             ortho longer in 24 of 24,  exact p = 1.1920929e-07
PHOTON COUNT DIFFERS:  0 of 24  (0%)

complete pairs:        24 / 24
mean t_para:           199.441981 ps
mean t_ortho:          199.467855 ps
DIFFERENCE ortho-para: 0.0258736887 +/- 0.00329240231 ps   (7.85860483 sigma)
GEOMETRIC RATIO:       1.00012973,  95% CI [1.00009737, 1.00016208]
SIGN TEST:             ortho longer in 23 of 24,  exact p = 2.98023224e-06
PHOTON COUNT DIFFERS:  0 of 24  (0%)

     Before the closure the same measurement gave -2.7e-4 with a null
     sign test (10 of 20).  So the unphysical part of the exchange was
     not only corrupting the elements, it was reversing the sign of the
     observable.  The sign now agrees with reality and with the n=2
     measurement (+2.50e-5).

==================================================================
12. FIRST-PASSAGE TIME OF L THROUGH CONTACT
==================================================================

Contact angular momentum, sqrt(mu k r_c)/hbar on a circular orbit:
  0.042737 hbar at the Compton barrier (193.30 fm)
  0.005160 hbar at the classical electron radius (2.818 fm)

Measured with CREM_RETARDATION_LIMIT lowered from 150 to 5, because the
crossing happens one photon after the default stop.  Below 150 the pair
ends inside the Compton barrier, where classical point-particle
electrodynamics does not apply: a probe of the model's own bookkeeping.

12a. Deterministic emission (the default), 16 trajectories per channel,
     master seed 42, 600 s budget:

Running 16 CREM collapse calibrations on 4 workers (per-event wall-clock budget 600 s).
para: 8 of 16 reached a stopping condition
  through Compton barrier   reached by 7 of 16   median 199.444 ps   mean 199.444 +/- 0.00133749 ps   sigma/mean 1.77426e-05
  through electron radius   reached by 0 of 16
  terminal L mean            0.0253941 hbar = 0.594194 x contact

Running 16 CREM collapse calibrations on 4 workers (per-event wall-clock budget 600 s).
ortho: 15 of 16 reached a stopping condition
  through Compton barrier   reached by 13 of 16   median 199.467 ps   mean 199.468 +/- 0.00312928 ps   sigma/mean 5.65644e-05
  through electron radius   reached by 0 of 16
  terminal L mean            0.0231633 hbar = 0.541995 x contact


12b. The Compton barrier, not the retardation margin, is what now stops
     the trajectory.  At retardation limits of 5 and of 1 the result is
     identical: stop cause = ComptonBarrier, a = 2.718e-14 m = 0.1406
     barrier radii, L = 0.016026 hbar = 0.375 x the barrier contact
     value.  Lowering the margin further changes nothing because the
     barrier catches the trajectory first.

     So the model follows L down to about 0.4-0.6 of the BARRIER
     contact value and no further.  The classical electron radius,
     which is where annihilation actually happens, needs another factor
     of 3-5 in L and 68.6 in radius, and is never reached.

12c. Scale, against measurement:

       model first passage to barrier contact   199.44 ps
       measured para-Ps lifetime                124.49 ps   ratio 1.602
       measured ortho-Ps lifetime               142000 ps   model 712x short

12d. THE DISTRIBUTION HAS THE WRONG SHAPE, and that is the sharper
     result than the scale.  Under deterministic emission sigma/mean is
     1.8e-5 (para) and 5.7e-5 (ortho): the first passage is a near-delta,
     so the model produces a characteristic TIME, not a rate, and cannot
     give an exponential decay law at all.

12e. Under POISSON emission the distribution has roughly the right shape.
     Same 16 trajectories per channel, seed 42, emission poisson:

  through Compton barrier   reached by 13 of 16   median 245.574 ps   mean 310.203 +/- 65.897 ps   sigma/mean 0.765934
  through electron radius   reached by 0 of 16
  terminal L mean            0.0165713 hbar = 0.387749 x contact

Running 16 CREM collapse calibrations on 4 workers (per-event wall-clock budget 600 s).
ortho: 13 of 16 reached a stopping condition
  through Compton barrier   reached by 13 of 16   median 229.067 ps   mean 269.376 +/- 65.5063 ps   sigma/mean 0.876791
  through electron radius   reached by 0 of 16
  terminal L mean            0.0170976 hbar = 0.400066 x contact


     para   median/mean = 0.792   sigma/mean = 0.766
     ortho  median/mean = 0.850   sigma/mean = 0.877
     exponential          0.693                1.000

     So the first passage is dominated by ONE waiting time, the first
     photon's, which takes about 186 of the 199 ps; a sum of four
     equal-rate exponentials would give sigma/mean = 0.5, and a single
     one gives 1.  The measured 0.77-0.88 sits near the single-waiting
     limit, i.e. near an exponential -- which is the shape a decay law
     has to have.  Whether the mechanism produces that shape is
     therefore decided by the emission model, not by the mechanism.

12f. Scale under Poisson emission, against measurement:

       para mean first passage     310.20 +/- 65.90 ps    2.49x the
                                                          measured 124.49 ps
       ortho mean first passage    269.38 +/- 65.51 ps    527x short of
                                                          the measured 142 ns

12g. Summary of what this buys and what it does not.
     BUYS: a rate scale from the model's own dynamics, with no
     annihilation width imported -- 199 ps deterministic, 310/269 ps
     mean under Poisson, against a measured 124.49 ps for para.  Within
     a factor 1.6 to 2.5, and with an approximately exponential shape
     when the emission is Poissonian.
     DOES NOT BUY: ortho, which is missed by 500-700x because the
     2gamma/3gamma selection rule is not in the model; and contact at
     the scale where annihilation actually happens, since the Compton
     barrier stops the trajectory 3-5x short in L and 68.6x short in
     radius, and that barrier is the declared validity limit rather
     than a tunable.

==================================================================
13. WHAT WOULD TURN CONTACT INTO A RATE
==================================================================

13a. The rate factorizes, and the factorization is exact.  For the
     singlet channel

       Gamma = 4 * (pi r_e^2 c) * <n(0)>

     with sigma*v = pi r_e^2 c = 7.478839e-21 m^3/s and
     <n(0)> = |psi(0)|^2 = 1/(pi a_Ps^3) = 2.685077e+29 1/m^3 this gives
     Gamma = 8.032503e+09 1/s, i.e. tau = 124.4942 ps against the
     measured 124.49 ps.  So there are exactly three factors and no
     free parameter.

13b. Which of the three the model can own.
       sigma*v = pi r_e^2 c   CLASSICAL IN FORM: the geometric disc of
                              the classical electron radius times c.
                              The model carries r_e and c already.
       the factor 4           spin statistics, the singlet channel.
                              An import, but a counting one.
       <n(0)>                 THE DENSITY OF THE RELATIVE COORDINATE AT
                              CONTACT.  This is what the model cannot
                              supply, and the reason is structural.

13c. Why it is structural, measured rather than argued.  A finite n(0)
     needs the orbit to pass through the origin, i.e. L -> 0 and e -> 1.
     Classical radiation reaction does the OPPOSITE: it circularizes.
     Started from the sampled band (CREM_INITIAL_BAND=1, seed 42, para)
     the eccentricity falls monotonically through the cascade:

       t(ps)      a(m)         e         L(hbar)
         0.000  1.1159e-10   0.078766   1.02365
       214.845  3.7855e-11   0.035280   0.59769
       229.336  8.7045e-12   0.029940   0.28666
       229.388  8.6984e-12   0.003764   0.28668
       229.440  8.6924e-12   0.000000   0.28687   <- and zero thereafter

     Fitted over the first two photons, e ~ a^0.74.  So the model's own
     dynamics drives the orbit AWAY from the only configuration that
     would have contact.  The channel is not small here; it is exactly
     closed, and radiation closes it.

13d. And the orbit stops short of the contact radius anyway: the
     smallest separation reached is a = 2.718e-14 m = 9.6 r_e, so the
     volume r < r_e is never entered and the density there is zero, not
     merely small.

13e. So the answer to "how to get a rate" is one import, and a precise
     one: <n(0)>.  Not a mechanism, not a coupling, not a
     cross-section -- the single quantum fact that a bound state has
     finite amplitude at zero separation while a classical orbit, being
     a curve, has none.  Supply that one number and the rest of the
     rate is classical and comes out at 124.4942 ps.

==================================================================
14. AUDIT OF THE 2gamma/3gamma SELECTION
==================================================================

14a. The channel is chosen by the FLAG, not by the dynamics.
     annihilationPhotonEnergiesFor(W, para, stream) is called with
     para = (selectedPhenomenon==1), fixed before the inspiral starts.
     The moments are prepared to match that choice; they do not make it.

14b. Ortho's configuration is exactly preserved.  Over every trajectory
     measured, endCos = -1 to 14 digits (drift 3e-15 .. 2e-14), so
     |S1+S2| = 1 hbar exactly and, with the orbital L now at 0.095 hbar,
     |J| lies in [0.90, 1.10] -- which a Landau-Yang gate reads as J=1
     and therefore 3 gamma, agreeing with the flag on every trajectory.

14c. Para's configuration is DESTROYED in 42% of trajectories.  Twelve
     para trajectories, master seed 42, terminal state:

       endCos     |S|     L_orb      |J| range        gate    class
        0.6239   0.4337   0.0959   [0.338, 0.530]     2g     para
        0.7151   0.3774   0.0967   [0.281, 0.474]     2g     para
        0.8682   0.2567   0.0950   [0.162, 0.352]     2g     para
        0.0370   0.6939   0.0967   [0.597, 0.791]     3g    ortho
        0.8249   0.2959   0.0960   [0.200, 0.392]     2g     para
       -0.1454   0.7568   0.0965   [0.660, 0.853]     3g    ortho
        0.8369   0.2855   0.0960   [0.190, 0.382]     2g     para
        0.2455   0.6142   0.0980   [0.516, 0.712]     3g    ortho
        0.3554   0.5677   0.0961   [0.472, 0.664]     3g    ortho
        0.8757   0.2493   0.0954   [0.154, 0.345]     2g     para
        0.1870   0.6376   0.0968   [0.541, 0.734]     3g    ortho
        0.7566   0.3488   0.0970   [0.252, 0.446]     2g     para

     crossed the model's own cos>=0.5 threshold : 5/12 = 42%
     a J selection rule would say 3g, not 2g    : 5/12 = 42%

     The same five trajectories in both columns.  So the model emits
     2 gamma from a terminal configuration that its own classifier,
     and a selection rule on the total angular momentum, both call
     ortho.

14d. The drift is always downward and is PRE-EXISTING, not caused by
     the L<->S closure.  Measured on c70ab4b (before it): endCos 0.478,
     0.079, 0.850, 0.219 on indices 2,3,5,7 against 0.868, 0.037,
     -0.145, 0.246 after -- comparable magnitudes, comparable crossing
     rate.  Para's aligned configuration is the UNSTABLE fixed point of
     the dipole-dipole dynamics, which is why only it drifts.

14e. One thing the L<->S closure did buy here.  A selection rule on J
     needs J to be spin-dominated, i.e. L_orb << |S|.  Before the
     closure L_orb was 0.215 hbar and the gate got 3 of 4 ortho
     trajectories WRONG (section 2 of this file).  With L_orb now
     0.095 hbar against |S| = 1 it is right on every one.  The gate
     became usable for ortho; para is what it cannot follow, because
     there the model's own |S| is no longer 0.

==================================================================
15. LETTING THE DYNAMICS CHOOSE THE CHANNEL
==================================================================

15a. The rule.  Momentum conservation makes a two-photon final state
     back to back, defining one axis; each photon has helicity +-1
     along that axis, so the pair can carry projection 0 or +-2 but
     never +-1.  A J=1 state therefore cannot go to two photons
     (Landau-Yang).  Implemented as: round |J| = |L_orb + S1 + S2| to
     the nearest integer and refuse two photons exactly when it is 1.
     annihilationPhotonEnergiesFor no longer sees selectedPhenomenon.

15b. Result, 12 trajectories per channel, master seed 42:

       prepared   2 gamma   3 gamma   |J| range        agrees with
                                                       its preparation
       para          7         5      [0.208, 0.738]      7/12 = 58%
       ortho         0        12      [0.917, 1.092]     12/12 = 100%

15c. How to read that.  ORTHO IS NOW A RESULT, not a label, and it
     comes out right on every trajectory: the anti-aligned
     configuration is the stable fixed point, |S1+S2| stays at 1 hbar
     to 14 digits, and with L_orb at 0.095 hbar the total rounds to 1
     every time.

     PARA IS RIGHT 58% OF THE TIME, and the other 42% is not a bug in
     the gate -- it is the model's known destruction of the singlet,
     now visible in the OBSERVABLE instead of hidden in the internal
     state.  The aligned configuration is the unstable fixed point, so
     by the time the pair annihilates it no longer has S=0, and the
     selection rule correctly refuses two photons for it.  Before this
     change the model emitted 2 gamma regardless and the defect did
     not appear in the output at all.

15d. WHAT IS STILL IMPORTED, and it has to be said plainly.  |J| comes
     out at 0.208, 0.738, 0.917, 1.092 -- classical continuous values,
     not integers.  Rounding them is the assumption that J is
     QUANTIZED, and that is an import, the same kind as the three the
     README already lists.  What conservation supplies for free is only
     the implication "J=1 cannot go to two photons"; deciding that a
     state with |J|=0.74 IS a J=1 state is not conservation, it is
     quantization.  So this replaces a strong import (the channel
     itself, taken from the command line) with a weaker one, rather
     than removing an import.

15e. Output change to be aware of: the leading-photon panel now mixes
     the two multiplicities within one --phenomenon batch, since the
     multiplicity varies trajectory by trajectory.  That is the
     mixture becoming visible, not a plotting fault.

     CREM_CHANNEL_FROM_FLAG restores the old behaviour for comparison.
     make validation 55/55 with the gate active.

==================================================================
16. REMOVING THE QUANTIZATION IMPORT FROM THE CHANNEL RULE
==================================================================

16a. The problem with the first version.  It rounded |J| to an integer,
     and |J| comes out at 0.208, 0.738, 0.917, 1.092 -- continuous
     classical values.  Calling a state with |J|=0.74 a J=1 state is
     quantization, not conservation.

16b. The identity that removes it.  Opposite charges invert the
     spin-moment relation, so cos(S1,S2) = -cos(mu1,mu2), and with the
     magnitudes held by BMT transport:

       (|mu1+mu2| / 2mu)^2  +  (|S1+S2| / hbar)^2  =  1   exactly.

     So the coherent total moment IS the two-photon weight and the net
     spin IS the three-photon weight, continuously, with the quantum
     endpoints falling out instead of being imposed:

       cos_mu   |S|/hbar   |m|/2mu    w_2g    w_3g
        1.000     0.0000    1.0000  1.0000  0.0000
        0.876     0.2490    0.9685  0.9380  0.0620
        0.355     0.5679    0.8231  0.6775  0.3225
        0.037     0.6939    0.7201  0.5185  0.4815
       -0.145     0.7566    0.6538  0.4275  0.5725
       -1.000     1.0000    0.0000  0.0000  1.0000

     Nothing is rounded.  And it is not a new quantity: the coherent M1
     channel already computes from m = mu1+mu2 and cancels exactly at
     cos = -1.  The weight is that same mechanism read as a branching
     ratio, so the channel is DRAWN rather than thresholded.

16c. Result, 12 trajectories per channel, master seed 42:

       prepared   w_2g range          mean     emitted 2g   expected
       para       [0.4273, 0.9379]   0.7575    8/12 = 67%      76%
       ortho      [0.0000, 0.0000]   0.0000    0/12 =  0%       0%

     Ortho gives weight exactly zero on every trajectory, so 3 gamma is
     certain with no quantization assumption anywhere.  Para gives a
     spread of weights reflecting how far the singlet has been
     destroyed; 67% against 76% is 0.7 sigma of binomial noise.

16d. What remains an assumption.  The ENDPOINTS are forced by the
     algebra above.  What is assumed is the INTERPOLATION between them,
     i.e. that the two-photon amplitude goes as the coherent total
     moment.  That is what the model's M1 channel already does, and it
     is the only interpolation linear in the amplitude that hits both
     endpoints.  Weaker than quantizing |J|, and it introduces no new
     constant.

     make validation 55/55.  Header isolation 37/37.

==================================================================
17. MAKING THE CHANNEL DETERMINISTIC
==================================================================

17a. What does NOT work: a geometric criterion on the orientation.
     A two-photon state carries projection 0 or +-2 hbar on its own
     axis, and projection 0 is achievable for ANY J by taking the axis
     perpendicular to J.  So 'does a permitted axis exist' forbids
     nothing and cannot discriminate.  Landau-Yang is not a condition
     on one projection; it is the vanishing of the whole amplitude for
     J=1 on EVERY axis, which is why it has to enter through the
     magnitude rather than the geometry.

17b. What does work: read the weight where the orientation is still
     exact.  At PREPARATION spin quantization fixes cos = +-1, so
     w_2g is exactly 1 or exactly 0 and the draw is degenerate --
     the channel is deterministic with no threshold and no rounding:

       para  idx 2,3,5   w_2g = 1   ->  2 photons
       ortho idx 2,3,5   w_2g = 0   ->  3 photons

     That is now the default.  The reason is not convenience: the drift
     away from cos = +-1 is a known defect of this model rather than
     physics.  Real positronium conserves S, so the annihilating state
     IS the prepared one; the classical pair loses the singlet because
     two classical vectors cannot represent an entangled one.  Putting
     that artefact into the photon multiplicity would place a model
     failure into an observable.

17c. The terminal reading is kept as a probe, CREM_CHANNEL_AT_ANNIHILATION.
     There para's weight spreads over [0.43, 0.94] instead of sitting at
     1 and the channel has to be drawn.  It measures how far the singlet
     has been destroyed; it is not a branching-ratio prediction.

17d. The rule degrades gracefully.  With --no-spin-quantization the
     PREPARED configuration is itself intermediate and the draw is
     genuinely needed.  Measured weights: para 0.763, 0.903, 0.910;
     ortho 0.067, 0.121, 0.706 -- i.e. the sampled band does not
     produce clean ortho, which is the same conclusion this project
     already records for the band.

     make validation 55/55.  Header isolation 37/37.

==================================================================
18. IS THE LOSS OF THE SINGLET A MODEL DEFECT OR PHYSICS?
==================================================================

The claim under test was: the drift of the mutual angle is a defect of
this model rather than physics, because real positronium conserves S
and a classical pair cannot represent an entangled singlet.  The
CONCLUSION survives; the MECHANISM I gave for it was wrong.

18a. The quantum side holds, and more strongly than I stated it.
     H_dd ~ [S1.S2 - 3(S1.n)(S2.n)]/r^3 has a scalar part (k=0) and a
     tensor part (k=2).  Wigner-Eckart kills both for S=0 -> S=1:

       transition          k   permitted S'   result
       singlet->triplet    0        0         VANISHES
       singlet->triplet    2        2         VANISHES
       triplet->triplet  0,2      1..3        allowed

     Two spin-1/2 have no S'=2, so the dipole-dipole interaction CANNOT
     mix singlet and triplet.  Not 'S is conserved in general' but: the
     very interaction that moves the angle classically is forbidden
     from moving it quantum mechanically.

     Caveat worth stating: the Zeeman term is rank 1 and DOES mix S=0
     with S=1,m=0 -- that is the measured magnetic quenching of
     ortho-Ps, and this model has --external-field.  A consistency
     note: for positronium mu1+mu2 ~ S1-S2, so the channel weight is
     exactly the operator that performs that mixing.

18b. The classical mechanism is derivable and explains the asymmetry.
     gamma_2 = -gamma_1, and the partner's dipole field satisfies
     B1 = -B2 for anti-aligned moments, B1 = +B2 for aligned.  So
       ortho: omega_1 = gamma_1 B_1 = -gamma_1 B_2 = omega_2
              -> both spins precess about the SAME vector, angle
                 preserved identically;
       para:  omega_1 = -omega_2
              -> the spins counter-rotate, angle changes.

18c. CORRECTION: it is a LIBRATION, not a drift.  Measured over 63
     checkpoints, seed 42:

       channel  start    min      max      end      span
       para     +1.000   +0.322   +1.000   +0.800   0.678
       ortho    -1.000   -1.000   -1.000   -1.000   0.000

     Para's cosine RETURNS TO 1 repeatedly -- it oscillates within a
     bounded range rather than running away.  And started from a
     sampled angle instead of the extremum the change goes BOTH WAYS in
     both channels: para 3 up / 5 down, ortho 4 up / 4 down.  So
     'always downward, the aligned configuration is unstable' was an
     artefact of starting exactly at the MAXIMUM of the oscillation,
     from which the first excursion can only go down.

18d. What survives.  The conclusion for the channel rule stands and is
     better founded: the terminal angle is a PHASE of that libration at
     the moment the trajectory stops, aliased by the checkpoint spacing
     (the precession period deep in the orbit is ~5e-6 ps against a
     0.023 ps checkpoint), so it measures nothing.  Reading the weight
     at preparation is right -- because the terminal value is a random
     phase, not because the singlet was destroyed along the way.

==================================================================
19. WHEN CAN EACH CHANNEL EMIT, FROM THE MOMENT CONFIGURATION
==================================================================

The model has two radiative channels during the inspiral and one at the
end, and the moment configuration enters exactly two of the three.

19a. E1, the electric dipole: DOES NOT depend on the moments at all.
     It comes from the charge separation d = q_eff r.  Measured over 63
     checkpoints, seed 42:

       channel   E1 min [W]     E1 max [W]
       para      1.1673e-08     3.8455e-04
       ortho     1.1673e-08     3.7804e-04

     Identical at the start and 1.7% apart at the end, which is the
     trajectories having diverged, not a channel difference.  This is
     the channel that actually fires every inspiral photon, and it is
     why the two collapse times differ by only 1.3e-4.

19b. M1, the magnetic dipole: depends on NOTHING ELSE.  It radiates
     from the coherent sum m = mu1 + mu2, so P_M1 ~ |m|^2:

       channel   |m|/mu           M1 share min    M1 share max
       para      1.626 .. 2.000   2.7392e-24      2.2375e-10
       ortho     0.000 .. 0.000   1.1532e-48      7.5789e-43

     PARA CAN EMIT M1, ORTHO CANNOT.  The two maxima differ by 32
     orders of magnitude, and ortho's residue is roundoff: |m| prints
     as exactly 0.000000e+00 at every checkpoint, and since both spins
     precess about the same vector (omega_1 = omega_2, section 18) the
     time derivative d(mu1+mu2)/dt vanishes identically too.

     So ortho's inability to radiate M1 is DYNAMICALLY PROTECTED, not a
     coincidence of preparation.  Para's ability varies over the
     libration -- |m|/mu runs 2.000 down to 1.626, a factor 1.51 in
     power -- but never vanishes.

19c. BUT NEITHER EVER ACTUALLY FIRES AN M1 PHOTON.  Para's M1 share
     peaks at 2.2e-10 of the radiated power.  The emission hazard is
     E1's throughout, so the moment configuration decides whether M1 is
     ALLOWED, not whether a photon is emitted.

19d. Annihilation: the same quantity decides the multiplicity.
     w_2g = |mu1+mu2|^2/(|mu1|+|mu2|)^2, so aligned moments give 1 and
     two photons, anti-aligned give 0 and three.

     That is the structural point worth carrying: ONE quantity,
     |mu1 + mu2|, gates both the M1 channel during the inspiral and the
     two-photon channel at the end.  It is the model's single mechanism
     with the shape of the C-parity selection rule, and it acts in both
     places for the same reason -- coherent cancellation of the two
     moments when they are anti-aligned.

==================================================================
20. DOES THE MODEL CONSERVE C-PARITY?
==================================================================

Three separate questions hide in that one, and they have three
different answers.

20a. IS THE DYNAMICS C-INVARIANT?  Yes, by construction.  C exchanges
     e- and e+, which on the model's quantities is q1 <-> q2, r -> -r,
     and mu -> -mu (since mu = gamma S and gamma ~ q, while the spin
     stays with the particle).  Term by term:

       quantity                             C   why
       q1 q2 / r          (Coulomb)         +   q1q2 unchanged
       d = q1 r1 + q2 r2  (E1 source)       -   d -> -d
       |d''|^2            (E1 power)        +   square of a C-odd
       mu1.mu2, (mu1.n)(mu2.n)              +   two C-odd factors
       m = mu1 + mu2      (M1 source)       -   m -> -m
       |m''|^2            (M1 power)        +   square
       q (mu2-mu1).(v x n)  (spin-orbit)    +   C-odd charge TIMES C-odd moment
       omega = gamma B    (BMT rate)        +   C-odd times C-odd
       dmu/dt = omega x mu                  -   consistent with mu being C-odd
       w_2g = |m|^2/(2mu)^2 (channel)       +   square

     Every observable comes out C-even, and every C-odd quantity
     reaches an observable only squared or paired with a charge.

20b. ARE THE STATES C EIGENSTATES?  No, and this is the real answer.
     In QED the +-1 comes from the EXCHANGE SYMMETRY of the
     wavefunction -- the singlet spin function is antisymmetric, the
     triplet symmetric -- and a classical configuration of two vectors
     has no exchange symmetry to be +-1.  There is nothing to conserve.

     The signature is measurable.  A C eigenstate must have zero
     expectation for every C-odd observable, and m = mu1 + mu2 is
     C-odd.  The model's para configuration has |m| = 2 mu, the largest
     value it can take.  So the classical para state FAILS the
     necessary condition for being a C eigenstate.  Ortho passes it
     (m = 0) but for the wrong reason: the vector itself vanishes
     rather than an expectation over a symmetric state.

20c. SO WHAT CARRIES THE SELECTION RULE?  Not a parity label -- the
     vanishing of a C-odd amplitude.  m is exactly the operator that
     connects C=+1 to C=-1 (for positronium m ~ S1-S2, which maps the
     singlet onto the m=0 triplet), and the model makes it vanish
     identically for ortho.  That is the right shape, reached without
     any parity quantum number.

20d. WHERE THE ANALOGY BREAKS, QUANTITATIVELY.  This is the part worth
     recording against the model:

       state      QM <m>   QM <m^2>/(g hbar)^2   classical |m|^2
       singlet      0              3                   1
       triplet      0              1                   0

     Quantum mechanically <m> vanishes in BOTH states, and <m^2>
     differs only by a factor of 3.  Classically |m|^2 is 1 for para
     and EXACTLY 0 for ortho, a ratio of infinity.  The model's exact
     cancellation therefore has no quantum counterpart: the triplet's
     magnetic-moment fluctuation does not vanish, it is merely three
     times smaller than the singlet's.  The model reproduces the SHAPE
     of the C selection rule through a quantity that, quantum
     mechanically, does not vanish where the model makes it vanish.

==================================================================
21. THE 1S E1 OBJECTION, CHECKED AGAINST THIS MODEL
==================================================================

The objection: positronium in 1S (L=0) cannot radiate E1, because
1S -> 1S with a photon is forbidden by angular momentum conservation,
Delta L = +-1.  Checked here, it lands harder than stated.

21a. THE MODEL NEVER OCCUPIES 1S, so the prohibition does not apply to
     the state it actually has.  Measured, L before the first emission
     is 1.0000 hbar, not 0: the ground configuration is the
     Bohr-Sommerfeld circular orbit n=1, k=1, which carries L = hbar.
     In quantum terms that angular momentum belongs to a P state, and a
     P state radiating E1 to an S state is allowed.  The model evades
     the objection by not being in 1S.

21b. BUT THE EVASION COSTS MORE THAN IT SAVES.  The model's own probe
     reports, for every photon, the angular momentum the photon demands
     and the amount the orbit actually surrenders:

       photon   n_eff    L before   demands hbar   surrenders   short   harmonic
         1     1.0000    1.0000        1.0000        0.4227    0.5773      1
         2     0.5773    0.5775        1.0000        0.3042    0.6958      1
         3     0.2724    0.2737        1.0000        0.1787    0.8213      1
         4     0.0815    0.1738        1.0000        0.1578    0.8422      1

     Every photon is harmonic 1, i.e. E1, and demands exactly one hbar.
     The orbit gives up between 0.42 and 0.16 of it.  The shortfall runs
     0.58 to 0.84 hbar per photon and GROWS down the cascade.

21c. WHY: THE MODEL QUANTIZES THE ENERGY AND NOT THE ANGULAR MOMENTUM.
     Energy leaves in discrete photons of hbar*omega, but the angular
     momentum is removed by the CLASSICAL law, angularExponent =
     -(1-e^2)/(2+e^2), which at e=0 is -1/2, i.e. L ~ |E|^(-1/2) for a
     circular orbit.  Verified against the measured ratios:

       photon   E ratio   L ratio measured   E^(-1/2)   agreement
         1      2.9999       0.577348        0.577361     1.0000
         2      4.4576       0.473278        0.473639     0.9992
         3      7.6954       0.349864        0.360482     0.9705

     The first three hit the classical law to four figures.  So the two
     bookkeepings are inconsistent with each other: a quantum of energy
     hbar*omega must carry a quantum of angular momentum, and here it
     does not.

21d. THE SHARPEST FORM OF THE OBJECTION.  L before the first emission is
     1.0000 hbar.  If the photon took its hbar, the orbit would land at
     L = 0 -- exactly the 1S state -- and further E1 would be forbidden,
     which is the objection in its original form.  The model removes
     0.4227 hbar instead and emits three more photons.  The entire
     cascade beyond the first photon exists only because angular
     momentum is not conserved photon by photon.

21e. Status.  The README already declares that 93.4% of the collapse
     runs below n=1 where no ladder exists, so the REGIME is a known
     declared limitation.  What is added here is the mechanism and its
     size: it is not merely that the ladder is absent, it is that the
     emission removes a fraction of hbar per photon, by a classical law,
     while charging a full quantum of energy for it.

==================================================================
22. FIXING THE FRACTIONAL-HBAR EMISSION
==================================================================

22a. The fix already exists, as CREM_SPIN_MAGNITUDE.  It takes the
     angular momentum a photon removes from the PHOTON'S OWN SPIN --
     the hbar-along-its-direction vector subtraction -- instead of from
     the classical secular law k(e) = -(1-e^2)/(2+e^2).  Three earlier
     routes to the same end are recorded at the switch and were each
     implemented, measured and rejected: a literal r x p (too small by
     v/c ~ 1e-3), a uniformly doubled spin vector (geometrically unable
     to DECREASE |L| once the kick is twice the model's own hbar scale),
     and solving for the magnitude along the sampled direction
     (unreachable on 10 of 10 seeds).

22b. THE RECORDED REASON FOR NOT USING IT IS STALE.  The comment said it
     'produces unphysical orbital elements', on a measurement of e^2
     after the first emissions reading -1.37, -0.487, +0.0152, +0.363,
     -8.83.  Remeasured now -- after this session's operator-split fix
     to the L<->S transport and envelope-shape fix to the secular step,
     both of which change what L is when a photon arrives -- it does not
     reproduce.  Ten emissions on four seeds:

       seed   emissions   e^2 after each
         2        1       0.7124
         3        3       0.0000, 0.0000, 0.3615
         4        1       0.4017
         5        5       0.0000 x5

     Never negative beyond machine zero.

22c. AND IT DOES WHAT IT WAS MEANT TO DO.  Angular momentum surrendered
     by the orbit against what the photon demands:

       magnitude source        n    mean     range
       classical k(e)          4    0.266    0.158 .. 0.423
       photon spin             10   0.879    0.451 .. 2.305

     So per-photon angular-momentum conservation goes from 27% to 88%.

22d. THE COST IS REAL AND IS WHY IT IS STILL NOT THE DEFAULT.  The
     resulting orbit is ECCENTRIC: e = 0.844 after the first photon on
     seed 42, against exactly 0 with k(e).  The eccentricity factor
     (1+e^2/2)/(1-e^2)^(5/2) is then 30.6, so the loss rate is thirty
     times higher, the checkpoints shrink accordingly, and trajectories
     censor on the default wall-clock budget -- the first run tried here
     reached 191.45 ps of a ~199 ps trajectory in 900 s and stopped
     unfinished.

22e. A CONSEQUENCE WORTH NOTING FOR THE ANNIHILATION QUESTION.  Section
     13 established that the classical model has no contact channel
     because radiation CIRCULARIZES the orbit and a circular orbit has
     zero density at the origin.  This switch does the opposite: it
     drives the eccentricity UP.  Whether it drives it far enough, over
     a full cascade, to open the contact channel was not measured here
     -- the trajectories are too expensive to run to the end at this
     budget -- and it is the obvious next question.

     Not made the default.  That is a production decision and it wants
     an ensemble, not the four seeds measured here.  The stale comment
     at the switch and the wrong one-line description in the
     configuration panel are corrected.

==================================================================
23. THE ORE-POWELL OBJECTION: THE 1000x IS ENTIRELY THE 3-GAMMA
    SUPPRESSION, AND WE HAVE ONE HALF OF IT
==================================================================

The objection: o-Ps has no E1 in the ground state and its 2-gamma
annihilation is C-forbidden, so it lives ~1000x longer purely because
it must go to 3 gamma, suppressed by 4(pi^2-9)alpha/(9 pi) ~ 1/1115.

23a. The formula checks out.
       4(pi^2-9)/(9 pi) * alpha = 8.977485e-04 = 1/1113.9
       measured 142 ns / 124.49 ps           = 1140.7
     Agreement 2.3%.

23b. Our channel separation is +1.28e-4 (ortho longer) against a
     physical 1140.7, i.e. too weak by a factor 8.91e6, about 6.9
     orders.  That is the 'seven orders' this project already declares,
     now stated against the correct target rather than a hand-wave.

23c. AND OUR E1 IS WORSE THAN THE OBJECTION SAYS.  In QM NEITHER
     channel radiates E1 from the ground state -- there is no lower
     state for either.  This model gives BOTH channels the same E1, and
     measured they are identical to the digit at the start and 1.7%
     apart at the end only because the trajectories have diverged.  So
     the objection about o-Ps applies equally to p-Ps here, and it is
     the same defect as the 1S one in section 21: the model radiates E1
     from a configuration that in QM cannot.

23d. CONSTRUCTIVE: WE ALREADY CARRY THE PHASE-SPACE HALF.  The
     Ore-Powell density inside annihilationPhotonEnergiesFor -- used
     only to sample the three photon energies, never as a rate --
     integrates over x in [0,1] to

       0.434802201  =  (pi^2 - 9)/2  exactly

     verified numerically against 0.434802201.  Multiplied by the
     8/(9 pi) prefactor that is 0.123023857, which is 4(pi^2-9)/(9 pi)
     to nine digits.  So the 1114 splits as

       Ore-Powell phase space :  1/8.13    <- PRESENT in our code
       one extra vertex, alpha:  1/137.04  <- ABSENT
       product                :  1/1113.9

     The model therefore holds the geometric half of the suppression
     already, in a function it uses for the spectrum shape and never
     for a width.  What it lacks is exactly one power of alpha, which
     is an annihilation amplitude -- the same thing section 13 found
     missing when it asked what would turn contact into a rate.

==================================================================
24. LOOKING FOR THE MISSING VERTEX
==================================================================

24a. The model already reproduces alpha^5, the power of the 2-gamma
     rate.  Both the annihilation lifetime and this model's collapse
     time scale as alpha^-5 * lambdabar_C/c:

       lambdabar_C / c                = 1.288089e-21 s
       measured tau_2gamma            = 2.0000 x alpha^-5 lambdabar_C/c
       this model's collapse time     = 3.2040 x alpha^-5 lambdabar_C/c
       ratio                          = 1.6021

     So the classical inspiral is not off by powers of alpha from the
     annihilation rate -- it is off by 1.6.

24b. And alpha itself is IN the model, as its own orbital velocity:

       measured v/c at a_pair = 0.003648676
       alpha/2                = 0.003648676   ratio 1.000000

     That is not a coincidence, it is the Bohr relation v/c = alpha/n
     evaluated on the orbit the model actually prepares.

24c. So BOTH factors of the 1/1114 are present as measured quantities:

       Ore-Powell phase space, from the model's own spectrum  0.123023857
       2 x (v/c at a_pair), i.e. alpha                        0.007297353
       product                                          8.977485e-04 = 1/1113.9
       physical 4(pi^2-9)alpha/(9 pi)                   8.977485e-04 = 1/1113.9

24d. THIS IS NOT A DERIVATION, and the weak point is worth stating
     plainly.  v/c is not a constant in this model -- it grows down the
     orbit:

       radius                v/c        2(v/c)/alpha   phase x 2(v/c)
       a_pair (start)        0.003649       1.00        1/1113.9
       0.05 a_pair           0.016317       4.47        1/249.1
       collision boundary    0.051600      14.14        1/78.8
       Compton barrier       0.085292      23.38        1/47.7

     Only at the STARTING radius does 2(v/c) equal alpha, because
     v/c = alpha/n is the Bohr relation and holds on the Bohr orbit.
     Choosing which v/c is therefore choosing the answer.  And knowing
     to multiply the two factors at all is precisely the QED content.

24e. CONCLUSION: the vertex is not a missing NUMBER -- both numbers are
     already here -- it is a missing RULE.  The rule is that one more
     photon costs one more power of the coupling.  Classical radiation
     theory has no such rule: a classical source emits any number of
     photons with no per-photon suppression, which is exactly what a
     vertex factor encodes and exactly what this model cannot supply.
     That is why two independent routes -- asking what turns contact
     into a rate, and decomposing the Ore-Powell suppression -- both
     land on the same single missing alpha.

==================================================================
25. DOES THE PHOTON-SPIN UPDATE REOPEN THE CONTACT CHANNEL?
==================================================================

Section 13 closed the contact channel structurally: classical radiation
CIRCULARIZES the orbit, and a circular orbit has zero density at the
origin, so there is nothing to annihilate from.  Section 22 found that
CREM_SPIN_MAGNITUDE drives the eccentricity the other way.  This is the
measurement of whether that survives a cascade.

25a. THE ECCENTRICITY IS SUSTAINED, NOT TRANSIENT.  Four seeds, para,
     retardation limit relaxed to 5, 1200 s budget each:

       seed  ckpts   e max   e final   a min (m)   peri/a   peri/r_e
         2    182    0.8440   0.8354   3.184e-11   0.1646     1860
         3    301    0.6105   0.6105   1.747e-11   0.3895     2415
         4    261    0.6338   0.6253   3.466e-11   0.3747     4585
         5    718    0.0181   0.0000   2.637e-11   1.0000     9278

     Against the default, where e is 0.000000 EXACTLY at every
     checkpoint and peri/a is 1 throughout.

     On three seeds of four the final eccentricity equals the maximum to
     within 1-2%, so radiation is not pulling it back down: the
     circularization that closed the channel is BROKEN.  On the fourth
     it never left zero, so the effect is not universal.

25b. WHAT THIS DOES NOT SHOW.  Every run was censored by wall clock,
     having reached only a ~ 1.7-3.5e-11 m against the default's
     terminal 2.718e-14 m -- three orders short.  So the periapsis
     these orbits would END at is not measured.  The cost is why: seed
     5 needed 718 checkpoints and 7.5 million revolutions to reach
     754 ps, against 63 checkpoints to 199 ps in the default.

25c. The extrapolation, flagged as unverified.  If e = 0.835 survived to
     the depth the default reaches, the periapsis would be 4.48e-15 m =
     1.59 r_e, i.e. contact, against the default's 9.6 r_e.  That is an
     extrapolation across three orders of magnitude in a and there is no
     evidence here that the eccentricity survives it.

25d. So the honest status: the STRUCTURAL obstacle of section 13 is
     removed -- radiation no longer circularizes, so 'the density at the
     origin is identically zero' is no longer true -- but whether the
     contact radius is actually reached is open, and answering it needs
     trajectories roughly thirty times more expensive than the default.

26. THE ONE LONG TRAJECTORY: WHERE DOES THE PERIAPSIS ACTUALLY STOP?

    Section 25c left an extrapolation that assumed e stays at 0.835 all
    the way down, and admitted there was no evidence for it.  This
    section replaces the assumption with a measured scaling law, and
    then tests the extrapolation method itself on runs that DO finish.

26a. WHAT THE DEFAULT RUN ACTUALLY DOES.  The same probe, same seeds,
     default switches, 300 s budget -- both finish in well under it:

       seed  stop            peri/barrier  P/t_light   L (hbar)  L/L_contact
         2   RetardationLimit    3.897         84.90    0.09500     2.223
         3   RetardationLimit    5.114         86.18    0.09665     2.262

     Both stop on the RETARDATION limit, not on the Compton barrier,
     which is exactly the doubt raised earlier: the default trajectory
     does not stop on physics, it stops on the numerical safety margin,
     at a periapsis four to five times the barrier and an angular
     momentum twice contact.  Both end exactly circular: h^2/(Aa) = 1.

26b. THE TWO VARIANTS DIFFER IN ECCENTRICITY, NOT IN DEPTH REACHED.
     Over the whole default run the eccentricity never leaves zero
     (max 0.0166, median 0.0000); under CREM_SPIN_MAGNITUDE=1 it sits
     at 0.83.  Compared at the SAME semi-major axis:

       a (m)      default e   default peri   spin=1 e   spin=1 peri   ratio
       5.0e-11      0.0000      12528 r_e     0.8440      1953 r_e     6.4x
       3.0e-11      0.0000      12520 r_e     0.8300      1810 r_e     6.9x
       2.7e-11      0.0000      12520 r_e     0.8202      1723 r_e     7.3x

     So the photon-spin update buys a factor 6-7 in periapsis at fixed
     a.  It does not buy depth: in ~195 ps the default contracts a by
     13.3x in 63 checkpoints, the spin=1 variant by 4.0x in 456.

26c. THE SCALING LAW, MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.  Section 25c held e
     fixed.  It is not fixed; what is stable is the exponent in
     L ~ a^p, fitted on the last 80% of each deep run and checked in
     four consecutive windows:

       seed  ckpts   t (ps)   p      windows                  trend
         2    468     194.9   0.266  0.255 0.269 0.281 0.234  stable
         3    701     975.4   0.660  0.719 0.654 0.664 0.679  stable

     p = 0.5 is the circular relation L = sqrt(mu k a), i.e. e constant.
     p < 0.5 means L falls more slowly than a^(1/2), so the orbit
     CIRCULARIZES; p > 0.5 means the eccentricity GROWS.  The two seeds
     fall on opposite sides, and both are far from 0.5, so section 25c's
     "e stays at 0.835" is wrong in both directions.

26d. WHERE EACH ONE LANDS.  Marching a downward under its own p, with L
     clamped at the circular ceiling and e recovered from
     L^2 = mu k a (1-e^2):

       seed  route                         stops on   a (m)      e       L (hbar)
         2   circularizes at a~2e-12,      barrier    1.93e-13  0.0000   0.0427
             then spirals in as a circle
         3   eccentricity grows to 0.85    barrier    1.28e-12  0.8530   0.0574

     L_contact at the barrier is 0.042737 hbar.  Both routes land on it
     to within 0 and +34%.  The retardation limit, relaxed to 5, is not
     what stops either: it would permit a down to ~3.6e-15 m.

     So the answer to "does L actually come down to 0.04 hbar" is YES,
     and the stop is then the Compton barrier -- the model-validity
     limit -- rather than the numerical margin.  The answer to "does it
     reach contact at r_e" is NO: that needs L = 0.005160 hbar, a
     further factor 8, and the barrier is 68.6 r_e by construction.
     Section 25c's 1.59 r_e was an artefact of holding e fixed.

26e. TESTING THE METHOD, AND THE LIMIT OF THAT TEST.  Fit L ~ a^p on
     checkpoints 20-60% of each DEFAULT run and extrapolate to that
     run's own measured terminal a:

       seed   fitted p   L predicted   L measured   error
         2     0.500       0.09489      0.09500     -0.1%
         3     0.500       0.09654      0.09665     -0.1%

     0.1% -- but this is a DEGENERATE test and must not be quoted as
     validation of the deep extrapolations.  The default trajectory is
     circular throughout, so L = sqrt(mu k a) identically and any fit
     recovers p = 0.500 by construction.  What the test confirms is the
     bookkeeping chain (period -> a -> L), not the extrapolation of an
     eccentric track across two orders of magnitude in a.

26f. WHAT WAS NOT DONE.  Neither deep run reached its own stop.  At
     5400 s of budget they had covered a factor 4.1 (seed 2) and 6.6
     (seed 3) in a, against the factor ~140 needed to reach the barrier.
     Extrapolating the observed contraction rate, each run needs roughly
     6000-10000 s more.  The results in 26c and 26d are therefore fits
     to a censored track, not terminal measurements, and are reported as
     such.

27. THE SINGLET CLAIM, TAKEN APART

    The sentence under test: "the classical pair loses the singlet because
    two classical vectors cannot represent an entangled state; that is why
    the weight is read at preparation, and CREM_CHANNEL_AT_ANNIHILATION
    shows the scale of the defect, 0.43 to 0.94."  Four assertions.  Three
    are wrong.  The fourth is right for a different reason.

27a. THE MECHANISM IS FIELD PARITY, NOT ENTANGLEMENT.  The mutual magnetic
     field has two parts with different parity under the exchange
     r -> -r, 1 <-> 2.  Measured separately at 0.2 a_pair by switching one
     off (tools probe, scratch):

       part                          |B1|      |B2|     B1.B2/|B||B|  parity
       motional (charge only), para  87.47     87.47      -1          odd
       motional (charge only), ortho 87.47     87.47      -1          odd
       dipole (moment only), para    97.89     97.89      +1          EVEN
       dipole (moment only), ortho   97.89     97.89      -1          odd
       both, para                    10.44    185.37      +1          --
       both, ortho                  185.37    185.37      -1          --

     The motional part is odd always; the dipole part is odd only for
     anti-aligned moments.  With -q1/m1 = -(-q2/m2):

       ortho: both parts odd -> B2 = -B1 exactly -> omega1 = omega2
              exactly.  Measured |omega1-omega2| = 0 at all 6551 substeps.
              Two identical rotation vectors acting on two anti-parallel
              moments preserve anti-parallelism exactly and forever.  This
              is an exact symmetry of the configuration, NOT stability.
       para:  the parts have opposite parity -> the two fields are neither
              equal nor opposite (10.44 against 185.37, a factor 17.8) ->
              omega1 != +-omega2.  Measured in production:
              |omega1-omega2|/|omega1| = 0.335.

     README previously derived omega1 = -omega2 for para from the dipole
     part alone.  That is wrong: it predicts a relative rate difference of
     2, and the measurement is 0.335.

     Consequence for how the 12/12 ortho result is quoted: it is not a test
     the model could have failed.  Ortho is preserved by construction.

27b. THE SINGLET IS RECOVERED, REPEATEDLY.  CREM_DEBUG_ALIGN now takes a
     sample count, so the angle can be followed at every spin-transport
     substep instead of at 63 checkpoints.  Para, full trajectory, 6193
     substeps: 3072 increments up, 3119 down, 121 sign changes.  Ortho:
     6551 substeps, zero increments either way.

     The pair's total spin obeys |S|/hbar = sqrt(2(1-cos))/2 identically
     (checked to 1.5e-8 against the new Smag field in the skip census):

       channel   |S| min      |S| max     |S| final
       para      0.000000     0.582287    0.295978
       ortho     1.000000     1.000000    1.000000

     Para's total spin oscillates between the singlet value 0 and 0.58 hbar
     and returns to exactly zero.  Over six seeds, cos exceeds 0.999
     between 52 and 192 times per run, the last time at 81-98% of the way
     through.  A state that returns to the singlet two hundred times, and
     is still doing it at the end, is not a destroyed state.

27c. THE AMPLITUDE IS RESET AT PHOTONS, NOT DAMPED.  In 5%-of-run windows
     the angle's standard deviation does not fall smoothly; it steps,
     exactly twice in a two-photon run:

       substep bound 0.05  : sigma 0.26 -> 0.16 -> 0.03
       substep bound 0.0125: sigma 0.27 -> 0.015 -> 0.18   (up again)

     An earlier six-segment reading of the same data looked like monotone
     damping.  It was an artefact of the segment boundaries; the finer step
     shows the amplitude rising again after the second photon.

27d. THE BAND CONVERGES, THE PHASE DOES NOT.  CREM_SPIN_SUBSTEP overrides
     the per-substep rotation bound.  Same seed, same channel, two
     halvings:

       bound     substeps   cos min   cos mean   cos max   cos FINAL
       0.05       5561       0.2269    0.8024     1.0000    0.8801
       0.025     10457       0.2270    0.8065     1.0000    0.8234
       0.0125    20734       0.2270    0.8054     1.0000    0.4880

     The band is converged to four digits.  The pointwise difference on a
     common grid is 0.70-0.75, and the terminal value walks from 0.88 to
     0.49.  The band is established before the first photon; what the
     photons reset is not step-converged.

     Per-seed bands, six seeds, early phase (before the photons widen them):

       seed   cos min   w min     cos max   w max
         2     0.2269   0.6134    1.0000    1.0000
         3     0.4331   0.7166    1.0000    1.0000
         4     0.2381   0.6191    1.0000    1.0000
         5     0.1193   0.5596    1.0000    1.0000
         6     0.4197   0.7099    1.0000    1.0000
         7     0.3312   0.6656    1.0000    1.0000

27e. WHAT THE DEFAULT RESTS ON NOW.  Reading the weight at preparation is
     still right, and the justification is stronger than the one it
     replaces: the terminal weight is a libration phase that does not
     converge under step refinement.  Feeding a non-convergent quantity
     into an observable is an objection on its own, independent of whether
     the libration is the model's physics.  What must stop being said is
     that the 0.43-0.94 spread measures how far the singlet has been
     destroyed.  It measures where in a converged, bounded oscillation the
     clock happened to stop.

26g. THE DEEP RUNS FINISHED THEIR BUDGET -- OUT-OF-SAMPLE TEST OF 26c/26d.
     Both were censored by the 5400 s budget as 26f predicted, neither
     reaching its own stop.  But they ran 2.5x further than the data 26c
     was fitted on, which makes the extra checkpoints a genuine
     out-of-sample test of the exponent.

       seed  ckpts (was)   t (ps)   a (m)      e       L (hbar)  peri/r_e
         2   1243 (468)     200.1   1.044e-11  0.7509   0.2072      923
         3   1711 (701)     985.5   1.151e-11  0.6432   0.2524     1457

     The exponent does NOT hold out of sample:

       seed   p in-sample   p out-of-sample   drift    p on all data
         2      0.266           0.359         +34.9%       0.351
         3      0.660           0.562         -14.8%       0.584

     The conclusion does, and for a reason worth stating: refitting on all
     the data moves the predicted angular momentum at the Compton barrier
     from 0.0427 to 0.0427 hbar (seed 2) and from 0.0574 to 0.0562 hbar
     (seed 3), i.e. by 0% and -2%.  Seed 2's is pinned exactly, because
     once the orbit circularizes L = sqrt(mu k a) identically and the
     barrier value is geometry, not fit.  Seed 3's moves by 2% because
     its eccentricity at the barrier is what the fit sets, and L there is
     L_contact*sqrt(1+e), which is weak in e.

     So 26d's answer -- L reaches 0.04 hbar, the stop is the Compton
     barrier, contact at r_e is not reached -- survives an exponent error
     of a third.  What does not survive is quoting the exponents
     themselves as measured constants; they drift.

     Both runs remain censored: seed 2 covered a factor 10.1 in a and
     seed 3 a factor 9.2, against the ~140 needed to reach the barrier.

28. DOES THIS MODEL'S n=1 MATCH BOHR'S n=1 FOR POSITRONIUM?

    Two separate questions: does the number agree, and does the model sit
    on it.  The first is yes and is a tautology; the second is no.

28a. THE CONSTANTS AGREE TO MACHINE EPSILON, BY CONSTRUCTION.

       quantity                       value                 relative diff
       a_pair (model)                 1.058354419074555e-10 m   --
       hbar^2/(mu k)                  1.058354419074555e-10 m   1.22e-16
       2 a0 (CODATA 2018)             1.058354421806000e-10 m  -2.58e-09

       -k/(2 a_pair)                 -6.802846579039651 eV      --
       -mu k^2/(2 hbar^2)            -6.802846579039652 eV     -1.77e-16
       -13.605693122994/2            -6.802846561497000 eV     -2.58e-09

       sqrt(mu k a_pair)/hbar         1.000000000000000         exact

     The 1e-16 agreements are the same formula evaluated twice: a_pair is
     DEFINED as hbar^2/(mu k), so -k/(2 a_pair) is algebraically
     -mu k^2/(2 hbar^2).  This checks that the constants are entered
     correctly and shows nothing else.

     The 2.58e-9 against the tabulated values is real but is internal
     consistency of the entered hbar, e, epsilon0 and m_e against CODATA's
     own a0 and Rydberg, not a statement about positronium.

28b. THE PREPARED STATE IS EXACTLY THERE.  The default preparation is
     sharp (f = 1, f_r = 0), so e_0 = 0, L = hbar and E = -R/1 exactly;
     --level n lands on n^2 a_Ps with L = n hbar by construction.  The
     sampled initial-velocity band that used to blur this is now behind
     CREM_INITIAL_BAND=1.  Measured at the first checkpoint (after some
     transport has already run): a/a_pair = 1.000004448, e = 0.000000000,
     L_orb = 0.999998373 hbar, binding 6.802869 eV.

28c. THE MODEL DOES NOT STAY THERE, AND THAT IS THE ANSWER.  n=1 is an
     initial condition the model is handed, not a state it finds.  Six
     seeds, default configuration, para:

       seed   terminal a (m)   a_pair/a   binding (eV)   /E_1    stop
         2     9.5507e-13       110.8        753.85      110.8   retard
         3     9.8865e-13       107.1        728.25      107.1   retard
         4     9.7598e-13       108.4        737.70      108.4   retard
         5     9.8497e-13       107.5        730.97      107.5   retard
         6     9.7501e-13       108.5        738.44      108.5   retard
         7     1.0160e-12       104.2        708.66      104.2   retard

     Mean 107.7x, range 104.2-110.8.  From level 2 it is 173.2-177.0 times
     a_Ps.  Every one stops on the retardation limit, not on any energy.

     So: at t = 0 the model's n=1 IS Bohr's n=1, in radius, energy and
     angular momentum, to machine epsilon -- because it was given it.  Two
     orders of magnitude later the pair binds 754 eV instead of 6.80 eV.
     The model reproduces the Bohr ground state as a starting point and
     not as a state.

28d. A STALE NUMBER CORRECTED.  The comment on groundStateSpecificEnergy
     said the pair "ends 375 times deeper".  Measured, it is 108x from
     level 1 and 173x from level 2; 375 matches neither and came from a
     configuration that no longer exists.  Replaced with the measured
     figures and the configuration they were taken under.

29. DOES A PHOTON FALL ON n=3 -> n=2 OR n=2 -> n=1?

    No.  The model has no rungs, so there is no jump to fall on; it
    radiates along a continuous classical inspiral and the photon energy
    is hbar*omega_orb, the orbit's own frequency, never an imported level
    spacing.  What follows measures where the photons actually land.

29a. THE MODEL'S OWN RULE, AND THE TWO BOHR STEPS IT IS COMPARED WITH.
     For a circular Kepler orbit at n^2 a_Ps the binding is R/n^2 and the
     correspondence quantum is hbar*omega_orb = 2R/n^3, so:

       from n   |E| (eV)   photon 2R/n^3 (eV)   Bohr step (eV)   ratio
         3      0.755872      0.503915            0.944840       1.8750
         2      1.700712      1.700712            5.102135       3.0000

     Both ratios are exact rationals: 135/72 and 3.  Note the coincidence
     at n=2, where 2R/n^3 = R/n^2 identically, so the correspondence
     photon exactly equals the binding energy there.

29b. MEASURED FROM n=2.  New probe CREM_PHOTON_LADDER prints each photon
     against the orbit it left.  Seed 42, index 2, para, 400 s: five
     photons, the trajectory completing on the retardation limit.

       #   E (eV)        a/a_pair    n_E        hbar*omega_orb (eV)
       1      1.700691    3.999979   1.999995      1.700691
       2      4.810136    1.999990   1.414210      4.810136
       3     18.038936    0.828405   0.910168     18.038936
       4    102.950357    0.259023   0.508943    102.950357
       5   1046.804660    0.052059   0.228165   1046.804660

     Every photon equals hbar*omega_orb at its own orbit, to all printed
     digits -- the rule is exactly what the model does.

     Photon 1 DOES fall at n=2, to 2.5e-6.  But it carries 1.7007 eV, one
     THIRD of the 5.1021 eV the Bohr step needs, and it leaves the pair at
     n = 1.414210 = sqrt(2), not at n = 1.  It halves the semi-major axis,
     because at n=2 the correspondence quantum equals the binding.

     Photon 2 then steps OVER n=1 entirely: it fires at n = 1.41421 with
     4.8101 eV and lands the pair at n = 0.910168, below the ground state.
     So the pair crosses n=1 in mid-flight between photon 2's departure
     and arrival, and nothing is emitted there.

29c. MEASURED FROM n=3.  A 400 s run saw nothing (the inspiral time scales
     as a^3, so the first emission sits near 85 ns against the n=1
     collapse's ~148 ps).  At 2400 s it fired:

       PHOTON_LADDER  E = 0.503916009 eV  a/a_pair = 8.999970
                      n_E = 2.999995      hbar*omega_orb = 0.503916009

     Predicted 2R/27 = 0.503914561 eV; measured 0.503916009, agreeing to
     3e-6, which is the gap between n_E = 2.999995 and 3.

     So a photon DOES fall at n=3, to 1.7e-6.  It carries 0.5039 eV
     against the 0.9448 eV the Bohr 3->2 step needs -- the exact ratio
     135/72 -- and energy conservation puts the landing at
     n = sqrt(R/(R/n^2 + E)) = 2.323962.  It does not reach n=2.  That
     landing arithmetic is the one verified directly at n=2 in 29b, where
     photon 1's predicted landing of 1.414214 is photon 2's own measured
     n_E of 1.414210.

29d. THE IMPORTED LADDER CHANGES NOTHING.  Rerun with
     gBohrLevelPhotonEnergy on (--bohr-photon-energy): the five photons
     are IDENTICAL bit for bit.  The ladder branch gates on n >= 2, and
     the orbit has already radiated to n_E = 1.999995 by the time the
     first photon fires, so the gate never opens.  This confirms the
     standing claim at quantumFor's comment ("0 of 20 emissions engage the
     ladder") at a different level and with the energies printed.

29e. THE HONEST ONE-LINE ANSWER.  A photon is emitted very near n=2, and
     never near n=1 or n=2 as a LANDING point.  Its energy is a third of
     the Bohr 2->1 step, and the sequence of landings is
     2 -> 1.414 -> 0.910 -> 0.509 -> 0.228, which is a geometric-looking
     cascade through the Bohr ladder rather than down it.

30. WHAT THE MOMENT CONFIGURATION SUPPLIES AS A SCALE

    Asked while planning how to free the model from its imposed n=1: can
    the magnetic-moment configuration set the energy levels itself?

30a. THE MOMENTS DO CARRY A LENGTH, AND IT IS THE COMPTON BARRIER.

       mu/(e c)              = 1.933035388e-13 m
       comptonBarrierRadius  = 1.933035388e-13 m   ratio 1.000000000000000
       mu/(ec) / a_pair      = 1.826453741e-03 = alpha*(g/2)/4

     The barrier is entered in physical_constants.hpp as g*hbar/(4 m c)
     with a localization argument.  It is algebraically the same number as
     the moment's own length mu/(ec), which is an independent reading of
     it: the pair's magnetic moment, divided by charge times c, IS the
     radius where the model stops.  Nothing was fitted.

30b. BUT THERE IS NO RESONANCE, SO NOTHING CAN BE QUANTIZED BY LOCKING.
     Scanned the ratio of spin-precession rate to orbital rate (para,
     moments along the orbit normal, circular orbit at each radius):

       a/a_pair    beta      w_spin/w_orb   r/lambda_C
       1.0e-01    0.0231       4.11e-04       27.41
       1.0e-02    0.0730       8.68e-03        2.74
       3.16e-03   0.1298       4.16e-02        0.867
       1.0e-03    0.2308       2.93e-02        0.274
       1.0e-04    0.7297       1.79e-03        0.027

     The ratio has a MAXIMUM of 0.0440 at r = 2.802e-13 m = 0.726
     lambda_C and turns over.  It is 22.7x short of 1 at its best, so the
     two frequencies never lock anywhere.

30c. WHY NO LEVELS EMERGE TODAY.  The emission rule is E = hbar*omega_orb
     and contains no moment at all, so the moments never enter the energy
     bookkeeping and have no way to select anything.  Section 29b's
     measured landing sequence, 2 -> 1.414 -> 0.910 -> 0.509 -> 0.228,
     shows no preference for integers.

30d. THE GAP IS ONE POWER OF ALPHA, AGAIN.  A length built from mu, e, m
     and c lands on the Compton scale; the Bohr scale is that times 1/alpha.
     Section 24 reached the same missing alpha from the annihilation-rate
     side.  Two independent routes to the same gap is the strongest
     evidence available here that it is real.

30e. THE ONE CANDIDATE FOR A SECOND SCALE is the zero-point field, which
     is the only ingredient carrying a scale independent of mu, e, m, c.
     Preliminary, from numbers the model already prints and NOT a
     measurement of the balance: the ZPF E_rms grows as omega^2 ~ r^-3
     against the binding field's r^-2, and the ratio is 9.93e-4 at
     a_pair, so the FIELDS equalize at 1.05e-13 m = 0.272 lambda_C --
     the Compton scale once more.  The SED balance is between POWERS, not
     fields, and has different exponents; measuring it is the single most
     important outstanding item.  Plan recorded in
     audits/2026-09-11-plan-uwolnienia-modelu.md.

31. CAN A HARMONIC LOCK MARK THE LEVELS?

    Section 30 only checked the 1:1 resonance and found it unreachable.
    That was the wrong test: locking can happen at any rational ratio, so
    the question is where the harmonic number N = w_orb/w_spin is, and how
    DENSE its integers are.

31a. THE HARMONIC NUMBER AT THE BOHR RADIUS IS 2/alpha^2.  Assuming the
     motional field dominates (it does at a_pair: the analytic motional
     rate 5.503e11 against a measured 5.513e11),

       w_spin = k_e v /(m c^2 r^2),  w_orb = v/r,  so
       N = w_orb/w_spin = m c^2 r / k_e = r / r_e = (2/alpha^2) n^2.

     Measured against it:

       n    a/a_pair   N measured    (2/alpha^2)n^2   ratio
       1        1         37492.4        37557.7      0.9983
       2        4        171408.4       150230.9      1.1410
       3        9        404965.9       338019.6      1.1981
       4       16        738412.6       600923.7      1.2288
       9       81       3905223.0      3042176.1      1.2837

     Exact to 0.17% at n=1 and drifting ~28% per decade away from it, so
     N = r/r_e is a LOCAL relation at the Bohr scale, not a law.  At n=1
     the pair makes 2/alpha^2 = 37558 revolutions per spin precession.

31b. THE HARMONICS ARE FAR TOO DENSE TO MARK ANYTHING.

       between   N runs from   to        integers crossed
       n=1,2       37492      171408        133916
       n=2,3      171408      404966        233557
       n=3,4      404966      738413        333447
       n=4,5      738413     1171810        433398

     Every radius between two Bohr levels is a resonance of some order,
     a hundred thousand of them per gap.  A condition met everywhere
     selects nothing.

31c. THE ONE STATIONARY HARMONIC IS AT THE COMPTON SCALE.  N is monotone
     through the whole Bohr region, so no harmonic is held there.  It has
     a single minimum:

       N_min = 22.71  at  a/a_pair = 2.6470e-3 = 0.7255 lambda_C
       band where N < N_min + 1:  a/a_pair 2.1931e-3 to 3.0874e-3,
                                  33.8% wide in radius

     dN/dr = 0 there, so the lowest harmonic is held across a third of a
     decade of radius.  That is the actual condition for resonant capture,
     and it is met at exactly one place in the model.

     It is not a Bohr level: n_E = 0.0514, binding 2570 eV against the
     6.803 eV of n=1 and the 1.022 MeV pair rest energy.  It corresponds
     to no recognizable threshold.

31d. ANSWER.  Yes, the orientation and precession do synchronize with
     orbital harmonics -- everywhere, which is why it cannot mark levels.
     The one harmonic that is stationary, and therefore the only one that
     could capture, sits at 0.726 lambda_C.  This is the third independent
     route (after 30a's mu/(ec) and 30b's frequency peak) on which the
     moment configuration points at the Compton scale and not the Bohr
     scale.

32. STEP 0 OF THE FREEING PLAN: DOES THE ORBIT-TIED ZPF BAND ADMIT A
    DISTINGUISHED RADIUS?

    The concern raised when the plan was proposed: the zero-point field's
