    band is tied to the orbital frequency ([0.5, 2] x omega_orb, 8 modes)
    and rides up with the orbit.  A field that looks the same at every
    radius cannot select one, and step 4 would be dead before starting.

32a. THE AMPLITUDE LAW IS THE CORRECT BROADBAND ONE, AND THAT DISPOSES OF
     HALF THE CONCERN.  makeZeroPointField sets amplitude = C*omega_orb^2
     with

       C = scale*sqrt(2 hbar (f_hi^4 - f_lo^4)/(8 pi^2 c^3 N eps0)),

     i.e. u = eps0<E^2> = hbar(f_hi^4-f_lo^4) omega_orb^4/(8 pi^2 c^3).
     That is exactly the integral of rho(omega) = hbar omega^3/(2 pi^2 c^3)
     over [f_lo, f_hi]*omega_orb.  Because the RELATIVE bandwidth is
     constant, a real broadband omega^3 field sampled at the orbital
     frequency gives the same omega^4 energy scaling.  The band-riding is
     a restriction to the part of the spectrum that acts, not a distortion
     of how it scales.

     What band-riding does still give up is the off-resonant spectrum and
     the phase history a fixed field would carry.  Those matter for the
     dynamics, not for whether a scale exists.

32b. THE FIELD'S GRIP ON THE ORBIT IS NOT SCALE-FREE.  Sampled directly
     (4096 phase points per radius, rms):

       a/a_pair   E_zpf (V/m)   E_bind (V/m)   ratio       r/lambda_C
       100        1.3899e+02    1.2856e+07     1.0811e-05  27407.2
       10         1.3903e+05    1.2856e+09     1.0814e-04   2740.7
       1          1.3915e+08    1.2856e+11     1.0824e-03    274.1
       0.1        1.3953e+11    1.2856e+13     1.0854e-02     27.4
       0.01       1.4071e+14    1.2856e+15     1.0946e-01      2.74
       0.001      1.4411e+17    1.2856e+17     1.1210e+00      0.274
       0.0001     1.5096e+20    1.2856e+19     1.1743e+01      0.027

     The ratio rises exactly as r^-1 (one decade per decade) and crosses 1
     near a/a_pair = 8.9e-4, i.e. r = 0.244 lambda_C.

32c. VERDICT ON STEP 0: THE BAND DOES ADMIT A DISTINGUISHED RADIUS, so
     step 4 is alive and the power balance is worth measuring.

     But the radius the FIELD ratio picks out is 0.244 lambda_C -- the
     Compton scale, for the fourth time, after mu/(ec) (30a), the
     frequency-ratio peak at 0.726 lambda_C (30b) and the stationary
     harmonic at 0.726 lambda_C (31c).

     This is a field-strength crossing, NOT the power balance.  Absorption
     is second order in a weak field while radiation is set by the orbit,
     so the two conditions sit at different radii and only the second one
     is the SED scale.  Measuring it is in progress.

33. STEP 4, FIRST ATTEMPT: THE BALANCE MEASUREMENT AS DESIGNED DOES NOT
    WORK, AND WHY

    Design: integrate the same circular orbit twice with the full engine,
    field on and field off, difference the final mechanical energies, and
    call the difference the ZPF contribution.  Scan the radius for a sign
    change.

33a. WHAT IT APPEARED TO SHOW.  20 orbits, 3 field seeds averaged:

       a/a_pair   r/lambda_C   dE_off (eV)   ZPF part (eV)   |ZPF/rad|
       1           274.07      +3.395e-05    -1.333e-01       3925
       0.3          82.22      -5.713e-03    -2.000e+00        350
       0.1          27.41      -1.401e-01    -1.214e+01         86.6
       0.03          8.22      -1.723e+00    -1.796e+02        104.3
       0.01          2.74      -5.763e+00    +1.834e+02         31.8
       0.003         0.82      numerical failure with the field on
       0.001         0.27      numerical failure with the field on
       0.0003        0.08      numerical failure with the field on

     A sign change between 0.03 and 0.01 a_pair, which is exactly the
     signature a balance radius would leave.  It is not one.

33b. THREE DISCRIMINATORS, TWO OF WHICH KILL IT.  At r = 0.1 a_pair:

     (a) Scale dependence.  Absorption from a weak field must go as
         scale^2, so contribution/scale^2 must be flat:

           scale    ZPF part (eV)   part/scale^2
           1          -3.811e+01      -3.811e+01
           0.5        -4.130e+01      -1.652e+02
           0.25       -1.299e+01      -2.079e+02
           0.125      -5.461e+00      -3.495e+02

         It varies ninefold, and the RAW contribution is larger at scale
         0.5 than at scale 1 -- non-monotonic in field strength.  This is
         not an absorption law; the response is not perturbative anywhere
         in the range tested.

     (b) Seed spread.  Six field seeds at scale 1, same radius, same
         everything else: +1.286, +3.210, +3.331, +1.788, +1.658 eV --
         against the -38.11 eV that seed 1000 gave in (a).  The sign is
         set by the seed.  The three-seed means in 33a therefore mean
         nothing.

33c. THE DESIGN FLAW, NAMED.  Differencing the INSTANTANEOUS mechanical
     energies of two trajectories that have diverged.  The band rides with
     the orbit, so the drive is resonant by construction and the
     perturbation accumulates; after 20 orbits the field-on and field-off
     runs sit at different orbital phases, and the endpoint difference
     measures that phase difference, not a secular drift.  Nothing about
     the sign change in 33a survives this.

33d. THE FIX.  Measure the ZPF work directly along ONE trajectory,
     integral of (q1 E.v1 + q2 E.v2) dt, or fit the secular trend of the
     orbit-averaged energy.  A work integral is immune to divergence
     between two runs because there is only one run.  That is what step 4
     must be rebuilt on.

33e. ONE RESULT THAT DOES SURVIVE.  At scale 1 the field destroys the
     orbit outright below about 0.003 a_pair (numerical failure with the
     field on, clean without it, at all three radii tested).  That is
     consistent with 32b: the ZPF field passes the binding field near
     0.244 lambda_C = 8.9e-4 a_pair, and an orbit cannot survive a
     transverse field stronger than the one binding it.  So the model's
     ZPF has a floor below which it cannot be run at physical scale, and
     that floor is again the Compton scale.

34. STEP 4 REBUILT: THE METHOD IS NOW RIGHT, THE STATISTICS ARE NOT

    Section 33 named the flaw -- differencing instantaneous energies of two
    diverged trajectories -- and the fix: measure along ONE trajectory.

34a. THE REBUILT MEASUREMENT.  Without the field, mechanical + radiated +
     Schott energy is conserved, and the engine records all three at every
     frame.  With the field on, the DRIFT of that same sum is the work the
     field did.  One run, nothing to diverge from, and the field-off
     residual is the method's own noise floor, printed beside every number
     it validates.

     r = 1 a_pair, 20 orbits, 400 frames: noise floor 4.77e-4 eV against a
     first four-seed mean of 1.30e-2 eV, a factor 27 above it.  The sign
     is now POSITIVE, i.e. the field pumps in, which is what the module
     says it does and the opposite of what section 33a reported.  The
     systematic artefact is gone.

34b. TIME SCALING SAYS THE MEAN IS NOT CONVERGED.  Four seeds, r = a_pair:

       orbits   ZPF work (eV)   work/orbit    work/orbit^2
         5        -9.765e-03    -1.953e-03     -3.906e-04
        10        +1.661e-02    +1.661e-03     +1.661e-04
        20        +1.299e-02    +6.495e-04     +3.247e-05
        40        +3.116e-02    +7.789e-04     +1.947e-05

     The sign flips between 5 and 10 orbits, and neither work/orbit
     (secular pumping) nor work/orbit^2 (resonant growth) is constant.
     Four seeds cannot tell them apart.

34c. THE SPREAD, MEASURED.  24 field seeds, r = a_pair, 20 orbits:

       n = 24          11 positive, 13 negative
       mean            +1.4810e-02 eV
       median          -7.3694e-04 eV      OPPOSITE SIGN
       sigma            7.8719e-02 eV
       standard error   1.6069e-02 eV      mean/error = 0.92
       largest value   +3.7625e-01 eV      4.6 sigma
       mean without that one seed  -9.0510e-04 eV   SIGN FLIPS

     The mean is within one standard error of zero, the median has the
     opposite sign to the mean, and deleting the single 4.6-sigma seed
     flips the mean.  At the Bohr radius, with 24 seeds, the model cannot
     even establish the SIGN of the ZPF work.

34d. THE COST, QUANTIFIED.  sigma/|mean| = 5.32, so 10% precision on the
     mean needs about 2825 seeds at ONE radius.  Each is a 20-orbit
     full-engine run (~13 s), so ~10 hours per radius and ~2 days for a
     five-point scan.  And the distribution is heavy-tailed -- one seed at
     4.6 sigma out of 24 -- so sigma/sqrt(N) UNDERSTATES what is needed.

34e. WHAT STANDS.  The radiated energy over the same 20 orbits is
     4.4017e-04 eV, matching the analytic Larmor value, so the loss side
     is right.  The crude four-seed means put |work|/radiation near 30 at
     a_pair and between 12 and 30 across the scanned radii -- but 34c says
     those means are not significant, so no balance radius is claimed and
     none should be quoted from this.

     Step 4 is therefore OPEN, with a correct method and a known price.
     It is the only route in the plan that can produce a Bohr scale, and
     it now needs a seed budget rather than a redesign.

35. PLAN STEPS 1 AND 2

35a. STEP 1 -- THE COMPTON BARRIER IS NO LONGER AN IMPORT.  It was entered
     as g*hbar/(4 m c) with a localization argument.  The same number is
     the electron's magnetic moment divided by charge times c:

       mu/(e c) = [(g/2)(e hbar/2m)]/(e c) = g hbar/(4 m c) = r*

     so r* is the only length that can be built from mu, e and c -- the
     length the moment configuration itself carries.  The localization
     reading is a second, independent argument landing on the same number,
     which is why it is now quoted as derived rather than chosen.

     Pinned in particle_species.hpp by

       static_assert(agreesTo(comptonBarrierRadius,
           magneticMoment(electron)/(elementaryCharge*speedOfLight),1e-15));

     BANDED, NOT EXACT, and the first attempt got this wrong: an exact
     static_assert failed.  The two sides associate the same
     multiplications differently and land 2.6e-16 apart, one or two ULPs.
     The band is still four orders tighter than the 1e-8 assert beside it.

     The expression itself is unchanged, so the constant stays bit-identical
     to every result measured with it.  Verified: the branch trace's
     default run reproduces t = 199.442442952 ps, a = 9.5507233815e-13 m,
     L = 0.0949953986509 hbar exactly.

35b. STEP 2 -- A CONTINUOUS STARTING RADIUS, AND WHAT IT SHOWS.
     --start-radius (metres, bounded by the Compton barrier below and 1e-6
     above) overrides a_n = n^2 a_pair; gInitialSeparationOverride carries
     it, CREM_START_RADIUS is the probe face.  Unset, the path is
     bit-identical.  Until now every trajectory this project ever ran
     started ON a Bohr radius, so "the model prefers the ladder" was
     untestable.

     Seven starts, para, seed 42, with the photon landing points:

       start (a_pair)   n at start   landings (n_E)
       1.0              1.0000       1.000 0.577 0.272
       1.5              1.2247       1.225 0.755 0.395
       2.0              1.4142       1.414 0.910 0.509 0.228
       2.5              1.5811       1.581 1.051 0.616 0.299
       3.0              1.7321       1.732 1.180 0.719 0.369
       3.5              1.8708       1.871 1.301 0.816 0.439
       4.0              2.0000       2.000 1.414 0.910 0.509 0.228

35c. THE CASCADE IS A CLOSED-FORM MAP WITH NO FIXED POINT.  Emitting
     hbar*omega = 2R/n^3 from a binding R/n^2 leaves R(n+2)/n^3, so

       n -> sqrt(n^3/(n+2)).

     Checked against all 20 measured transitions above: agreement 6e-7 to
     1.5e-4 on the early steps, degrading to 6e-3 on the last, where the
     continuous credit between photons is largest.

     n_new/n = sqrt(n/(n+2)) < 1 for every n > 0, so the map is strictly
     decreasing, and n = sqrt(n^3/(n+2)) requires n+2 = n.  THERE IS NO
     FIXED POINT AND NO ATTRACTOR.  Nothing in the model singles out an
     integer n, and this is now a closed-form statement rather than the
     inference from one start in section 29b.

35d. THE CASCADE IS MEMORYLESS.  Starting at 2.0 a_pair (n = 1.4142)
     reproduces the TAIL of the 4.0 a_pair cascade:

       from 2.0    1.414207  0.910161  0.508923  0.228108
       from 4.0    1.414210  0.910168  0.508943  0.227763
       difference  2.1e-06   7.7e-06   3.9e-05   1.5e-03

     Where the pair came from leaves no trace; only where it is now
     matters.  Step 2's prediction is therefore confirmed, and confirmed
     more strongly than expected -- not "no preference was observed" but
     "the map that generates the cascade provably has none".

36. PLAN STEP 3: AN EMISSION RULE WITH MOMENTS IN IT -- PREDICTION
    FALSIFIED

    The default quantum hbar*omega_orb contains no moment, which is why
    section 35c's cascade map has no fixed point.  Step 3 replaces it with
    hbar*omega_spin -- one photon per spin precession -- so the moments
    enter the energy bookkeeping for the first time.  Added as
    CREM_PHOTON_FROM_SPIN, an option rather than a replacement, so nothing
    already measured is endangered; with it unset the branch trace is
    bit-identical (t = 199.442442952 ps, 3 photons).

36a. WHICH RATE, AND WHY NOT THE OBVIOUS ONE.  The mutual libration rate
     |omega1-omega2| is exactly zero for ortho by the exchange symmetry of
     section 27a, so it would switch emission off in one channel entirely.
     max(|omega1|,|omega2|) is defined in both and is what "per spin
     precession" means.  The orbit is resized to the energy each call is
     given, a = k/(2E) with |L| scaled as sqrt(a), so the quantum tracks
     the emission's own orbit as the classical branch does.

36b. THE DIRECTION WAS PREDICTED BEFORE MEASURING, AND HELD.
     omega_spin/omega_orb is 2.7e-5 at a_pair, so the quantum is four to
     five orders SMALLER and the photons become far more frequent, not
     rarer.  The plan's "one photon per 23 orbits" was a statement about
     the frequency ratio, not about the threshold an energy rule sets.
     Measured, from n=2:

       rule        collapse    photons    photon energy   stop
       default      199.4 ps         3    1.70 eV first   4.94 barriers
       spin        2188.4 ps    122245    8.63e-06 eV     14.55 barriers

36c. THE PREDICTION THAT MATTERED IS FALSE.  The plan said the cascade
     would acquire a distinguished radius at 0.726 lambda_C, the peak of
     omega_spin/omega_orb.  Photon density per unit ln(a), retardation
     limit relaxed to 2 so the run reaches 1.118 lambda_C -- within a
     factor 1.5 of the predicted peak:

       r/lambda_C    photons        r/lambda_C    photons
        960.3         40784          67.9            670
        736.8         25755          39.9            347
        565.3         17958          23.5            108
        433.7         12847          13.9             47
        332.8          9066           8.2             17
        255.3          4830           4.8              7
        195.9          3982           2.8              1
        150.3          2733           1.7              0
        115.3          1409           1.3              1

     Strictly monotone over three decades.  No peak anywhere, and none at
     0.726 lambda_C, where the density is one photon per bin and still
     falling.

36d. WHY THE PREDICTION WAS WRONG, and it was my own error in the plan.
     A peak in omega_spin/omega_orb is a peak in photons per ORBIT.  What
     marks a radius is photons per unit ln(a), which is the energy
     radiated per ln(a) divided by the quantum.  The quantum hbar*omega_s
     grows far faster than the available energy as the orbit tightens, so
     the density falls monotonically and the ratio's peak never reaches
     the observable.  Conflating the two is what made the prediction look
     falsifiable in the right way; it was falsifiable, and it is false.

36e. WHAT STANDS AFTER STEP 3.  Putting the moments into the emission
     rule does not give the model a level, a preferred radius, or any
     structure at all -- it only changes the graininess of a cascade that
     remains smooth and monotone.  Three routes in section 30-31 said the
     moments carry the Compton scale and cannot quantize; this is the
     fourth, and the first one that tested it dynamically rather than
     kinematically.

     The protected results are intact: validation 55/55, header isolation
     37/37, and the exchange-parity signature is unchanged (ortho
     |omega1-omega2| = 0 exactly, para 0.335074, both identical to
     section 27a).

37. PLAN STEP 5: THE ALPHA GAP, AND THE PLAN'S OWN FRAMING OF IT WAS WRONG

    The plan said: no rule built from mu, e, m and c can give the Bohr
    scale, because that needs alpha, and alpha in QED comes from the
    vertex.  Then two options -- declare alpha as the single import, or
    record a negative result.  Measured, the first half of that is false
    and the second half asks the wrong question.

37a. ALPHA IS NOT MISSING.  It is in the model already, as the coupling
     itself, and now named:

       alpha = k e^2/(hbar c) = 7.297352573749e-03,  1/alpha = 137.035999000
       k = alpha hbar c            agreement 3.9e-16 (ULP)

     And every length is the reduced Compton wavelength times a power of
     it, with nothing left over:

       a_pair / lambda_C = 274.0720     = 2/alpha        (ULP: 3.7e-16)
       r*     / lambda_C =   0.5006     = (g/2)/2        EXACT
       r_e    / lambda_C = 7.297353e-03 = alpha          EXACT
       R      = alpha^2 m c^2 / 4 = 6.802847 eV

     fineStructureConstant and reducedComptonWavelength are now derived
     constants in physical_constants.hpp, and particle_species.hpp pins all
     four relations with static_asserts.  qed_reference.hpp had been
     defining its own copy of the same alpha expression; that duplicate is
     gone, so there is one constant with four uses instead of two
     definitions.

     So the model has ONE dimensionful input, lambda_C -- which the moment
     configuration carries, since r* = mu/(e c) from section 35a -- and ONE
     dimensionless input, alpha.  Both are ordinary measured constants, and
     alpha is an input in QED too.  Nothing is missing on that side.

37b. WHAT IS ACTUALLY MISSING IS A REASON TO SIT AT a_pair.  The model
     computes the Bohr scale correctly and then has no mechanism that
     selects it.  Classically, at FIXED angular momentum,

       E(r) = L^2/(2 mu r^2) - k/r,  minimised at r = L^2/(mu k),

     and with L = hbar that is a_pair to 1.1e-13.  So the classical scale
     comes entirely from the assertion L = hbar -- import 1 in the README's
     table -- and not from alpha at all.

     That assertion is a Bohr-model argument, and quantum mechanics
     superseded it: the real 1S ground state has L = 0, so the scale cannot
     come from quantizing angular momentum.  In QM the same minimum comes
     from hbar^2/(2 mu r^2), the LOCALIZATION energy, which has the same
     algebra and a different origin.

37c. THE HONEST RESTATEMENT.  The model's scale content is not six imports
     and not one alpha.  It is exactly one assertion, L = hbar, standing in
     for a localization energy the model does not have.  The other five
     imports are rules about discreteness -- the emission quantum, the spin
     orientation, the photon's hbar -- and rules do not set a scale.  That
     is why steps 1 to 3 could remove the barrier from the import list,
     free the starting radius and put the moments into the emission rule
     without any level appearing anywhere: none of them touched the one
     assertion that carries the scale.

37d. AND IT IS WHY STEP 4 IS THE ONLY ROUTE LEFT.  A zero-point field
     supplies exactly what L = hbar is standing in for: a fluctuation that
     resists collapse without fixing the angular momentum.  That is the
     mechanism, not the scale -- the scale is already right.  Step 4's
     power balance is therefore not one option among several; after 37b it
     is the only thing in the model that could replace the assertion.

     Its price is known from section 34d: about 2825 field seeds per
     radius, and a heavy tail that makes that an underestimate.

38. STEP 4 AGAIN: ANTITHETIC PAIRING BUYS THE MEASUREMENT

    Section 34 left the method correct and the statistics hopeless -- 24
    seeds gave mean/error = 0.92 and a price of ~2825 seeds per radius.
    Paying that was the wrong move; the noise had structure to exploit.

38a. THE TRICK, AND WHY IT APPLIES EXACTLY HERE.  The field enters
     ZeroPointField::sample only through sin and cos of a phase carrying
     +phase[n], so adding pi to every phase gives exactly -E and -B.  The
     work then splits:

       W = W1 + W2 + O(F^4),
       W1 = integral of q E.v along the UNPERTURBED orbit  -- ODD in F,
       W2 = the absorption, where v itself responds to E   -- EVEN in F.

     W1 is the large random part that averages to zero only statistically;
     W2 is the physics.  Averaging a field with its own negation cancels W1
     PER PAIR rather than over an ensemble.

38b. MEASURED, r = a_pair, 20 orbits, 12 antithetic pairs (24 runs, the
     same cost as section 34c):

       estimator      mean (eV)     sigma (eV)    error       mean/error
       single field   2.7775e-04    2.0128e-02    5.8104e-03      0.05
       antithetic     3.3262e-03    5.0670e-03    1.4627e-03      2.27

     sigma falls 4.0x, so 16x fewer runs for the same precision, and the
     significance goes from nothing to 2.3 sigma on twelve pairs.  Eleven
     of the twelve pair values are positive.

38c. WHAT THAT SETTLES.  The SIGN is now established: the zero-point field
     PUMPS ENERGY IN, which is what the module always claimed and what
     section 33a's broken estimator had reported backwards.  And the size
     is large -- |work|/radiated = 7.63 at the Bohr radius over the same 20
     orbits, with the radiated 4.3620e-04 eV matching analytic Larmor.

     If that ratio stays above 1 at every radius, the field always wins and
     this model's SED has no bound state at all -- which is the known
     self-ionization problem of SED hydrogen, not a bug here.  The balance
     radius, if it exists, is where the ratio crosses 1.  That scan is
     running.

38d. THE REMAINING PRICE.  sigma/|mean| = 1.52 after pairing, so 10%
     precision needs about 232 pairs (464 runs) per radius rather than
     2825 seeds.  Two of twelve pairs still sit at four times the others,
     so the tail is thinner but not gone.

39. STEP 4 IS BLOCKED, AND THE BLOCKER IS THE ZPF'S ORBIT-TIED BAND

    Antithetic pairing (section 38) made the work measurable.  The first
    thing it made measurable is that the quantity being measured is not the
    one the balance needs.

39a. THE WORK GROWS AS t^2, NOT t.  r = a_pair, 10 antithetic pairs per
     point:

       orbits   work (eV)      error      mean/err   work/orbit   |W|/rad
         5      -3.3098e-04   2.50e-04     -1.32    -6.6195e-05    3.015
        10      +8.9244e-04   5.63e-04     +1.59    +8.9244e-05    4.069
        20      +3.7703e-03   1.72e-03     +2.19    +1.8851e-04    8.593

     work/orbit is not constant; it rises.  Two independent readings of the
     exponent agree:

       from the work,   10 -> 20 orbits:  4.22x  ->  p = 2.08
       from |W|/rad,    10 -> 20 orbits:  2.11x  ->  p = 2.08

     (The radiated energy is exactly linear in t, so |W|/rad scales as
     t^(p-1) and gives the exponent independently.)

     p = 1 is secular pumping, which is what an SED balance is made of.
     p = 2 is RESONANT absorption: a driven oscillator on exact resonance
     gains amplitude proportional to t and energy to t^2.

39b. WHAT THAT DOES TO THE RADIUS SCAN.  |W|/radiated is therefore not a
     physical quantity -- it depends on how long the run was.  At a_pair it
     is 3.0 after 5 orbits, 4.1 after 10 and 8.6 after 20, and would keep
     climbing.  The scan below is all at 20 orbits, so its rows are
     comparable with each other and none of them is a balance:

       a/a_pair   r/lambda_C   work (eV)     mean/err   |W|/rad at 20 orbits
       3           822.22      1.3540e-04      2.09        4.790
       1           274.07      3.3262e-03      2.27        7.626
       0.3          82.22      9.4805e-02      1.90       11.104
       0.1          27.41      1.7899e+00      2.02       14.832

     The trend is real and monotone -- the field wins harder the deeper the
     orbit -- but "|W|/rad = 1 is the balance radius" cannot be applied to
     it, and the crossing an extrapolation would put near 200 a_pair is not
     a result.  No balance radius is claimed.

39c. THE CAUSE IS STRUCTURAL AND WAS HALF-ANTICIPATED.  Section 32a
     checked that the orbit-tied band preserves the correct broadband
     AMPLITUDE law and concluded step 4 was alive.  That was right about
     the amplitude and wrong about the dynamics: because the band rides
     with the orbit, a mode at omega_orb stays at omega_orb forever, so the
     drive never decoheres.  A real broadband zero-point field has a finite
     correlation time -- the orbit drifts through the spectrum -- and gives
     absorption linear in t.  This one gives t^2 because it is permanently
     on resonance by construction.

39d. WHAT WOULD UNBLOCK IT.  A fixed broadband field instead of one tied
     to the orbital frequency.  The module's own comment says why the band
     was tied: its upper edge sets the integrator step, so a wide fixed
     band is expensive.  That is a real cost, not an oversight, and it is
     the next thing step 4 needs -- not more seeds.

     So step 4 closes here with the blocker identified and measured rather
     than with a balance radius.  Three things now stand that did not
     before: the estimator is correct (section 34a), its variance is
     tractable (38b), and the obstacle is a named modelling choice rather
     than statistics.

40. CORRECTION TO SECTION 39: THE t^2 EXPONENT WAS NOT MEASURED

    Section 39 concluded that the ZPF work grows as t^2, that the ratio to
    the radiated energy is therefore run-length dependent, and that the
    orbit-tied band is the blocker.  A fourth time point contradicts it.

40a. THE DATA, NOW WITH 40 ORBITS.  r = a_pair, 10 antithetic pairs each:

       orbits    work (eV)              significance   work/orbit
         5      -3.3098e-04 +- 2.50e-04    -1.32       -6.6196e-05
        10      +8.9244e-04 +- 5.63e-04    +1.59       +8.9244e-05
        20      +3.7703e-03 +- 1.72e-03    +2.19       +1.8851e-04
        40      +4.1928e-03 +- 2.91e-03    +1.44       +1.0482e-04

     Pairwise exponents:  10->20 gives 2.08, 20->40 gives 0.15, 10->40
     gives 1.12.  The 1-sigma band on the 10->40 ratio runs 0.88 to 21.56,
     so p lies anywhere from -0.09 to 2.22.  Both p = 1 and p = 2 fit.
     THE EXPONENT IS NOT MEASURED.

40b. THE SECOND ERROR, WHICH MATTERED MORE.  Section 39a called the work
     column and the |W|/radiated column "two independent readings" that
     agreed on p = 2.08.  They are not independent: the radiated energy is
     linear in t by construction, so dividing by it is an algebraic
     rearrangement of the same two numbers.  Two views of one measurement
     were presented as corroboration.

40c. A THIRD CONTRADICTION, from the mode scan started to test 39c: 8
     modes, band [0.5,2], 6 antithetic pairs, work(10) = 9.4437e-04 and
     work(20) = 3.1186e-04 -- work FALLING with run length, p = -1.60,
     against the 10-pair run's +3.77e-03 at the same 20 orbits.  Six pairs
     and ten pairs disagree by a factor of twelve at one configuration.

40d. WHAT STANDS, AND WHAT DOES NOT.

       stands   the antithetic estimator and its 4.0x variance reduction
                (38b) -- that was measured against a matched control
       stands   the sign, weakly: the field pumps energy IN, at about
                2 sigma on twelve pairs
       stands   the method's correctness (34a): the field-off residual is
                the noise floor and the radiated energy matches Larmor
       FALLS    "the work grows as t^2"
       FALLS    "|W|/radiated is run-length dependent, so the radius scan
                cannot be read" -- the premise is unmeasured, so the
                conclusion is unsupported rather than wrong
       FALLS    "the orbit-tied band is the blocker" -- that was inferred
                from the t^2, and there is no t^2 to infer it from

     The radius scan's monotone trend (4.79, 7.63, 11.10, 14.83, 15.62 at
     3, 1, 0.3, 0.1, 0.03 a_pair) is at about 2 sigma per row and neither
     confirmed nor refuted here.

40e. WHAT STEP 4 ACTUALLY NEEDS, restated without the false blocker.  More
     statistics, at the price section 38d already gave: sigma/|mean| = 1.52
     after pairing, so roughly 232 pairs per point rather than the 10 to 12
     used above.  Every exponent, trend and ratio quoted from 10-pair runs
     in sections 38 and 39 is below that bar.

41. CORRECTION TO SECTION 38: THE SIGN WAS NOT ESTABLISHED EITHER

    Section 38c said the antithetic estimator settled the sign -- the field
    pumps energy in, at about 2 sigma on twelve pairs.  Four more pairs
    remove that.

41a. SIXTEEN PAIRS, r = a_pair, 20 orbits, same seeds extended:

       sample                       mean (eV)     significance
       first 12 pairs (section 38)  +3.3262e-03      +2.27
       all 16 pairs                 -6.2400e-04      -0.18
       16 pairs, largest removed    +2.8372e-03
       16 pairs, trimmed one each   +2.0172e-03
       median of 16                 +1.0678e-03       (11 of 16 positive)

     Pair 14 alone, at -5.2542e-02, is -3.7 sigma and flips the mean.  The
     sign depends on which pairs are in the sample, so it is not measured.

41b. A BETTER ESTIMATOR WAS TRIED AND DOES NOT HELP.  The endpoint
     difference uses two of 400 frames; least-squares fitting the drift of
     the conserved sum against time uses all of them.  Run on the SAME
     trajectories:

       estimator     mean (eV)     sigma (eV)    significance
       endpoint     -6.2393e-04    1.4211e-02       -0.18
       slope fit    -8.7635e-04    1.4167e-02       -0.25

     sigma ratio 1.00 -- no gain at all.  The reason is that the noise is
     not a random walk being sampled once at the end; it is a handful of
     trajectories that are strongly perturbed for their whole length, and
     fitting the whole length does not average those away.

41c. WHAT THE TAIL ACTUALLY IS.  Antithetic cancellation works only while
     the field is a linear perturbation.  E_zpf/E_bind is 1.08e-3 at
     a_pair, but the band rides with the orbit, so the perturbation
     accumulates over the 20 orbits and on some seeds stops being linear.
     Those are the outliers, and on them the odd-in-F part no longer
     cancels, which is why they dominate.

     That is a real statement about the measurement, not the earlier
     inference about t^2 dressed up again: it is read off the outliers'
     existence, not off an exponent.

41d. STATE OF STEP 4, WITH BOTH CORRECTIONS APPLIED.

       stands   the method (34a): field-off residual is the noise floor,
                radiated energy matches analytic Larmor to 4 digits
       stands   antithetic pairing reduces sigma against the single-field
                estimator -- 1.42e-02 here against 7.87e-02 in 34c, a
                factor 5.5 measured on comparable samples
       FALLS    the t^2 exponent (corrected in section 40)
       FALLS    the sign of the ZPF work (this section)
       FALLS    "the orbit-tied band is the blocker" as an inference;
                it survives only as the explanation of the OUTLIERS above

     Nothing about the physics of the balance is established.  What is
     established is the cost: at sigma 1.42e-02 against a mean of order
     3e-03, and a tail that puts 3.7 sigma single pairs in a sample of 16,
     the honest requirement is several hundred pairs per point AND a
     shorter run or a smaller field scale so the perturbation stays linear.

     Two announcements in a row were made from 10-16 pairs and both fell.
     No further claim from this measurement should be made below that bar.

42. STEP 4 MEASURED WHERE IT WORKS: 0.01 a_pair

    Sections 40 and 41 withdrew the exponent and the sign because 10 to 16
    pairs at the Bohr radius could not determine either.  The reason is now
    clear and it is not the method: at a_pair the ZPF work is small against
    its own fluctuation (sigma/|mean| = 1.52), while deep in the orbit it
    is large (0.33).  So the controls belong at the deep radius.

42a. THE WORK AT 0.01 a_pair IS WELL DETERMINED.  Running mean over
     antithetic pairs, 20 orbits:

       pairs   mean (eV)     error      significance
         4     3.7380e+02   7.16e+01       5.22
         8     4.1882e+02   4.21e+01       9.94
        12     4.7728e+02   4.29e+01      11.13
        16     4.6392e+02   3.77e+01      12.31
        20     4.3153e+02   3.92e+01      11.01

     Stable from four pairs on, 11 to 12 sigma, sigma/|mean| = 0.33.  This
     is the opposite situation to section 41a, where four extra pairs
     flipped the sign.

42b. A NOISY DENOMINATOR, FOUND AND REPLACED.  The |W|/radiated column in
     sections 38 and 39 divided by the radiated energy of ONE perturbed
     trajectory, which jumped between 1.64 and 14.27 on the same
     configuration.  The analytic Larmor energy for a circular orbit,
     20 P T with |d''| = e omega^2 r, is exact and checks out: 4.4293e-04 eV
     at a_pair over 20 orbits against the probe's measured 4.4111e-04, a
     0.4% agreement.  Every ratio below uses the analytic value.

42c. THE EXPONENT, MEASURED.  Same radius, 10 pairs per point:

       orbits   work (eV)             significance   work/orbit   |W|/Larmor
         5     1.4899e+02 +- 3.27e+01     4.56         29.80        13.455
        10     2.3495e+02 +- 4.71e+01     4.99         23.49        10.609
        20     4.3153e+02 +- 3.92e+01    11.01         21.58         9.742

       pairwise p:  5->10 gives 0.657,  10->20 gives 0.877,  5->20 gives 0.767
       weighted fit: p = 0.787 +- 0.160

     p = 1 is 1.3 sigma away; p = 2 is 7.6 sigma away.  SECTION 39'S t^2 IS
     REFUTED, not merely unsupported, and the growth is if anything
     slightly SUBLINEAR -- the pumping saturates a little rather than
     running away.  With it goes the inference that the orbit-tied band is
     a blocker: a permanently resonant drive would give p = 2, and p is
     0.79.

42d. AND THERE IS NO BALANCE HERE.  |W|/Larmor is 9.74 at 20 orbits and
     falling as t^(p-1) = t^(-0.21).  Reaching 1 would need a run 3e7 times
     longer, which is not an extrapolation worth making -- the statement
     that survives is that over every accessible run length the field pumps
     about ten times more than radiation drains at this radius.  The model's
     zero-point field does not hold the orbit there; it drives it outward.

42e. WHAT IS AND IS NOT SETTLED NOW.

       settled   the work at 0.01 a_pair: 4.3e+02 eV over 20 orbits at 11
                 sigma, positive, stable from 4 pairs to 20
       settled   the exponent there: p = 0.787 +- 0.160, so secular rather
                 than resonant, and section 39 withdrawn on measurement
                 rather than on lack of it
       settled   no balance at 0.01 a_pair -- the field wins by ~10x
       OPEN      the Bohr radius itself, where sigma/|mean| = 1.52 still
                 needs the several hundred pairs section 41d priced
       OPEN      whether the ratio crosses 1 anywhere; the scan's trend
                 with analytic denominators (12.78, 12.18, 9.74 at 0.1,
                 0.03, 0.01 a_pair) is roughly flat, which suggests not,
                 but only the last of those rows has the statistics

43. THE POWERED MEASUREMENT AT THE BOHR RADIUS: 400 PAIRS

    Section 41 withdrew the sign of the ZPF work because 16 pairs could not
    determine it.  Section 41d priced the fix at several hundred pairs.
    Paid: 400 antithetic pairs, 800 trajectory runs, four workers.  The
    numbers below are the complete sample; an earlier version of this
    section quoted the 388 pairs that had landed when it was written, and
    the last twelve moved the ratio from 2.052 to 1.924 and the sign test
    from 7.62 to 7.40 sigma, changing no conclusion.

43a. THE SIGN IS ESTABLISHED, AND BY STATISTICS THE TAIL CANNOT MOVE.

       statistic                     value                significance
       mean                     +8.5225e-04 +- 2.90e-04      2.94 sigma
       median                   +8.3348e-04
       sign test                274/400 positive             7.40 sigma
       Wilcoxon signed rank                                   6.20 sigma

     The sign test is the one that matters here: it is immune to the
     outliers that flipped the 16-pair mean, and 274 of 400 is 7.4 sigma
     from a coin.  THE ZERO-POINT FIELD PUMPS ENERGY INTO THE ORBIT at the
     Bohr radius.  That is what the module always claimed, what section 33a
     got backwards, what section 38c announced prematurely, and what is now
     measured.

     The mean's own convergence is slow and visibly tail-driven: across
     sample sizes the median sits between 8.7e-04 and 1.15e-03 while the
     mean wanders from 2.1e-03 down to 9.1e-04 as a -6.5 sigma pair
     arrives.  sigma/|mean| = 6.5, so 10% on the MEAN would need ~4200
     pairs.  The median needed about 100.

43b. THE MAGNITUDE, AGAINST THE ANALYTIC LARMOR LOSS.

       radius        |W|/Larmor      sample
       3    a_pair      4.765        12 pairs, 2.1 sigma  -- not usable
       1    a_pair      1.924 +- 0.655   400 pairs, 2.9 sigma
       0.01 a_pair      9.743        20 pairs, 11 sigma

     At the Bohr radius the field pumps about twice what radiation drains,
     and that is only 1.41 sigma above the balance value of 1 -- so a
     balance AT a_pair is not excluded.  Deep in, at 0.01 a_pair, the field
     wins by a factor 10 and a balance is excluded.

43c. NO BALANCE RADIUS IS CLAIMED, AND HERE IS WHY NOT.  Two well-measured
     points, 1.924 at a_pair and 9.743 at 0.01 a_pair, would give a ratio
     going as a^-0.352 and crossing 1 near 6.4 a_pair.  The third point
     contradicts that: 3 a_pair gives 4.765, which is ABOVE the a_pair
     value, so the trend is not monotone.  That point is 12 pairs at 2.1
     sigma and cannot settle it either way, which is exactly the situation
     a two-point extrapolation must not be built on.

     A further caveat that applies to every ratio above: they are all at 20
     orbits, and section 42c measured p = 0.787 +- 0.160 for the work's
     growth with run length, so the ratio drifts slowly as t^-0.21.  At
     a_pair the exponent is not measured at all.

43d. WHAT STEP 4 HAS PRODUCED, AFTER TWO RETRACTIONS.

       settled   the zero-point field adds energy to the orbit at the Bohr
                 radius, 7.4 sigma by sign test on 400 pairs
       settled   it adds about 1.9x the radiated loss there over 20 orbits,
                 and about 10x at 0.01 a_pair
       settled   the growth is secular, p = 0.787 +- 0.160, not resonant
       settled   the estimator, its variance reduction, and its noise floor
       OPEN      whether a balance radius exists.  At a_pair the ratio is
                 1.41 sigma from 1, so the answer could be yes just outside
                 the Bohr radius; the one intermediate point measured so far
                 is inconsistent with the trend and too weak to arbitrate.
                 Settling it needs ~400 pairs at 3 and 10 a_pair, which is
                 the same price again per point.

44. HUNTING THE CROSSING OUTWARD: 3 a_pair, AND WHY THE HUNT STOPS HERE

    Section 43 left one question with a price attached: does the
    pumped-to-radiated ratio cross 1 outside the Bohr radius?  400 pairs at
    3 and 10 a_pair were launched to answer it.

44a. 10 a_pair WAS ABANDONED ON COST.  Nothing had completed after three
     minutes there against two pairs at 3 a_pair, so all four workers were
     moved to the cheaper and more informative point.  The retarded history
     a wide orbit carries is what makes it expensive.

44b. THE 12-PAIR SCAN ROW AT 3 a_pair WAS AN OUTLIER ARTEFACT.  Section
     39b recorded 4.765 there and section 43c used it to reject a monotone
     trend.  209 pairs give 0.656 +- 0.755.  The old value is about 5 sigma
     away from the new sample, so it is withdrawn, and with it section
     43c's reason for rejecting the trend.

       radius        |W|/Larmor        pairs   sign test   from 1
       1    a_pair   1.924 +- 0.655     400     7.40 s     +1.41 s
       3    a_pair   0.656 +- 0.755     209     4.91 s     -0.46 s
       0.01 a_pair   9.743              20     11 s (mean) +11 s

     The sign is positive at every radius measured -- the field pumps in
     everywhere -- and the ratio is of order 1 to 2 across 1 to 3 a_pair
     against about 10 deep in.

44c. AND THE CROSSING STILL CANNOT BE LOCATED.  The difference between the
     two outer points is

       1.924 - 0.656 = 1.27 +- 1.00,  i.e. 1.27 sigma.

     Not significant.  Resolving a difference of that size at 3 sigma needs
     errors near +-0.24 on each, which is about 3000 pairs at a_pair and
     1700 at 3 a_pair -- roughly twelve times the compute already spent.
     Finishing the running 800 pairs would bring 3 a_pair to about +-0.34
     and the difference to 1.35 sigma, so it does not change this.

44d. WHAT IS ESTABLISHED, AND WHAT THE OBSTACLE ACTUALLY IS.

       established   the field pumps energy in at every radius measured:
                     7.40 sigma at a_pair, 4.91 at 3 a_pair, 11 at 0.01
       established   the ratio is about 10 at 0.01 a_pair and of order 1
                     at 1 to 3 a_pair; those DO differ significantly
       not established  any difference between 1 and 3 a_pair, hence no
                     crossing radius, hence no self-selected scale
       withdrawn     the 3 a_pair value of 4.765 from section 39b

     The obstacle is no longer statistics-in-general but one number: the
     ratio sits within about one standard error of 1 over the whole outer
     range, so the crossing is not a sharp feature to find -- it is a broad
     region where pumping and radiation are comparable.  Locating it would
     need the estimator's variance cut by another factor of 3, not more
     seeds at the present variance.

     A caveat that outlives all of this: every ratio here is at 20 orbits,
     and section 42c measured the work growing as t^0.787 +- 0.160.  If p
     is genuinely below 1 the ratios all drift with run length and a
     "crossing radius" is not a fixed property at all.  p = 1 is 1.3 sigma
     away and untested at these radii.

45. PAIRING ACROSS RADII: THE VARIANCE CUT SECTION 44d ASKED FOR

    Section 44d said the crossing needs the estimator's variance cut by
    another factor of three, not more seeds at the present variance.  There
    was a way to do that, and it comes from the same property that made the
    orbit-tied band suspect in the first place.

45a. WHY IT WORKS.  The band rides with the orbit, so one field seed gives
     the same mode directions, polarizations, frequency factors and phases
     at EVERY radius -- only the frequency scale moves.  A seed that
     happens to sit hot is therefore hot at both radii.  Running both radii
     on the SAME seed and taking the per-seed difference of the
     Larmor-normalized works cancels that shared part, which is most of the
     variance.

     Measured on 104 seeds, 1 and 3 a_pair, 20 orbits, antithetic within
     each:

       correlation between radii    rho = +0.765
       difference, unpaired         +0.769 +- 1.150   (0.67 sigma)
       difference, paired           +0.769 +- 0.603   (1.28 sigma)
       gain                         1.91x in sigma, 3.6x in seed count

     A first reading at 16 seeds gave rho = 0.922 and a 2.95x gain; that
     did not hold, and the estimate settled into 1.67 to 1.91 over 44 to
     104 seeds.  The 16-seed figure is withdrawn.

45b. THE ABSOLUTE VALUES AGREE WITH THE INDEPENDENT SAMPLES, which is the
     check that matters for a new estimator:

       radius       paired run (104 seeds)   independent run
       1 a_pair     1.777 +- 0.934           1.924 +- 0.655  (400 pairs)
       3 a_pair     1.008 +- 0.671           0.656 +- 0.755  (227 pairs)

45c. AND THE DIFFERENCE IS STILL UNDER 2 SIGMA.  Across the run it has
     wandered +0.718, +0.878, +0.911, +1.088, +0.769 at 56, 68, 80, 92 and
     104 seeds, with the error falling from 0.767 to 0.603 -- so the
     significance has moved 0.94, 1.32, 1.53, 1.78, 1.28 and is not
     climbing monotonically.

     At the present error, 3 sigma on a difference of 0.77 needs about 570
     seeds; the run is configured for 480 and will land near 2.7 sigma if
     the central value holds.  So the pairing turned an unreachable
     measurement into a marginal one, and did not turn it into a result.

45d. WHERE STEP 4 STANDS, ALL TOLD.

       established   the field pumps energy in at every radius measured
                     (7.4 sigma at a_pair on 400 pairs)
       established   the ratio is ~10 deep in at 0.01 a_pair and of order 1
                     across 1 to 3 a_pair; those two regimes differ
       established   the work grows as t^0.787 +- 0.160, i.e. secular
       established   antithetic pairing (5.5x) and cross-radius pairing
                     (1.9x) as the two variance reductions that work
       NOT established  any difference between 1 and 3 a_pair, so no
                     crossing radius, so no self-selected scale
       withdrawn     the t^2 exponent, the 3 a_pair ratio of 4.765, the
                     orbit-tied band as a blocker, and the 16-seed
                     correlation of 0.922

     Three claims withdrawn and four established, all from the same
     measurement.  The pattern is worth stating plainly: every claim that
     came from fewer than about a hundred samples failed, and every one
     that came from several hundred held.

46. THE PAIRED RUN AT 303 SEEDS: A 2-SIGMA INDICATION, NOT A RESULT

    Section 45 reported the paired estimator on 104 seeds.  The run
    continued; at 303 seeds the numbers have settled.

46a. FINAL STATE, 1 against 3 a_pair, 20 orbits, antithetic within each:

       |W|/Larmor at 1 a_pair      +1.156 +- 0.740
       |W|/Larmor at 3 a_pair      +0.309 +- 0.561
       correlation                 rho = +0.851
       difference, unpaired        +0.847 +- 0.928   (0.91 sigma)
       difference, paired          +0.847 +- 0.394   (2.15 sigma)
       gain                        2.35x in sigma, 5.5x in seed count

     The variance reduction held and improved as the sample grew -- 1.67x
     at 44 seeds, 1.91x at 104, 2.35x at 303 -- so the cross-radius pairing
     is a real and measured gain, better than the factor of three section
     44d asked for when counted in seeds.

46b. THE DIFFERENCE DID NOT CONVERGE TO SIGNIFICANCE.  Across the run:

       seeds     difference        significance
        56       +0.718 +- 0.767      0.94
       104       +0.769 +- 0.603      1.28
       164       +1.180 +- 0.536      2.20
       176       +1.281 +- 0.510      2.51
       252       +1.181 +- 0.407      2.91
       276       +1.118 +- 0.376      2.98
       303       +0.847 +- 0.394      2.15

     It climbed to 2.98 sigma at 276 seeds and fell back to 2.15 when a
     single outlying seed arrived and raised the variance as well as
     lowering the mean.  A quantity that walks from 2.15 to 2.98 and back
     on one seed in three hundred is not measured to three sigma, whatever
     the best intermediate reading said.  The remaining 180 seeds of the
     run can bring it to about 2.7 sigma at most.

46c. WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE CROSSING.  The ratio is of order 1 at both
     radii and the difference between them is about +0.85 +- 0.39 -- a
     2-sigma indication that it falls outward, consistent with a crossing
     somewhere beyond a_pair, and not enough to place one.  The honest
     statement is the one section 44d already reached and this run
     sharpens rather than overturns: over 1 to 3 a_pair pumping and
     radiation are comparable to within the measurement, and no radius in
     that range is singled out.

46d. THE METHODOLOGICAL RESULT IS THE DURABLE ONE.  Two variance
     reductions were found, measured against matched controls, and both
     hold: antithetic phase pairing (5.5x in seeds, section 38b) and
     cross-radius seed pairing (5.5x in seeds, here).  Together they are
     about 30x in seed count against the naive estimator, which is what
     made a 2-sigma statement possible at all -- the naive version needed
     4200 seeds per point for 10% and would have needed something like
     10^5 for this difference.

     That is where step 4 ends for now: the estimator is as good as it can
     be made without a different physical measurement, and the physics it
     can see is that the field pumps everywhere, hard deep in and
     marginally at the Bohr scale.

47. LOCKING N5, AND IT TURNS OUT TO BE AN EXACT IDENTITY

    N5 was recorded as "the collapse time scales as alpha^-5 lambda_C/c,
    the same power as Gamma_2gamma".  Written down properly it is not a
    scaling but an equality.

47a. THE ALGEBRA.  The dipole inspiral coefficient is

       C = 2 q^2 k /(6 pi eps0 c^3 mu^2),

     which for e+e- (q = e, mu = m/2, k = alpha hbar c) reduces to

       3 C = 16 alpha^2 lambda_C^2 c,

     so the closed-form time from a_pair = 2 lambda_C/alpha to zero is

       t = a_pair^3/(3C) = lambda_C/(2 alpha^5 c) = (1/2) alpha^-5 lambda_C/c

     against the QED lifetime tau_2gamma = 2 hbar/(alpha^5 m c^2)
     = 2 alpha^-5 lambda_C/c.  Measured: t/unit = 0.500000000 to nine
     digits, t = 31.1235 ps, tau_2gamma = 124.4942 ps, ratio exactly 4.

47b. WHAT IS AND IS NOT THE CONTENT.  alpha enters this model in exactly
     one place, the coupling k = alpha hbar c.  The exponent 5 then comes
     out of a^3/C with a ~ 1/alpha and C ~ alpha^2 -- three powers from the
     radius and two from the coefficient.  Nothing put a fifth power
     anywhere.  That is the non-tautological part.

     The factor 4 between the two times is NOT predicted by anything here
     and is not claimed to be.  It is the whole gap between a classical
     inspiral time and an annihilation rate, and sections 24 to 26 are
     about why.

47c. LOCKED as inspiral-alpha-power, asserting t/(alpha^-5 lambda_C/c) =
     1/2 to 1e-12 for the positronium pair and finiteness for the others,
     which carry their own reduced mass and dipole charge.  Verified to
     fail: moving the target to 0.6 gives FAILED: inspiral-alpha-power at
     56/57.

     Locked because the emission rule, the starting radius and the
     preparation have all been changed in this codebase without anyone
     checking that this identity still held.

47d. STATE OF THE SIX PROTECTED RESULTS.

       N1  mu/(e c) = the Compton barrier      static_assert (section 35a)
       N2  M1 cancels at anti-alignment        coherent-M1 check, 1e-12
       N3  exchange parity of the mutual field mutual-field-exchange-parity
       N5  the alpha^-5 identity               inspiral-alpha-power
       N4  |S| libration returning to zero     NOT LOCKED -- needs a
                                               trajectory, not algebra
       N6  dipole-dipole vs the hyperfine gap  NOT LOCKED

     Four of six, and the suite is at 57/57.

48. LOCKING N6: THE ONE HYPERFINE TERM THE MODEL HAS CONTRIBUTES NOTHING

    N6 was recorded as "the classical dipole-dipole energy as a fraction of
    the hyperfine splitting, with the remainder correctly identified".  The
    lockable part is the boundary, not the fraction.

48a. THE STATEMENT.  The measured o-Ps/p-Ps splitting is
     (7/12) alpha^4 m c^2 = 204.4 GHz and divides into a Fermi contact term
     (4/7) and virtual annihilation (3/7).  Neither exists in this model.
     What does exist is the tensor coupling

       U = (mu0/4 pi r^3) [mu1.mu2 - 3 (mu1.nhat)(mu2.nhat)],

     whose spherical average is EXACTLY zero, since
     <(mu1.nhat)(mu2.nhat)> = mu1.mu2/3 leaves mu1.mu2 - 3(mu1.mu2/3) = 0.

     So the model contributes nothing at all to an S-state splitting, and
     the few-percent figure it reports is an ORBIT-PLANE average -- a
     different quantity, which must not be read as a fraction of 204.4 GHz.
     That distinction is the substance of N6 and it is what needed locking.

48b. LOCKED as dipole-tensor-s-state-average on the same 50-point Lebedev
     rule the far-field flux probes use, with a generic pair of
     non-parallel moments so neither the scalar nor the tensor part is
     accidentally zero.  Measured 5.81e-16 against a 1e-14 bound.

     Verified to fail: changing the coefficient 3 to 2 reports FAILED:
     dipole-tensor-s-state-average at 57/58.

48c. THE SIX PROTECTED RESULTS, FIVE NOW LOCKED.

       N1  mu/(e c) = the Compton barrier      static_assert
       N2  M1 cancels at anti-alignment        coherent-M1, 1e-12
       N3  exchange parity of the mutual field mutual-field-exchange-parity
       N5  the alpha^-5 identity               inspiral-alpha-power
       N6  tensor term vanishes in S           dipole-tensor-s-state-average
       N4  |S| libration returning to zero     NOT LOCKED

     Suite at 58/58.  N4 is the only one left and the only one that needs a
     trajectory rather than an identity: for ortho it already follows from
     N3, since the configuration is rigid, but for para the claim is that a
     6193-substep libration returns |S| to exactly zero, and there is no
     cheap algebraic stand-in for that.

49. LOCKING N4, THE LAST ONE -- AND A TRAP FOUND WHILE DOING IT

    N4 is the result that was originally recorded backwards: para's mutual
    angle was called a progressive drift destroying the singlet, and section
    27 corrected it to a bounded libration that returns.  It is the one of
    the six with no algebraic stand-in, since "it returns" is a statement
    about a trajectory.

49a. THE TRAP, WHICH WOULD HAVE MADE A HOLLOW TEST.  The first attempt put
     the two parallel moments along the ORBIT NORMAL, the same geometry the
     exchange-parity lock uses.  In that configuration cos stays exactly
     1.000000000 through 6001 substeps and |S| stays exactly 0 -- because
     the precession axes lie along the normal as well, so rotating the
     moments about it changes nothing.

     The rate gap is 0.8545 there and the angle still does not move, since
     d(mu1.mu2)/dt = (omega1-omega2).(mu1 x mu2) and the cross product
     vanishes.  A libration test in that geometry would have passed its
     bounds while exercising nothing.  The moments must be TILTED off the
     normal, which is also what the production sampler does, since it draws
     the spin direction.

     This also sharpens what section 27a's lock does and does not say: the
     rate gap is the mechanism, but a gap alone does not move the angle.

49b. LOCKED with the moments tilted, 48 advances of the coupled secular
     solver out to 24 precession turns, and three bounds each guarding a
     different failure:

       min cos > 0.5      stays near alignment; a regression driving it
                          towards -1 is the destruction this result denies
       max cos > 0.999    it comes back; a one-way excursion passes the
                          first bound and fails this one
       min cos < 0.95     it actually moves; a frozen transport passes both
                          bounds above and fails this one

     Measured: cos from 0.79901963 to 0.99989868, so |S| up to 0.31700187
     hbar and back to 0.0000 within the sampling.

     Both failure modes verified: putting the moments back along the normal
     gives cos 1..1 and FAILED: mutual-angle-libration at 58/59, and
     tightening the return bound to 0.99999 fails it as well.

49c. ALL SIX PROTECTED RESULTS ARE NOW LOCKED, suite at 59/59.

       N1  mu/(e c) = the Compton barrier      static_assert
       N2  M1 cancels at anti-alignment        coherent-M1, 1e-12
       N3  exchange parity of the mutual field mutual-field-exchange-parity
       N4  the angle librates and returns      mutual-angle-libration
       N5  the alpha^-5 identity               inspiral-alpha-power
       N6  tensor term vanishes in S           dipole-tensor-s-state-average

     The plan's part 3 is complete.  It was supposed to come before steps 2
     and 3 and came after them instead; nothing turned out to have been
     broken in the meantime, which is luck rather than method.

50. THE PAIRED RUN FINISHED, AND TWO NUMBERS MOVE

    Section 46 recorded the paired estimator at 303 seeds and projected
    "about 2.7 sigma at most" once the run reached 480.  It reached 455 and
    went the other way.

50a. FINAL PAIRED STATE, 455 seeds, 1 against 3 a_pair, 20 orbits:

       |W|/Larmor at 1 a_pair      +0.784 +- 0.656
       |W|/Larmor at 3 a_pair      +0.274 +- 0.491
       correlation                 rho = +0.896
       difference, unpaired        +0.510 +- 0.820   (0.62 sigma)
       difference, paired          +0.510 +- 0.307   (1.66 sigma)
       gain                        2.67x in sigma, 7.1x in seed count

     The variance reduction kept improving with sample size, 1.67x at 44
     seeds through 2.35x at 303 to 2.67x here, and rho settled at 0.90.
     That part of section 45 stands and strengthens.

     The DIFFERENCE went the other way: +0.847 +- 0.394 (2.15 sigma) at 303
     seeds, +0.510 +- 0.307 (1.66 sigma) at 455.  The projection in 46b
     assumed the central value would hold while the error fell; it did not.
     Projections of significance are only as good as that assumption, and
     this is the second time in this measurement it failed.

50b. AND THE a_pair RATIO ITSELF IS NO LONGER ABOVE 1.  The paired run is
     an INDEPENDENT sample at the same radius -- seeds 50000 up against
     20000 up -- so the two combine:

       independent run, 400 pairs   1.924 +- 0.655
       paired run, 455 seeds        0.784 +- 0.656
       agreement between them       1.140 +- 0.927   (1.23 sigma)
       COMBINED                     1.355 +- 0.464   (+0.77 sigma from 1)

     Section 43b reported 1.924 +- 0.655, "only 1.41 sigma above the
     balance value", and read that as the field winning marginally at the
     Bohr radius.  Combined with the second sample it is 0.77 sigma above
     1 -- fully consistent with balance, and no longer evidence of anything
     winning there.

50c. WHAT STEP 4'S PHYSICS COMES TO, FINALLY.

       solid     the field pumps energy IN at every radius measured.  The
                 sign test at a_pair gave 7.40 sigma on 400 pairs and it is
                 tail-robust; nothing since has touched it.
       solid     the ratio is about 10 at 0.01 a_pair, at 11 sigma.  Deep in
                 the orbit the field wins by an order of magnitude.
       solid     the growth is secular, p = 0.787 +- 0.160, not resonant.
       consistent with balance   the ratio at 1 to 3 a_pair: 1.355 +- 0.464
                 at a_pair and 0.274 +- 0.491 at 3 a_pair, neither
                 distinguishable from 1 and not from each other (1.66
                 sigma on 455 paired seeds).
       not found no crossing radius, no self-selected scale.

     So the picture the measurement supports is a broad region around the
     Bohr radius where pumping and radiation are comparable, and a strongly
     pumping interior.  Whether the comparable region contains a genuine
     stationary point is exactly what 1.66 sigma cannot say.

51. IS MAGNETIC DIPOLE REPULSION THE REASON TO SIT AT a_pair?

    Proposed as the replacement for L = hbar: the pair is held not by a
    quantized angular momentum but by the repulsion between its magnetic
    moments.  The shape is right -- a repulsive r^-3 term outgrows the r^-1
    Coulomb attraction at short range, so the two MUST produce a minimum
    with L = 0 and no quantization.  The question is where.

51a. WHERE THE MINIMUM IS, EXACTLY.  For point dipoles aligned and
     perpendicular to the separation, U = +C/r^3 with C = (mu0/4pi) mu^2, and
     V(r) = -k/r + C/r^3 has dV/dr = 0 at r_min = sqrt(3C/k).  The ratio C/k
     is an identity:

       C/k = (mu/(e c))^2 = r*^2        measured ratio 1.000000000000

     so the minimum is

       r_min = sqrt(3) r* = 3.348e-13 m = 0.867 lambda_C = a_pair / 316.1
       depth  V(r_min) = -(2/3) k/r_min = -2.867 keV   (Bohr n=1: -6.80 eV)

     The mechanism is real, exact and needs no L = hbar -- and it puts the
     pair at the Compton scale, 316 times inside the Bohr radius, bound 420
     times more deeply.  At a_pair itself the ideal force ratio is 1.0e-5.

51b. WHY IT CANNOT LAND ANYWHERE ELSE.  C/k = r*^2 contains mu, e and c and
     nothing else, so any balance of dipole repulsion against Coulomb sits
     at r* times a pure number.  a_pair = 2 lambda_C/alpha is r* times
     4/(alpha g/2): one power of alpha away, the same gap as sections 30-31.
     This is the sixth independent route to the Compton scale.

51c. THE MODEL'S OWN FORCE AGREES, AND ADDS TWO OBSTACLES.  Full covariant
     dipole force against Coulomb, circular orbit, moments along the orbit
     normal (tools/para_ortho_dipole_force):

       R/a_pair   para F_radial   para |F_d/F_C|   ortho F_radial   ortho |F_d/F_C|
       1          attractive        1.66e-05        attractive        3.67e-05
       0.2        attractive        4.78e-05        attractive        5.48e-04
       0.05       REPULSIVE         1.62e-03        attractive        6.39e-03
       0.005      REPULSIVE         3.26e-01        attractive        4.76e-01
       0.00183    REPULSIVE         5.23e-01        attractive        5.28e-01

     (1) At the Bohr radius the para dipole force is not repulsive at all;
     it turns repulsive only between 0.2 and 0.05 a_pair.
     (2) Ortho is ATTRACTIVE at every radius.  Anti-aligned moments have no
     barrier and no minimum, so the mechanism could hold at most one of the
     two channels and cannot be what sets a common scale.

     Against the ideal-dipole ratio 3 r*^2/r^2 the model tracks well down to
     0.005 a_pair (0.33 against 0.40) and then falls short at the barrier
     (0.52 against 2.99), so inside the model the balance point is not
     reached above r*.  Which correction does that was not isolated; the
     short-range regulator is not it, since at 193 fm against its 83.6 fm
     radius and exponent 12 it is within a part in 10^4 of unity.

51d. AND THE ORIENTATION AVERAGE REMOVES IT ENTIRELY.  U is repulsive only
     for moments perpendicular to the separation; along it, U = -2C/r^3 and
     attracts.  Its average over directions is exactly zero -- the identity
     already locked as dipole-tensor-s-state-average (section 48).  So for
     any state not pinned to one orientation the repulsion supplies no
     radial barrier at all.

51e. VERDICT.  The dipole repulsion is a genuine stationarity mechanism that
     replaces L = hbar, and it is worth recording as such.  It does not
     explain a_pair: it balances Coulomb at sqrt(3) mu/(e c), the Compton
     scale; at a_pair it is 1e-5 of Coulomb and of the wrong sign; it
     attracts in the ortho channel; and it averages to zero over
     orientations.

52. THE 0.52-AGAINST-2.99 DISCREPANCY AT THE BARRIER, RESOLVED -- AND IT
    CORRECTS SECTION 51

    Section 51c left open why the model's dipole force at the barrier is
    0.52 of Coulomb against 2.99 for ideal dipoles.  The suspicion raised
    was that not all magnetic fields are included.  They are; the gap is
    three other things, and one of them hides a real result.

52a. NOTHING IS MISSING.  The force on the target is grad(mu_lab.B +
     p_lab.E), with the partner's field = Lienard-Wiechert charge field
     (E and B) + retarded magnetic dipole field (E and B).  Rebuilding it
     from those exact fields, channel by channel, reproduces the model's
     own force to four digits wherever no regularization acts:

       r/r*   model F      sum of exact channels
       3.0    1.841e-04    1.841e-04
       2.5    3.901e-04    3.900e-04
       2.2    6.596e-04    6.593e-04
       2.0    9.751e-04    9.747e-04

52b. CAUSE 1 -- MY "IDEAL" OMITTED TWO REAL CHANNELS.  The partner's CHARGE
     field acts on the moving target twice: its magnetic field on mu, and
     its electric field on the induced electric dipole p = gamma v x mu/c^2.
     Both are attractive and equal to four digits (-2.252e-05 and
     -2.252e-05 at 3 r*), together -19.7% of the dipole repulsion at 3 r*
     and -15% at 1.6 r*.  This is genuine physics, of relative size
     ~ beta r/r*, and it is what turns para's dipole force attractive above
     ~0.1 a_pair.  The dipole's own electric field on p is +0.2%.

52c. CAUSE 2 -- THE DIPOLE FIELD IS REGULARIZED, DIFFERENTLY BY BRANCH.
     separationFloor() returns comptonBarrierRadius for e+e-, and
     clampedSeparationVector replaces r by sqrt(r^2 + r*^2).

       static source   Plummer-softened at EVERY radius, on every path
                       including the one named "exact":
                       B/B_ideal = q^3/(q^2+1)^(3/2), matched to 5e-6 at
                       q = 0.8..8; force = q^5/(q^2+1)^(5/2), matched to
                       2.5e-4 (0.9212 at 5.5 r*, 0.1776 at r*)
       moving source   exact point dipole above 2 r* (0.9995 of ideal at
                       1.75 r*), smoothstep blend towards the softened field
                       between r* and 2 r*, softened below r*
       charge field    exact above r* (1.00000), FROZEN at its r* value
                       below (E at 0.8 r* equals E at r* exactly) -- a hard
                       clamp, not the Plummer form

     The code states the reason for the floor and it is NUMERICAL: a kink
     in the force at the old hard clamp stalled the adaptive integrator's
     step-doubling probe.  The Plummer form removed the kink.  Three fields
     now carry three different short-range treatments.

52d. CAUSE 3 -- AT AND BELOW r* EVERYTHING IS FLOORED.  The "exact" dipole
     field itself returns 0.34 of ideal at r = r*, and the charge-field
     channels collapse there (-1.5e-05 against -7.5e-04 at 1.1 r*).

52e. THE RESULT HIDDEN BY THE REGULARIZATION.  With exact fields on the
     model's circular orbit, the dipole repulsion net of the motional
     attraction DOES overtake Coulomb:

       r/r*    exact F / F_Coulomb
       1.750      0.8358
       1.700      0.8880
       1.660      0.9334
       1.630      0.9696
       1.610      0.9949
       1.600      1.0080
       1.550      1.0771
       1.500      1.1533

     Crossing at r = 1.606 r* = 310 fm.  So classical electrodynamics with
     point moments has a stationary point for the para geometry, needing no
     L = hbar -- and it sits exactly inside the r*..2r* zone the model
     regularizes, which is why the model's ratio peaks near 0.5 and never
     reaches 1.  A numerical fix for an integrator kink erases the one
     classical stationary point the moments produce.

     Caveat on the number: the circular speed assumes Coulomb alone
     supplies the centripetal force, which stops being true as the ratio
     approaches 1.  The moving crossing 1.606 r* and the static ideal
     crossing sqrt(3) r* = 1.732 r* therefore bracket the true stationary
     radius rather than fix it.

52f. CORRECTIONS TO SECTION 51.
       51a  "minimum at sqrt(3) r*" holds for static IDEAL dipoles only; the
            model's static branch is Plummer-softened and never reaches it
            (peak 3q^3/(q^2+1)^(5/2) = 0.5577 at q = sqrt(3/2)); with motion
            and exact fields the crossing is at 1.606 r*.
       51c  "which correction does that was not isolated" -- now isolated:
            the r*..2r* dipole regularization, plus two motional channels the
            ideal reference left out.  "The short-range regulator is not it"
            stands: that was the w(r) weight, 0.99996 at the barrier; the
            responsible softening is the separation floor, a different
            mechanism.
       51e  The verdict on a_pair stands and is sharpened: the stationary
            point exists classically but lies at 1.6-1.7 r*, the Compton
            scale, 341 times inside a_pair.

53. DYNAMIC TEST, STEP 1: THE FLOOR SWITCH, AND THE BARRIER HOLDS PARA

    Section 52 found a classical stationary point for para at 1.6-1.7 r*,
    hidden because separationFloor() regularizes the r*..2r* zone.  A force
    balance on a prescribed circular orbit is not a trajectory, so this
    tests it with real integration.

53a. THE SWITCH.  CREM_SEPARATION_FLOOR_SCALE multiplies separationFloor(),
     read once per process; unset, the function returns
     comptonBarrierRadius itself.  Checked: header isolation 37/37,
     validation 59/59, and the default branch trace bit-identical
     (t = 199.442442952 ps, L = 0.0949953986509 hbar).  The stopping rule
     reads comptonBarrierRadius directly and does not move.

53b. THE PROBE.  runMechanicalTrajectory, full engine, radiation reaction
     DISABLED so the outcome reflects forces and the integrator only.  Para
     = moments aligned along z, perpendicular to the x separation (the
     repulsive geometry); ortho = anti-aligned.  Released at r0 with a
     tangential speed that is a fraction of the circular speed.

53c. AN ENERGY CHECK THAT IS NOT ONE.  Frame boundFieldEnergy is the
     "exact discrete world-tube balance" reservoir -- bound near field,
     retardation, interference AND discretization remainder -- assigned as
     balanceStart - dE_mech - dE_rad.  E_mech + E_rad + boundField is
     therefore conserved BY CONSTRUCTION and says nothing.  The independent
     quantity is the mechanical energy, and every energy statement below
     uses it.

53d. PARA IS HELD, AND THE RESULT DOES NOT DEPEND ON THE FLOOR.
     Start 3 r*, v_t = 0.2 v_circ:

       floor scale   r_min (r*)   r_max after min (r*)   outcome
       0.5            1.3243        3.0015                full run
       0.25           1.3243        3.0017                full run, 11 minima
       0.1            1.3244        3.0009                full run

     Converged to four digits once the floor is below the zone: the
     barrier comes from the exact fields, not from the regularization.
     Mechanical energy at 0.25: largest excursion 2.3e-2 k/r0, returning
     to within 2.1e-3.  More energy gives a deeper turn, as a repulsive
     barrier must: start 4 r* reaches 1.2415 r* against 1.3243 from 3 r*.

     Released at the predicted stationary point, 1.65 r* with
     v_t = 0.02 v_circ: para oscillates between 1.650 and 1.825 r*, i.e.
     about 1.74 r*, against sqrt(3) r* = 1.732 for static ideal dipoles --
     at beta = 0.006 the motional channels are negligible, so that is the
     number to expect.  Mechanical energy constant to 1.6e-3 k/r0.

53e. ORTHO FALLS THROUGH.  From 3 r* at every floor scale, and from
     1.65 r*, ortho plunges below r* and the integrator fails (scale 1:
     r_min 0.218 r*; scale 0.25: 0.663 r*; from 1.65 r*: 1.050 r*), with
     mechanical energy jumping by +1.5 to +3 k/r0 just before.  No barrier,
     then numerical breakdown in the regime the model declares invalid.
     This failure also occurs at the default floor, so the switch did not
     cause it.

53f. AT THE DEFAULT FLOOR THE REGULARIZED CORE DOES WORK.  Scale 1, para:
     the barrier is suppressed, the pair passes to 0.208 r* and is thrown
     out to 60.5 r*, its mechanical energy going from -0.8338 to +0.3765
     k/r0 with radiation off -- a gain of 1.21 k/r0, taken from the
     reservoir term.  A bound start cannot reach 60 r* conservatively.  So
     beyond hiding the stationary point, the default floor is not a
     conservative force field for an orbit that crosses it: three fields
     carry three different short-range treatments (section 52c), and no
     single potential generates the resulting force.

53g. A DEGENERACY AT ZERO RELATIVE VELOCITY.  Para released exactly
     radially (L = 0) fails at t = 0; a near-radial para orbit (v_t = 0.02)
     fails at its turning point 1.27 r* after four clean bounces, with
     mechanical energy conserved to 6.5e-3.  Ortho at L = 0 runs normally.
     Diagnosed with CREM_DEBUG_RETREAT: the moving-dipole field is built as
     a two-charge limit, and for ALIGNED moments at zero relative velocity
     the pole-cancellation detector fires on every probe -- 103080 times,
     cancellation up to 2e5 times its design value, every retreat
     rejected.  A field-construction defect, not dynamics.  It makes the
     radial turning points of para orbits unintegrable, so tests must keep
     some angular momentum until it is fixed.

53h. WHAT STANDS.  With the floor moved out of the zone, para released near
     the barrier stays bound and off contact, oscillating about ~1.74 r*;
     ortho falls through.  This is only for moments pinned perpendicular
     to the separation.  Section 48's lock says the orientation average of
     the interaction is exactly zero, so precessing moments may lose the
     barrier -- that is step 3 and it is not yet done.

     If the barrier survives step 3, the model predicts para is held OFF
     contact while ortho reaches it: the reverse of the measured
     lifetimes, where p-Ps annihilates about a thousand times faster.

54. DYNAMIC TEST, STEP 3: PRECESSING MOMENTS -- THE BARRIER IS NOT A
    PROPERTY OF A TRAJECTORY

    Section 53 showed the para dipole barrier holding for moments pinned
    perpendicular to the separation.  Section 48's lock says the orientation
    average of the interaction is exactly zero, so real, precessing moments
    might lose it.  Floor scale 0.25, radiation reaction off throughout.

54a. THE PREDICTION, STATED BEFORE MEASURING.  The separation sweeps the
     orbit plane fast and the moments precess slowly, so with the moment
     tilted theta from the orbit normal, <(mu.n)^2> = sin^2(theta)/2 and the
     orbit-averaged interaction carries 1 - 1.5 sin^2(theta): a barrier only
     below the magic angle, 54.74 deg.

54b. THE INSTANTANEOUS FORCE HAS A LARGE OUT-OF-PLANE PART.  Model dipole
     force over Coulomb, circular orbit, separation along +x:

       tilt   r/r*   para F_rad   para F_z    orto F_rad   orto F_z
        0     1.2     +1.834      +0.000       -2.352      +0.000
       30     1.2     +0.299      +1.745       -0.748      -1.875
       60     1.2     -2.747      +1.697       +2.488      -1.923
       90     1.2     -4.188      -0.130       +4.188      -0.130

     For tilted moments the out-of-plane force exceeds Coulomb, so short
     trajectories leave the orbit plane (separation up to 40 deg out of it
     within 0.15 of an orbit).  This is point-dipole physics, not a defect.

54c. ORBIT-AVERAGED, THE OUT-OF-PLANE PART VANISHES AND THE RADIAL PART
     CHANGES SIGN NEAR THE MAGIC ANGLE.  72 orbital phases, moments fixed:
     <F_z> = 0.0000 at every tilt.  Radial average over Coulomb crosses zero

       channel   r = 2 r*     r = 1.6 r*
       para      50.6 deg     51.1 deg      (repulsive below)
       orto      58.1 deg     57.8 deg      (repulsive above)

     about 4 deg either side of 54.74.  At the magic angle both channels are
     attractive by about the same amount (-0.071 and -0.068 at 2 r*): the
     dipole-dipole part averages away and the motional charge-field
     channels, attractive in both, are what remains and shift both
     boundaries.

54d. LONG DYNAMIC RUNS WITH TILTED MOMENTS ARE BLOCKED.  At 30, 50 and 60
     deg both channels stop as numerical failures after about 0.05 of the
     observation window, before the first bounce.  Not the pole-cancellation
     defect of 53g: only 139 and 20 retreats, all accepted.  The GRAD
     anomaly print is a magnitude threshold, not a rejection.  Raising
     CREM_MAX_DEPTH from 12 to 20 fails at the same time and only lets the
     integrator push further into bad states (mechanical-energy error 250-310
     k/r0).  runMechanicalTrajectory already documents this: at the bottom of
     the orbit the step-doubling error scales as dt^1 instead of dt^3, so
     depth is a treadmill; the force evaluation there does not smooth as the
     step shrinks.

     At 0 deg the control reproduces section 53 (para r_min 1.3244 r*).  At
     90 deg the roles swap cleanly: ortho is held (r_min 1.396 r*, five
     bounces), para falls through.

54e. AND THE TILT IS NOT CONSERVED, SO THE CONDITION CANNOT HOLD A PAIR.
     Coupled secular spin-orbit solver at 2 r*, stepped incrementally, with
     the total angular momentum tracked:

       |L_circ(2 r*)| = 0.0604 hbar;  ortho |S| = 1 hbar, |S|/|L| = 16.55
       J direction drift over 200 turns: 0.00 deg; relative J residual
                                         at most 1.1e-17

       start      after 5 turns    range over 200 turns    orbit normal swing
       30 para       ~58 deg          57.8 - 67.2 deg            96 deg
       30 orto       ~66 deg          65.5 - 71.4 deg           119 deg
       60 para       ~64 deg          63.6 - 77.9 deg           130 deg
       60 orto       ~73 deg          73.5 - 76.2 deg           153 deg

     J is exactly conserved, so this is genuine exchange: at the Compton
     scale the orbital angular momentum is an order of magnitude below the
     spin, and the orbit normal is slaved to the spin precession rather than
     the other way round.  Para's total spin grows from 0 to about 0.2 hbar
     in the process.  The tilt crosses the 51/58 deg boundaries within a few
     turns.

     Caveat on these numbers: the secular sub-operator holds the semi-major
     axis fixed, and |L| here climbs above the circular value (to 0.21 and
     0.30 hbar), which a Kepler ellipse at that axis cannot have.  The drift
     values are therefore qualitative.  That the tilt is not conserved, with
     J exact and S/L = 16.5, is not.

54f. WITHDRAWN.
       - "If the barrier survives, para is held off contact and ortho
         reaches it -- the reverse of the measured lifetimes" (53h and the
         step-1 report): true only at tilt 0 deg; at 90 deg it is the other
         way round.
       - "For isotropic spins para is held in ~37% of orientations and ortho
         in ~53%, the same direction as the lifetimes": a count of static
         orientations, which is meaningless for a trajectory whose tilt
         wanders across both boundaries within a few precession turns.

54g. WHAT STANDS.  The dipole repulsion creates a real classical stationary
     point at 1.6-1.7 r* (sections 52-53), converged in the floor and
     dynamically confirmed for pinned moments.  Orbit-averaged, it survives
     for para below ~51 deg and for ortho above ~58 deg.  But at that scale
     the spins carry sixteen times the orbital angular momentum and drag the
     orbit plane with them, so neither channel keeps the condition, and no
     channel-specific protection from contact follows.  The long dynamic
     test that would show what a precessing pair actually does is blocked
     by the documented dt^1 accuracy defect at the bottom of the orbit.

55. THE ACCURACY DEFECT AT THE BOTTOM OF THE ORBIT: TWO NODE CROSSINGS

    Section 54 was blocked by the documented defect: near the barrier the
    step-doubling error stops shrinking with the step, so CREM_MAX_DEPTH is
