    a treadmill.  Reproduced on the tilted para case (floor 0.25 r*, tilt 30
    deg, NumericalFailure at 1.1189 r*) by copying the engine one outer step
    before the failure and sweeping the evaluation time across the failing
    step.  Both causes below are the same fact about the history:
    interpolatedCharge() is a cubic Hermite through position and velocity,
    so it is C1 -- the source acceleration JUMPS at every stored node.  On
    this orbit the jump is 1-6% of |a| at all 119 nodes, and the stored node
    accelerations sit halfway between the left and right limits (checked
    against a second-order finite difference of the stored velocities: the
    residual matches the truncation estimate at every node).  Any force whose
    retarded time crosses a node therefore steps, and step doubling sees a
    step as an error of order dt^0-dt^1.

55a. CAUSE 1: THE TWO POLES OF THE POINT DIPOLE.  The near-field dipole is
     built as the limit of two charges 1e-5 r apart with charge ~1/separation.
     Each pole solved its own retarded time, so within separation/c of a node
     the poles read accelerations from different segments and the jump was
     multiplied by the pole charge: the retarded time landed 5.9e-30 s from
     node 106 (jump 0.35%) and the dipole field was wrong by up to a factor 9
     in a window whose width scaled with the pole separation.  FIX: both poles
     are expanded from ONE segment -- the one holding the central retarded
     time -- as (x, v, a, j) Taylor series, which reproduce the cubic exactly
     inside it and continue it smoothly past its ends
     (historicalChargeWithJerk).  Bad windows at pole separations 1e-5, 1e-6,
     1e-7: 0; the dipole-field jump fell 3500x (6.3e-1 -> 1.8e-4).  The
     far-zone two-charge construction is NOT changed: its poles' emission
     times differ by ~r/c, so they legitimately sit on different segments.
     The trajectory still failed at the same place.

55b. CAUSE 2: THE SIX PROBES OF THE GRADIENT FORCE.
     covariantDipoleGradientForce takes U = mu.B + p.E at target +- 1e-4 r
     along each axis.  Its spatial part jumped 3.68e-2 -> 2.21e-2 between
     tau = 6.75e-26 and 6.76e-26 s, while the material term (9.11e-3) and
     DU/Dt stayed smooth.  Probe by probe, at those two times:

       probe    U change      its retarded time to the nearest node
       x+       7.0e-19       1.6e-30 s -> 1.2e-28 s (crosses)
       others   ~2e-22        1.6e-26 .. 1.2e-25 s

     Both channels of x+ step together (Lienard-Wiechert 5.9115 -> 5.9137
     e-17, dipole 1.349815 -> 1.350488 e-15); no pole-cancellation retreat
     fired (ratios 1.63-1.65).  FIX: RetardedSegmentPin.  While the six probes
     are evaluated, every charge read of the source within the segment of the
     unshifted target's retarded time, widened by 8 gradientStep/c, uses that
     segment continued past its ends; historicalCharge and
     historicalChargeWithJerk honour the pin, and nothing else sets it.  The
     widening is ~1e-3 of the node spacing, and the pin declines when it
     would exceed a quarter of the segment.

55c. REJECTED: A C2 HISTORY.  Quintic Hermite on position, velocity and the
     stored acceleration removes every node jump at once, and the probe
     table became smooth.  Measured and rejected:

                                        cubic + pin      quintic
       validation                         59/59           57/59
       trajectory tol 1e-5/6/7        5.55e-6/1.38e-6/  5.55e-6/1.57e-6/
                                          3.89e-7          3.26e-6
       long-horizon balance dE/Efar       0.0594           0.1736
       tilted runs (r_min, 0/30/60 deg)   same within a few % of r*

     Adding the radiation-reaction kick to the stored acceleration (it
     enters the momentum but not the stored value) changed nothing (0.17363
     -> 0.17365), so that is not the reason; the cause of the regression was
     not found.

55d. CHECKS.  Validation 59/59, header isolation 37/37.  Production branch
     trace (seed 42, index 2, para): t = 199.442442952 ps and 3 photons
     unchanged; peri/barrier 3.89708318338 -> 3.89708426473, a
     9.55072339889e-13 -> 9.55072331763e-13 m, L 0.0949953986509 ->
     0.0949953983332 hbar.

55e. WHAT THE TRAJECTORIES DO NOW (floor 0.25 r*):

       tilt  channel  before (r_min, outcome)      after
       0     para     1.3244 r*, held (obs. limit)   1.3244 r*, held
       0     orto     0.6378 r*, failure             0.2986 r*, one bounce to
                                                     0.85 r*, failure
       30    para     1.1189 r*, failure             0.2416 r*, failure
       30    orto     0.7059 r*, failure             0.3182 r*, one bounce to
                                                     1.08 r*, failure
       60    para     1.2073 r*, failure             0.2310 r*, one bounce,
                                                     failure
       60    orto     0.8938 r*, failure             0.6643 r*, failure

     The failures at 1.1-1.2 r* are gone: every tilted pair now passes
     through the barrier.  None of them is held.  Para at 30 deg -- the case
     the stationary point of 54 predicts to be held -- goes straight to the
     floor.

55f. WHAT IS STILL OPEN.  The integrator still fails, now at or below the
     floor, and every failing run has a mechanical-energy error of 40-400
     k/r0 by then.  Live order diagnostic at the new para 30 deg failure
     (r = 0.353 r*, dt = 7.95e-27 s, metric 1.6e-5 against 1e-5): the error
     halves per halving for four levels, then falls 29x, 1.5x, 11870x, and
     only then settles at 4x.  A third discontinuity therefore sits inside
     the failing step.  Not located.  The candidates follow from 55: every
     retarded read whose time crosses a node steps, and the charge-charge
     Lienard-Wiechert force at the particle itself is one such read that no
     pin covers; its radiation term grows with |a| and so matters most at
     the floor.  That is a hypothesis, not a measurement.  Whether the energy
     error is the integrator's or the mismatch between the softened force and
     the unsoftened mechanical energy below the floor was not examined
     either.

56. THE THIRD DISCONTINUITY (55f): A FLOOR CLAMP APPLIED PER POLE

    Section 55f left the tilted para 30 deg run failing at 0.33 r* (floor
    0.25 r*), with a step-doubling metric that fell irregularly (29x, then
    11870x) and a guess that the charge-charge Lienard-Wiechert force was
    the unpinned culprit.  The failing substep was captured from inside the
    engine (start state, history, dt = 7.95e-27 s; a temporary hook, since
    removed) and every force component was evaluated on a 400-point grid
    across it.

56a. THE GUESS WAS WRONG.  Second differences over the grid, normalized by
     the force, against their own median:

       component                          max / median
       Lienard-Wiechert, both particles       1.5
       dipole-field Lorentz force            3.1-3.3
       gradient force on the positron         4.5
       gradient force on the electron     2.6e6   at 0.732 dt

     The electron's gradient force is 56x its Coulomb force here.  Its
     spatial part is smooth; the jump is in the material term, whose rate
     went -1.1884e8 -> -1.2055e8 (1.4%) between evaluation times 4e-31 s
     apart.  Of the rate's three time samples only the one a
     derivativeStep back moved (by 2.85e-20, the other two by ~5e-23), and
     only in its dipole channel.  Two further guesses were measured and
     refuted: all three samples sit in the same history segment, 1.7e-23 s
     from the nearest node, so no node crossing (a segment pin written for
     it was removed again); and the moment-derivative stencil sits 5.6e-24 s
     from any node, with the moment and both derivatives smooth to 1e-9.

56b. THE CAUSE.  In twoChargeLimitDipoleField each pole's field used
     max(distance, nuclearCutoff, separationFloor()) of ITS OWN retarded
     distance.  At the failing sample the dipole's retarded distance was
     4.832589933e-14 m, 1.46e-20 m above the floor, and the two pole
     distances differ by 4.1e-20 m: one pole was clamped, the other was
     not.  Their difference -- the dipole field -- then carried the
     unbalanced 1/d^2 of one pole, amplified by 1/poleSeparationFraction:

       pole separation fraction      1e-5          1e-6          1e-7
       U across 4e-31 s          -1.61649e-14  -1.567469e-14  -1.567468e-14
                              -> -1.61617e-14  (flat)        (flat, noise)

     A linear ramp of 2e-4 within 4e-31 s, i.e. 5e26 1/s against the
     ~5e21 1/s the physics allows, and a value 3% off the limit the finer
     separations agree on.  The estimated slope of one unbalanced pole,
     |pole| * 2v/d = 9e26 1/s, matches.  The blend in
     retardedMagneticDipoleField weights the exact field low here, so the
     force saw 2.85e-20 of it -- enough to fail at maximum depth.

56c. FIX.  Both poles are regularized by one factor set by the dipole's own
     retarded distance d_c: fieldDistance = distance * max(1,
     max(nuclearCutoff, floor)/d_c).  Above the floor no pole is clamped;
     below it both are scaled alike; at the floor the factor is exactly 1,
     so the field is continuous.  On the captured state the 1e-5 field is
     now -1.5674687e-14, flat to 3e-7 over the window, and agrees with 1e-6
     and 1e-7.  The far-zone construction has no per-pole clamp and is
     unchanged.

56d. CHECKS.  Validation 59/59 (the only changed line is the coherent/LL
     timing ratio), header isolation 37/37.  Production branch trace
     (seed 42, index 2, para) bit-identical to section 55d: t =
     199.442442952 ps, peri/barrier 3.89708426473, 3 photons.

56e. TRAJECTORIES (floor 0.25 r*).  Para 30 deg now passes the old failure:
     r_min 0.2416 r*, one bounce to 0.3587 r*, failure at 0.527 r*.  Every
     other case is unchanged from 55e to the printed digit, including
     r_min and the maximum mechanical-energy error, so their failures lie
     elsewhere.

56f. WHAT IS STILL OPEN.  The new para 30 deg failure (r = 0.527 r*,
     dt = 1.45e-26 s, metric 1.9e-5) is NOT a discontinuity: on the same
     400-point decomposition no component exceeds 4.4x its median second
     difference.  But the gradient force on the positron there is 126
     against a Lienard-Wiechert force of 0.042, 3000x, and the gradient
     force on the electron varies fast (median second difference 1.1e-5 of
     itself per 1/400 step).  By then the run already carries a mechanical
     energy error of 81 k/r0, which arose before the failure and is the same
     number as before this fix.  In every tilted run the energy error of
     40-400 k/r0 builds up first; where, and whether it is the integrator or
     the mismatch between softened forces and the unsoftened mechanical
     energy below the floor, is the next question, not yet measured.

57. THE "ZERO-VELOCITY DEGENERACY" OF 53g: MISDIAGNOSED, AND FIXED

    53g recorded that para released radially (L = 0) fails at t = 0 and a
    near-radial para (v_t = 0.02) fails at its turning point, and blamed the
    two-charge dipole field: at zero relative velocity the pole-cancellation
    detector fires on every probe and every retreat is rejected.
    Reproduced after section 56: failure at t = 3.76e-24 s, dt = 2.35e-25
    s, metric 1.25e-5; 132672 retreats, all rejected.

57a. THE DETECTOR IS A FALSE ALARM, NOT THE FAILURE.  The ratio is exactly
     2/fraction (2e5 at 1e-5, 2e7 at 1e-7).  That is what the construction
     gives at zero source velocity: the detector compares the poles' dual
     magnetic fields, whose centre-velocity part cancels between opposite
     poles and whose moment-derivative part adds, so the sum is twice one
     pole by construction, not a cancellation failure.  Every force
     component on a 400-point grid across the failing step is smooth (max
     second difference at most 5.6x its median).

57b. THE CAUSE: THE DERIVATIVE STENCIL FOLLOWED THE INTEGRATION PATH.
     Step-doubling error on the captured state, per halving of dt:
       all forces           velocity error falls 4x (local error dt^2)
       dipole forces off    falls 8x (dt^3, as kick-drift-kick should)
     Velocity dependence of the forces predicts 1.7e-7 m/s of the measured
     5.0e-2, and switching the gradient force's material term off changed
     nothing.  The coarse step and the two half steps differ in one more
     thing: the half-step path stores a midpoint.  The same end state
     evaluated with both histories:

       dt/dt_fail     gradient force   dipole field   Lienard-Wiechert
       4                 2.2e-2           3.93             0
       1                 1.7e-3           0.38             0
       1/32              5.7e-5           8.4e-3           0

     Linear in dt, and zero where no stencil is used.  Four retarded
     kinematics took their derivative step as twice the gap between the
     last two stored nodes.  appendStateHistory() overwrites the leading
     node until a full spacing has passed, so that gap is the time since the
     last genuine node, and it differs between the two paths by the step.

57c. FIX.  historicalDipoleKinematics, historicalElectricDipoleKinematics and
     historicalIntegratedDipoleKinematics now difference over twice the
     MEAN GENUINE spacing, (t[n-2] - t[0])/(n-2), which the leading-node
     overwrite does not touch.  The two histories now give identical forces
     (difference exactly 0 at every dt), and the step error at dt_fail fell
     from 1.25e-5 to 2.2e-9.
     Two variants were measured and rejected:
       - a stencil from the grid formula 4 r/c / 128: it assumes physical
         time scales, and on the validation's unit-time history it rounds to
         nothing (coherent-magnetic-dipole power ratio 4 -> 0, 58/59);
       - applying it also to electricQuadrupoleThirdDerivative and
         electricDipoleDerivativeStep (radiation sector): no gain for these
         runs, so those two keep the former rule; they have the same path
         dependence, unmeasured.

57d. CHECKS.  Validation 59/59 (boosted-tensor residual on trajectory states
     7.0e-10 -> 2.9e-11), header isolation 37/37.  Production branch trace:
     t = 199.442442952 ps and 3 photons unchanged, peri/barrier
     3.89708426473 -> 3.89708460123.

57e. TRAJECTORIES (floor 0.25 r*):

       release                 channel  before              after
       radial, L = 0           para     fails at t = 0      full window, 2
                                                            bounces at 1.2669
                                                            r*, dE_mech 6.5e-3
       v_t = 0.02, 5 T         para     fails at 4th turn   full window, 11
                                                            minima at 1.2675
                                                            r*, dE_mech 1.0e-2
       radial                  orto     fails at 0.376 r*   full window, but
                                                            ionized: dE_mech
                                                            312 k/r0, r = 238 r*
       tilt 0/30/60 deg        orto     all fail            all full window,
                                                            all ionized
                                                            (dE_mech 13-342)
       tilt 30/60 deg          para     fail                still fail (0.24,
                                                            0.23 r* minima)

     The radial para is held off at 1.27 r* by the dipole barrier of 52-53,
     now with L = 0 -- the restriction "tests must keep some angular
     momentum" of 53g is lifted.

57f. OPEN.  (1) The detector still fires on every probe at low source
     velocity (289340 rejected retreats in the radial run), which costs
     evaluations and no longer misleads, but it should compare a channel
     that genuinely cancels.  (2) Every ortho run that goes below the floor
     comes out with a mechanical energy of +180 k/r0 or more and leaves --
     the energy error of 55f/56f, now no longer hidden by a numerical
     failure.  (3) The radiation-sector stencils of 57c.

58. WHERE THE MECHANICAL-ENERGY ERROR OF THE DEEP PASSAGE COMES FROM

    Sections 55f-57f left every run that goes below ~0.5 r* with a
    mechanical-energy error of 40-400 k/r0 and asked whether it is the
    integrator or a mismatch between softened forces and energy.  Tilted
    para 30 deg, floor 0.25 r*, radiation reaction off, engine stepped at
    1/512 of the local Kepler period.  Per accepted step the change of
    E_mech is split as
       (dKE - W)   W = trapezoid work of the forces the integrator uses
       (dU + W)    force vs energy-function consistency
       dU_mom      change of U from the moments alone (positions fixed)

58a. NOT THE INTEGRATOR.  Summed over the passage, per r/floor bin [k/r0]:

       bin      dE_mech    dKE - W    dU + W    dU_mom
       >4        +0.69      -0.003     +0.71     -0.02
       2-4     -306.4       -6.3     -289.1     -11.1
       1-2     +331.8       +2.2     +378.3     -48.7
       <1       -19.2       -0.4      -18.8     +0.06

     Nearly all of it is dU + W.  (At 1/512 of the period this run did not
     fail; it left the pair ionized at +7.3 k/r0.  The failure at 0.53 r* is
     a consequence of the state this produces.)

58b. THE SECTOR: THE GRADIENT FORCE AGAINST THE DIPOLE ENERGY TERM.
       bin     W_gradient   -dU_dip   static-force work   W_LW   -dU_Coulomb
       >4        +5.49       +4.85        +4.84           +1.95   +1.91
       2-4     -263.4       +27.8        +15.6            +0.85   +0.66
       1-2     +301.5       -30.3        -84.2            -5.71   +0.01
     Far out the gradient force matches the energy (ratio 1.22, cos 0.995
     at 3.5 floors).  In 1-4 floors its work is ten times larger and of the
     opposite sign.  The Lienard-Wiechert channel stays consistent.

58c. THE MECHANISM: RETARDED CONFIGURATION, ROTATING FAST.  Channel split of
     the gradient force along the passage, radial components.  The
     regularized low-velocity dipole channel (the whole field below ~1.5
     floors, where the blend weight is ~0) equals the STATIC force of the
     partner placed at its retarded position with its retarded moment:

       r/fl  beta  sep. angle  moment angle  retarded-static  reg. channel  instantaneous static
       2.47  0.36    0.236        0.035         -0.090          -0.094          -0.158
       1.22  0.57    0.407        0.064         -0.579          -0.614          +0.912
       1.03  0.68    0.555        0.090         +1.389          +1.063          +1.734
       1.51  0.71    0.350        0.230         +1.704          +1.097          -2.740
       2.62  0.57    0.140        0.076         -1.768          (exact) -1.83   -0.012

     The separation direction turns by up to 0.56 rad during r/c, and the
     force follows that retarded configuration while E_mech is instantaneous.
     The exact moving-dipole field above 2 floors does the same: the partner's
     acceleration there (1e29-1e30 m/s^2) changes its velocity by 0.1-0.7 c
     within r/c, so no extrapolation to the present position applies.

58d. THE SOFTENING IS CONSISTENT ONLY TO ORDER OF MAGNITUDE.  Same run with
     the instantaneous force model (useRetardedExternalForces = false):
       bin      dE_mech   dKE - W   dU + W   dU_mom
       2-4       +3.12    +0.017    +2.61    +0.50
       1-2       -8.43    -0.28    -15.24    +7.09
     and it fails at 0.40 r*.  Twenty times smaller than the retarded
     mismatch, but not zero: the instantaneous dipole forces and the moment
     dynamics also leak ~10 k/r0 through the softened zone.

58e. WHAT FOLLOWS.  The energy error is not a numerical defect that a
     tolerance or a stencil can remove.  It is produced by retarded dipole
     fields at beta 0.5-0.8 and r ~ 1-4 floors, set against an instantaneous
     mechanical energy, with a smaller, separate inconsistency in the
     instantaneous softened model.  Whether the retarded exchange is physical
     field-energy exchange cannot be told from this model: boundFieldEnergy
     closes the balance by construction.  Its size (hundreds of k/r0 swinging
     within one passage, against a dipole interaction energy of ~30 k/r0 at
     those radii) is far above any plausible stored field energy.  Removing
     it requires a choice about the physics in the short-range zone, not a
     numerical fix.

59. THE SWITCH: INSTANTANEOUS DIPOLE SECTOR BELOW N FLOORS

    Following section 58 (and the user's choice of a switch plus measurement
    over a change of default): CREM_DIPOLE_INSTANTANEOUS_BELOW=N replaces the
    whole dipole sector of retardedExternalForces -- the charge's Lorentz
    force in the partner's dipole field and covariantDipoleGradientForce --
    by the instantaneous one of allExternalForces (regularizedDipoleForce
    plus chargeDipoleForces) below N floors, blending by a smoothstep in the
    present separation between N and 2N floors.  The charge-charge
    Lienard-Wiechert force stays retarded.  Unset: validation 59/59 with a
    validation printout identical line for line, header isolation 37/37,
    production branch trace identical to 57d (t = 199.442442952 ps,
    peri/barrier 3.89708460123).

59a. THE RETARDED EXCHANGE IS GONE.  Tilted para 30 deg, same split as 58a
     [k/r0], summed per r/floor bin up to t = 2.3e-20 s:

                      2-4 floors            1-2 floors
                   dU+W     dU_mom       dU+W     dU_mom
       off        -289.1    -11.1       +378.3    -48.7
       N = 2        +5.7     +0.6        -16.9     +7.9
       N = 4        +2.7     +0.5         +6.2     +0.4
     (58d, fully instantaneous forces: +2.6 / +0.5 and -15.2 / +7.1)

     dKE - W stays below 0.3 k/r0 in every case.  What is left is the
     smaller inconsistency of the instantaneous softened model itself.

59b. TRAJECTORIES (floor 0.25 r*, radiation reaction off).  max|dE_mech| in
     k/r0; "bound" = full window without leaving:

       case          off                       N = 2                    N = 4
       radial para   bound, 6.5e-3             bound, 6.5e-3            bound, 1.4e-2
       radial orto   ionized to 238 r*, 312    leaves to 15.7 r*, 2.5   BOUND, 73 minima
                                                                        0.46-1.59 r*, 12.7
       0 deg para    bound 1.3244 r*, 2.3e-2   same                     bound 1.3073 r*, 1.3e-2
       0 deg orto    ionized to 819 r*, 342    leaves to 43 r*, 2.5     BOUND, 356 minima
                                                                        0.47-1.20 r*, 14.4
       30 deg para   fails at 0.24 r*, 128     fails at 0.33 r*, 10.5   fails at 0.27 r*, 8.7
       30 deg orto   ionized to 759 r*, 221    fails at 0.53 r*, 1.5    fails at 0.57 r*, 0.47
       60 deg para   fails at 0.23 r*, 45      fails at 0.51 r*, 5.0    fails at 0.54 r*, 2.3
       60 deg orto   ionized to 138 r*, 13     fails at 0.50 r*, 2.8    BOUND, 525 minima
                                                                        0.52-0.67 r*, 13.7

59c. READING.  With N = 4 the energy errors drop from 13-342 to 0.5-14
     k/r0, and no ortho run ionizes any more: three of four ortho cases stay
     bound below r* for the whole window.  But the switch does not make the
     tilted runs integrable: five of six tilted runs fail, now at 0.27-0.57 r*
     and with small energy errors, so these failures are NOT the energy
     corruption of 55-58 and their cause is not yet measured.  The remaining
     10-17 k/r0 in the 1-2 floor zone at N = 2 belongs to the instantaneous
     model (58d), with a moment-dynamics share of about +8 k/r0.

59d. OPEN.  (1) The failures of 59b under the switch.  (2) The ~10-17 k/r0
     inconsistency of the instantaneous softened dipole model.  (3) The
     default stays retarded; whether the short-range zone should be
     instantaneous is still the user's decision.

60. THE OPEN ITEMS OF 59d: TWO DEFECTS OF THE INSTANTANEOUS DIPOLE MODEL

60a. FORCES WERE NOT THE GRADIENTS OF THE ENERGIES (the 10-17 k/r0 leak).
     The instantaneous Coulomb and dipole-dipole forces are gradients with
     respect to the CLAMPED separation r_c = sqrt(r^2 + floor^2) n, while
     conservativeParticleEnergy is a function of the true positions.  Against
     a finite-difference gradient of the energies, 200 random orientations
     per distance, maximum relative residual:

       r/floor   dipole raw   dipole J^T F   Coulomb raw   Coulomb J^T F
       0.3        2.47          1.0e-9          0.713          8.5e-10
       1.0        0.414         8.1e-10         0.293          2.5e-10
       2.0        0.118         4.4e-9          0.106          2.4e-10
       8.0        7.8e-3        6.8e-10         7.7e-3         2.4e-10

     FIX: clampedSeparationForceToTruePositions() applies J^T (radial part
     times r/r_c, transverse part times r_c/r) in coulombForces, in
     mutualForces' dipole force, and in the switch of section 59.

60b. THE SWITCHED STEP WAS FIRST ORDER (the failures of 59b).  On a captured
     switched failure (tilt 30 para, N = 4, 0.40 r*) no force component
     jumps (total force second difference 1.1-1.2x its median), and the
     retarded history no longer matters (1e-7).  But the step-doubling
     velocity error halved per halving of dt.  Toggling one piece at a time:

       configuration                     per halving   error at dt_fail
       everything                           2x            3.1e-9
       without chargeDipoleForces           4x            3.9e-11
       without dipole-dipole force          2x            9.8e-9
       without precession                   2x            3.1e-9
       chargeDipoleForces on real history   4x            3.7e-11

     chargeDipoleForces took its Thomas-BMT moment derivatives from a
     ONE-STATE history, which extrapolates the partner back by r/c with the
     acceleration stored in the state -- the previous step's value inside a
     step.  FIX: the switch passes the real retarded history.
     allExternalForces still passes the one-state history (the instantaneous
     model and causalInitialHistory); not changed, not measured.

60c. CHECKS.  Validation 59/59, header isolation 37/37.  The production
     branch trace moves, because causalInitialHistory builds the initial
     history from allExternalForces, whose Coulomb force now carries J^T:
     t = 199.442442952 -> 199.442511177 ps, peri/barrier 3.89708460123 ->
     3.89733269045, 3 photons unchanged.

     bound ortho run below r* and by 66 k/r0 in the 30 deg para run; the
     split above puts about half of what remains in the moment dynamics.
     (2) allExternalForces' one-state history.  (3) The default stays
     retarded; the switch is off unless set.

61. OPEN ITEM 60e(2): THE INSTANTANEOUS MODEL'S ONE-STATE HISTORY

61a. allExternalForces gives chargeDipoleForces a one-state history.  Its
     Thomas-BMT moment derivatives extrapolate the partner back by r/c with
     the acceleration stored in the state, which inside a step is the
     previous step's value.  Step-doubling order of the instantaneous model
     (useRetardedExternalForces = false) on the captured state of 60b:

       configuration                             per halving   error at dt_fail
       as it was                                     2x           3.1e-9
       without chargeDipoleForces                    4x           3.9e-11
       without precession                            2x           3.1e-9
       one-state history with zero accelerations     4x           3.8e-11

     FIX: the one-state history keeps position and velocity and zeroes the
     accelerations, so the extrapolation is uniform motion fixed by the state
     itself.  Validation 59/59, header isolation 37/37, production branch
     trace identical to 60c (t = 199.442511177 ps, peri/barrier
     3.89733269045).

62. OPEN ITEM 60e(1): THE ~14 k/r0 DRIFT OF THE BOUND SWITCHED RUNS

    Ortho, tilt 0 deg, floor 0.25 r*, N = 4, radiation reaction disabled,
    three Kepler periods of r0 = 3 r*.  Per step, the work of each force
    sector against its energy terms, summed per r/floor bin.

62a. WHEN.  E_mech falls by 14.36 k/r0 within the first 0.7 T, while the
     pair sinks from 2.1 r* to 0.55 r*, and is then flat to five digits for
     the remaining 2.3 T at r = 0.5475 r*.  The final relative speed is
     1.75e-3 c: the pair has come to rest at the dipole-barrier equilibrium.
     It is not a secular drift.  Precession on or off gives identical
     numbers to every printed digit.

62b. WHERE [k/r0]:
       bin          dKE-W     W_lw + dCoul + dDarwin   W_inst + dDip + dChDip (moments included)
       >8 floors   -3e-5          +0.009                       -0.28
       4-8         -2e-4          +0.13                        -0.73
       1-4         -0.023        -14.86                        +0.42
     The instantaneous dipole sector is consistent to under 1 k/r0.  The
     whole loss is the RETARDED charge-charge force against the Coulomb plus
     Darwin energy.

62c. WHY.  Two candidates, same bins:
       bin      mismatch   hard clamp vs Plummer   -E_cross (pair dipole
                           Coulomb energy          radiation cross term)
       4-8       +0.13          +0.078                 -0.077
       1-4      -14.86          +0.40                 -27.9
     The regularization mismatch between the Lienard-Wiechert field (hard
     max(r, floor)) and the energy (Plummer) accounts for +0.4 k/r0 only.
     The loss has the sign and the order of the cross term of the pair's
     dipole radiation, P_cross = mu0 q1 q2 a1.a2 / (3 pi c): each particle's
     retarded field carries the partner's radiation, so the mutual forces
     radiate even with the self radiation reaction switched off, and the
     loss stops when the pair stops accelerating.  It is 0.53 of the
     nonrelativistic cross term; the Schott boundary terms vanish between
     rest-like end states, so the missing factor (relativistic corrections at
     beta ~ 0.3 inside 1-4 floors, or the clamps) is not accounted for.

62d. CONCLUSION.  The bound runs' energy drift is radiation carried by the
     mutual retarded fields, not a numerical or consistency defect; the
     consistency defects of 58-61 are fixed.  Its size relative to the
     nonrelativistic cross term (0.53) is unexplained, and the 66 k/r0 of
     the long tilt-30 para run (60d), where the moments also turn over, was
     not split.

63. WHAT REMAINS OF THE DRIFT: THREE MEASUREMENTS, NO CODE CHANGE

    Tilted para 30 deg, floor 0.25 r*, N = 4, one Kepler period of r0.
    E_mech swings from -66 to +34 k/r0 and the pair ends flying apart at
    beta 0.73.  Same split as section 62.

63a. THE MOMENTS: NOT PRECESSION, THE VELOCITY DEPENDENCE OF THE LAB MOMENT.
       1-4 floors [k/r0]       precession on    precession off
       W_inst                     +223.0            +99.4
       -dU at fixed moments       +223.0           -206.1
       -dU from moments alone     -234.9           +322.1
     The instantaneous dipole force matches the energy at fixed lab moments
     (223 vs 223), but the lab moments firstDipole/secondDipole change by
     hundreds of k/r0 of interaction energy even with precession switched
     off.  They follow the velocity through synchronizeCovariantDipoles, so
     the dipole energy is a velocity-dependent potential that the force does
     not differentiate, and KE + U is not the energy the dynamics conserves.
     Fixing that is a reformulation (a Lagrangian energy function, or forces
     in terms of the proper moments), not a patch.

63b. THE COULOMB SECTOR BELOW THE FLOOR: +44 k/r0, UNEXPLAINED.  Inside one
     floor the charge-charge mismatch is +44.3 k/r0, against -7.8 of pair
     radiation cross term and -0.12 from the hard-clamp versus Plummer
     Coulomb energy (with the clamp potential written correctly as linear
     inside r_e).  Neither accounts for it.

63c. THE FAR-ZONE FLUX OF THE DIPOLE SECTOR IS WRONG AT SHORT RANGE.  The
     flux diagnostic (computeOutwardFlux) reported 9.7e4 k/r0 radiated in one
     deep passage.  Split on captured states:
       state                   charge sector vs |q1a1+q2a2|^2   dipole sector vs mu0|m''|^2/6pi c^3 + induced p = v x m/c^2
       3 r*, at rest              5.70 vs 5.71 W                   3.4e-6 vs 1.1e-5 W
       0.40 r*, beta 0.43         1.30e8 vs 1.64e8 W               6.25e10 vs 6.8e7 W (920x)
       0.33 r*, beta 0.66         5.8e7 vs 1.24e8 W                2.67e10 vs 5.4e8 W (49x)
     Independent of the sphere radius (x0.01..x100) and of dropping the
     velocity fields, so it is radiation, not near field.  Expanding both
     far-zone poles from one history segment (the near-field fix of 55a)
     changed it by 0.2% and was reverted.  Cause not found.  The flux does
     not feed back into the trajectory.

63d. OPEN.  The lab-moment velocity dependence of 63a; the +44 k/r0 of 63b;
     the far-zone dipole flux of 63c; the 0.53 of 62c; and the default model
     (retarded dipole sector) remains the user's decision.

64. STEP 1 OF THE PLAN: TILTED MOMENTS AT THE DEFAULT FLOOR

    Default model (retarded dipole sector, no switch), default floor
    (scale 1, floor = r*), radiation reaction off, start 3 r* with
    v_t = 0.2 v_circ, three Kepler periods of r0.  Integrator as of 57-61.

64a. OUTCOMES.  No run fails numerically any more (53e: ortho failed at
     0.218 r*).  No pair is held:

       tilt   channel  r_min (r*)  after the minimum          max|dE_mech| (k/r0)
       0      para       0.209     ejected to 32.6 r*              3.61
       0      orto       0.219     ejected to 55.3 r*              3.26
       30     para       0.790     ejected to 12.2 r*              2.31
       30     orto       0.406     ejected to 82.8 r*              3.50
       60     para       0.322     ejected to 94.0 r*              4.15
       60     orto       0.933     ejected to 9.4 r*               1.30
       90     para       0.100     contact at t/obs 0.045          4.60
       90     orto       0.623     ejected to 49.2 r*              2.40

     Seven of eight go below r* once and leave; the pair starts bound at
     about -0.9 k/r0, so every ejection needs an energy gain.

64b. WHERE THE GAIN COMES FROM.  Work of each retarded force sector against
     its energy term over one period, bins in floors (= r* here) [k/r0]:

       para 0 deg     W_LW    -dCoul   |  W_grad   -dU_dip   | dChDip
       1-2 floors    +0.020   +0.002   |  +0.305   -0.0004   | +0.10
       < 1 floor     +0.512   -0.001   |  +0.095   -0.0017   | -0.17

       para 30 deg    W_grad   -dU_dip
       1-2 floors    +3.192   +3.113
       < 1 floor     -2.400   -3.037

     Inside the floor the Lienard-Wiechert force (hard clamp max(r, floor):
     constant magnitude k/floor^2) does +0.51 k/r0 of work while the Plummer
     Coulomb energy does not move, and the gradient force adds +0.3 k/r0 in
     1-2 floors with no dipole-energy change.  This is the non-conservative
     core of 53f, now measured sector by sector: the three short-range
     treatments of section 52c (LW hard clamp, Plummer energies, dipole
     blend) generate no common potential.  Radiation would remove energy;
     these add it.

64c. READING.  At the default floor the question "does the dipole barrier
     hold a pair" cannot be answered: the barrier zone r*..2r* is inside the
     regularization (52-53), and what the trajectories show instead is the
     ejection produced by that non-conservative core.  The same inconsistency
     is the likeliest home of the unexplained +44 k/r0 below the floor in
     63b.  Making the short-range treatments derive from one potential is a
     precondition for any statement about the core, at any floor.

65. UNIFYING THE SHORT-RANGE REGULARIZATION, STEP 1: THE CHARGE FIELD

    Following 64c, with the user's agreement to change the default model:
    every short-range treatment is to derive from one Plummer potential.
    Step 1 is the charge sector.

65a. CHANGE.  lienardWiechertField scales the whole field by
     (R_rest/R_c)^3, R_c = sqrt(R_rest^2 + floor^2), where R_rest = gamma
     kappa R is the distance in the source's rest frame (a Lorentz scalar).
     For a source at rest it is -grad of -k/sqrt(r^2 + floor^2), the Coulomb
     energy conservativeParticleEnergy and coulombForces already used.  It
     replaces the hard clamp max(R, floor).  A first version scaled by the
     lab distance R; it broke Lorentz covariance (boosted field residual
     8.4e-3, boosted tensor on trajectory states 2.9e-11 -> 1.2e-7, particle
     boost F 1.3e-12 -> 2.5e-6); with R_rest all of these return to their old
     values (1.3e-12, 1.5e-11, 6.8e-13).  Pairs without a floor keep
     max(R, nuclearCutoff).

65b. VALIDATION.  59/59 after two changes to the checks, both measured:
     - The boosted-charge field and invariant checks compared against the
       bare point field; the reference now carries the same softening in the
       rest frame (residuals 1.2e-16 and 1.4e-16, threshold 1e-10).
     - far-field-convergence demanded monotonic radius residuals at 1e4-1e6
       a0 where they are ~4e-8, three hundred times inside the 1e-5 bound;
       their order flipped (3.6e-8 / 5.3e-8) when the shared visual state
       moved.  Monotonicity is now required only above 1e-6.
     Header isolation 37/37.  retarded Gauss on the Maxwell block rose from
     8.4e-6 to 2.0e-4 (not a gated check): the softened field is not the
     field of the deposited point-like clouds.

65c. PRODUCTION MOVES.  Branch trace seed 42 / index 2 / para:
       t 199.442511177 -> 199.442154855 ps, 3 photons unchanged,
       binding 753.84 -> 755.69 eV, L 0.094996 -> 0.094880 hbar,
       peri/barrier 3.897 -> 4.929 (now equal to a/barrier).
     The periapsis comes from dipoleAwarePeriapsis on the secular elements
     after the last photon and is sensitive to it; the non-covariant first
     version gave 4.074.  The softening reaches the production pericentre at
     3.9 r*, where the Coulomb force is 9% weaker than the old hard clamp.

65d. DEFAULT FLOOR, CHARGE SECTOR NOW CONSISTENT.  Para passage, one period,
     work against energy [k/r0]:
                     W_LW    -dCoul       (before: < 1 floor +0.512 vs -0.001)
       2-4 floors   +0.406   +0.393
       1-2 floors   +0.341   +0.296
       < 1 floor    +0.008   +0.001
     The dipole sector still is not: para 30 deg, W_grad against -dU_dip
     +0.554 vs +0.397 (2-4 floors) and -0.185 vs -0.353 (1-2 floors), about
     +0.33 k/r0 per passage.  Its field blends a point dipole at the clamped
     separation with one at the true separation through a position-dependent
     weight, and is unsoftened above two floors while the energy is softened.

66. UNIFYING THE SHORT-RANGE REGULARIZATION, STEP 2: THE DIPOLE SECTOR

    The user chose the Plummer form for the dipole sector too, giving up the
    magnetic regularization profile for pairs with a separation floor.

66a. CHANGE.  One potential, rho^2 = r^2 + floor^2 on the TRUE separation:
       B = mu0/(4 pi) [3 r (r.m)/rho^5 - m/rho^3],  U_dd = -m_t . B,
       q-mu:  B_charge = mu0 q (v x r)/(4 pi rho^3),
     implemented as plummerDipoleField, pairDipoleField,
     pairDipoleInteractionEnergy, pairDipoleForce (analytic -grad U).  Used by
     conservativeParticleEnergy, makeFrame, mutualForces, the switch of 59,
     chargeDipoleInteractionEnergy, chargeDipolePairForces/chargeDipoleForces,
     and the secular collapse model (azimuthAveragedDipoleEnergy, the orbit-
     averaged dipole force).  In the retarded two-charge construction each
     pole now carries lienardWiechertField's covariant Plummer factor, so the
     pole pair IS this dipole in the static limit; with a floor
     retardedMagneticDipoleField and retardedElectricDipoleField return the
     exact construction directly (no low-velocity core, no blend), and a
     static source returns plummerDipoleField.  Pairs without a floor are
     unchanged (they keep regularizedDipoleField and the blend).

66b. VALIDATION 59/59 after four reference changes, each forced by the model
     change and each tighter afterwards:
       static electric dipole E   point -k p/r^3 -> Plummer   5.0e-6 -> 5.4e-10
       tensor gradient static     regularizedDipoleForce -> pairDipoleForce
                                                              1.1e-5 -> 3.1e-8
       retarded static limit      profile at clamped r -> pairDipoleField
                                                              5.3e-4 -> 0
       regularization-radius scan with a floor the radius must now leave the
                                  trajectory EXACTLY unchanged (was: must
                                  change it); measured 0 / 0 / 0
     Informational, not gated: "dipole boosted-static B" 3.7e-8 -> 5.0e-6
     (point-dipole reference at beta 0.9995-0.9999).  Header isolation 37/37.

66c. CONSISTENCY at the default floor, one period, work against energy
     [k/r0]:

       2-4 floors      -0.066  -0.064                       +0.217  +0.294
       1-2 floors      -0.002  -0.001                       -0.312  -0.392
       < 1 floor       -0.003  -0.001
     Net per passage 0.003 k/r0 at 30 deg (65d: +0.33).  The Lienard-
     Wiechert sector stays consistent (+0.178 vs +0.179).

66d. PRODUCTION.  Branch trace seed 42 / index 2 / para: t 199.442154855 ->

67. PLAN STEP 2 AFTER THE UNIFICATION: KEEP THE RETARDED DEFAULT?

    The switch CREM_DIPOLE_INSTANTANEOUS_BELOW (59) was introduced because
    the retarded dipole forces exchanged hundreds of k/r0 with the
    instantaneous energy.  Sections 65-66 removed the regularization part of
    that; this checks what remains and whether the switch still changes
    anything that matters.

67a. PROBE FLOOR 0.25 r*, retarded default, tilt 30 para, same passage as
     58 (to t = 2.25e-20 s), work against energy [k/r0]:
                     W_grad   -dU_dip   static-force work
       2-4 floors    +10.8    +15.9       +16.5          (58: -263 vs +27.8)
       1-2 floors     -9.3    +29.8       +29.9          (58: +302 vs -30.3)
     The static force now matches the energy; the retarded force still
     differs by -5 and -39 k/r0.  That is the retardation of 58c (beta ~0.5,
     configuration turning during r/c), no longer amplified by an
     inconsistent regularization, and it lives below r*.

67b. DEFAULT FLOOR, three periods, switch N = 2 against no switch (66e):
       tilt  channel  no switch                        N = 2
       0     para     bound, r_min 0.951, dE 2.9e-2     bound, r_min 0.953, dE 1.5e-3
       0     orto     oscillates 0.18-1.35 r*, 1.4e-1   contact at t/obs 0.058
       30    para     bound, r_min 1.023, 4.7e-2        bound, r_min 1.026, 1.3e-2
       30    orto     oscillates 0.16-1.44 r*, 1.9e-1   contact at t/obs 0.58
       60    para     r_min 0.670, 2.0e-1               r_min 0.668, 1.3e-2
       60    orto     oscillates 0.12-1.54 r*, 2.7e-1   contact at t/obs 0.91
       90    para     r_min 0.352, 3.2e-1               r_min 0.348, 9.5e-3
       90    orto     held above r*, 14 minima, 3.0e-2  held above r*, 25 minima, 2.1e-2

     Which pairs the barrier holds above r* is identical (para 0 and 30 deg,
     ortho 90 deg), with the same minimum radii to three digits.  The switch
     lowers the energy errors by up to thirty times and changes only what
     happens BELOW r*: ortho that fell through reaches contact instead of
     oscillating in the core.

67c. DECISION STANDS.  The default stays retarded and the switch stays an
     option: at the default floor it does not change any result the model
     declares valid, and below r* neither variant is a claim.  It remains
     useful for probes below the floor, where 67a shows the retarded
     exchange is still tens of k/r0.

68. PLAN STEP 3: THE FAR-ZONE DIPOLE FLUX ON PRODUCTION ORBITS

    63c found the far-zone flux of the dipole sector 49-920x above
    mu0|m''|^2/6 pi c^3 plus the induced p = v x m/c^2 on deep states.  The
    plan was to fix it only if it reaches production observables.

68a. IT DOES REACH THEM, IN PRINCIPLE.  integrateElectrodynamicStep books
     orbitalRadiatedEnergy = (whole far-zone flux) - (analytic M1 flux), and
     estimateCremCollapse adds it to radiatedEnergyTotal and to the energy
     loss per orbit.  The far-zone dipole sector and its interference with
     the charge sector therefore enter the continuous radiation channel.

68b. SIZE ON PRODUCTION ORBITS.  Unified model, default floor, circular
     para orbits with moments along the orbit normal, after half a period of
     history, far-zone sphere 1e6 a0 [W]:

       r/r*    beta    charges   pair Larmor   dipoles    M1 + induced   cross term
       548     0.0036  1.16e-8    1.16e-8      3.5e-22     1.0e-26      -1.0e-17
       50.2    0.0120  1.65e-4    1.65e-4      6.9e-14     2.4e-16      -2.0e-10
       10.9    0.0250  6.90e-2    6.89e-2      1.1e-8      7.7e-10      -7.3e-6
       6.61    0.0294  0.450      0.446        3.7e-7      5.6e-8       -1.5e-4

     The dipole sector is 3e-14 to 8e-7 of the charge sector (still 7-35000x
     its expected size).  The cross term is larger, -1e-9 to -3.4e-4 of the
     charge sector, and does not change with the quadrature (50 and 194
     directions agree to 0.4%), so it is not integration error; by parity
     E1-M1 interference should integrate to zero, so it is a construction
     defect, but a small one.  The charge sector matches the pair's Larmor
     power to 0.1% at 10.9 r* and 0.8% at 6.6 r*.

68c. DECISION: NOT FIXED.  At the terminal radii the continuous channel is
     contaminated by at most 3.4e-4 of itself, and estimateCremCollapse
     measures that channel at 0.002-0.018% of the radiated energy (photons
     carry the rest), so the effect on lifetimes is below 1e-7.  The defect
     stays recorded for anyone who uses the far-zone flux below ~5 r* or at
     relativistic speeds, where 63c shows it is large.

69. PLAN STEP 4: A LAGRANGIAN TREATMENT OF THE LAB-MOMENT VELOCITY DEPENDENCE?

    63a found that the lab moments (lorentzBoostDipole of the proper moments
    with the particle velocity: transverse part x gamma, plus p = gamma v x
    m/c^2) changed the dipole energy by hundreds of k/r0 per deep passage
    with precession off, which the instantaneous dipole force at fixed lab
    moments does not account for.  Two reformulations were on the table: a
    potential built on PROPER moments, or the full Lagrangian with the
    hidden-momentum force -d/dt(dU/dv) and energy KE + U - v.dU/dv.  Re-
    measured after the unification of 65-66, instantaneous dipole sector
    (N = 4), splitting the moment part of dU into the velocity dependence at
    fixed proper moments and the precession of the proper moments.

69a. PROBE FLOOR 0.25 r*, tilt 30 para, one period, 1-4 floors [k/r0]:
                         W_dd   -dU_lab  -dU_proper  velocity  precession
       precession on    +34.9   +34.5     +34.5      -3.9      +8.6
       precession off   +36.2   +33.3     +33.4      -2.0       0
     The velocity dependence is now 2-4 k/r0 (63a: +-230-320, before the
     regularizations were made consistent).  An energy on proper moments is
     no better conserved than one on lab moments (residuals 0.43 vs 0.36 with
     precession, 2.8 vs 2.9 without).

69b. DEFAULT FLOOR, three periods, all bins [k/r0]:
       tilt   velocity part     |W_dd - (-dU_lab)|
       0      <= 6.4e-5              <= 7e-6
       30     <= 1.4e-4              <= 1.2e-3
       90     <= 2.2e-4              <= 1.9e-4

69c. DECISION: NOT IMPLEMENTED.  Where the model is valid the effect is below
     3e-4 k/r0 over three periods, and the proper-moment variant would not
     even reduce the residual.  The Lagrangian variant could remove the
     remaining 2-4 k/r0 per passage below r* at the probe floor, a region
     the model declares invalid.  The motivating defect of 63a was the
     inconsistent regularization, not the lab-moment velocity dependence.

70. DO THE FAILURES BEHIND THE RETARDATION STOP STILL HAPPEN?

    estimateCremCollapse stops when period/light-crossing <= 150.  Its comment
    and CollapseStopCause call this a NUMERICAL safety margin: with only the
    Compton-barrier stop active, 23 of 100 trajectories once failed
    numerically with that ratio between 37.8 and 71.6, and 150 is a factor ~2
    above that band.  Re-measured on the current code (sections 55-69),
    default configuration (--level 1, no ground-state floor), para, master
    seed 42, indices 0-99, CREM_RETARDATION_LIMIT=0 so only the barrier stops
    a trajectory.  The last evaluated ratio and periapsis were read through a
    temporary hook in the estimator, removed afterwards.

70a. 100 TRAJECTORIES, 120 s WALL BUDGET EACH:
       NumericalFailure                       0   (was 23)
       ended below ratio 72 / below 37.8      68 / 5
       lowest ratio reached                   29.8
       last periapsis                         1.19 - 11.2 r*
     All 100 ended on the wall budget, none at the barrier, so this alone
     does not show that they would get there.

70b. THE 8 LOWEST RATIOS, 1200 s WALL BUDGET:
       index  outcome           last ratio  last periapsis   photons  wall
       4      barrier           15.8        0.185 r*         4        154 s
       10     barrier           16.5        0.200 r*         4        142 s
       23     barrier           15.3        0.173 r*         4        160 s
       34     barrier            1.77       0.911 r*         3        220 s
       49     barrier            0.92       0.865 r*         3        258 s
       51     barrier           17.2        0.218 r*         4        114 s
       54     observation limit 10.1        1.77 r*          3        205 s
       87     barrier            0.90       0.944 r*         3        309 s
     Seven reach the barrier, none fails; the ratio goes down to 0.9.  The
     trajectories that fire a fourth photon overshoot to 0.17-0.22 r*, the
     single-photon jump noted in the estimator's own comment.  The lab
     collapse time is 199.438-199.448 ps in every case, the same as with the
     150 stop: the last stretch from ratio 150 to the barrier takes well under
     0.01 ps.

70c. READING.  The numerical failures that justified the margin no longer
     occur, so the stop is no longer needed for the integrator.  The comment's
     other justification is physical and remains: condition (b) marks where
     the Darwin/retardation approximation behind the secular energy signal
     stops holding, and a period shorter than one light crossing (ratio 0.9)
     is far outside it.  Lowering the threshold would therefore change which
     stop ends a trajectory, not its collapse time, and would carry it into a
     regime the secular model does not describe.  Not changed.

71. FLOOR 1 AGAINST SMALLER FLOORS: WHAT IS PHYSICS AND WHAT IS REGULARIZATION

    With the Plummer softening of 65-66 the floor reaches further than the
    old hard clamp: at 1.3 r* the Coulomb force is 19% weaker with floor
    0.5 r* and still 1% with 0.1 r*.  So the comparison runs down to 0.05 r*.
    Unified model, retarded default, no switch, radiation reaction off.

71a. TILTED MOMENTS (start 3 r*, v_t = 0.2 v_circ, three periods).  Minimum
     separation, and whether the pair stays above r* for the whole window:

       case          floor 1        0.5            0.25           0.1            0.05
       para 0 deg    0.951 held     1.231 held     1.301 held     1.321 held     1.323 held
       para 30 deg   1.023 held     0.676 falls    0.320 falls    0.273 falls    -
       ortho 90 deg  1.035 held     0.104 falls    0.306 falls    1.082 held     1.127 held
       ortho 0 deg   0.182 falls    0.100 falls    0.323 falls    0.410 falls    0.370 falls
       ortho 30 deg  0.162 falls    0.100 falls    0.334 falls    0.415 falls    -
     ("falls" runs end below r* in contact or a numerical failure in the
     region the model declares invalid; energy errors there reach 1-115
     k/r0, against at most 0.07 k/r0 for the held runs at small floors.)

     - Para at 0 deg is PHYSICS: held at every floor, with the minimum
       converging to 1.32 r* (1.321 / 1.323 at 0.1 / 0.05; 53d found 1.3243
       before the unification).  Floor 1 moves that minimum down by 28%.
     - Para at 30 deg is REGULARIZATION: held only at floor 1.
     - Ortho at 90 deg is held at floor 1 and again at 0.1 and 0.05
       (minimum 1.08, 1.13 r*, not yet converged), but falls at 0.5 and 0.25:
       a barrier that is marginal at this start, so held at floor 1 for a
       partly wrong reason.  Unresolved.
     - Ortho at 0 and 30 deg falls at every floor.

71b. PRODUCTION (estimateCremCollapse, --level 1, no ground-state floor,
     master seed 42, indices 0-23, 200 s budget, retardation stop at 150).
     All 96 end on the retardation stop with 3 photons.

       channel  mean t floor 1     mean t floor 0.5    paired diff (sd)          binding eV
       para     199.442597 ps      199.442422 ps       -1.7e-4 ps (1.6e-3), 13+  729.4 -> 730.5
       ortho    199.467878 ps      199.467903 ps       +2.5e-5 ps (2.2e-3), 13+  761.2 -> 761.3

     No trajectory changes its photon count.  The paired differences are
     1e-6 of the collapse time and of random sign; the channel difference
     ortho - para is 0.02528 ps at floor 1 and 0.02548 ps at floor 0.5.  The
     production lifetimes and their para/ortho difference do not depend on
     the floor: they are decided far above r*, before the retardation stop.

71c. READING.  The regularization matters only for what happens within a
     few r* of the barrier.  There, floor 1 is not a small correction: it
     turns a physically falling para at 30 deg into a held one and shifts the
     held para's turning point by 28%.  Dynamical statements about the
     barrier need a floor of 0.1 r* or below; the production lifetimes do
     not, because production stops well before the barrier.

72. CAN THE "FREEZE AT THE BARRIER" BE REMOVED NOW?

72a. WHERE IT CAME FROM.  ecf7380 (2026-08-21, "Zamroz prawa sily/energii
     pod bariera Comptona") introduced separationFloor() =
     comptonBarrierRadius and clampedSeparationVector (then max(r, floor))
     for every conservative force, energy and field, so that nothing grew
     below r*.  The reason was numerical: the CREM floor study's trajectory
     (seed 42) failed at periapsis ~490 fm, and with the clamp it reached
     ~28 fm.  f477866 (2026-08-22) replaced the hard clamp by Plummer
     softening because its kink stalled the integrator, and rebuilt the
     secular layer (regularizedPotentialEnergy = -(A/floor) atan(floor/r),
     criticalRadius from A r^3 = L^2 (r^2 + floor^2), regularizedTurningPoints,
     regularizedPeriod) on the force A/(r^2 + floor^2).  Sections 60 and 65-66
     changed the dynamic model to the Plummer POTENTIAL -A/sqrt(r^2+floor^2),
     force A r/(r^2+floor^2)^(3/2); the secular layer still uses the old law.

72b. THE REASONS, RE-CHECKED.
     - The integrator failures it was built against are gone (70: 0 of 100
       fail with only the barrier stop).
     - validation with CREM_SEPARATION_FLOOR_SCALE=0.05: 59/59.
     - Dynamics above r* need floor <= 0.1 r* to converge (71a).

72c. PRODUCTION at floors 0.05 and 0.01 (same 48 trajectories as 71b), all
     ending on the retardation stop with 3 photons, no failure:
       channel  floor 1        0.5            0.05           0.01
       para     199.442597 ps  199.442422     200.163010     200.163012
       ortho    199.467878     199.467903     199.468118     199.468183
     47 of 48 agree with floor 1 to about 1e-3 ps.  One para trajectory
     (index 1) takes 216.739 ps at 0.05 and 0.01 against 199.441 at 1 and
     0.5; ortho index 9 moves by 0.0075 ps at 0.5 and below.  The checkpoint
     trace of para index 1 shows why: at checkpoint 11 (t = 186.7 ps), still
     at the n = 1 radius ~550 r*, floor 1 fires the first photon and floor
     0.05 fires it one checkpoint later.  The softening changes the force
     there by ~1e-6, enough to move a threshold across a checkpoint
     boundary -- the discrete-branch sensitivity already documented for the
     collapse time, not physics near the barrier.

72d. READING.  Nothing that motivated the freeze still requires a floor at
     r*: the integrator no longer fails, validation holds at 0.05 r*,
     production lifetimes do not move except by branch flips triggered far
     from the barrier, and the barrier dynamics are only correct at small
     floors.  What still needs a floor at all is below r*: at small floors
     the probe runs that fall through end in numerical failures with energy
     errors of 1-115 k/r0 (71a), a region the stopping rules keep production
     out of.  Two things would have to go with a change of default: a small
     softening must stay (floor = 0 sends e+e- down the no-floor paths --
     the nuclear cutoff and the old magnetic regulator -- which are exactly
     the inconsistencies 65-66 removed), and the secular layer's atan
     potential should be replaced by the Plummer one so that the secular
     turning points and period describe the same force as the dynamics.

73. THE BARRIER FREEZE REMOVED: DEFAULT FLOOR 0.05 r*, SECULAR LAYER ON PLUMMER

    With the user's agreement after 72.

73a. CHANGES.
     - separationFloor() defaults to 0.05 r* (CREM_SEPARATION_FLOOR_SCALE,
       now the floor in units of r*, unset = 0.05; 1 restores the old
       default).  A floor is kept as a numerical guard: floor = 0 would put
       e+e- on the no-floor paths (nuclear cutoff, magnetic regularization
       profile) that 65-66 made consistent only for floored pairs.
     - The secular layer of estimateCremCollapse now uses the dynamics'
       potential: regularizedPotentialEnergy = -A/sqrt(r^2 + floor^2) (was
       -(A/floor) atan(floor/r), the potential of the pre-60 force
       A/(r^2+floor^2)); criticalRadius solves A r^4 = L^2 (r^2+floor^2)^(3/2)
       (was A r^3 = L^2 (r^2+floor^2)); the circular-orbit margin scale in
       regularizedTurningPoints is force(r) r = A r^2/(r^2+floor^2)^(3/2).
       regularizedPeriapsis and regularizedPeriod follow.
     The stopping rules still read comptonBarrierRadius.

73b. CHECKS.  Validation 59/59, header isolation 37/37.  Production branch
     trace seed 42 / index 2 / para: t 199.450091864 -> 199.442203387 ps,
     binding 755.78 -> 755.26 eV, 3 photons, peri/barrier 4.932.

73c. PRODUCTION SAMPLE, the 48 trajectories of 71b/72c, all ending on the
     retardation stop with 3 photons, no failure:
       channel  floor 1 (old)   floor 0.05, old secular   new default
       para     199.442597 ps   200.163010                200.163020
       ortho    199.467878      199.468118                199.468089
     New default against floor 0.05 with the atan secular layer: mean
     difference 1e-5 ps (para) and -3e-5 ps (ortho), sd 2-3e-4 ps, so the
     secular-layer change is invisible in the lifetimes.  Against the old
     default: the one branch flip of 72c (para index 1, 216.739 against
     199.441 ps, decided at the first photon near n = 1) and nothing else
     above 0.01 ps; no photon count changes.

73d. WHAT THE DEFAULT NOW MEANS.  The Compton barrier is where the model
     stops (the stopping rule) and where it declares itself invalid, not a
     radius below which forces are frozen.  Above r* the default dynamics
     are the floor -> 0 dynamics of 71a (para at 0 deg held with turning
     point 1.32 r*; para at 30 deg not held; ortho at 90 deg held but not
     converged).

74. STEP 4, MEASUREMENT 1: FREE EVOLUTION UNDER THE ZPF, FIRST ROUND

    Sections 42-50 measured the power ratio at fixed radii and found no
    crossing.  This asks the direct question instead: started at different
    radii and left alone under the zero-point field and radiation reaction,
    do orbits drift toward one radius?

74a. WHY NOT THE COLLAPSE ESTIMATOR.  estimateCremCollapse extrapolates the
     secular energy loss as deltaEnergyPerOrbit = -orbitalRadiatedEnergy/mu:
     the radiated flux only.  The ZPF's work never enters the secular
     evolution, so the estimator cannot show a ZPF balance at all.  The
     measurement has to integrate the orbit directly.

74b. SETUP.  Circular orbit, moments along the normal, default model (unified
     Plummer, floor 0.05 r*), --radiation-reaction individual (continuous
     Landau-Lifshitz, no photons, no ladder), ZPF scale 1, band [0.3, 3] x
     omega_orb, 64 modes.  Antithetic pairs: the same field realization with
     every phase shifted by pi, so the pair mean cancels the drift that is
     linear in the field.  Radius measured by the energy, a = -k/(2 E_mech),
     because the instantaneous separation of a non-circular orbit is
     misleading (at 0.01 a_pair with the field off it rose by 14% while the
     energy fell).  6 seeds x 2 phases per radius.

74c. RESULT.  ln(a/a0) at mid-window and end:

       start       orbits  field  pairs expanding  ln a at 50%      ln a at 100%
       0.01 a_pair   40     off         -          -0.049           -0.095
       0.01 a_pair   40     on        5 of 5      +0.67 +- 0.17     +2.3 +- 0.8
       0.03 a_pair  200     off         -          -0.077           -0.178
       0.03 a_pair  200     on        5 of 6      +1.81 +- 0.80     +2.6 +- 1.0

     (unbound states counted as ln a = 10; one run at 0.01 a_pair failed
     numerically after passing through an unbound state, its pair dropped.)
     Without the field the orbit shrinks, as it must.  With it, deep orbits
     are driven OUTWARD fast: 7 of 12 runs at 0.01 a_pair and 2 of 12 at
     0.03 a_pair pass through positive mechanical energy.  At 0.01 a_pair
     the mid-window expansion is 4 sigma; 0.03 a_pair expands from the
     start in 10 of 12 runs.

74d. WHAT IT DOES NOT YET SAY.  Several 0.03 a_pair runs slow down in the
     second half (ln a 1.35 -> 1.56, 1.26 -> 1.34), others keep rising or
     unbind.  Whether the expansion stops near the Bohr radius -- where the
     fixed-radius ratio was consistent with 1 (50c) -- or continues to
     ionization cannot be read from 200 orbits.  That is the question.

74e. COST.  1-8 s per orbit.  The instantaneous force model gives the same
     drift to 1e-3 in ln a (0.2797 vs 0.2834; 0.80473 vs 0.80474; -0.00230
     vs -0.00228 at a_pair) but is only 1.5x faster: the cost is the 64-mode
     field sampling and the step count, not the retardation.  Near a_pair
     radiation changes the radius by 1% in ~2.5e4 orbits, so a convergence
     test there needs ~1e4 orbits per run: about 3 hours per run.

75. MEASUREMENT 1, LONG RUNS: WHERE THE ZPF BALANCES RADIATION, AND WHAT
    HAPPENS THERE

75a. SETTINGS, CHECKED FIRST.  Instantaneous forces with tolerance 1e-5
     (0.97 s/orbit) against the retarded reference at 1e-6 (2.7 s/orbit),
     same field realization, 0.3 a_pair, lnA every 20 orbits for 200 orbits:
       orbit 100:  0.0625 (ret 1e-6)  0.0600 (inst 1e-6)  0.0601 (inst 1e-5)
                   0.0608 (ret 1e-5)
       orbit 200: -0.0052            -0.0037             -0.0059
                  -0.0040
     Differences of order 1e-3, of both signs, not growing -- trajectory
     divergence under a fluctuating drive, against a fluctuation of lnA of
     +-0.06 itself.  The long runs use instantaneous forces at 1e-5, 64
     modes (16 modes would change the realization and save only 15%).

75b. RUNS.  Everything else as 74b (ZPF scale 1, band [0.3,3] x omega_orb,
     64 modes, continuous Landau-Lifshitz reaction, a = -k/(2E)).  4
     antithetic pairs and one field-off control per start: 1 a_pair for
     10000 orbits, 0.3 a_pair for 5000, 0.1 a_pair for 2000.  8 h on 4
     cores.  The pumping share is 1 - (pair mean lnA)/(control lnA): 0 means
     the field does nothing secular, 1 means it cancels radiation, above 1
     the orbit grows.

75c. RESULT.
       start       end    control lnA   pair mean lnA        pumping share
       1 a_pair    10000  -0.0227       -0.0237 +- 0.0079    -0.04 (+-0.35)
       0.3 a_pair   5000  -0.0878       -0.0638 +- 0.0110    +0.27 (+-0.13)
       0.1 a_pair   2000  -0.218        -0.029 +- 0.152 (2)  +0.87, see below
       0.03 a_pair  200   -0.178        +2.6 +- 1.0 (74c)    >> 1
       0.01 a_pair  40    -0.095        +2.3 +- 0.8 (74c)    >> 1

     - At the Bohr radius the field does not balance radiation: the orbit
       shrinks at the radiative rate, 3.0 sigma from a share of 1.
     - At 0.3 a_pair it removes a quarter of the loss; the orbit still
       shrinks (5.8 sigma).
     - At 0.1 a_pair it balances on average, but the runs do not share a
       fate: seed 3 shrinks (both phases, lnA -0.09 and -0.27 against the
       control's -0.22), seed 2 grows slightly (+0.18, +0.07), and seeds 0
       and 1 make excursions to 2-4.6 times the start separation; two runs
       ionize (orbits 1659, 1746) and one fails numerically after an
       excursion (orbit 775).
     - Inside 0.03 a_pair the field drives the orbit out fast.

75d. A START TRANSIENT, AND WHAT IT DOES TO SECTIONS 42-50.  The pumping
     share is high at first and settles: at 0.3 a_pair 0.75-0.95 over the
     first 750 orbits, 0.2-0.3 from 1500 on; at 1 a_pair +0.18 to +0.24
     over the first 1500 orbits, about 0 after 2500.  A circular orbit with
     no field-driven motion absorbs faster until its fluctuation has built
     up.  The fixed-radius work measurements of sections 42-50 integrated
     20 orbits from exactly that state, so they read the transient: that is
     the likeliest reason they found a ratio consistent with 1 at a_pair
     (1.355 +- 0.464, 50b) where the secular share is 0 (3 sigma).  They also
     used a different band and mode count ([0.5,2], 8 modes), so this is an
     explanation, not a demonstrated identity.

75e. WHAT STEP 4 COMES TO.  The secular pumping share falls with radius and
     crosses 1 near 0.1 a_pair (about 27 lambda_C), not at the Bohr radius.
     So the model does single out a radius -- but not as a stationary state:
     at that radius the drive is fluctuation-dominated, orbits wander by
     factors of 2-4, and a quarter of the runs ionize within 2000 orbits.
     That is the known SED picture (a balance only in the mean, with
     self-ionization through large excursions), not a ground state.  Orbits
     that start at the Bohr radius shrink at the radiative rate toward that
     region; following them there takes ~radiative-collapse times (6e5
     orbits), which was not attempted.

75f. LIMITS.  4 pairs and one control per radius; the orbit-tied band
     [0.3,3] x omega_orb and 64 modes throughout (a fixed broadband field
     was not tested); no start between 0.1 and 0.3 a_pair, so the crossing
     is bracketed, not located; the ionizations at 0.1 a_pair were not
     checked against the energy bookkeeping.

76. DIPOLE->ORBIT AND ORBIT->DIPOLE IN THE SKIPPED ORBITS, PARA AND ORTHO

76a. WHAT THE SECULAR LAYER CARRIES.  Per checkpoint estimateCremCollapse
     integrates one orbit and skips up to 200000.  Across the skip the
     magnetic couplings enter only through advanceCoupledSecularSpinOrbit
     (two halves around the photon step):
       - orbit->dipole: both moments precess at the orbit-averaged
         Thomas-BMT rate, fields from localRelativisticFields (LW field of
         the other charge, the other dipole's field, ZPF);
       - dipole->orbit: L = J - S1 - S2 with J held fixed, so L tilts and
         changes length (e changes at fixed a);
       - the apsidal line is only parallel-transported with the plane.
     Not carried: in-plane apsidal precession (neither 1PN nor magnetic),
     the radiative M1 torque on the moments (the engine has one), and the
     measured orbit's dL, which is printed but not applied (known, c1a9a7a).

76b. MEASUREMENT.  Probe dipolb.cpp: full engine, retarded forces, reaction
     OFF, tol 1e-9, 64 steps per Kepler period, 20 orbits, e=0.3, moments
     tilted from L; against it advanceCoupledSecularSpinOrbit from the same
     start over the same time.  Control: the same orbit with zero moments.
     Final values after 20 orbits:

     a/a_pair  a/r*   case        dS1 dir/sec     tilt L dir/sec    |S|/|L|
     1         548    para 40     0.0608/0.0608   0.0428/0.0428     0.52
     1         548    ortho 40    0.1426/0.1426   0.1494/0.1495     0.52
     0.1       55     para 40     0.2760/0.2754   4.275/4.280       1.66
     0.1       55     ortho 40    2.3426/2.3066   7.796/7.651       1.66
     0.1       55     para 90     1.5966/1.5820   0/0               1.66
     0.1       55     ortho 90    1.5684/1.5815   5.212/5.244       1.66
     0.01      5.5    para 40     1.306/1.216     23.2/21.3         5.23
     0.01      5.5    ortho 40    1.526/1.571     16.3/16.5         5.23
     (angles in degrees)

     Spin precession and the L<->S exchange agree to 0.0-1.6% down to
     55 r*.  At 5.5 r*, where |S| is five times |L|, L precesses about J in
     about ten orbits and swings between 16 and 88 degrees.  The secular
     transport follows that swing for para within a few degrees; for ortho
     the phase slips in between (after 6 orbits 41 against 80 degrees) and
     realigns by orbit 20.  |L| and e from the secular solve stay at their
     start values (dL/L 1e-7) as they do in the engine outside the deepest
     case.  The engine's own J drifts by 1e-6 per orbit at a_pair and 3e-5
     at 0.1 a_pair; the zero-moment control drifts identically, so this is
     not magnetic.

76c. WHAT IS MISSING: THE APSIDAL LINE.  Apsidal precession per orbit, in
     degrees, with the zero-moment control (1PN of the retarded Coulomb
     field) subtracted to give the magnetic part:

     a/a_pair   control 1PN   para 40    ortho 40   para 90   ortho 90
     1          +0.0106       -0.0017    +0.0144
     0.1        +0.1049       -0.1638    +0.5772    +0.2398   -0.2244

     The secular layer holds the apsidal line fixed: 0 for both.  The
     magnetic part has opposite signs for para and ortho, for ortho it is
     larger than 1PN already at a_pair, and it grows as a^-1.6 (ortho 40).
     Over one skip of 200000 orbits at a_pair the ortho line would turn
     through about 2900 degrees.  At 5.5 r* the in-plane angle is not
     usable (eccentricity 0.11-0.37 in the engine), so no rate is given.

76d. DOES THE FROZEN APSIS CHANGE ANYTHING?  Probes apsens.cpp and
     m1share.cpp rotate the apsidal direction through 36 values at fixed
     L and moments.
     - Precession rate: |w1| varies by at most 0.3% and its direction by at
       most 0.18 degrees (a=0.01 a_pair, e=0.9); below 0.01% elsewhere.  So
       76b's agreement does not depend on the missing apsidal precession.
     - M1 source <|mu''|^2>: depends strongly on the apsis (ortho 0.5-1.8
       times the mean), but ortho M1 power is 1e-48..1e-33 of E1, i.e. zero
       up to round-off, since the moments cancel.  For para the M1 share is
       2e-15 at a_pair, 2e-7 at 0.1 a_pair e=0.9, and 3.1-3.8% at
       0.01 a_pair e=0.9, where the frozen apsis spreads it 0.28-1.67 times
       the mean.  The effect on the photon hazard is thus below 2% of the
       loss and only for deep eccentric para.
     - a and e in the engine: with moments, da/a at 0.1 a_pair differs from
       the control by a bend that levels off (ortho 40: slope -1.6e-4 per
       orbit at the start, -0.3e-4 at the end), i.e. an exchange with the
       dipole energy as the moments turn, not a secular drift.  The control
       itself drifts by -6.7e-5 per orbit with reaction off; not magnetic.

76e. VERDICT.  The suspicion is not borne out for the spin<->orbit exchange
     itself: orbit->dipole precession and the dipole->orbit tilt of L are
     carried through the skipped orbits and match the full dynamics to
     about 1% down to 55 r*, a few percent at 5.5 r*, for para and ortho
     alike.  What the skipped orbits do omit is the apsidal precession,
     magnetic and 1PN, with a para/ortho-asymmetric magnetic part.  It does
     not feed back into the spin rates; it reaches the observable only
     through the M1 emission of deep eccentric para, at most about 2% of
     the loss.  Adding an apsidal rate to the secular state would be
     correct but is not expected to move the lifetimes.

76f. LIMITS.  20 orbits per case, one eccentricity (0.3) in the engine,
     radiation reaction off, so the radiative M1 torque on the moments was
     not measured; one start azimuth of the moments; the 5.5 r* comparison
     is a sampled phase at every second orbit, not a fitted precession
     frequency.

77. APSIDAL PRECESSION ADDED TO THE SECULAR LAYER

77a. WHAT WAS ADDED.  orbitAveragedApsidalRate (secular_spin_orbit.hpp):
     the secular Gauss equation for the Runge-Lenz vector, averaged in time
     over the Kepler ellipse, projected on the in-plane direction normal to
     e and divided by |e|.  advanceCoupledSecularSpinOrbit evaluates it once
     per substep at the midpoint state and rotates the apsidal line about
     the new normal.  e and the plane stay governed by |L| and J as before.
     CREM_NO_APSIDAL_PRECESSION restores the old behaviour.  Below e=1e-4
     the rate is set to zero.

77b. WHICH FORCES.  Two parts, both measured against the engine (76c).
     - Relativistic: darwinForces plus the 1PN kinetic term of
       d(gamma m v)/dt = F.  Darwin alone gives 0.00790 degrees per orbit at
       a_pair (3/4); with the kinetic term 0.01053 = pi k^2/(c^2 L^2), the
       engine's 0.0106.
     - Magnetic: retardedExternalForces with the moments minus without,
       one-state history.  The instantaneous pair (pairDipoleForce +
       chargeDipoleForces) was tried first and rejected.  Its dipole-dipole
       sector equals the retarded one, but its spin-orbit sector is exactly
       4/3 of it (0.01105 against 0.00829 per particle at a_pair), which
       puts ortho 30% high (0.0186 against the engine's 0.0144).  That
       mismatch between the two force models is itself open; it only
       matters where CREM_DIPOLE_INSTANTANEOUS_BELOW is set.
     - One-state history against causalInitialHistory: 6e-5 at a_pair,
       6e-4 at 0.1 a_pair, 0.5-1% at 0.01 a_pair for e=0.3, at 1/8 of
       the cost.  Quadrature: 16 to 128 nodes agree to 1e-6.

77c. AGAINST THE ENGINE, 20 ORBITS, e=0.3 (apsidal angle, degrees).

     case                 secular   engine   difference
     a_pair   para 40     +0.1776   +0.1789  -0.7%
     a_pair   ortho 40    +0.4978   +0.4991  -0.3%
     0.1      para 40     -1.1815   -1.1778  +0.3%
     0.1      ortho 40    +13.344   +13.643  -2.2%
     0.1      para 90     +6.454    +6.894   -6.4%
     0.1      ortho 90    -2.229    -2.389   -6.7%

     Before this change the secular column was 0 throughout.

77d. PRODUCTION.  16 trajectories per channel, seed 42, with the rate and
     with CREM_NO_APSIDAL_PRECESSION:

     channel  mean change   sd of change   moved   photons changed   wall
     para     -0.000005 ps  0.000130 ps    5/16    0                 +0.1%
     ortho    +0.000005 ps  0.000054 ps    2/16    0                 +0.5%

     The largest single change is 0.00038 ps.  As 76d predicted, the
     apsidal line reaches the lifetime only through the M1 share, and that
     is negligible along these trajectories.  Validation 60/60 with the new
     check secular-apsidal-rate (1PN reference to 1e-3, zero magnetic rate
     without moments, para/ortho mirror for moments normal to L, zero on a
     circle); header isolation 37/37.

77e. LIMITS.  The comparison in 77c uses the engine runs of 76b (20
     orbits, reaction off, one eccentricity); the 90-degree cases differ by
     6-7%, not resolved further.  First-order averaging loses its meaning
     where the precession approaches a revolution per orbit (0.01 a_pair
     at e=0.9); the rate is applied there unchanged.

78. HIGHER MULTIPOLES: WHAT THE FULL FLUX ADDS TO E1 + M1

78a. QUESTION.  An outside review says the model has E1/M1/E2 but no
     explicit, independent treatment of higher multipoles (E3, M2).  Code
     state: electromagneticFieldFluxRates integrates the far-zone Poynting
     flux of the full Lienard-Wiechert charge fields plus the exact boosted
     dipole fields.  It is not a multipole expansion, so E3, M2, toroidal
     terms and all classical interference are in it.  Production uses it
     for the one measured orbit per checkpoint (radiatedEnergyBookkeeping
     true, orbitalRadiatedEnergy).  The photon hazard for the skipped orbits
     uses only larmorOrbitAveragedPower (E1, non-relativistic, Coulomb
     acceleration of a Kepler ellipse) plus the coherent M1; the mechanical
     quantized path uses E1 + M1 + E2.  E2 is identically zero for
     positronium but absent from the secular path for asymmetric pairs.
     So the review is right for the hazard and wrong for the flux.

78b. MEASUREMENT.  Probe fluxcmp.cpp: engine with retarded forces,
     reaction disabled, flux bookkeeping on, 3 Kepler periods at 128 steps,
     first period discarded.  Along the same trajectory: Larmor with the
     Coulomb acceleration k/(mu r^2) (what the secular formula averages) and
     E1 with the engine's instantaneous forces allExternalForces (Coulomb,
     Darwin, dipole forces).  beta^2 is (v_rel/c)^2 at radius a.

     a/r*   e    moments      beta^2    flux/Larmor-1   flux/E1(forces)-1
     548    0    none         5.3e-5    -3.17e-5        -1.84e-5
     55     0    none         5.3e-4    -3.21e-4        -1.86e-4
     16     0    none         1.8e-3    -1.09e-3        -6.22e-4
     5.5    0    none         5.3e-3    -3.45e-3        -1.87e-3
     2.7    0    none         1.1e-2    -7.44e-3        -3.74e-3
     5.5    0.5  none         5.3e-3    +4.42e-4        +1.87e-3
     55     0    para along L 5.3e-4    -1.56e-3        +5.77e-4
     5.5    0    para along L 5.3e-3    -1.65e-1        +9.59e-4
     5.5    0    ortho, anti  5.3e-3    +4.60e-1        +1.41e-2

     M1 share of the flux: 0 without moments, 9e-10 for para at 5.5 r*
     (moments along L do not precess).  e=0.9 at 5.5 r* was discarded: the
     bound orbit grew to 4.8 a within two periods, so it is no test.

78c. WHAT IT SAYS.
     - Without moments the whole difference scales as -0.60 beta^2 (all
       five radii), with a beta^4 coefficient near -9 at 2.7 r*.  That is
       the relativistic correction to E1, one order below E3 and M2
       (beta^4).  E3/M2 cannot be separated at this resolution; their share
       is at most the beta^4 residual, 1e-3 at 2.7 r* and 2.8e-9 at a_pair.
     - Against E1 built from the engine's own forces the remainder halves
       (-0.35 beta^2): part of the Larmor gap is the Darwin force.
     - The dominant piece with moments is not a multipole.  The flux follows
       the real acceleration, which contains the dipole-dipole force.  With
       moments along L that force repels for para and attracts for ortho,
       and the E1 power moves with it: -1.2e-3 for para at 55 r*, -16% for
       para and +46% for ortho at 5.5 r* (the ortho orbit also shrinks to
       0.73 a in two periods).  Against E1 from the engine forces the flux
       agrees to 1e-3 (para) and 1.4% (ortho).
     - larmorOrbitAveragedPower sees none of this.  Its error for the photon
       hazard is thus about -0.6 beta^2 from relativity and about 2 F_dd/F_C
