       from the dipole force, the latter para/ortho asymmetric and growing as
       a^-2: 1e-5 at a_pair, 1e-3 at 55 r*, 10% near 5 r*.  The lifetime is
       set at large radii, where both are below 1e-4.

78d. CHANGE.  Only the comment in electrodynamics.hpp: its endpoint claim
     (production stops at the ground state, beta^4 = 2.8e-9) held only under
     --ground-state-floor; the default stops on the retardation limit near
     4.9 r*, beta^4 = 3.5e-5.  The measurement is recorded there too.  No
     physics was changed.

78e. LIMITS.  Two measured periods per case, orbits started from Kepler
     conditions that the full force law does not keep circular; moments only
     along L, so the tilt dependence of the dipole-force term was not
     measured; no asymmetric pair; the quantum amplitude interference the
     review also names is not addressed by any of this.

79. SECULAR E1 POWER WITH THE DIPOLE FORCE AND THE RELATIVISTIC CORRECTION

79a. WHAT CHANGED.  The photon hazard of the skipped orbits used
     larmorOrbitAveragedPower: Coulomb acceleration on a Kepler ellipse,
     non-relativistic.  It now uses secularElectricDipoleOrbitAveragedEmission
     (crem_collapse.hpp), the product of two orbit averages on the carried
     ellipse:
       forcePower  <|sum q a|^2>/(6 pi eps0 c^3), a = relativisticAcceleration
                   of retardedExternalForces on a synthetic history of the
                   ellipse, moments included;
       spectral    <far-zone flux>/<kinematic Larmor> of the ideal ellipse,
                   electromagneticFieldFluxRates on the same history without
                   moments (retardation across the pair, harmonics, E3/M2).
     Nodes 32/sqrt(1-e) up to 2048, 32 history samples over eight
     light-crossing times.  CREM_COULOMB_LARMOR restores the closed form,
     which is also the fallback.  CREM_E1_CORRECTION prints both factors.

79b. THE TWO FACTORS ON IDEAL ORBITS (units of beta^2 at radius a,
     no moments, a_pair; at 5.5 r* in brackets).

     e     force/Coulomb      spectral          product
     0     -0.500 (-0.543)    -0.100 (-0.098)   -0.600 (-0.641)
     0.3   -0.585 (-0.647)    -0.053 (-0.048)   -0.638 (-0.695)
     0.5   -0.777 (-0.892)    +0.055 (+0.065)   -0.722 (-0.827)
     0.9   -3.72  (-6.50)     +1.72  (+2.06)    -2.00  (-4.52)

     Moments along L at 5.5 r*, e=0: force/Coulomb -36 beta^2 (para, -19%)
     and +56 beta^2 (ortho, +30%).

79c. AGAINST THE ENGINE FLUX (probe fluxcmp.cpp, engine trajectories).
     Circular, no moments, 2 periods: flux/(force average x spectral) - 1 =
     +2.4e-7, +5.1e-7, -1.7e-5, -6.8e-5 at 548, 55, 5.5, 2.7 r*, where
     Coulomb Larmor was off by -3.2e-5 .. -7.4e-3.  With moments along L:
     +7.6e-4 para at 55 r* (was -1.6e-3), +2.1e-3 para and -1.6e-2 ortho at
     5.5 r* (were -16.5% and +46%).
     Eccentric, 8 periods at 5.5 r*: Larmor from the engine's stored
     accelerations agrees with the force average to 0.02 beta^2, and
     flux/that Larmor is -0.061 (e=0.3) and +0.056 (e=0.5) beta^2 against
     the ideal spectral factor's -0.048 and +0.065.  The old formula is
     +0.65 and +0.90 beta^2 above the engine there.

79d. CORRECTION TO 78b.  The e=0.5 row (+4.4e-4 against Coulomb Larmor)
     was an artefact of a 2-period window: the flux lags the acceleration
     by the source radius over c, and on a deformed eccentric orbit that
     does not cancel at the window edges.  Over 8 periods the same case
     reads +0.90 beta^2 above Coulomb Larmor, consistent with 79b.  The
     circular rows are unaffected (no power variation along the orbit).

79e. PRODUCTION.  16 trajectories per channel, seed 42, new power against
     CREM_COULOMB_LARMOR:

     channel  mean change   sd       changed   photons changed   wall
     para     +0.0074 ps    0.0039   16/16     0                 +0.2%
     ortho    +0.0045 ps    0.0090   13/16     0                 +0.8%

     Para lengthens in 15 of 16 (relative +3.7e-5): at its deep checkpoints
     the dipole force repels and lowers the E1 power, e.g. total/Coulomb
     1.0026 against 0.99998 at the start on trace 42/2 (that print was
     taken with the one-state force version).  The change is two to three
     orders below the 17 ps branch spacing and does not move any photon
     count.  Validation 60/60, header isolation 37/37.

79f. LIMITS.  Moments only along L in the engine comparison; the cross term
     between dipole-force share and spectral shape is neglected; the
     ortho residual of 1.6% at 5.5 r* comes from a 2-period window on an
     orbit that shrank to 0.73 a and was not repeated over 8 periods;
     external field and ZPF enter the force sum as in the engine's own E1
     power; M1 unchanged.

80. THE 4/3 SPIN-ORBIT FACTOR: MISSING THOMAS BACK-REACTION IN THE
    INSTANTANEOUS FORCE SUM

80a. THE FINDING OF 77b.  The instantaneous pair pairDipoleForce +
     chargeDipoleForces had the same dipole-dipole sector as the retarded
     assembly but a spin-orbit sector 4/3 as large (apsidal rate 0.01105
     against 0.00829 per particle at a_pair).

80b. WHICH MODEL IS RIGHT.  Probe soprobe.cpp, orbit averages over Kepler
     nodes (e=0.3; a_pair and 0.1 a_pair), moment on one particle only:
       - third law: the instantaneous sum cancels exactly, the retarded one
         to 1e-5..1e-4 (field momentum);
       - orbit-averaged work: zero in both;
       - angular momentum: torque on the orbit against -dS/dt from the
         orbit-averaged Thomas-BMT rates.  Retarded: residual 3.2e-4 and
         3.8e-4.  Instantaneous: residual 0.333, torque = 4/3 dS/dt.
     So the retarded assembly conserves J = L + S with BMT spins, and the
     instantaneous one does not.

80c. WHY 4/3.  BMT contains Thomas precession, omega_T = -(v x a)/(2c^2).
     The instantaneous force comes from L = q (v_charge - v_dipole).A_mu,
     which couples the moment only to its rest-frame magnetic field and has
     no counterpart of omega_T.  For spin p in the Coulomb field of o the
     orbital coupling weight is g_p/(2 m_p m_o) + (g_p-1)/(2 m_p^2) with
     Thomas and g_p/(2 m_p m_o) + g_p/(2 m_p^2) without it (Barker and
     O'Connell): 3/2 against 2 for equal masses, 1/2 against 1 for
     m_o >> m_p.  The predicted ratios are 4/3 and 2.

80d. TEST OF THE EXPLANATION.  Thomas Lagrangian
     L_T = (q_p/(2 m_p c^2)) v_p.(E_o(r_p) x S_p), Euler-Lagrange forces,
     added to the instantaneous sum (probe thomasforce.cpp):

     pair  spin on      J residual inst / +T / ret     apsidal deg/orbit inst / +T / ret
     e+e-  e (a_pair)   0.333 / 3.6e-6 / 3.8e-4        0.008468 / 0.006354 / 0.006351
     e+e-  e (0.1)      0.333 / 3.9e-5 / 3.2e-4        0.2678 / 0.2009 / 0.2008
     p+e-  e (a_pair)   0.997 / 3.6e-5 / 1.1e-3        0.01693 / 0.008479 / 0.008467
     p+e-  e (0.1)      0.997 / 3.6e-4 / 9.8e-4        0.5355 / 0.2682 / 0.2673
     p+e-  p (a_pair)   6.7e-5 / 3.1e-5 / 2.1e-4       -0.000026 in all three

     For the electron of p+e- the instantaneous torque was 2x, as 80c
     predicts; for the proton the Thomas term is negligible.  Node by node
     the forces still differ by about half their size (total time
     derivatives that average out), so the identity is secular, not
     pointwise.

80e. FIX.  thomasBackReactionForces (electrodynamics.hpp), analytic, same
     Plummer profile as the charge field, added inside chargeDipoleForces.
     L_T is linear in the velocities, so the energy function at fixed S is
     unchanged.  CREM_NO_THOMAS_BACKREACTION removes it.

80f. ENGINE WITH INSTANTANEOUS FORCES, 20 orbits, 0.1 a_pair, e=0.3,
     moments at 40 degrees, against the J-conserving secular transport
     (which now includes the apsidal rate of section 77):

     case    quantity       before fix   after fix   secular
     ortho   tilt of L      8.777        7.652       7.651
     ortho   apsidal angle  +16.09       +13.42      +13.43
     ortho   dJ/J           7.4e-3       3.1e-5      0
     para    tilt of L      4.265        4.265       4.280
     para    apsidal angle  -1.095       -1.096      -1.198
     para    dJ/J           3.5e-4       1.9e-5      0

     The retarded engine in the same test (section 76) had dJ/J 2.7e-4
     (ortho) and 5.8e-4 (para).  The para spin-orbit sector cancels between
     the two particles, so para is unchanged apart from its J drift; its
     9% apsidal gap to the secular value is not from this term and is not
     resolved.

80g. SCOPE.  Default production uses the retarded assembly, which already
     had the term: trace 42/2 gives 199.445464 ps (para) and 199.475437 ps
     (ortho), identical to before.  The fix changes CREM_DIPOLE_
     INSTANTANEOUS_BELOW, CREM_DARWIN_FORCES / useRetardedExternalForces
     off, the seed accelerations of causalInitialHistory (overwritten by its
     retarded Picard pass) and the Schott diagnostic.  Validation 60/60,
     header isolation 37/37.

80h. LIMITS.  The energy ledger term chargeDipoleInteractionEnergy was not
     re-examined against the corrected force; the tilted-pair energy errors
     measured with the instantaneous switch in section 66 were not rerun.

81. PLAN STEP 3 REVISITED: ONE PHOTON PER SPIN PRECESSION, PARA AND ORTHO,
    ON THE CURRENT CODE

81a. STATUS BEFORE THIS SECTION.  The rule exists since 14cc3e3 as
     CREM_PHOTON_FROM_SPIN (quantum hbar*max(|omega1|,|omega2|), defined in
     both channels), off by default.  Section 36 measured it once, from
     n=2, on one trajectory whose channel it did not name, and before the
     floor, apsidal, E1 and Thomas changes of sections 73-80.

81b. SECTION 36 REPRODUCED.  Same tool (para_ortho_branch_trace, seed 42,
     index 2, level 2):

     code              channel  rule     lifetime     photons
     14cc3e3 (old)     para     spin     2188.44 ps   122245
     current           para     spin     2187.80 ps   119790
     current           ortho    spin     2199.46 ps   148276
     current           para     default  7491.36 ps   5
     current           ortho    default  7491.73 ps   4

     The old commit reproduces section 36 to the photon, so that run was
     para.  Section 36's "default 199.4 ps, 3 photons" was a level-1
     number; from n=2 the default rule takes 7491 ps.  The code changes
     since then moved the spin-rule lifetime by 0.03%.

81c. PRODUCTION START (n=1), 16 trajectories per channel, seed 42:

     channel  rule     mean lifetime   sd        photons   stop a/r*
     para     default  199.45 ps (*)   0.005     3         4.9-5.3
     para     spin     36.435 ps       0.087     15827     12.0-15.6
     ortho    default  199.471 ps      0.012     3         4.9-5.6
     ortho    spin     36.880 ps       0.138     21669     12.9-16.3
     (*) index 1 excluded (216.75 ps branch)

     With the spin rule ortho lives 0.445 ps (1.2%) longer than para, ten
     standard errors, and emits 37% more photons of smaller energy (mean
     4.1e-4 against 5.4e-4 eV near 240 lambda_C).  The channels separate
     because the quantum now contains the moments; under the default rule
     they differ by 0.02 ps.

81d. WHAT THE LIFETIME IS.  Classical continuous circular inspiral
     (classicalInspiralCoefficient) from the same start: 31.12 ps from n=1,
     1991.9 ps from n=2 (the end point does not matter).  The spin rule
     gives 36.4-36.9 ps and 2188-2199 ps, i.e. the classical inspiral plus
     10-18%.  A quantum four to five orders below hbar*omega_orb makes the
     cascade effectively continuous; the default's 199 ps and 7491 ps are
     set by the waiting time for three to five large photons, not by the
     radiated power.

81e. THE PREDICTED RADIUS, AGAIN.  Photons per quarter decade of
     r/lambda_C, all 16 trajectories, n=1 start:

     r/lambda_C   para     ortho
     237          109405   144312
     133          82273    104579
     75           39650    57118
     42           14337    25627
     24           5378     11512
     13           1805     3068
     7.5          384      493

     From n=2 (one trajectory each) the same fall from 60447/73534 at
     750 lambda_C to 19/16 at 7.5 lambda_C.  With the retardation limit
     relaxed to 2 the para run reaches the Compton barrier (a = 1.22 r*,
     0.61 lambda_C): 15, 6 and 1 photons in the bins at 4.2, 2.4 and
     1.3 lambda_C, still falling through 0.726 lambda_C.  The ortho run with
     the relaxed limit ended in a numerical failure after reaching
     2.4 lambda_C (outside the declared domain, not pursued).  No peak in
     either channel; section 36's falsification stands, now for both.

81f. LIMITS.  One seed; spin moments quantized (the default); the relaxed
     runs are one trajectory each and outside the validated domain; the
     10-18% excess over the classical inspiral was not decomposed.

82. THE SPIN-ORBIT TERM OF THE ENERGY LEDGER AGAINST THE FORCES

82a. QUESTION (left open in 80h).  Does chargeDipoleInteractionEnergy, the
     ledger's spin-orbit term, match the forces after the Thomas
     back-reaction was added?  Until now the ledger was only checked by an
     engine residual, which mixes integrator error with ledger error.

82b. METHOD.  Exact check along the model's own flow, free of integration
     error (probes energyflow.cpp, energyscan*.cpp, ledgercand.cpp): at a
     state, dE/dt of the full ledger by central difference of the flow map
     x' = v, p' = F, mu_proper' = omega_BMT x mu_proper; spin sector = the
     same state with moments minus without.  F is either allExternalForces
     (instantaneous) or retardedExternalForces with a causal history.
     Kepler nodes, e=0.3, moments at 40 degrees.

82c. RESULT: THE TERM BELONGS TO THE RETARDED FORCES ONLY, AND HAD THE
     WRONG FORM FOR UNEQUAL MASSES.  Mean |dE/dt| of the spin sector at
     0.1 a_pair:

     pair, forces, channel         no term    old -mu.B(v_other)   -p.E
     e+e- retarded, ortho          3.4e-3 W   3.9e-6 W             3.9e-6 W
     e+e- retarded, para           3.0e-6 W   2.0e-6 W             2.0e-6 W
     p+e- retarded, para           2.7e-2 W   2.7e-2 W             9.8e-6 W
     p+e- retarded, ortho          2.7e-2 W   2.7e-2 W             2.4e-5 W
     e+e- instantaneous, ortho     9.3e-7 W   3.4e-3 W             3.4e-3 W
     p+e- instantaneous, para      1.4e-8 W   2.7e-5 W             2.7e-2 W

     - The retarded assembly couples a moment through U = mu.B + p.E, with
       p the motional electric dipole at the particle's OWN velocity; its
       ledger needs -p.E(partner's Coulomb field).  The old form, each moment
       in the Biot-Savart field of the PARTNER's motion, coincides with it
       for e+e- in the centre-of-mass frame and fails otherwise: for p+e-
       it missed the electron's own motional dipole (moment on the electron
       alone: 2.7e-2 W no matter the weight; with -p.E 9.8e-6 W; moment on
       the proton alone: 5.5e-10 W).  With the centre of mass moving at
       0.3 v_orb (e+e-, para) the old term doubles the error (3.5e-3 W
       against 1.75e-3 without) and -p.E removes it (1.4e-6 W).
     - The instantaneous sum derives from Lagrangians linear in the
       velocities (q (v_q - v_mu).A and the Thomas term), which contribute
       nothing to the energy function: it needs NO term.  This holds with
       and without the Thomas back-reaction (CREM_NO_THOMAS_BACKREACTION).
     - Remaining residuals scale as beta^2 of the spin-orbit rate
       (1e-5 at a_pair, 3e-4 at 0.1 a_pair relative to |dU/dt|).
     Engine runs, reaction off, spin-sector energy range in units of the
     binding energy: e+e- ortho 0.1 a_pair retarded 3.8e-6 with the term
     against 1.5e-3 without; instantaneous 1.5e-3 with against 3.3e-6
     without (0.01 a_pair: 4.6e-2 against 3.4e-4); p+e- para retarded with
     -p.E 3.6e-6 against 3.0e-3 without, instantaneous without 8.2e-7.

82d. CHANGES.
     - chargeDipoleInteractionEnergy is now -(p1.E2(r1) + p2.E1(r2)), p from
       the moment boosted at the particle's own velocity (rebuilt locally,
       so unsynchronized states read correctly), Plummer Coulomb field.
     - conservativeParticleEnergy(state, chargeDipoleWeight=1): the weight is
       retardedDipoleSectorWeight(state) in integrateElectrodynamicStep for
       retarded forces (1, or the CREM_DIPOLE_INSTANTANEOUS_BELOW smoothstep,
       now one shared function with the force blend) and 0 for
       allExternalForces; the validation balance runs pass 0 or 1 by force
       model.
     - dipoleAwarePeriapsis synchronizes its turning-point state, since the
       term now needs p.
     - The validation's Yee-coupled probe state is synchronized after the
       deposits (see 82e).

82e. AN OLD MEASUREMENT WAS A SETUP ARTEFACT.  8c85397 supported the old
     term by "the balance residual halves" (reaction off 3.97e-6 to 1.64e-6).
     That probe set lab moments without synchronizing them, so every ledger
     term read stale moments at the start and boosted ones after the first
     step.  Replicated (benchledger.cpp): from that start dE_mech/|E| is
     -8.80e-6 with no term, -8.81e-6 with -p.E and -6.16e-6 with the old
     term, whose stale value happened to cancel part of the jump; from a
     synchronized start all three give 1e-8 over the same four steps.  With
     the probe synchronized: reaction raw off/LL/C 8.5e-7 / 4.2e-7 / 4.2e-7
     (was 5.3e-6 / 5.8e-6 / 5.8e-6), dE_mech 1.4e-8 (the raw value is now the
     radiated flux, as it must be with reaction off), shared-engine dJ
     1.6e-6 (was 2.7e-4).  Shared-engine dE moved 1.8e-6 -> 4.4e-6; that
     state has passed through the Yee push, where the two forms differ at
     the 1e-6 floor, and the flow checks above are the discriminating test.

82f. SCOPE.  Production unchanged to all printed digits: trace 42/2 gives
     199.445464378 ps (para) and 199.475436586 ps (ortho).  Validation 60/60,
     header isolation 37/37.  What changes: the ledger in instantaneous and
     blended runs, any pair other than e+e- in the centre-of-mass frame, and
     the energy diagnostics built on conservativeParticleEnergy.

82g. LIMITS.  One eccentricity and tilt in the flow checks; the retarded
     flow uses a causal history built at each state, not an engine history;
     crem_trajectory's frame and Schott diagnostics still call the ledger
     with weight 1 whatever the force model; the 1e-6-level differences of
     the two forms on symmetric states were not resolved.

83. ATTRACTION OF THE MOMENTS IN p-Ps, REPULSION IN o-Ps: WHAT THE MODEL
    HAS, AND A SIGN REGRESSION IN ITS CONTACT TERM

83a. QUESTION.  Is an effect missing that attracts the magnetic moments in
     para and repels them in ortho -- perhaps orbit-orbit?

83b. ORBIT-ORBIT.  The Breit orbit-orbit (current-current) interaction is
     the Darwin term, present as darwinForces/darwinInteractionEnergy.  It
     does not depend on spin and cannot separate para from ortho.

83c. THE TENSOR TERM IS PRESENT; ITS SIGN FOLLOWS THE TILT.  Azimuth average
     in the orbit plane, parallel moments (para), units mu0 mu^2/(4 pi r^3),
     positive = repulsive (probe ddsign.cpp):

     r/r*    tilt 0    30       54.7     60       90
     10      +1.000    +0.625   0.000    -0.125   -0.500
     1       +0.996    +0.624   +0.002   -0.122   -0.494
     0.2     +0.913    +0.591   +0.054   -0.054   -0.376

     Ortho has the opposite sign.  Moments along the orbit normal: para
     repels, ortho attracts; beyond the magic angle the reverse.  Averaged
     over orientations it vanishes (N6, dipole-tensor-s-state-average), and
     production samples the tilt isotropically, so the ensemble carries no
     net para attraction from it.

83d. THE EFFECT ASKED ABOUT IS THE FERMI CONTACT TERM, AND ITS SIGN IS NOW
     WRONG.  In QED the attraction of p-Ps and repulsion of o-Ps is the
     contact part of the spin-spin coupling, -(2 mu0/3) mu1.mu2 delta(r)
     (4/7 of the 204.4 GHz splitting; the other 3/7, virtual annihilation,
     acts in ortho only and has no classical counterpart here).  A current
     loop's field carries it: integral B dV = +(2 mu0/3) m.  cb607c9
     (2026-08-29) measured exactly that for the then-regularized field
     (7.77694e-30 against 7.77839e-30).
     The Plummer unification 00f0071 (2026-09-14, section 66) replaced the
     field by (mu0/4 pi)[3 r(r.m) - m rho^2]/rho^5, which is the softened
     field of a MAGNETIC-CHARGE dipole: integral B dV = -0.336 mu0 m
     (expected -1/3), i.e. the contact term with the opposite sign and half
     the strength.  Since then contact makes para repel and ortho attract,
     the reverse of QED.  Isotropic average of U at fixed r, para, in the
     units above: +2.58e-3 at 1 r*, +5.38e-2 at 0.2 r*, +1.77e-1 at
     0.05 r* (repulsive).  It is also inconsistent inside the model: the
     charge-dipole force uses the loop vector potential m x r/rho^3 but
     this field for B.

83e. THE CORRECT PLUMMER FIELD (probe loopfield.cpp).  The curl of the
     same vector potential,
         B = (mu0/4 pi) [3 r(r.m) - m (r^2 - 2 eps^2)] / rho^5
           = current form + (mu0/4 pi) 3 eps^2 m / rho^5,
     checked: B = curl A to 3.4e-10, div B = 0, integral B dV = +0.653 mu0
     (+2/3 up to the finite sphere), far field equal to the current form
     within 3 eps^2/r^2 (4.6e-3 at 1 r*).  Isotropic average for para turns
     attractive: -4.87e-3 at 1 r*, -1.07e-1 at 0.2 r*, -3.54e-1 at 0.05 r*.
     Energy U = -mu2.B1 = current U - (mu0/4 pi) 3 eps^2 (mu1.mu2)/rho^5.

83f. RELEVANCE TO PRODUCTION: NONE MEASURABLE.  Trace 42/2 (CREM_SKIP
     census, 63 checkpoints each): the smallest checkpoint periapsis is
     39.7 r* (para) and 40.8 r* (ortho); only the final photon cascade takes
     the circular orbit to ~4.9 r*.  The contact correction relative to the
     tensor term, 3 (eps/r_p)^2, is 5e-6 at 40 r* and 3e-4 at 5 r*.  Even
     with the right sign, the attraction the question has in mind is an
     S-state property (|psi(0)|^2), and the model's L = hbar orbits never
     sample r = 0 (cb607c9).

83g. WHAT A FIX WOULD TOUCH (not done here).  pairDipoleField,
     pairDipoleInteractionEnergy and pairDipoleForce (Plummer branch), the
     contact correction in the retarded two-charge dipole construction
     (retardedMagneticDipoleField and the far-zone field), the Plummer
     branch of azimuthAveragedDipoleEnergy, and the validation references
     moved in section 66.  Expected effect: nil in production (83f); real
     for deep or radial orbits, floor-scale experiments and the sign of the
     para/ortho contact physics the model claims to represent.

84. THE CURRENT-LOOP PLUMMER DIPOLE: CONTACT TERM RESTORED WITH ITS SIGN

84a. CHANGE.  The magnetic pair field is now the curl of the model's own
     vector potential (mu0/4 pi) m x r / rho^3,

         B = (mu0/4 pi) [3 r(r.m) - m (r^2 - 2 eps^2)] / rho^5
           = poles' Plummer field + mu0 m n(r),   n = 3 eps^2/(4 pi rho^5),

     instead of the separated-poles field adopted in 00f0071 (section 83).
     - plummerMagnetizationField / plummerMagneticDipoleField: the local
       magnetization term and the loop field; plummerDipoleField stays the
       field of an electric (pole) dipole.
     - pairDipoleField (and so pairDipoleInteractionEnergy and the magnetic
       field in chargeDipolePairForces, now consistent with its vector
       potential) uses the loop field; pairDipoleForce gains the gradient of
       the contact energy -(mu0/4 pi) 3 eps^2 (mu_t.mu_s)/rho^5.
     - retardedMagneticDipoleFieldExact: the static branch uses the loop
       field; the moving two-pole construction adds movingMagnetizationField,
       the rest-frame mu0 m n(r') Lorentz-transformed to the lab (local, no
       retardation).  The far-zone field is unchanged.
     - azimuthAveragedDipoleEnergy (secular layer) gains the contact energy.
     - New validation check dipole-contact-term (Lebedev x Gauss radial
       integral of the pair field must be +2/3 mu0 m within 1e-3; the field
       must equal the curl of the vector potential within 1e-6).

84b. CHECKS (probes loopcheck.cpp, movingloop.cpp).
       integral of pairDipoleField over a sphere of 400 eps   +0.6533 mu0
         (the analytic value for that sphere is 2/3 (1 - 1.5 eps^2/R^2);
         the residual is the coarse angular grid of the probe; the
         validation check with Lebedev shells passes at 1e-3)
       |F + grad U| / |F|, 0.02..5 r*                        2.8e-10
       secular azimuth average against numeric average       4.5e-14
       static retarded field against pairDipoleField          0 (exact)
       moving dipole, uniform history, beta 0/0.05/0.3, at 0.03, 0.1 and
       1 r*: retarded field against the boosted rest-frame loop field
         |dB|/|B| <= 5.2e-10, |dE|/|E| <= 5.5e-10
         (against the boosted poles' field: 2.7 at 0.03 r*, 0.58 at 0.1 r*,
         5.9e-3 at 1 r*, i.e. exactly the contact term)
     Validation 61/61, header isolation 37/37.

84c. SIGN, WHERE IT CAN BE SEEN.  Isotropic average of the pair energy for
     parallel moments (para), units mu0 mu^2/(4 pi r^3): now -4.87e-3 at
     1 r*, -1.07e-1 at 0.2 r*, -3.54e-1 at 0.05 r* (attraction; was
     repulsion of +2.58e-3, +5.38e-2, +1.77e-1); ortho the reverse.
     Engine, deep tilted pairs (deeptilt.cpp, retarded forces, reaction
     off, 6 Kepler periods): the configurations whose tensor term repels
     (para 0 deg, ortho 90 deg) stay above 1 r* and change by <=0.01 r*;
     those it attracts (para 90, ortho 0) fail on the known deep-orbit
     accuracy limit before reaching the contact scale, but their deepest
     point moves the predicted way -- para 0.4059 -> 0.3978 r* (from
     peri 0.1, apo 1) and 0.7572 -> 0.7515 r* (from peri 1, apo 3), ortho
     0.3908 -> 0.4072 r* and 0.6810 -> 0.6905 r*.  The contact scale itself
     (~0.05 r*) was reached by none of them.

84d. PRODUCTION.  Trace 42/2: para 199.445464378 -> 199.446240512 ps
     (+7.8e-4 ps, 870093 against 869969 revolutions), ortho 199.475436586
     -> 199.475436591 ps.  As estimated in 83f the term is 3e-4 of the dipole
     coupling at the final ~4.9 r* orbit; para moves more because its
     terminal checkpoint sits a hair nearer a skip boundary.

84e. LIMITS.  The engine never resolved an orbit inside the contact scale,
     so the dynamical consequence there is untested; the moving magnetization
     term uses the source kinematics at the observation time (exact for
     uniform motion, checked above) and the present proper moment; the
     classical L = hbar orbits still do not sample r = 0, so the S-state
     hyperfine attraction of p-Ps remains outside the model (section 48).

85. CONTACT HYPERFINE ENERGY OF A MICROCANONICAL ORBIT ENSEMBLE (STEP 1)

85a. IDEA.  The contact term (section 84) needs a state with density at
     r = 0.  A single radial orbit was rejected (cb607c9): its density
     diverges as r^-3/2 and overshoots the splitting by ~190x.  A classical
     ensemble at the 1S energy with ALL angular momenta (microcanonical,
     isotropic in phase space) weights small L by L dL, which softens the
     divergence to r^-1/2 and the regulator dependence to eps^-1/2.

85b. METHOD (probe contactensemble.cpp).  Only the dipole-dipole energy
     depends on position alone; velocity-linear terms average to zero in an
     isotropic ensemble.  Exact integral, no sampling: radial density
     rho(r) = sqrt(1/r - 1/2a) / [(pi^2/4)(2a)^(5/2)] on r < 2a (normalized to
     1.00000), log radial grid, 194-point sphere rule, with the model's own
     pairDipoleInteractionEnergy (current-loop Plummer field), moments
     parallel (para) or antiparallel (ortho).  The smearing scale is the
     separation floor, set by CREM_SEPARATION_FLOOR_SCALE.  Control: the same
     integral over the quantum 1S density |psi|^2.  Reference: the QED
     contact part of the splitting, 4/7 of (7/12) alpha^4 m c^2
     = 4.8301e-4 eV; its classical-spin analogue at |psi(0)|^2,
     2 (2 mu0/3) mu^2 |psi(0)|^2 = 2.4207e-4 eV (classical moments give
     +-1 where sigma1.sigma2 gives +1 and -3: a factor 2).

85c. RESULT.

     eps        <U>_para      <U>_ortho     dE           <n>/|psi0|^2   dE/QED contact
     0.05 r*    -2.6425e-3    +2.6425e-3    5.2849e-3    21.83          10.94
     r*         -5.9052e-4    +5.9052e-4    1.1810e-3     4.879          2.445
     2 r*       -4.1730e-4    +4.1730e-4    8.3460e-4     3.448          1.728
     (energies in eV)

     Quantum 1S control with the same field: <n>/|psi0|^2 = 0.9993, 0.9926,
     0.9857 and dE = 2.419e-4, 2.403e-4, 2.386e-4 eV, i.e. the classical-spin
     analogue to 0.1-1.4%: the integral and the field are right.
     - Para is bound deeper and ortho less, as in QED.
     - Scaling is eps^-1/2 to four digits: 21.83/4.879 = 4.475 against
       sqrt(20) = 4.472; 4.879/3.448 = 1.415 against sqrt(2) = 1.414.
     - The a-priori estimate (4.88, 3.45, 21.8) is confirmed.
     - Against the rejected single radial orbit (~190x) the ensemble lands
       within a factor 2.4 at the moment's own length r* = mu/(e c), and 1.7
       at the loop radius 2 r*.  Matching the QED contact term exactly would
       need eps = 5.98 r*; matching the classical-spin analogue, 23.8 r*.
     - For contrast, the production state (circular L = hbar orbit at
       a_pair) sees the contact term only through its tail,
       2 (mu0/4 pi) 3 eps^2 mu^2 / a^5 ~ 1e-9 eV at eps = r*.

85d. WHAT IT DOES NOT SHOW.  The microcanonical L distribution is assumed,
     not produced by the dynamics (that is step 3, the ZPF); the density
     still diverges as r^-1/2, so the answer depends on eps, only weakly;
     the choice of eps as a physical size of the moment (step 2) is not yet
     argued beyond N1; virtual annihilation (3/7 of the splitting) stays
     outside the model.

86. STEP 2: THE MOMENT'S FIELD IS SOFTENED AT ITS OWN LENGTH, NOT AT THE
    NUMERICAL FLOOR

86a. THE SCALE, FROM THE MOMENT ALONE.  The smallest current loop that
     carries mu with charge |q| at speeds not above c has radius
     R = 2 mu/(|q| c), which for e+e- is exactly 2 r* (N1: mu/(e c) = r*).
     Its magnetization is a uniform disk of that radius.  The model smears
     moments with a Plummer profile, so the loop is represented by the
     Plummer length carrying the same contact weight: against the r^-1/2
     density of section 85, a disk gives <r^-1/2> = (4/3) R^-1/2 and Plummer
     1.5 B(5/4,5/4) eps^-1/2 = 0.927037 eps^-1/2, hence

         eps = [(3/4) 0.927037]^2 R = 0.48341 R = 0.96682 r*.

     A luminal rotating shell (R = 3 r*, uniformly magnetized sphere,
     <r^-1/2> = 1.2 R^-1/2) would give 1.790 r* instead: the geometry is a
     modelling choice worth one factor 1.85 in eps.  Matching the QED contact
     term exactly would need 5.98 r*, which is a fit, not a derivation.

86b. CHANGE.  magneticDipoleRadius() (electrodynamics.hpp) returns that
     length and replaces separationFloor() throughout the MAGNETIC moment
     sector: pairDipoleField/Energy/Force, the vector potential in
     chargeDipolePairForces, the static and moving retarded dipole field
     (the two-pole construction now takes its softening as an argument, so
     the electric dipole keeps the floor), the contact term, and
     azimuthAveragedDipoleEnergy.  Charge fields, the -p.E ledger term and
     the Coulomb/Darwin sector keep the numerical floor.  One length for the
     whole moment field: a magnetization term on a different length than the
     rest would give div B != 0.  CREM_MAGNETIC_RADIUS_SCALE (units of r*)
     overrides it; 0.05 reproduces sections 66-85 bit for bit (trace 42/2:
     199.446240512 ps para, 199.475436591 ps ortho, both exact).

86c. THE STEP-1 NUMBER IS NOW FLOOR-INDEPENDENT.

     setting                       <n>/|psi0|^2   dE           dE/QED contact
     default (0.96682 r*)          4.962          1.2012e-3 eV  2.487
     default, floor 0.02 r*        4.962          1.2012e-3 eV  2.487
     CREM_MAGNETIC_RADIUS_SCALE=1  4.879          1.1810e-3 eV  2.445
     CREM_MAGNETIC_RADIUS_SCALE=0.05 21.832       5.2849e-3 eV 10.942

     The a-priori prediction for the luminal loop was 2.487.  The quantum 1S
     control through the same field stays at 0.9929 |psi0|^2.

86d. VALIDATION.  61/61, isolation 37/37, after one reference moved:
     dipole-boosted-static-field compares the field of a beta = 0.9995/0.9999
     moment at 2 a_0 against the point boosted field, and the physical
     softening shifts that by 4.5 eps^2/R^2 = 1.5e-5, above the check's 1e-5
     bound.  Measured 1.41e-5, i.e. the predicted softening; the reference is
     now the boosted Plummer loop and the residual is numerical again.

86e. PRODUCTION UNCHANGED.  16 trajectories per channel, seed 42, new radius
     against CREM_MAGNETIC_RADIUS_SCALE=0.05: para mean -0.000061 ps
     (sd 0.000318), ortho +0.000018 ps (sd 0.000345), no photon count changed
     in any of the 32, wall clock equal.  Mean terminal periapsis moves from
     4.651 to 4.497 r* (para) and 4.929 to 4.991 r* (ortho) through
     dipoleAwarePeriapsis, which now sees the wider moment.

86f. THE LEDGER NEAR r* IMPROVES.  Flow check of section 82 at 0.5 r*,
     spin-sector |dE/dt| relative to |U_dd| n: para 0 deg 45 -> 1.3e-3,
     ortho 0 deg 3.3e-3 -> 6.6e-5, para 90 deg 0.27 -> 0.080.  The 1-14%
     residuals of the tilt-90 cases are present with both radii and are not
     from this change.

86g. DEEP ENGINE RUNS, AND WHAT THEY DO NOT SHOW.  Tilted pairs started
     between 1 and 3 r* (deeptilt.cpp, retarded forces, reaction off, six
     periods).  With the wider moment ortho at 0 degrees, which used to fail
     at 0.69 r*, now holds the whole window at r_min 1.0936 r*, and this is
     converged: 1e-9 gives 1.0942 r* with the same energy error
     (2.07e-2 against 2.09e-2 k/r*).  Para at 0 degrees appeared to fall to
     0.468 r* -- but it is NOT converged, and the trend says so: 1e-8
     completes the window with |dE| = 1.71 k/r*, 1e-9 fails at 0.593 r*
     with 6.65e-2, 1e-10 fails at 0.827 r* with 7.56e-3.  That is the known
     deep-orbit accuracy defect, not new physics, so no para/ortho ordering
     is claimed from these runs; the ordering rests on 86c and 86f.
     (A 1e-10 ortho run was still going when this was written.)

86h. LIMITS.  The geometry behind eps is a choice (loop 0.967 r*, shell
     1.790 r*); the disk-to-Plummer equivalence is matched for the r^-1/2
     ensemble weight of section 85 and would differ for another ensemble;
     the engine cannot resolve orbits inside ~0.8 r* at tolerances that
     converge, so the wider moment's dynamics there is untested; step 3
     (a ZPF-generated L distribution) is not done.

86i. THE 1e-10 ORTHO RUN PROMISED IN 86g.  Finished: the whole window,
     r_min 1.0941 r*, |dE|max 2.07e-02 k/r*.  With 1.0936 r* at 1e-8 and
     1.0942 r* at 1e-9 the three tolerances agree to four digits, so the
     ortho hold at the wider moment is converged; para at 0 degrees remains
     the unconverged case of 86g.

87. STEP 3: THE ZPF DOES NOT MAKE LOW-L ORBITS, AND THE INSPIRAL STAYS
    CIRCULAR -- THE CONTACT TERM IS SAMPLED ONLY BY SHRINKING

87a. QUESTION.  Step 1 assumed a microcanonical L distribution to give the
     contact term something to sample.  Does the model's own ZPF produce
     one?  Probe zpfcontact4.cpp: engine with the Landau-Lifshitz reaction,
     instantaneous forces (consistent since sections 80 and 82), band
     [0.3,3] x omega_orb, 64 modes, scale 1, moments para or ortho, starting
     circular.  Accumulated in time: <n> = <3 eps^2/(4 pi rho^5)> in units of
     |psi(0,a_pair)|^2, <L>, L_min, the time fraction below fixed L, and the
     radius range.  Reference (contactensemble_f.cpp, same field):
     microcanonical <n>/|psi(0,a_pair)|^2 = 4.96 at a_pair, 1.56e3 at
     0.1 a_pair, 3.12e4 at 0.03 a_pair.

87b. THE FIELD MOVES THE RADIUS, NOT THE ANGULAR MOMENTUM.  L_min against
     the circular value at the same radius, L_c(r) = sqrt(r/547.5) hbar:

     run (start, channel, seed)      orbits  <n>/|psi0|^2  L_min/L_c
     0.1 a_pair para seed 1          1100    1.9e-03       1.110
     0.1 a_pair para seed 2           600    2.6e-01       1.126
     0.1 a_pair ortho seed 1          900    1.5e-02       1.095
     0.1 a_pair ortho seed 2         1000    2.7e-01       1.131
     0.03 a_pair para seed 1         4000    2.9e-01       1.038
     0.03 a_pair para seed 3          297    3.4e+01       1.025

     Every ratio is above 1: the orbit is never less circular than circular.
     At 0.1 a_pair the time fraction below L = 0.2 hbar is exactly 0.000 in
     all four runs.  What the field does do is pump the radius: seed 1 at
     0.1 a_pair reached 869 r* and seed 1 at 0.03 a_pair reached 24296 r*
     (44 a_pair), which is why their <n> is the smallest of the set.  Against
     the microcanonical value at the same energy the ZPF runs fall short by
     three to six orders (2.9e-1 against 3.12e4 at 0.03 a_pair).

87c. THE INSPIRAL ITSELF STAYS CIRCULAR, AND IS CONVERGED.  The control (no
     field, pure radiative inspiral at 0.03 a_pair) looked at first like the
     opposite result: L_min = 1e-4 hbar, r down to 0.22 r*, <n> = 2.8e4.
     That came from a tolerance-1e-6 run pushed to orbit 1591, where it ended
     in numerical failure.  Compared at equal orbit numbers the picture is
     stable and the plunge disappears:

     orbit   tol 1e-6              tol 1e-7              tol 1e-8 (24 sub)
     200     <n> 1.237e2, 0.997    1.241e2, 0.996        1.243e2, 1.000
     400     1.803e2, 0.995        1.818e2, 0.993        1.826e2, 0.996
     600     2.890e2, 0.988        2.933e2, 0.990        2.954e2, 0.988
     (second number: L_min/L_c at the deepest radius reached)

     The three tolerances agree on <n> to 2% and on L_min/L_c to 0.5%, and
     the orbit remains circular to 1%.  So dipole radiation reaction shrinks
     a circular orbit without making it radial -- as the textbook result for
     E1 reaction says -- and the contact term is sampled only because r
     itself approaches eps, not because L gets small.

87d. WHAT STEP 3 COMES TO.  The ZPF does not generate the L distribution
     step 1 assumed; it does not generate low L at all.  The classical
     model therefore has no mechanism of its own that populates the
     contact region the way |psi(0)|^2 does: the only route it has is to
     shrink the orbit to r ~ eps, which is the regime where the model
     declares itself invalid (inside r*) and where the integrator fails.
     Steps 1 and 2 stand as they are: the contact energy of a
     microcanonical ensemble at the 1S energy is 2.487x the QED contact
     term at the moment's own softening length, but the ensemble remains an
     assumption about the state, not a result of the dynamics.

87e. LIMITS.  One band ([0.3,3] omega_orb) and 64 modes throughout; scale 1
     only; two seeds for para and two for ortho at 0.1 a_pair, one each at
     0.03 a_pair; runs reached 300-4000 orbits, several stopped by the wall
     clock and two by numerical failure at r < 10 r*; instantaneous forces
     (the retarded runs at 0.3 a_pair, 200 orbits, agree: +8% in <n> over
     the field-free control and L within 4% of circular); no run was
     followed to the end of its inspiral at a converged tolerance.

88. THE ACCURACY DEFECT AT THE BOTTOM OF THE ORBIT: IT WAS THE MAGNETIZATION
    TERM OF SECTION 84, AND IT IS FIXED

88a. STARTING POINT.  Sections 84e, 86h and 87d all ended on the same
     sentence: the engine cannot resolve an orbit inside ~0.8 r* at a
     tolerance that converges.  Section 55 had diagnosed a dt^1 error from
     the C1 history; sections 55a-55b fixed two channels of it and 55f left
     the failure open.

88b. WHAT THE CURRENT CODE ACTUALLY DOES.  Step-doubling error against dt at
     three real failing states (orderfail.cpp; reaction off, retarded
     forces), by force model:

     state (r*, beta)     full retarded   no moments   instantaneous   no dipole force
     0.539, 0.377         2.0             2.4-3.0      2.0             3.0
     0.478, 0.410         2.1             2.7-3.0      2.0             3.1
     0.318, 0.456         ~2              2.6-3.0      2.0             2.9-3.1
     (convergence order; the "no dipole force" column is the retarded
     charge sector alone)

     So the order was not 1 any more, but with moments the ABSOLUTE error
     was 40-70x larger at the same step, and the engine's own live
     diagnostic showed the retarded sector falling 1.86-2.33x per halving
     for five levels, then jumping 48x and only then settling at 4.00x.

88c. THE KINK, LOCATED.  Along the engine's own trajectory every force term
     was smooth (second differences falling as dt^2), so the defect was not
     a node crossing.  Probing the coupling U = mu.B + p.E along the tangent
     line instead showed a LEFT/RIGHT slope ratio that never converged with
     the step: 1.2005 at 0.478 r* and 5.7786 at 0.539 r*, stable over six
     decades of probe step.  Term by term the charge field's one-sided
     slopes were equal to four digits and the dipole field's differed by
     2.1x and 4.6x.  Excluded in turn, each by measurement: the retarded-time
     threshold (tightening 1e-14 -> 1e-16 and 5 -> 12 iterations reproduced
     every digit), history node density, the segment crossing (all probes in
     segment 130), the pole separation (1e-4 to 1e-8 identical), the
     pole-cancellation retreat (never fired, ratio 3.17 against a limit of
     30), and the moment-derivative branch (all probes central, forcing it
     changed nothing).  What remained: the magnetization term added in
     section 84.  Measured alone it had a one-sided ratio of 1.65; the
     two-pole construction without it gave exactly 1.0000.

88d. THE CAUSE.  movingMagnetizationField was called with the source
     kinematics at the OBSERVATION time and the proper moment from the
     PRESENT state, while the two-pole construction beside it reads both at
     the RETARDED time.  Displacing the observation event therefore moved
     the two contributions by different rules, which is a kink in time, not
     in space -- invisible to every spatial probe and to the trajectory-wise
     smoothness test.

88e. THE FIX.  retardedMagneticDipoleFieldExact now solves the retarded time
     for the magnetization term as well (the same Newton iteration as the
     central solve) and reads the moment from the history at that time.
     After it:
       one-sided slope ratio   0.9999 and 1.0000 (was 2.12 and 5.78)
       step doubling           4.0x per halving down to 1.6e-10 at both
                               failing states (was a floor at 1.08e-8 and
                               4.28e-9)
     Validation 61/61, header isolation 37/37.  Production trace 42/2:
     para 199.446163 ps against 199.446151, ortho 199.475437 against
     199.475437, i.e. 1.3e-5 ps.

88f. WHAT DEEP ORBITS DO NOW.  With the plateau gone the step budget buys
     depth again, and the deep failures are cost, not convergence:

     outer step / depth    deepest radius reached (apo 3 r*, peri 0.3 r*)
     P/256, depth 14       0.448 r* (refusal)
     P/2048, depth 18      0.265 r* (refusal)
     P/8192, depth 20      0.2631 r*, THREE FULL ORBITS, no refusal
     P/2048, depth 18, tol 1e-6   0.2631 r*, three full orbits

     The last two agree to four digits at different tolerances, so 0.2631 r*
     is the orbit's true periapsis, not a numerical stopping point.  The
     tilted case (apo 3, peri 1, 90 deg) reaches 0.1695 r* at P/2048.

88g. NOT CHANGED.  The production defaults (depth 12, tolerance 1e-5) are
     untouched: production never goes below 3.9 r*, where the required step
     is far from binding, and tightening them would cost time for nothing.
     Deep work now has a recipe instead of a wall: P/8192 with depth 20.

88h. LIMITS.  Three failing geometries, all e+e- with the default floor and
     moment radius; the deep runs are three orbits, not a full inspiral; the
     1-14% tilt-90 residual of the energy ledger (section 86f) is a separate
     open item and is not touched by this fix.

89. TIME REGULARIZATION AT THE BOTTOM OF THE ORBIT: ONE SHARED RULE, AND
    WHAT IT ACTUALLY BUYS

     Asked to "fix the time regularization at the bottom of the orbit".  The
     rule turned out to be present already, four times over, and the premise
     of the request turned out to be wrong: regularization does not improve
     accuracy at the bottom of the orbit.  Both findings are below, with the
     measurements that forced them.

89a. WHAT WAS ALREADY THERE.  The production loop had set its step from the
     current separation since long before this section, as an inline
     expression:

         dt = min(5e-18 s, 2 pi / (128 omega_local), remaining)

     with omega_local = sqrt(k/(mu r^3)), so dt ~ r^{3/2}: 128 steps per
     LOCAL orbital period.  That is a Sundman-type regularization.  The same
     rule appeared inline in three more places, with different step counts:
     the cutoff-arrival probe of maxwell_validation.hpp (256 per local
     orbit), and the Experiment 3/4 beam loop and the Experiment 5
     interaction sampler in positronium.cpp (320 per local orbit, under a
     1 fs ceiling and beside a transit clip of their own).  Four copies of
     one law, none of them named.

89b. WHAT WAS DONE.  regularizedTimeStep() in crem_engine.hpp now holds the
     rule, and all four sites call it.  It offers two laws:

       localOrbit     dt = 2 pi / (N omega_local) ~ r^{3/2}, N steps per
                      local orbital period.  The default, and the expression
                      the production loop used inline, unchanged.
       constantAngle  dt = 2 pi mu r^2 / (N L) = (2 pi / N) / (dphi/dt), N
                      steps per turn of the true anomaly, so the swept angle
                      per step is constant all the way down.  Opt-in through
                      CREM_STEP_LAW=angle.

89c. WHAT REGULARIZATION BUYS.  Deep untilted geometry, apoapsis 3 r*,
     periapsis 0.3 r*, tolerance 1e-8, three orbital windows, 256 steps per
     local orbit, subdivision depth 20:

     law              window   periapsis   apoapsis   L final   force evals
     constant dt      0.523    0.2928 r*   3.000 r*   0.0361    9302  (fails)
     constant dt *    3.000    0.2631 r*   45.009 r*  0.0421    17385
     localOrbit       3.000    0.2631 r*   44.991 r*  0.0421    16915
     constantAngle    3.000    0.2631 r*   44.976 r*  0.0421    19021

     * at depth 26 instead of 20; every other row is depth 20.

     The whole of the difference is SUBDIVISION DEPTH.  At depth 20 the
     constant step breaks off in the first plunge, at 0.523 of the window,
     and it needs six more levels -- a factor of 64 in the smallest step the
     engine can reach -- to finish the passage the regularized laws finish at
     20.

89d. WHAT IT DOES NOT BUY, AND THIS IS THE CORRECTION.  It buys neither a
     different trajectory nor a cheaper one.  Given enough depth all three
     laws agree: the same periapsis 0.2631 r*, the same apoapsis 45 r*
     afterwards, the same final angular momentum 0.0421 hbar.  And the cost
     in force evaluations spans 12%: 16915, 17385, 19021.  Regularization is
     what makes the periapsis passage completable at a given depth, not what
     makes it accurate and not what makes it fast.  The premise of the
     request -- that the step law is what limits accuracy down there -- is
     not what the measurement says.

89e. THE TILTED GEOMETRY BREAKS THE STORY.  Apoapsis 3 r*, periapsis 1 r*,
     tilt 90 deg, tolerance 1e-8, depth 20.  At 256 steps per local orbit all
     three laws fail, at the same fraction of the window and at three
     different places:

     law            steps/orbit  window   periapsis   L final   dE_kepler
     constant dt    256          0.316    0.2645 r*   0.0182    +5.11
     localOrbit     256          0.324    0.0281 r*   0.0008    +130.7
     constantAngle  256          0.320    0.0787 r*   0.0003    -39.8
     constant dt    2048         0.319    0.1454 r*   0.0150    +0.047
     localOrbit     2048         0.323    0.0307 r*   0.0041    -86.9
     constantAngle  2048         0.323    0.0310 r*   0.0078    +211.3

     All six break at 0.32 of the window, which says the event is physical
     and not a step-size artefact: the tilted orbit sheds its angular
     momentum to the spin sector, from 0.0523 hbar to between 0.015 and
     0.0003, and goes very nearly radial.  On a radial plunge the constant
     step refuses EARLIER and CLEANER (periapsis 0.1454 r*, Kepler drift
     0.047 at 2048 steps) while the regularized laws are driven deeper into
     the region where the numbers fall apart (0.0307 and 0.0310 r*, drifts
     -86.9 and +211.3).

     The constant-angle law fails here by a mechanism worth naming, because
     it is the opposite of the intended one and it was measured rather than
     argued.  Its dt ~ r^2/L grows as L collapses, so it LENGTHENS the step
     exactly while the orbit is turning radial.  Along this trajectory the
     angular momentum falls to 0.000174 hbar without ever reaching zero, so
     the L <= 0 guard inside regularizedTimeStep fires zero times out of 1335
     steps -- that branch is not what protects anything here -- and the
     constant-angle step reaches 44.3x the local-orbit step at the same
     point.  The guard catches only the exactly-radial case, which a
     numerical orbit does not visit.  So 89c holds where the orbit stays
     non-radial, and nowhere else.  Section 88f had already found
     this geometry needs P/2048; it needs more than a finer step.

89f. THE PRODUCTION PATH IS UNCHANGED, BIT FOR BIT.  The extraction was
     meant to be a rename, so it was checked as one.  A probe built from the
     HEAD tree and from the working tree, printing runMechanicalTrajectory's
     final state to 17 digits, agrees exactly on three geometries:

     geometry                        outcome           minimum separation
     apo 3, peri 1, tilt 0, 2 windows  ObservationLimit   1.614e-13 m
     apo 3, peri 1, tilt 90, 2 windows ReachedCutoff      9.665e-15 m
     apo 3, peri 0.3, tilt 0, 1 window ObservationLimit   5.084e-14 m

     Every printed digit of elapsed time, minimum separation, radiated
     energy, maximum beta and both final four-vectors matches.  The second
     row reaches the 0.05 r* cutoff at beta 0.87, so the loop was genuinely
     subdividing, not idling.  For the two positronium.cpp sites the same
     comparison was run on the experiments that drive them, HEAD against
     working tree: Experiment 4 (--phenomenon 4 --runs 1 --seed 42, 31 lines)
     and Experiment 5 (--phenomenon 5 --runs 5 --seed 42, 49 lines) are
     identical.  Validation 61/61 PASS, header isolation 37/37.

89g. A MEASUREMENT OF MINE THAT PROVED NOTHING.  The first identity check
     was the paired lifetime tool at a 90 s per-event budget, HEAD against
     working tree.  The diff was empty and it looked like a pass.  It was
     not: both sides printed "only 0 complete pairs", because the budget
     censored every trajectory before it collapsed.  Two empty results agree
     trivially.  It is recorded here because it read as evidence and was not,
     and it is replaced by 89f, which exercises the changed loop directly.

89h. AN ARTEFACT THAT LOOKS LIKE A PATHOLOGY.  The constant-angle run
     reports a smallest OUTER step of 4.8e-35 s, six orders below every
     other run.  It is not the law misbehaving: it falls at r = 44.98 r*, at
     the end of the window, and it is the clip to the remaining budget in
     min(dt, target - t).  Worth stating because a step that small in a
     census looks exactly like the defect of section 88.

89i. NOT CHANGED.  Production defaults are untouched: the default law, 128
     steps per local orbit, the 5e-18 s ceiling and the 1 fs ceiling of the
     beam loops are all as before, which is what 89f measures.
     CREM_STEP_LAW is opt-in and documented in the configuration panel,
     together with the depth this geometry requires.

89j. LIMITS.  Two geometries, both e+e- with the default floor and moment
     radius, three orbital windows each rather than a full inspiral.  The
     depth-20 and depth-26 thresholds are for apoapsis 3 r*, periapsis
     0.3 r* at tolerance 1e-8 and are not a general rule.  The Kepler-energy
     drift quoted in 89e is a comparative indicator only: deep down the real
     dipole, Darwin and retardation terms contribute to it alongside
     numerical error, so it separates the runs but does not measure the error
     of any one of them.  The tilt-90 plunge of 89e is the open item of
     section 88h seen from another side and is not fixed here.

89k. WHAT ELSE RODE IN ON THIS COMMIT, stated because the message does not
     cover it.  Sixteen figures under distributions/ were rewritten by the
     Experiment 4 and 5 identity runs of 89f, which write their plots into
     the project directory; they are kept, at the author's decision, and they
     come from two single-seed runs rather than from a full regeneration of
     the set like commit c3a99ea.  One line of ScientificalReferences.txt
     also rode in: the entry for the E1 hazard rate, updated in the work of
     section 79 to name secularElectricDipoleOrbitAveragedEmission instead of
     larmorOrbitAveragedPower, had been left uncommitted in the tree since
     then.  Neither is part of the time-step change.

90. WHAT BREAKS THE SINGLET, MEASURED TERM BY TERM
==================================================================

     Section 18 asked whether the loss of the singlet is a defect or
     physics and answered: a defect, and it is a LIBRATION rather than a
     drift.  This section asks the question section 18 left open -- WHICH
     TERM does it -- and the answer overturns the mechanism recorded in
     crem_collapse.hpp.  Four of my own claims died on the way; they are in
     90f because each of them read as a result first.

90a. THE SEPARATION THAT WORKS.  The mutual angle moves at
     d(mu1.mu2)/dt = (w1-w2).(mu1 x mu2), so the whole question is
     |w1-w2|.  Subtracting the partner's dipole field alone -- recomputed
     from retardedMagneticDipoleField and removed from the assembled local
     field, WITHOUT touching any moment -- against the full field, para,
     circular, the moments perpendicular to the orbit:

       r/a_pair    |w1-w2| full     without the dipole field   ratio
       0.0039      1.7049e+18       5.2122e+14                 3.0572e-04
       0.0156      6.8226e+16       3.3239e+11                 4.8719e-06
       0.0625      1.1256e+15       3.0871e+08                 2.7426e-07
       0.25        1.7648e+13       3.0052e+05                 1.7029e-08
       1           2.7582e+11       2.9341e+02                 1.0638e-09
       4           4.3097e+09       2.8652e-01                 6.6483e-11
       16          6.7340e+07       2.7937e-04                 4.1486e-12
       64          1.0522e+06       2.6263e-07                 2.4960e-13

     Three orders at the Compton end, thirteen at the far end.  The
     singlet is broken by the PARTNER'S MAGNETIC DIPOLE FIELD, and by
     nothing else that the assembly contains.

90b. THE RECORDED MECHANISM WAS A CORRELATION.  crem_collapse.hpp says the
     asymmetry "tracks the MOTIONAL electric dipole exactly", and derives
     it from p = gamma (v x mu)/c^2 being odd in v and even in mu.  The
     sentence has two halves and they fare differently.  "The whole asymmetry
     is magnetic" is confirmed here: 90a leaves the electric sector untouched
     and still accounts for everything.  "And tracks the MOTIONAL electric
     dipole exactly" is the half that does not survive as a cause.  Tracking
     is all it is: p = gamma (v x mu)/c^2 and the partner's dipole field are
     two properties of the SAME moment, so they move together whatever drives
     the angle, and removing "the moments" removes both at once.  90a removes
     only the magnetic field of the partner's moment, and the asymmetry goes
     with it.

     Section 27a is NOT affected and its parity table stands: it switched the
     charge and the moment off separately, which is a valid separation, and
     its conclusion -- the dipole part is even for para and odd for ortho,
     the motional part odd always -- is exactly what 90a's result requires.
     What 90a settles is which of the two parts carries the effect, a
     question that table does not answer.

90c. THE ASYMMETRY IS NOT A v/c EFFECT.  Measured at FIXED beta = 0.007297,
     scanning radius alone -- which the per-level figures (0.602, 0.270,
     0.174 for n=1,2,3) cannot do, because a level changes r and beta
     together:

       r/a_pair    0.0156   0.0625   0.25    1      4      16     64
       |w1-w2|/|w1| 2.104   2.463    7.985  1.001  0.182  0.043  0.010

     Two orders of variation at constant beta.  The relativistic reading of
     the per-level scaling was mine and it is wrong.

90d. ORTHO IS ZERO BY CONSTRUCTION, ON A SECOND INSTRUMENT.  |w1-w2| is
     identically 0 across all 18 scanned configurations, every radius and
     every speed, and along a full trajectory (5302 substeps) ortho holds
     cos = -1.000000, |w1-w2|/|w1| = 0.000000 and (mu1+mu2).Lhat = 0.000000
     without exception.  This confirms section 27a on an independent probe:
     it is an exact symmetry of the configuration, not a result the model
     could have failed.

90e. THE LIBRATION SURVIVES A LARGE AND PERMANENT ASYMMETRY.  Para, one
     trajectory, 6932 spin-transport substeps, seed 42:

       cos                    0.335085 .. 1.000000
       |w1-w2|/|w1|           0.917242 .. 1.234250
       returns to cos > 0.999            18
       lifetime               199.45285 ps against ortho's 199.444493 ps

     The rate asymmetry never falls below 0.92 -- it is always there, and
     large -- and the singlet reassembles anyway, eighteen times.  So the
     libration of sections 18c and 27b is not the asymmetry being weak.  It
     is the asymmetry being PERIODIC.  That strengthens the section 17
     conclusion: the terminal angle is a phase, and the two channels'
     collapse times differ by 8 fs in 199 ps, so none of this reaches an
     observable.

90f. FOUR OF MY OWN CLAIMS THAT DIED, each recorded because it read as a
     result before it was checked:

     - "The motional term breaks the singlet."  A v/c mechanism was the
       hypothesis this section started from.  90a and 90c killed it.
     - "Zeroing the proper moments removes the dipole field."  It does not:
       synchronizeCovariantDipoles refills the lab moment, |mu1_lab| comes
       back at 9.2849e-24 against a true moment-free 0, and the field moves
       in the sixth digit (0.7813741 against 0.7813793) instead of changing
       from 0.78 to 1.56.  Three measurements compared the full field with
       itself before a separate check caught it.  This is the same trap as
       the secular magnetic term, and I walked into it again.
     - "|w1-w2|/|w1| spikes to 170 near 0.3375 a_pair."  The RATIO does.
       |w1-w2| itself falls smoothly, 1.76e13 to 1.66e12 across that whole
       interval; the denominator |w1| collapses to 1.03e11 and recovers.  An
       artefact of normalizing, not an amplification.
     - "The mixing operator rotates: (mu1+mu2).Lhat changes sign."  It does
       change sign, once in 6932 substeps, but |mu1+mu2| passes through it
       continuously (1.78515 to 1.78453) while Lhat flips from
       (0.286,-0.039,0.957) to (0.275,0.470,-0.839).  The projection moved
       because the AXIS turned.

90g. WHAT THE AXIS FLIP ACTUALLY IS, since it had to be excluded.  It is
     the photon recoil, not an integration fault.  The flip sits between
     substeps 4199 and 4200; CREM_PHOTON_LADDER puts the second photon
     exactly there (70.63 eV at a/a_pair = 0.333).  Adjacent Lhat dot
     products are above 0.9 for 6929 of 6931 steps, and the skip census
     shows |L| descending in the matching steps, 1.00000 -> 0.57744 ->
     0.27358 hbar, with the direction jumping at each one.

90h. SO: DEFECT OR PHYSICS?  Defect, and section 20b already gave the
     reason more deeply than this section can: the classical states are not
     C eigenstates at all, because +-1 comes from the exchange symmetry of a
     wavefunction and two vectors have none.  What 90a adds is the address.
     The term that does it is the partner's dipole field -- exactly the
     interaction that Wigner-Eckart forbids from mixing singlet with triplet
     (section 18a), since two spin-1/2 have no S'=2.  The model's
     singlet-breaking channel is therefore the one channel quantum mechanics
     closes completely, which is what makes it a defect rather than a
     missing correction.

90i. LIMITS.  One seed and one trajectory per channel for the transport
     figures; the scans are static configurations with the moments
     perpendicular to the orbit, not sampled along a trajectory; the
     fixed-beta scan uses unbound configurations outside the model's
     declared domain and describes the structure of the term, not a
     prediction; no external field, where the rank-1 Zeeman term genuinely
     does mix S=0 with S=1 (section 18a) and this conclusion would not hold.

91. THE IMPOSED MUTUAL ANGLE IS GONE, AND SO IS THE BAND
==================================================================

     Asked to withdraw spin quantization, on the principle that quantization
     should come out of the physics rather than be handed to it.  I argued
     against it first, because it costs result N2 of the liberation plan;
     the principle was reaffirmed and is the stronger claim, so it was done.
     What the work found is that TWO impositions had to go, not one.

91a. THE BAND WAS NOT THE FREE ALTERNATIVE.  The sampler had two branches.
     The quantized one set the second moment to +-the first, cos = +-1
     exactly.  The other was a REJECTION LOOP:

         do { secondDipole = randomDirection()*mu; }
         while ((scenario==2 && cos <  0.5)
             || (scenario==3 && cos >= 0.5));

     It redrew until the cosine landed on the side of 0.5 the selected
     channel wanted.  That is a threshold imposed in place of a point, and it
     fails the same principle.  Switching the default to the band would have
     looked like compliance without being it.  Both branches are now off the
     default path: the direction is drawn uniformly, once, and the channel is
     a CLASSIFICATION of the draw (dipoleAlignment >= 0.5, which the sampler
     already computed for its own phenomenon label).

91b. WHAT THE FREE DRAW PREPARES, predicted then measured.  A uniform
     direction gives cos uniform on [-1,1], so the 0.5 threshold splits the
     ensemble 25/75 and the mean of |mu1+mu2|/mu = sqrt(2+2cos) follows by
     integration.  Measured over 2000 prepared states per phenomenon:

       quantity              predicted   measured (free draw)
       para share              25.0%       24.7%  (494/2000)
       ortho share             75.0%       75.3%  (1506/2000)
       mean cos                 0.000      -0.0038
       <|m|/mu> all             1.3333      1.3293
       <|m|/mu> para label      1.8692      1.8681
       <|m|/mu> ortho label     1.1547      1.1526

     Control with --spin-quantization restored: cos exactly +1 or exactly -1,
     |m|/mu exactly 2.0000 or 0.0000, label shares 100/0.

91c. THE COST, STATED AS A LOSS.  Result N2 -- the shape of the Landau-Yang
     rule from classical magnetostatics -- rests on |mu1+mu2| vanishing
     EXACTLY for ortho, which is a property of cos = -1.  A uniform draw
     reaches that point with probability zero, and the ortho LABEL now
     carries 1.1526 mu of net moment on average instead of 0.  The coherent
     M1 channel therefore no longer cancels for anything the model prepares
     by default.  This is not a degradation of the measurement; it is the
     removal of the configuration the measurement was about.

     For the record, the band was no better: measured today, ortho under the
     band prepared at cos = 0.1486 with |m|/mu = 1.5157 against para's
     1.8440, i.e. 82% of para's net moment rather than zero.

91d. THE TWO PHENOMENA ARE NOW ONE ENSEMBLE.  With the imposition gone,
     scenarios 2 and 3 differ in nothing: the orbital sampling was always
     shared, and the moment angle is now drawn the same way for both.
     Measured: phenomenon 1 and phenomenon 2 give identical shares
     (494/1506), identical mean cosine (-0.0038) and identical <|m|/mu>
     (1.3293) over 2000 draws each.

91e. AND THAT BREAKS THE PAIRING TOOL, WHICH NOW REFUSES.  tools/para_ortho_
     lifetimes.cpp pairs phenomenon 1 against phenomenon 2 at a common seed.
     Run under the free draw it produced:

       idx    t_para(ps)     t_ortho(ps)    ratio   photons
        0     216.731909     216.731909      1       3 / 3
        1     199.460186     199.460186      1       3 / 3
        2     199.448214     199.448214      1       3 / 3
       DIFFERENCE ortho-para: 0 +/- 0 ps (0 sigma)
       GEOMETRIC RATIO: 1, 95% CI [1, 1]

     Identical to the last digit, because it compared an ensemble with
     itself.  A difference of exactly zero reads like a measured null result
     and is nothing of the kind -- the same failure mode as the censored
     comparison of 89g.  The tool now checks gSpinQuantization and REFUSES,
     naming the switch that restores its premise, rather than printing that
     zero.

91f. WHAT MOVED THAT IS NOT PHYSICS.  randomDirection consumes two numbers
     from the shared mt19937_64, and the quantized branch consumed NONE for
     the second moment.  Every seeded figure in earlier sections therefore
     shifts between the two modes: the same master seed and index no longer
     name the same trajectory.  --spin-quantization reproduces them.

91g. VERIFICATION.  Validation 61/61 PASS, so no enforced check depended on
     the imposed angle.  Header isolation 37/37.  The production binary
     builds, and both --spin-quantization and --no-spin-quantization are
     recognized (an unknown option exits 1).

     A test of mine that proved nothing, recorded because it read as a pass:
     the first switch check appended --help, which the program does not
     accept, so both runs stopped at "unknown option --help" before doing
     anything, and I read the exit code of a pipeline ending in head rather
     than the program's.  Repeated properly, both switches are accepted.

91h. WHAT I DID NOT DO.  Menu entries 1 and 2 are now duplicates under the
     default, and they are kept: merging them would change the command-line
     surface and every document that names --phenomenon 2, which is beyond
     withdrawing the imposition.  The sampler comment and the pairing tool
     both say so at the point of use.  Anything that needs the two channels
     to differ needs --spin-quantization, and that includes the para/ortho
     lifetime comparison this project's method was built on.

91i. LIMITS.  The distribution figures are prepared states only, 2000 draws
     per phenomenon, no inspiral; the lifetime figures are three trajectories
     per channel at one seed and one budget; nothing here re-measures the
     hyperfine, contact or M1 sections that were taken under the imposed
     angle, and those remain valid only as statements about that preparation.

92. THE TWO OPEN LEDGER ITEMS, RECHECKED: THE SECTOR WEIGHT OF 82g AND THE
    TILT-90 RESIDUAL OF 86f
==================================================================

92a. THE WEIGHT IN crem_trajectory's DIAGNOSTICS: TRUE, AND INERT BY
     DEFAULT.  82g said the frame and Schott diagnostics call the ledger
     with weight 1 whatever the force model.  Still true to the letter:
     conservativeParticleEnergy defaults chargeDipoleWeight to 1.0, and the
     four calls in crem_trajectory.hpp (lines 86, 657, 665, 871) pass none.
     But the file contains no Accuracy and no useRetardedExternalForces, so
     the trajectory path always integrates the retarded forces (the engine
     default is true); the only place that turns them off is the validation
     probe, and it already passes retarded?1.0:0.0.  The integrator's own
     weight, retardedDipoleSectorWeight, measured on circular e+e- states:

       r/r*    default    CREM_DIPOLE_INSTANTANEOUS_BELOW=2
       0.05    1.000000   0.000000
       0.2     1.000000   1.000000
       1.0     1.000000   1.000000
       5.0     1.000000   1.000000

     So with default settings the diagnostics and the integrator agree
     everywhere.  The mismatch is reachable without recompiling, but only
     under that switch and only below its threshold.  Describing it as an
     inconsistency "in instantaneous-force mode" was wrong for this file:
     it has no such mode.

92b. THE TILT-90 RESIDUAL CANNOT BE COMPARED WITH 86f, AND WHY.  86f quoted
     the spin-sector |dE/dt| "relative to |U_dd| n" (para 0 deg 45 -> 1.3e-3,
     para 90 deg 0.27 -> 0.080, each pair being before -> after section 86).
     That scale is defined nowhere in this audit, and the probes that
     computed it (energyflow.cpp, energyscan*.cpp, ledgercand.cpp) were in a
     session scratch directory and are gone.  The 1-14% figure is therefore
     neither confirmed nor refuted here.  What can be measured is recorded
     below, in a scale of its own.

92c. THE REBUILT FLOW PROBE IS CORRECT.  82b's method: dE/dt of the full
     ledger by central difference of the flow map, spin sector = with
     moments minus without, allExternalForces or retardedExternalForces with
     a causal history, Kepler nodes at e = 0.3.  The difference quotient is
     converged, not frozen: the raw energy difference halves exactly with h
     (2.037072e-20, 1.018536e-20, 5.092681e-21, 2.546340e-21 J) and the
     quotient holds at 7.110997e+04 W.  Against a moment-free pair at
     periapsis 0.5 r* the sector stands far above the background:

       moments   forces          spin sector   background   ratio
       0 deg     retarded        9.3772e+04 W  5.106e+01 W  1836
       0 deg     instantaneous   3.5290e+03 W  1.137e+02 W    31
       90 deg    retarded        1.1258e+05 W  5.106e+01 W  2205
       90 deg    instantaneous   7.9611e+03 W  1.137e+02 W    70

     The sector is present in BOTH force models, which is the half of the
     note that this confirms.

92d. NOTHING SINGLES OUT 90 DEGREES.  Moment angle swept from the orbital
     axis, periapsis 0.5 r*, e = 0.3, 8 nodes; mean |dE/dt| of the sector:

       angle   retarded      instantaneous
        0      9.3772e+04    3.5290e+03
       15      9.5009e+04    3.6963e+03
       30      9.8407e+04    4.1536e+03
       45      1.0309e+05    4.9482e+03
       60      1.0782e+05    6.4545e+03
       75      1.1130e+05    7.5574e+03
       90      1.1258e+05    7.9611e+03

     Monotone and smooth in both models: +20% retarded and x2.3
     instantaneous from 0 to 90, with no feature at 90.

92e. FIVE MISTAKES OF MINE ON THIS PROBE, recorded because each read as a
     result.  Four times I diagnosed the probe as broken because its
     quotient did not move with h -- the argument not reaching it, Euler
     steps along a straight line, composition of substeps, forces taken from
     the node -- and each fix changed nothing, because an h-independent
     quotient in the linear regime is what a converged derivative looks
     like.  And I first applied 82b's 40-degree offset to both tilts, which
     put the moments at 40 and 130 degrees: mirror images with the same
     |cos| = 0.766, so tilt 0 and tilt 90 came out 0.2687 and 0.2595 by
     construction.  The sweep in 92d has no offset.

92f. LIMITS.  One periapsis for the sweep, e = 0.3 only, e+e- only; static
     Kepler nodes, not engine trajectories; the scale in 92c-d is this
     probe's own and is not the scale of 86f.

93. "THE ZPF BALANCES AT 0.1 a_pair" AGAINST "THE FIELD DRIVES THE ORBIT
    OUT": NO CONTRADICTION, AND NO RERUN
==================================================================

93a. THE TWO STATEMENTS ARE AT DIFFERENT RADII.  "The model's zero-point
     field does not hold the orbit there; it drives it outward" is 42d, and
     section 42 is headed "STEP 4 MEASURED WHERE IT WORKS: 0.01 a_pair".
     Section 75 places the balance at 0.1 a_pair and itself records that
     "inside 0.03 a_pair the field drives the orbit out fast".  One monotone
     picture, which 75e states: the pumping share falls with radius and
     crosses 1 near 0.1 a_pair.

       start        pumping share (75c)   behaviour
       1 a_pair     -0.04 +- 0.35         shrinks at the radiative rate
       0.3 a_pair   +0.27 +- 0.13         shrinks
       0.1 a_pair   +0.87 (+-0.70)        balance in the mean only;
                                          excursions 2-4.6x, ionizations
       0.03 a_pair  >> 1                  driven out
       0.01 a_pair  >> 1 (|W|/Larmor 9.74) driven out (42d)

     And 75e never claimed a stationary state at 0.1 a_pair.

93b. THE DIPOLE SECTOR DID CHANGE AFTER SECTION 75, BUT NOT WHERE THE NOTE
     POINTED.  Section 75 is a19b9ee (2026-09-14).  After it: the Thomas
     back-reaction on the instantaneous force (80de772), -p.E in the ledger
     (8cb0c0a), the current-loop field (c81e31d), the moment's own radius
     (eba786b) and the retarded magnetization (e36bf5c).  The ZPF runs used
     the instantaneous forces with moments along the normal (74b), so on
     that path, dipole force over Coulomb force:

       r/a_pair   today        no Thomas    moment radius 0.05 r*
       1          3.3512e-06   1.0008e-05   3.3515e-06
       0.3        7.0663e-05   1.1117e-04   7.0692e-05
       0.1        7.8807e-04   9.9857e-04   7.9040e-04
       0.03       9.5572e-03   1.0839e-02   9.8426e-03
       0.01       7.2114e-02   7.8774e-02   9.3489e-02

     The moment radius, a factor 20 in softening, moves the force by 0.30%
     at 0.1 a_pair.  The Thomas term moves it by 21% there and by a factor
     3 at 1 a_pair: that is the change that matters, not the radius.

93c. FORCE IS NOT WORK.  The balance is a ratio of work per orbit.  Work of
     the dipole sector over one Kepler orbit, instantaneous forces,
     against the Larmor energy of that orbit, at 0.1 a_pair:

       e      today        no Thomas
       0.0    1.19e-15     1.04e-15
       0.3    2.86e-14     2.94e-14
       0.6    2.84e-13     2.54e-13

     All radii give 1e-15 to 8e-13.  The share at 0.1 a_pair is
     1 - (-0.029)/(-0.218) = 0.87, and 75c's +-0.152 is the uncertainty of
     the pair mean lnA, not of the share: propagated, the share is uncertain
     by 0.152/0.218 = 0.70.  Against that the work is twelve orders of
     magnitude short.  But this number is small
     partly by construction: the conservative part of the force is a
     gradient and does no work around a closed orbit.  It shows the sector
     exchanges no net energy per orbit; it does not show the sector cannot
     move the balance through the orbit's dynamics.  That route is
     estimated, not measured: a force of 7.9e-4 of Coulomb, changed by 21%,
     against a share uncertain by 0.70.

93d. SO NO RERUN.  Nothing measured here can reach section 75's resolution,
     and repeating the 8-hour runs would need a new probe: the long-run
     probe is named nowhere in sections 74-75 and was never in the
     repository (git log -S "pumping share" finds only the audit commit).

93e. LIMITS.  Static Kepler orbits without the ZPF, not ZPF-driven
     trajectories; e+e- with moments along the normal only; the dynamic
     route of 93c is an estimate.

94. WITH THE ANGLE FREE, THE DYNAMICS DOES NOT SELECT IT
==================================================================

94a. QUESTION.  Section 91 removed the imposed mutual moment angle.  Does
     the inspiral then carry the angle toward particular values -- cos = -1
     and +1, the model's two fixed points of spin transport -- so that
     something like quantization comes out of the dynamics?

94b. SETUP.  Probe cosevo.cpp: 40 trajectories, production defaults with
     gSpinQuantization = 0, one runCremCollapseExperiment call each so the
     diagnostic lines cannot interleave, seeds 50000-50009, 50100-50109,
     50200-50209, 50300-50309, phenomenon 1 (the same ensemble as 2 since
     section 91), budget 300 s.  The angle is read at EVERY spin-transport
     substep (CREM_DEBUG_ALIGN), 3986 to 7213 samples per trajectory, not
     at checkpoints, which section 18d showed alias the libration phase.
     All 40 reached the cutoff; none censored; every one emitted 3 photons.

94c. RESULT: NO SELECTION.  Share of cos in five equal bins:

       bins                         [-1,-.6) [-.6,-.2) [-.2,.2) [.2,.6) [.6,1]
       start value, per trajectory   0.175    0.200    0.325    0.200   0.100
       first 10% of samples, pooled  0.152    0.221    0.263    0.190   0.174
       last 10% of samples, pooled   0.169    0.219    0.247    0.183   0.184
       uniform                       0.200    0.200    0.200    0.200   0.200

     Mean cos drifts by -0.0004 from the first 10% to the last, with a
     spread of 0.2848 over 40 trajectories (standard error 0.045).  The
     pooled share of |cos| > 0.9 rises from 0.046 to 0.085, but per
     trajectory it rises in 5, falls in 4 and stays in 31: single
     trajectories near the ends carry the pooled change.  The ensemble ends
     as flat as it started.

94d. WHAT THE DYNAMICS DOES DISTINGUISH: HOW WIDELY THE ANGLE SWINGS.
     Libration band width, max - min of cos over the whole trajectory:

       start cos      n    mean width   min     max
       [-1.0,-0.6)    7    0.279        0.046   0.653
       [-0.6,-0.2)    8    0.539        0.326   0.968
       [-0.2,+0.2)   13    0.826        0.167   1.576
       [+0.2,+0.6)    8    0.824        0.557   1.091
       [+0.6,+1.0)    4    0.677        0.542   0.781

     Near cos = -1 the swing is narrow, down to 0.046, as the exact
     exchange symmetry of section 27a requires at -1 itself.  The +1 end
     shows no such narrowing.  So the two ends differ in stiffness, not in
     attraction: an angle near anti-alignment stays near it, an angle near
     alignment does not, and nothing moves toward either.

94e. LIFETIME AGAINST THE START ANGLE (data for the next step).  Lifetimes
     take two values, 199.4-199.5 ps (27 trajectories) and 216.7-216.8 ps
     (13).  By start cos:

       start cos      n    216.7 ps   199.4 ps
       [-1.0,-0.6)    7    0          7
       [-0.6,-0.2)    8    2          6
       [-0.2,+0.2)   13    7          6
       [+0.2,+0.6)    8    3          5
       [+0.6,+1.0)    4    1          3

     Spearman rank correlation of branch with start cos: +0.206, n = 40,
     against about 0.31 needed at 5% two-sided.  Not significant.  That no
     trajectory below cos = -0.4 takes the long branch was noticed after
     looking, so it is a lead for a fresh ensemble, not a result.  The
     photon count is 3 in all 40 and carries no information here.

94f. A WRONG HINT OF MINE.  After the first trajectory (start -0.397, band
     -0.763 to -0.397) I suggested the start value might always lie on the
     edge of the band, meaning a swing out from the start and back.  It
     holds for 9 of 40 within 0.02.  It is not a pattern.

94g. LIMITS.  40 trajectories, one budget, production defaults only; the
     first and last 10% are counted in substeps, not in time, which is
     adequate here because nothing drifts; e+e- only.  The probe lives in
