     the session scratch directory and is recorded here by what it does.

95. LIFETIME AGAINST THE START ANGLE: THE REGRESSION THAT REPLACES PAIRING
==================================================================

95a. WHY.  Since section 91 phenomena 1 and 2 are one ensemble, so the
     para/ortho pairing tool refuses to run.  The start angle is now a
     continuous variable, so the comparison becomes a regression over one
     ensemble.  Section 94e left one lead, noticed after looking: no long
     lifetime branch below cos = -0.4, in 7 trajectories.

95b. HYPOTHESES FIXED BEFORE THE RUN (written 2026-09-17T07:26:46+02:00,
     fresh seeds 60000-60079, censored runs to be excluded and counted):
       H1  no 216.7 ps branch at start cos < -0.4
       H2  the branch correlates with the start cos (Spearman, 5% two-sided)
       H3  within each branch the lifetime correlates with the start cos

95c. SETUP.  80 trajectories, production defaults, budget 300 s, one call
     each.  The start angle is read from the prepared state exactly as
     estimateCremCollapse prepares it (simulate on splitMix64(masterSeed)
     with a 1e-24 s window), with no integration.  Checked first against the
     40 trajectories of section 94, read there from the first transport
     substep: 40 of 40 agree, largest difference 4e-7.  p-values by 20000
     permutations.

95d. RESULT.  All 80 reached the cutoff, none censored, all emitted 3
     photons.  Lifetimes again take exactly two values: 199.4-199.5 ps (53)
     and 216.7-216.8 ps (27).

       hypothesis                     measured                     verdict
       H1 no long branch below -0.4   2 of 23 long                 refuted
       H2 branch ~ start cos          Spearman +0.390, p = 0.0003  holds
       H3 short branch, t ~ cos       Spearman -0.415, p = 0.0023  holds
       H3 long branch, t ~ cos        Spearman -0.266, p = 0.18    not sig.

     H2 and the short-branch H3 also pass a correction for four tests
     (threshold 0.0125).

       start cos      n    long branch
       [-1.0,-0.6)   13     1
       [-0.6,-0.2)   20     1
       [-0.2,+0.2)   20    12
       [+0.2,+0.6)   15     7
       [+0.6,+1.0)   12     6

     Below cos = -0.2 the long branch is taken 2 times in 33, above it 25
     times in 47.  That reads like a step rather than a line, but the cut
     was placed after looking and is description only.

95e. THE SIZE OF EACH EFFECT.  Choosing the branch moves the lifetime by
     17.3 ps.  Within the short branch the slope is -7.6 +- 2.5 fs per unit
     of cos, about 15 fs from anti-aligned to aligned, 7.6e-5 of the
     lifetime; the long branch gives -6.2 +- 4.6 fs, the same sign, not
     significant.  So the start angle acts on the lifetime almost entirely
     through which branch the trajectory takes.

95f. WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT MEAN.  The regression resolves structure
     the pairing could not: the paired comparison under quantization ended
     with a sign test of p = 1 (README, "Co z tego wynika dla porownania
     kanalow").  But the branch is discrete, a 17.3 ps gap with nothing in
     between across 120 trajectories, and tools/para_ortho_branch_trace.cpp
     was written on the premise that the checkpoint skip integer of the
     secular path selects it.  That premise is untested.  If it holds, the
     start angle is steering a discretization of the numerical method, and
     the strong H2 is a property of the method, not of positronium.  Which
     one it is, is the next question.  For scale: the physical para/ortho
     lifetime ratio is about 1000; the continuous effect here is 7.6e-5.

95g. LIMITS.  One budget and production defaults only; e+e- only; the
     photon count is 3 throughout and carries no information here; the step
     near cos = -0.2 is post hoc.

96. WHAT SELECTS THE LIFETIME BRANCH: THE CHECKPOINT CLOCK, AND THE
    LIFETIME IS NOT CONVERGED IN IT
==================================================================

96a. QUESTION.  Sections 94 and 95 found two lifetimes, 199.4 and 216.7 ps,
     with nothing in between, and a branch that correlates with the start
     angle (Spearman +0.390, p = 0.0003).  Is the branch physics or the
     secular path's discretization?  The branches of README "Co wybiera
     galaz" are a different pair (0.278 ps apart, a defect already fixed).

96b. NOT THE INITIAL STATE.  The orbit is prepared sharply: over 120
     trajectories the radius is identical and the energy differs only in
     the fifth digit.  No prepared quantity separates the branches: the best,
     |mu1+mu2| projected on the total angular momentum, classifies 83.3%,
     against 66.7% with no information and 68.3% for the mutual angle.

96c. THE FIRST PHOTON'S CHECKPOINT.  Two pairs with close start features
     from different branches, traced with CREM_SKIP_CENSUS and
     CREM_PHOTON_LADDER.  At n = 1 one checkpoint skips 61438 orbits,
     18.675 ps.  In both pairs the 13.606 eV ground-state photon fires after
     checkpoint 10 in the short branch and after checkpoint 11 in the long
     one, and everything after it shifts rigidly:

       pair            photon 2 (short / long)   photon 3 (short / long)
       50009 / 60002   198.4973 / 215.7906 ps    199.4427 / 216.7434 ps
       60040 / 60075   198.4968 / 215.7877 ps    199.4532 / 216.7490 ps

     A shift of 17.29-17.30 ps each time.  It is one checkpoint minus 1.38 ps
     by which the next stage runs shorter in the long branch; excess hazard
     carried past the threshold is the likely reason, not verified.

96d. WHY A CHECKPOINT DECIDES IT.  Each checkpoint adds its whole skip's
     hazard at once, and the emission loop fires while the running hazard
     exceeds the threshold.  The position of the photon inside the skip,
     sAtPhoton, is computed, but it sets only the photon's energy ratio and
     recoil speed.  The clock does not use it: crem_collapse.hpp advances
     simulatedTimeTotal += checkpointProperTime, the full checkpoint.  So
     the photon's consequences start at the next checkpoint, and a crossing
     that lands just before or just after a checkpoint boundary moves the
     rest of the cascade by about one checkpoint.

96e. THE DECISIVE TEST: SHRINK THE CHECKPOINT.  maximumJumpParameter is a
     constexpr, so the variants were built from temporary copies of the tree
     (production code untouched), same four seeds:

       s_max           50009      60002      60040      60075     photon 1
       0.30 (default)  199.4503   216.7510   199.4600   216.7566  after 10/11
       0.15            207.7439   207.7501   207.7397   207.7409  after 21
       0.075           203.3851   203.3806   203.3816   203.3775  after 41

     Halving the checkpoint removes the branches: all four seeds agree to
     about 10 fs.  The branch is a discretization artefact.

96f. WHY IT CORRELATED WITH THE ANGLE.  The three grids bracket the first
     photon's true threshold crossing.  Recorded at the end of its
     checkpoint, it lies in (186.74, 205.42] at 0.30, in (186.74, 196.08] at
     0.15 and in (186.76, 191.43] at 0.075: between 186.76 and 191.43 ps.
     The default grid has a boundary at 186.745 ps, right at the lower end.
     The start angle changes the radiated power very slightly, which moves
     the crossing across that boundary.  So H2 of section 95 is the angle
     nudging a crossing that happens to sit on a grid line: a property of
     the method.  The within-branch slope of 95e, -7.6 +- 2.5 fs per unit
     of cos, is the size a real power dependence would have, but it is not
     separated from the method here.

96g. THE LIFETIME ITSELF IS NOT CONVERGED.  With the clock advancing in
     whole checkpoints, the reported lifetime is late by up to one
     checkpoint.  Across the three grids it moves from 199.45 or 216.75 to
     207.74 to 203.38 ps.  The cascade after the first photon takes 11.3 to
     12.7 ps in every case, so the converged value would lie roughly between
     198 and 203.4 ps.  The README table showing the lifetime flat within
     0.4% over s_max 0.045-0.30 (199.428, 198.970, 198.944, 199.747 ps) does
     not show convergence in general: an error of up to one checkpoint only
     looks flat when the crossing happens to fall inside each grid's
     checkpoints.  Every lifetime quoted at the default s_max carries this
     quantization.

96h. WHAT FOLLOWS, NOT DONE HERE.  The clock should advance to the emission
     point inside the skip, or the checkpoint should end at the first
     emission, and lifetime convergence in s_max should then be measured
     again.  Until then the discrete branches, and the angle dependence of
     95d, belong to the method.

96i. LIMITS.  Four seeds, three s_max values, production defaults, e+e-
     only; the 1.38 ps residue of 96c is not explained.

97. THE CHECKPOINT CLOCK ENDS AT THE PHOTON
==================================================================

97a. THE DEFECT (section 96).  A stochastic checkpoint added its whole skip
     to the proper clock and to the lab clock even when a photon fired
     partway through it.  The orbits after the photon were counted at the
     old orbit's period, and the photon's consequences began only at the
     next checkpoint.  The lab clock already split each checkpoint at its
     photons, but only to weight each piece by gamma; the total was still
     the whole checkpoint.  Lifetimes were late by up to one checkpoint,
     18.675 ps at n = 1, and fell on two branches.

97b. THE FIX.  The hazard is linear in the number of orbits, and the
     stochastic orbit does not change between photons.  So the exact hazard
     per orbit of one checkpoint predicts on which orbit the next checkpoint
     reaches the threshold, and orbitsToSkip is cut to that orbit before the
     checkpoint runs.  Both clocks, the revolution count and both spin-orbit
     half-steps derive from orbitsToSkip, so they stay consistent without
     further change, and the photon now sits at the end of its checkpoint,
     where the lab clock's gamma split already puts it.  The rate is unknown
     on a new orbit, so the first checkpoint of a trajectory and the first
     after every photon run one orbit.  A slightly wrong prediction costs at
     most about one orbit, 0.3 fs at n = 1.  CREM_CHECKPOINT_CLOCK=whole
     restores the old clock.

97c. VERIFIED ON THE LAB CLOCK.  Same four seeds as section 96,
     lifetimeSecondsLab in ps, the variants built from copies of the
     working tree:

       clock, s_max    50009     60002     60040     60075     mean
       whole, 0.30     199.450   216.751   199.460   216.757   two branches
       new,   0.30     198.988   198.995   198.980   198.992   198.989
       new,   0.15     198.974   198.978   198.975   198.974   198.975
       new,   0.075    198.942   198.945   198.945   198.943   198.944

     The branches are gone at the production setting: the four seeds agree
     to 15 fs where they differed by 17.3 ps.  The dependence on s_max drops
     from about 13 ps (section 96) to 45 fs.  The lab clock and the proper
     clock differ by 2e-6 fs in every run, 1e-11 relative: the photon recoil
     is too slow for its dilation to matter, so the proper-clock figures of
     sections 94-96 were not misleading.

97d. A RESIDUE THAT IS NOT CONVERGING, AND IS NOT EXPLAINED.  The 45 fs is
     not the one-orbit prediction error, which is 0.3 fs.  Nor does it shrink
     like a discretization error: from 0.30 to 0.15 the mean moves by -14 fs,
     from 0.15 to 0.075 by -32 fs.  One candidate is the measured mechanical
     orbit each checkpoint credits with its real retarded-field flux, whose
     number doubles with every halving (62, 125, 254 checkpoints); it is
     untested.  Until it is found, lifetimes carry a step dependence of
     about 2e-4 relative.

97e. COST.  Wall time per trajectory at s_max 0.30 rises from 138-161 s to
     193-203 s, about +35%, from the one-orbit checkpoints after each photon
     and the cut checkpoints.

97f. WHAT CHANGES FOR EARLIER NUMBERS.  Every stochastic lifetime quoted at
     the default setting before this commit was measured on the whole-
     checkpoint clock and is late by a fraction of one checkpoint: -0.46 ps
     for the short branch here, -17.8 ps for the long one.  The branch
     correlation of section 95 was an artefact of that clock (section 96).
     README figures have not been recomputed.

97g. LIMITS.  Four seeds, three s_max values, production defaults, e+e-
     only.  Validation 61/61, header isolation 37/37, production builds.

98. PARA AGAINST ORTHO ON THE LAB CLOCK, AFTER THE CLOCK FIX
==================================================================

98a. QUESTION AND DESIGN.  Is there a collapse-time difference between para
     and ortho once the checkpoint clock no longer quantizes lifetimes
     (section 97)?  Hypotheses fixed before the run (2026-09-17T09:50:33),
     lab clock throughout:
       A  --spin-quantization pairs at s_max 0.30, seeds 70000-70019:
          H1  mean ortho - para differs from zero (sign test, Wilcoxon)
       B  the same pairs at s_max 0.15, seeds 70000-70009:
          H2  same sign and a consistent size
       C  default free draw at s_max 0.30, seeds 71000-71039:
          H3  lab lifetime correlates with the start cos (Spearman)
     The pairing premise was checked first: at a common seed the prepared
     states have cos exactly +1 and -1 and an identical first moment.

98b. RESULT.  No run censored; photon counts equal in every pair.

       stage            n     para (ps)    ortho (ps)   ortho - para
       A  s_max 0.30    20    198.971131   198.873207   -97.9 +- 14.4 fs
       B  s_max 0.15    10    198.955581   198.869469   -86.1 +- 20.2 fs

     A: ortho shorter in 20 of 20, sign test and exact Wilcoxon p ~ 2e-6.
     B: ortho shorter in 9 of 10, sign test p = 0.022, Wilcoxon p = 0.006.
     On the ten common seeds A gives -78.4 fs and B -86.1 fs, with the same
     sign in 9 of 10 pairs.  So the difference is not the 45 fs step residue
     of section 97.  H1 and H2 hold.

     C: Spearman of lab lifetime against the start cos +0.472, permutation
     p = 0.0024; lifetimes 198.856 to 199.077 ps.  Grouped by the 0.5 label,
     para 198.975111 ps (n = 9, error 5.0 fs) and ortho 198.943312 ps
     (n = 31, error 8.5 fs).  The gap is smaller than in A because the ortho
     label spans every angle below 0.5.  H3 holds: in the default model the
     lifetime grows with the mutual cosine.

98c. SIZE.  About -90 fs on 198.9 ps, 4.5e-4 relative.  Under the whole-
     checkpoint clock a per-trajectory error of up to 18.675 ps buried it;
     the paired comparison of README "Co z tego wynika dla porownania
     kanalow" ended with a sign test of p = 1.

98d. SEEN AFTER LOOKING, NOT TESTED.  Across seeds, para barely moves
     (spread 27 fs in A) while ortho moves a lot (184 fs), so the pair
     difference runs from -2 to -191 fs.  The seed sets only the direction
     of the first moment relative to the orbit, so that direction matters
     for ortho and hardly for para.  The mechanism is not identified.

98e. AGAINST THE REAL SYSTEM.  In positronium ortho outlives para by a
     factor of about 1100.  Here the sign is the opposite and the effect is
     4.5e-4.  This model's collapse time is not an annihilation lifetime, so
     the comparison is orientation only.

98f. LIMITS.  20, 10 and 40 trajectories; two checkpoint sizes; production
     defaults otherwise; e+e- only.

99. THE PARA/ORTHO LIFETIME DIFFERENCE IS NOT THE MOMENTS' ATTRACTION
==================================================================

99a. THE OBJECTION.  Section 84 restored the contact term's sign, so the
     moments attract in para and repel in ortho.  Attraction should make para
     collapse faster, yet section 98 found ortho about 90 fs shorter.

99b. WHAT SECTIONS 83-84 ALREADY SAID.  The contact correction relative to
     the tensor term is 3 (eps/r)^2: 5e-6 at 40 r*, where the production
     checkpoints stop, and 3e-4 at the final ~4.9 r* orbit (83f).  Its
     measured effect on production was +0.78 fs for para and nothing for
     ortho (84d).  The long-range tensor term changes sign with the tilt of
     the moments and averages to zero (83c).  So neither was expected to set
     the lifetime ordering.

99c. MEASURED BY SWITCHING TERMS OFF.  Four pairs from section 98 A,
     --spin-quantization, s_max 0.30, lab clock, ortho - para in fs.
     Predictions written first (2026-09-17): contact off changes each pair by
     under 10 fs; dipole forces off remove most of the difference.

       seed    default   contact off   dipole forces off   Thomas off
       70012   -191.08   -190.07       -170.18             -191.08
       70003   -158.63   -158.41       -143.75             -158.63
       70001     -2.05     -2.07        -12.86               -2.05
       70005    -15.11    -15.92        -37.14              -15.11

     Contact off: CREM_MAGNETIC_RADIUS_SCALE=0.05, which shrinks the contact
     term about 370-fold.  Dipole forces off: CREM_NO_DIPOLE_FORCE.  Thomas
     off: CREM_NO_THOMAS_BACKREACTION.

99d. READING.
     - The contact term moves each difference by at most 1.0 fs.  The first
       prediction holds: the section 84 fix plays no part here.
     - With every dipole force off, the difference stays at -13 to -170 fs.
       The second prediction fails: the lifetime difference is not an
       attraction or repulsion effect.
     - The Thomas switch leaves every digit unchanged, so it does not reach
       this path at all.

99e. WHAT IS LEFT, NOT TESTED.  Para and ortho still differ in two ways that
     need no dipole force.  Their total spin is 0 and hbar, so the secular
     spin-orbit exchange that conserves J acts only in ortho; that would also
     explain why ortho depends on the direction of the first moment (184 fs
     spread) and para hardly at all (27 fs), section 98d.  And the coherent
     M1 channel radiates only for para.  Neither is established.

99f. LIMITS.  Four pairs, one checkpoint size, production defaults
     otherwise; CREM_FORCE_M1 acts at photon selection and was not used to
     isolate M1.

100. WHAT MAKES ORTHO SHORTER: THE SECULAR SPIN-ORBIT TRANSPORT, BY THE
     SIGN OF L.S
==================================================================

100a. A SWITCH THAT WAS MISDESCRIBED.  The next test was meant to use
      CREM_AXIAL_SPIN, which the configuration panel described as "constrain
      the spin to the orbital axis".  It does not.  It takes each photon's
      angular momentum as hbar along the orbital axis instead of along the
      photon's direction of flight, and never touches the particles' spins.
      The panel now says so.  No switch freezes the spin-orbit exchange.

100b. A CORRELATION FOUND AFTER LOOKING.  The 20 pairs of section 98 A,
      against the direction of the first moment relative to the orbital
      normal z (six correlations checked, permutation p):

        ortho - para   ~ cos z    Spearman -0.716   p 0.0005
        ortho          ~ cos z             -0.681     0.0013
        para           ~ cos z             +0.123     0.60
        para           ~ |cos z|           +0.672     0.0017
        ortho - para   ~ |cos z|           +0.075     0.76

      By the sign of cos z: mean ortho - para -147.8 fs for the ten pairs
      with cos z > 0 and -48.0 fs for the ten with cos z < 0.  Ortho depends
      on the SIGN, para only weakly on the magnitude.

100c. WHAT THE SIGN MEANS.  The first particle is the electron (gyromagnetic
      ratio -1.761e11).  For ortho at seed 70012 the first moment has
      cos z = +0.8265 and the total spin S.z = -0.8265 hbar with |S| = hbar;
      the orbital angular momentum points along +z.  So cos z > 0 is total
      spin antiparallel to the orbit, L.S < 0, and that is where ortho is
      shortest.  Para has S = 0 and no L.S at all.

100d. THE CAUSAL TEST.  A temporary copy of the tree skips the secular
      spin-orbit half-step (advanceSpinOrbitHalf) under an environment
      variable; the repository is unchanged.  Prediction written first: the
      split by sign disappears.  Four pairs, s_max 0.30, lab clock:

        seed    cos z    default    transport frozen
        70012   +0.83    -191.08    -17.03 fs
        70003   +0.83    -158.63    -15.46 fs
        70001   -0.89      -2.05    +11.16 fs
        70005   -0.54     -15.11    +11.69 fs

      Control: 70001 from the same copy without the variable gives -2.05 fs,
      identical to the repository build.  The mean over the four pairs falls
      from -91.7 fs to -2.4 fs.

100e. READING.  The secular spin-orbit transport produces nearly all of the
      para/ortho lifetime difference, through ortho's total spin: with L.S < 0
      ortho is shorter by about 150 fs, with L.S > 0 by about 50 fs.  The
      prediction holds only approximately: a residue of about +-14 fs keeps
      the sign dependence after freezing (-16 fs for L.S < 0, +11 fs for
      L.S > 0), from some other sign-dependent term that is not identified.

100f. CAVEATS.  Freezing the half-step removes both the exchange of angular
      momentum between spin and orbit and the precession of the moments, and
      this test cannot tell the two apart.  Para, which has no L.S, still
      moves by +17 to +22 fs when frozen, so part of what the freeze removes
      is not L.S.  The correlation in 100b was found after looking; the
      causal test has four pairs.

100g. LIMITS.  --spin-quantization pairs only, one checkpoint size,
      production defaults otherwise, e+e- only.

101e. THE 86f PROBE REPRODUCES 86f, AND SHOWS A REGRESSION.  Built on the

101f. NOT DIAGNOSED.  Two candidates, neither tested.  A real inconsistency:
      the force now reads the magnetization at the retarded time while the
      ledger's contact energy is instantaneous, and with a moment radius of
      0.97 r* that term is not small at 2 r*.  Or a probe artefact: the flow
      is taken on a causal history synthesized from one state, and e36bf5c
      iterates the retarded time over that history.  Production stops near

101g. LIMITS.  The tools are copies of the probes as they ran, with headers

102. THE LEDGER REGRESSION OF e36bf5c IS REAL ON ENGINE TRAJECTORIES
==================================================================

102a. QUESTION.  Section 101e found the spin sector of the ledger far less
      conservative since e36bf5c, but on a flow taken over a history
      synthesized from one state, which e36bf5c's retarded-time iteration
      reads.  Real inconsistency or probe artefact?

102b. TEST.  tools/ledger_engine_drift.cpp: the engine itself, retarded
      forces, reaction off, circular start, moments along the orbital
      normal, four orbits at 256 steps each; range of conservativeParticleEnergy
      over the window in units of |U_dd|.  Built on 776a57e (before) and on
      HEAD 049a4f7 (after).  Decision rule written first: real if the drift
      with moments grows at least tenfold while the moment-free drift is
      unchanged; artefact if within threefold.  Exposure of production (at
      5 and 10 r*, rule added before those runs): HEAD drift with moments at
      least three times the moment-free drift, and different from before.

102c. RESULT.

        r/r*  case        776a57e             HEAD
        1     no moments  0.270               0.270
        1     para        436.5 (to 0.26 r*)  11.9 (out to 22.8 r*)
        1     ortho       0.079               14.8, FAILED at 0.23 r*
        2     no moments  0.190               0.190
        2     para        0.201               1.540 (out to 3.74 r*)
        2     ortho       0.341               34.4, FAILED at 0.30 r*
        5     no moments  0.111               0.111
        5     para        0.063               0.222
        5     ortho       0.290               4.611 (down to 3.11 r*)
        10    no moments  0.133               0.133
        10    para        0.115               0.037
        10    ortho       0.184               0.341

      The moment-free drift is identical to every digit on both commits,
      and tolerance 1e-9 reproduces 1e-8 at 2 r* on both (0.2013 and 1.540).

102d. READING.  Real.  With moments, HEAD pumps energy into para and drains
      it from ortho far above the moment-free background: at 2 r* ortho
      grows 100-fold and the engine fails below the barrier, at 1 r* ortho
      186-fold.  Before e36bf5c both channels at 2 r* held the background
      level.  But e36bf5c also removed a worse defect: para at 1 r* went from
      +436 |U_dd| and a plunge to 0.26 r* to +11.9.  It traded one defect for
      another.  Trajectories diverge between the commits, so part of each
      number is where the orbit went; the direction of the energy change
      (up for para, down for ortho) is systematic.

102e. PRODUCTION.  Exposed at 5 r*: ortho's drift is 42 times the background
      and 16 times its value before, with the orbit sinking from 5 to 3.1 r*
      in four orbits.  Para at 5 r* stays below the threshold, and at 10 r*
      both channels are within a factor two.  Production's last cascade ends
      near 4.9 r*, while its checkpoints sit at 40 r* and beyond.  The effect
      on lifetimes, including the para/ortho difference of sections 98-100,
      is not measured.

102f. LIKELY PLACE, NOT DIAGNOSED.  The force reads the magnetization term at
      the retarded time since e36bf5c; the ledger's contact energy is
      instantaneous, and with a moment radius of 0.97 r* that term is not
      small inside ~10 r*.  This is a candidate, not a finding.

102g. LIMITS.  Tilt 0 only, four orbits, one start per radius, e+e- only.

103. THE MAGNETIZATION TERM, CENTRED CORRECTLY: e36bf5c's LEDGER REGRESSION
     FIXED WITHOUT BRINGING BACK SECTION 88's KINK
==================================================================

103a. STEP 1: SECTION 98 DOES NOT DEPEND ON THE REGRESSION.  The pairs of 98A
      on HEAD with ONLY e36bf5c reverted (tools/build_variant.sh --revert;
      reverting to 776a57e would also have brought back the whole-checkpoint
      clock).  Rule written first: 98 changes if the mean per-seed change of
      ortho - para exceeds three standard errors and 10 fs.
        ortho - para, reverted     -98.099 +- 14.416 fs   (98A: -97.924)
        per-seed change            -0.175 +- 0.092 fs, n = 20
      Not met; ortho shorter in 20 of 20.  Production's checkpoints sit at
      40 r* and beyond, where the regression is invisible.

103b. THE CAUSE: TWO ERRORS IN ONE LINE.  Section 84b's check -- a uniformly
      moving moment's retarded field against the boosted rest-frame loop --
      had drifted out of use (its reference still used the numerical floor).
      Redone with magneticDipoleRadius(), B relative error:
        beta   r      776a57e    HEAD (e36bf5c)
        0.05   1 r*   3.8e-10    8.3e-02
        0.30   1 r*   3.6e-10    5.8e-01
        0.30   2 r*   4.9e-10    3.2e-01
      e36bf5c read the magnetization term at the retarded time, which is what
      removed section 88's kink, but (i) centred the boosted field on the
      RETARDED position, an error first order in beta, and (ii) passed
      mu_lab/gamma from historicalIntegratedDipoleKinematics where
      movingMagnetizationField boosts a PROPER moment, an error second order
      in beta.  On an orbit (i) leaves a tangential force that does work:
      the energy pumping of sections 101e-102.

103c. THREE RECIPES, MEASURED.  All keep the retarded proper moment.

                              one-sided   86f probe     engine drift, |U_dd|
      centre and velocity     slope       2 r* para 0   2 r* ortho  5 r* ortho
      e36bf5c (retarded)      1.0000      3.20e-01      34.4 FAIL   4.61
      A  first-order extrap.  1.0000      3.02e-03      1.59        0.469
      B  observation time     5.84        2.31e-06      0.341       0.290
      C  second-order extrap. 1.0000      2.31e-06      0.300       0.287
      776a57e (before)        5.78*       2.31e-06      0.341       0.290
      (* section 88's value before its fix)

      B restores the ledger exactly and brings the kink straight back.  A is
      smooth and exact in uniform motion but misses a dt^2/2 and turns the
      velocity by omega dt on a circular orbit.  C extrapolates the retarded
      event with the retarded acceleration the pole fields already use:
      smooth, exact in uniform motion, and every ledger digit of the probe
      back to its pre-regression value (2.31e-6, 2.14e-6, 1.22e-4, 7.71e-8 at
      2 and 1 r*; 1.32e-3, 6.60e-5, 8.00e-2, 0.141 at 0.5 r*).  C is adopted.

103d. VERIFIED ON THE WORKING TREE.
      - Engine drift, |U_dd| over four orbits (776a57e / HEAD / C):
          2 r* para 0.201 / 1.540 / 0.209;  5 r* para 0.063 / 0.222 / 0.063;
          10 r* para 0.115 / 0.037 / 0.115; 10 r* ortho 0.184 / 0.341 / 0.184;
          1 r* ortho 0.079 / 14.8 FAIL / 0.073; no moments identical in all.
      - One-sided slopes 1.0000 at both section 88 geometries.
      - Section 98 pairs (70012, 70003, 70001, 70005): each lifetime moves by
        at most 0.93 fs, each ortho - para by at most 1.1 fs.
      - Validation 62/62, header isolation 37/37, production builds.

103e. THE REGRESSION NOW HAS A CHECK.  The existing dipole-boosted-static-field
      check sits at 2 Bohr radii, ~274 r*, where the magnetization term is
      ~1e-5 of the field, so even a 58% error in it passed.  New check
      dipole-moving-magnetization: full B and E of a uniformly moving loop at
      beta 0.05 and 0.3, 1 and 2 r*, against the boosted loop, bound 1e-8
      (correct code ~5e-10).  With HEAD's electrodynamics.hpp restored,
      validation reports 61/62 with exactly this check failing.

103f. A CORRECTION TO SECTIONS 88f AND 89.  Their deep passage (apoapsis 3 r*,
      periapsis 0.3 r*) reaching 0.2631 r* in three windows at depth 20 was
      measured with the mis-centred field.  With C the same passage dips to
      0.2095 r*; at depth 20 it breaks off in the first plunge (one-sided
      slopes 1.0000 at the failing state: not a kink), at depth 26 it finishes
      three windows with the periapsis 0.2095 r* at both tolerance 1e-8 and
      1e-6 (20992 and 2311 force evaluations).  The deep work recipe becomes
      depth 26; crem_engine.hpp and the panel say so.  Section 89's comparison
      of step laws was not repeated.

103g. STILL OPEN.  Para at 1 r*, tilt 0, plunges to 0.29 r* and gains 293
      |U_dd| over four orbits under C.  Before the regression it gained 436;
      HEAD's 11.9 came from the very energy pumping fixed here.  Whether that
      is the deep-orbit ledger or step resolution is not determined.

103h. TOOLS.  moving_magnetization_field.cpp and coupling_one_sided_slopes.cpp
      join tools/; build_variant.sh gains --revert.  Raw data in
      audits/data/section-103.

104. PARA AT 1 r*: NOT THE LEDGER, NOT THE STEP -- THE RETARDED DIPOLE
     SECTOR PROPELS THE PAIR AT SHORT RANGE
==================================================================

104a. THE CASE.  Engine, retarded forces, reaction off, circular start at
      1 r*, para with moments along the orbital normal: over four orbits the
      ledger gains 293 |U_dd| (section 103g).  At r* the identity mu/(e c) = r*
      makes |U_dd| equal to k/r*, about 3.7 keV, so the gain is ~1.1 MeV.

104b. RESOLUTION: RULED OUT.  Rule written first: numerical if the gain falls
      at least twofold per fourfold refinement; not numerical if gain and
      periapsis agree within 10%.
        steps/orbit  depth  gain          r_min
        256          20     +292.9 |U_dd|  0.2899 r*
        1024         26     +292.9         0.2893 r*
        4096         26     +292.9         0.2893 r*
      Sixteen-fold refinement leaves the fourth digit alone.  (A tolerance
      1e-10 run had not finished when this was written.)

104c. THE LEDGER: RULED OUT.  Split into its terms along the run, the gain is
      kinetic: +288.8 |U_dd| kinetic against at most ~15 in Coulomb,
      dipole-dipole, -p.E, Darwin and the dipole constraint together.

104d. WHAT IT IS: THE PAIR AS A WHOLE ACCELERATES.
        t/P    r         beta_cm   |P|/(m c)   K_cm   K_rel   (|U_dd|)
        0.25   0.59 r*   0.24      0.50        23     9.7
        1.00   0.53 r*   0.31      0.69        41     9.7
        2.00   1.48 r*   0.56      1.36        130    0.6
        3.00   1.77 r*   0.67      1.84        187    0.8
      Relative kinetic energy stays small; the gain is centre-of-mass motion,
      and the mechanical momentum reaches 1.84 m c (~940 keV/c), hundreds of
      times the interaction energy scale, so it is not exchange with field
      momentum.  The forces on the two particles are not equal and opposite.
      It starts in the first plunge and grows in steps at deep passages (the
      largest, +49 |U_dd|, at 0.29 r*).

104e. WHICH TERM.  Rule written first: the source is the term whose removal
      takes beta_cm after one orbit from 0.31 below 0.02.
        variant (one orbit)                         beta_cm  gain    r_min
        control, retarded                           0.315    +51.3   0.37 r*
        CREM_NO_DIPOLE_FORCE                        0.000    -0.16   0.98 r*
        CREM_MAGNETIC_RADIUS_SCALE=0.05             0.015    +0.04   1.00 r*
        instantaneous forces                        0.041    -3.3    0.39 r*
        CREM_DIPOLE_INSTANTANEOUS_BELOW=20          0.020    -1.5    0.37 r*
          (instantaneous dipole sector below 1 r*, blend to 2 r*)
      - The dipole sector is the source: without it the momentum is exactly
        zero and the orbit stays at 1 r*.
      - The plunge and the propulsion are separate.  The plunge to ~0.4 r*
        happens with retarded and with instantaneous forces, so it is the
        model's own short-range moment attraction.  The propulsion is eight
        times stronger with retarded forces (0.315 against 0.041), and
        replacing the dipole sector by its instantaneous form below 1 r*
        brings it to 0.020 with the same plunge (the last rule, <= 0.05,
        written before that run).  The small-radius run passes the threshold
        without deciding anything: that orbit never goes deep.
      So: the retarded dipole sector breaks action and reaction at short
      range, inside ~1 r*, and a bound pair that passes there is propelled.

104f. PRODUCTION.  Production's last cascade ends near 4.9 r* and its
      checkpoints sit at 40 r* and beyond; nothing here reaches it.  The
      existing switch CREM_DIPOLE_INSTANTANEOUS_BELOW already removes the
      effect for deep work.

104g. NOT DONE.  Which part of the retarded dipole construction carries the
      momentum imbalance (the per-pole softened two-pole field, the
      magnetization term, or the material-derivative force) is not
      identified.  The instantaneous sector also leaves a residue (0.02-0.04
      per orbit) that was not examined.  Tilt 0 para only; one start radius.

105. WHAT BREAKS ACTION AND REACTION: THE FORCE ON THE MOMENT IN THE
     PARTNER'S CHARGE FIELD, NOT THE POLES, THE MAGNETIZATION OR THE
     MATERIAL TERM
==================================================================

105a. QUESTION.  Section 104 traced the propulsion of para at 1 r* to the
      retarded dipole sector.  Which part: the two softened poles, the
      magnetization term, or the material-derivative term?

105b. THE SECTOR HAS TWO ROADS, NOT ONE.  retardedExternalForces moves the
      orbit through (1) the Lorentz force on each CHARGE in the partner's
      dipole field, poles plus magnetization, and (2) the force on each
      MOMENT, covariantDipoleGradientForce, which differentiates U = mu.B + p.E
      in the partner's FULL field -- its Lienard-Wiechert charge field as well
      as its pole and magnetization fields -- and adds the material term
      gamma v DU/Dt / c^2.  So there are six contributions, charge or moment in
      the partner's charge, pole or magnetization field, and the material
      term runs across all of road (2).

105c. METHOD.  A temporary tree adds six run-time probe switches
      (tools/build_variant.sh --probe-switches).  The probe follows the section
      104 control trajectory and at each of its 256 steps evaluates F1+F2 on
      the same state and engine history under nine switch combinations;
      the fields are linear in each part, so differences isolate each
      contribution.  Rule written first: the source carries more than 50% of
      the integrated net force along the pair's final momentum.

105d. RESULT, one orbit.
        contribution                                along P     share
        1 charge in the partner's charge field      +0.015 mc     2.1%
        2 charge in the partner's POLE field        -0.035 mc    -5.2%
        3 charge in the partner's MAGNETIZATION     +0.106 mc    15.6%
        4 MOMENT in the partner's CHARGE field      +0.601 mc    88.1%
        5 moment in the partner's pole field        +0.002 mc     0.3%
        6 moment in the partner's magnetization     -0.007 mc    -1.0%
          the MATERIAL term, all fields             -0.041 mc    -6.0%
      Checks: the six sum to the full net force within 2.9e-13; the integral
      of F1+F2, 0.682 mc, matches the mechanical momentum gained, 0.688 mc.

105e. CONFIRMED ON THE TRAJECTORY.  With CREM_PROBE_NO_LW_IN_MOMENT (road 2
      loses the partner's charge field), beta_cm after one orbit is 0.047
      against 0.315 (rule, written first: below 0.05), with the same plunge to
      0.36 r* and a ledger change of -1.1 |U_dd| instead of +51.  The residue
      is the size of the instantaneous sector's (0.041, section 104e).

105f. READING.  None of the three named suspects carries it.  The propulsion
      is the spin-orbit road: the force on each moment in the partner's
      retarded charge field.  Its reaction is the force on each charge in the
      moment's field (2 and 3), which adds only +0.07 mc, so it does not
      balance it.  Of the three suspects only the magnetization matters, at
      15.6%, and it acts on the CHARGE, not on the moment.

105g. NOT DONE.  Why road (2)'s spin-orbit force is unbalanced in the
      retarded sector is not established.  One lead, untested: the Thomas
      back-reaction added in section 80 sits in chargeDipoleForces, the
      instantaneous sum, and does not appear in retardedExternalForces.
      Hidden momentum and field momentum were not computed.  One
      configuration (para, tilt 0, 1 r*), one orbit.

106. PARA AND ORTHO: THE PROPULSION IS FORBIDDEN BY SYMMETRY IN ORTHO AND
     GROWS WITH THE DEPARTURE FROM IT
==================================================================

106a. QUESTION.  Does the self-propulsion of sections 104-105 act the same
      way in para and in ortho?  In ledger_engine_drift.cpp the stored
      vectors are the magnetic moments: para has mu1 = +mu2, ortho mu1 = -mu2.

106b. SYMMETRY.  Let I be space inversion combined with particle exchange and
      charge conjugation.  Inversion reverses positions and velocities and
      leaves axial vectors alone; C reverses charges and moments.  A circular
      start is I-invariant exactly when mu1 = -mu2, at any tilt: that is
      ortho.  The forces then obey F1 = -F2 at every instant, so the total
      momentum stays zero, whatever the retarded sector does to each particle.
      Para has no such symmetry.  At tilt 90 only rotation by 180 degrees
      about the normal, again with exchange and C, protects para, and only the
      in-plane momentum.  (This replaced a weaker first rule, written before
      any tilt-45 or momentum-component run; both are in prereg_106.txt.)

106c. TRAJECTORIES, one orbit, retarded forces, reaction off.
        start   tilt   para beta_cm / r_min      ortho |P| / r_min
        1 r*    0      0.315  / 0.37 r*          0 / 1.00 r* (moves out)
        1 r*    45     |P| 1.54 mc / 0.22 r*     exactly 0 / 1.00 r*
        1 r*    90     P_z 19.8 mc, FAIL 0.03    0 / 1.00 r*
        0.5 r*  0      0.504  / 0.27 r*          exactly 0, FAIL at 0.158 r*
        0.5 r*  45     |P| 1.49 mc / 0.20 r*     exactly 0 / 0.50 r*
        0.5 r*  90     0.943  / 0.10 r*          0 / 0.50 r*
        0.35 r* 0      0.438  / 0.25 r*          FAIL at 0.13 r*
        0.35 r* 90     0.527  / 0.19 r*          0 / 0.35 r*
      Runs with momentum components (depth 26) give ortho P = 0.000e+00 in
      all three components, bit for bit, including the passage to 0.158 r*,
      deeper than para's 0.27 r* at the same start and tilt.  Para at
      tilt 90: in-plane P 4.7e-9 m c at step 64, 2.1e-7 at the end against
      P_z 19.8 m c, i.e. 1e-8 relative, the integration tolerance.  Rule 3'
      met.  Rule 1 (compare at matched depth, same tilt) decides nothing:
      ortho either leaves 1 r* or dives below every para periapsis.

106d. AWAY FROM EXACT ORTHO.  Start 0.5 r*, tilt 0, second moment turned by
      delta about y (0 = ortho, 180 = para).  |P| at step 64 (quarter orbit):
        delta   |P|/(m c)    share of para   r_min
        0       0            0               0.158 FAIL
        5       3.46e-3      0.35%           0.158 FAIL
        20      2.11e-2      2.1%            0.158 FAIL
        60      1.65e-1      17%             0.157 FAIL
        120     6.58e-1      67%             0.292
        180     9.83e-1      100%            0.270
      The imbalance returns at once and grows smoothly; at step 32 the ratio
      20/5 is 4.5, linear in delta, and 6.1 by step 64 as the deviation
      feeds back.  Rule 4: linear onset met; "ortho is protected only at a
      point" (share at 20 degrees above 10%) not met, 2.1%.  So the
      protection is a smooth suppression around exact ortho, not a knife edge.

106e. READING.  The non-reciprocal force of section 105 exists in both
      channels.  In exact ortho the two particles are I-mirror images, so
      their unbalanced parts cancel pairwise; in para they add.  Ortho also
      seldom gets there: from 1 r* and from 0.5 r* at tilts 45 and 90 the
      moments repel and the orbit leaves.  The labelled ortho band of
      production (cos >= 0.5 of the spins) reaches 60 degrees from exact
      ortho, where in this geometry the imbalance is already 17% of para's.

106f. NOT DONE.  Precession moves the moments away from mu1 = -mu2 only if
      something breaks I, and nothing here does; the angle scan sets the
      departure by hand.  The section 105 decomposition was not repeated,
      since no exact ortho run had momentum to split.  Engine failures below
      ~0.16 r* end several runs early.  Production checkpoints sit at 40 r*
      and beyond, where none of this acts (104f).

107. THE ENGINE FAILURES BELOW ~0.2 r*: A SUPERLUMINAL EXTRAPOLATION, A
     STENCIL SWITCH, AND A HISTORY TOO COARSE FOR THE PLUNGE
==================================================================

107a. QUESTION.  Section 106 left several runs ending in engine failures
      between 0.13 and 0.23 r*.  Why, and what can be fixed?

107b. THE ENGINE'S OWN DIAGNOSTICS (POSITRONIUM_DEBUG_DIPOLE,
      CREM_DEBUG_ORDER_LIVE) SPLIT THEM INTO TWO KINDS.
        run                     where      reason
        para 1 r* t90, depth 20  0.217 r*   accuracy: step-doubling error 2x
                                            per halving, then 1.6e6x lower
        para 1 r* t90, depth 26  0.030 r*   accuracy, same signature
        ortho 0.5 r* t0          0.158 r*   non-finite trial step
        ortho 0.35 r* t0         0.139 r*   non-finite trial step
      An error that halves with the step and then collapses is a force
      that jumps in time inside the step, not a lack of convergence.

107c. NON-FINITE: THE MAGNETIZATION TERM EXTRAPOLATES PAST c.
      - First lead, refuted: with reaction disabled the step still
        evaluated the Landau-Lifshitz self force, only for a validity ratio
        that disabled and stochasticElectricDipole never read; the NaN came
        through it.  Skipping it (rule H1) leaves every output identical
        and the step three times cheaper, but the failures stay: the trial
        forces themselves are NaN.
      - Cause (H2, both parts met): section 103 continues the source to the
        observation time with v + a lag, lag ~ r/c.  In the plunge a r/c is
        comparable to c; a debug build caught |v + a lag| = 1.06 c just
        before the first NaN, where the Lorentz factor of
        movingMagnetizationField is undefined.
      - Fix: continue the four-velocity, u = gamma v + (gamma a +
        gamma^3 v (v.a)/c^2) lag, v = u/sqrt(1+u^2/c^2).  Same slope at lag
        0, exact in uniform motion, never reaches c.  Both ortho runs pass
        their failure step (0.35 r* completes the orbit).

107d. ACCURACY: A STENCIL SWITCH IN THE MOMENT DERIVATIVES.
      tools/step_failure_jump.cpp replays the engine's recursion to the
      failing step (same dt 8.8389e-29 s, error 3.551e-8, 116 history nodes)
      and scans the step endpoint:
      - the moment force |G| jumps 0.2292 -> 0.3435 within 1e-12 of the step;
        the pinned history segment, the retarded segment and the material
        rate do not change;
      - of the six gradient probes, one (axis y-) changes its coupling U by
        1.7e-3; split by field, only the pole part moves; in the pole trace
        every quantity is equal except the sum of the two poles;
      - per pole: pole -1's moment derivatives jump, m' 6.67e-21 ->
        4.47e-21, m'' 42.96 -> 14.35, while its retarded time, velocity and
        acceleration are continuous.  historicalIntegratedDipoleKinematics
        switches from the central to the backward stencil when time + h
        passes the present: there present - t_pole = h = 1.582e-22 s.
      h is twice the mean history spacing.  appendStateHistory keeps
      max(1e-20 s, 4 r/c) with 128 nodes, so below ~3.9 r* h stops
      shrinking; h c = 0.245 r*.  Every retarded moment derivative whose
      source is about 0.25 r* away therefore sits on a hard switch.
      - Fix (H4, met): blend the two stencils with a C1 weight for present -
        t between h and 2h; unchanged outside that band and next to the
        history front.  Para 1 r* t90 depth 20 now runs to step 195 instead
        of 42.
      - Rejected (H3): dropping the 1e-20 s floor (h = r/16c) removed this
        jump but added others (a 0.7% jump in the charge field at 0.158 r*,
        NaN again at 0.35 r*) and changed trajectories at 1 r*.

107e. NOT FIXED: THE HISTORY IS TOO COARSE FOR THE DEEPEST PASSAGES.
      The failures that remain (ortho 0.35 r* at 0.179 r*, para t90 at
      0.115 r*) jump in the partner's CHARGE field: ortho's |E| steps
      3.44e17 -> 9.52e17 V/m exactly where the retarded time crosses
      history node 73/74.  The history is a C1 cubic: position and velocity
      continuous, acceleration not.  With the 1e-20 s floor the nodes are
      7.8e-23 s apart, while the passage lasts ~1.3e-22 s, so the cubic
      cannot follow the acceleration and its node jumps are of order one.
      A finer history is the remedy; the cheap version of it (107d, H3) broke
      other things, so this stays open.

107f. SECTION 106 RUNS ON THE FIXED CODE (one orbit; steps completed and
      the deepest separation reached, r*).
        run                        before            after
        para 1 r* t90, depth 20    42, 0.228         195, 0.120
        para 1 r* t90, depth 26    208, 0.033        207, 0.011
        ortho 0.5 r* t0            94, 0.158         full orbit, 0.124
        ortho 0.35 r* t0           38, 0.132         44, 0.132 (107e)
        ortho 0.5 r*, delta 5      94, 0.158         full orbit, 0.122
        ortho 0.5 r*, delta 20     92, 0.158         98, 0.118
        ortho 0.5 r*, delta 60     89, 0.157         100, 0.110
      Exact ortho keeps P = 0 bit for bit.  Section 106's numbers move
      slightly where the blend band is crossed: para 0.5 r* t0 |P| 1.189 ->
      1.194 m c.

107g. WHAT THE RUNS SHOW BEFORE THEY FAIL.  None of these trajectories is
      physical by the time the engine gives up: para t90 passes beta 0.98
      and +1322 |U_dd|; ortho 0.5 r*, which now completes, gains +71 |U_dd|
      in one passage to 0.124 r* and is thrown out to 11.9 r* with zero
      total momentum.  The ortho symmetry of 106 cancels the non-reciprocal
      force in momentum, not in energy.  The failures are the last symptom
      of the retarded dipole sector at short range (104-105), not the
      problem itself.

107h. PRODUCTION AND CHECKS.
      - Validation 62/62, header isolation 37/37.
      - Section 103 pairs (--spin-quantization), lab lifetimes: para
        identical in all four seeds; ortho identical for 70003 and 70012,
        -1e-9 ps for 70001 and -6.9e-5 ps (0.069 fs) for 70005.  The
        Landau-Lifshitz skip alone is bit-identical; the extrapolation
        alone moves 70005 by -0.53 fs.  HEAD reproduces the section 103
        values exactly.
      - One pair, seed 70001: 229 s on HEAD, 189 s fixed.
      - Engine drift grid of 103d: 0.209, 0.063, 0.115, 0.184 and 0.073
        |U_dd| as there (5 and 10 r* to every digit), 2.3-2.4x faster.
      - Near-barrier ledger probe (86f): tilt 90 unchanged; the small tilt 0
        values move within their size (1 r* para 1.2e-4 -> 5.8e-5, 1 r*
        ortho 7.7e-8 -> 6.5e-7).  That probe builds its history from one
        state, so it sits in the blend band.

107i. NOT DONE.  The node-crossing jumps of 107e.  The sibling stencils
      (historicalDipoleKinematics and the electric one) keep the hard switch;
      they are not on the force path measured here.  Why the history floor
      is 1e-20 s is not recorded anywhere; it was not changed.

108. THE COLLAPSE TIME IS NOW THE TRANSIT a_pair -> 0.005 a_pair, AND WHY
     THE QUANTIZED CHANNEL READS 199 ps INSTEAD OF 31 ps
==================================================================

108a. THE REQUEST.  Report as the collapse time the transit from the pair's
      ground-state separation to its minimal physical separation, so that it
      can be compared with the closed-form classical inspiral
      t = mu^2 c^3 r0^3/(4 e^4 k^2) [1-(r/r0)^3] = 31 ps for e+e-.

108b. THE PREMISE, CHECKED FIRST: THE CLOCK WAS ALREADY THAT.  Production
      prepares an exact circular orbit at a_pair (sharp preparation, --level
      1) and stops at the Compton barrier or the retardation margin, so the
      reported time already ran from a_pair to the bottom.  The end radius
      barely matters either: t scales as r^3, so moving the end from the
      retardation margin (~0.015 a0) to 0.01 a0 changes the total by ~1e-5 ps.
      The gap between 199 ps and 31 ps is therefore not the definition.

108c. WHAT IT IS, MEASURED.  Seeds 42, 43 and 44, default free draw:
        channel                                     lab time
        continuous reaction (individual LL)          30.686 ps
        closed form, a_pair -> 0.005 a_pair          31.123 ps
        quantized emission, total                    198.93-199.04 ps
          of which: before the first photon          186.67-186.79 ps
          of which: the cascade after it             12.24-12.26 ps
      The first photon carries hbar omega = 13.6047 eV = 2 |E0| at a_pair,
      and under the quantized channel the orbit does not move between
      photons, so the pair waits 2|E0|/P = 6 x |E0|/(3P) there -- six times
      the whole classical inspiral.  That factor is the emission model.
      (A probe added the emission lab time to LabFramePhoton for this; the
      field is not kept.)

108d. WHAT IS NOW REPORTED (the choice made).  The collapse time is the
      CONTINUOUS transit from a_pair to collisionBoundaryRadius = 0.005
      a_pair (0.01 a0 for e+e-, the pair-general form of the historical
      constant).  Implementation:
      - estimateCremCollapse takes the reaction model as a parameter; the old
        signature reads the command line as before.
      - runCremCollapseExperiment runs each trajectory a second time under
        individual Landau-Lifshitz and fills collapseTransitSeconds,
        collapseTransitTailSeconds, collapseTransitReferenceSeconds and
        collapseTransitOutcome.  The transit run stops where the engine's own
        limits stop it and the remainder to the boundary is added in closed
        form; that tail is ~1e-6 of the total.
      - The survival curve, the histogram and the theory panel of experiments
        1 and 2 now use the transit; the quantized lifetimeSecondsLab is
        reported beside them as the photon-cascade time.
      - gMeasureCollapseTransit (CREM_COLLAPSE_TRANSIT=0) switches the second
        run off; the seven tools that read only the cascade time set it false,
        so their cost is unchanged.

108e. VERIFIED.
      - Production, 4 trajectories, seed 42, budget 600 s, para and ortho
        alike (identical since audit 91): collapse time 30.6732 +- 0.0052 ps
        against the closed form 31.1235 ps, ratio 0.9855, sigma/mean 3.4e-4;
        photon-cascade time 198.985 +- 0.0197 ps.
      - Cascade times bit-identical to before: seed 70001 gives para
        198.974732528 ps and ortho 198.971579221 ps, as in section 107.
      - Validation 62/62, header isolation 37/37.
      - Cost: experiment 1 with 4 trajectories took 388 s against 190 s of
        cascade alone, i.e. the second run roughly doubles it.

108f. LIMITS.  The transit is not defined, and is reported as such, with
      --ground-state-floor (the floor holds the orbit at a_pair), with
      --level > 1 or --start-radius (the preparation is not at a_pair), or
      when the transit run is censored.  The measured transit sits 1.4% below
      the closed form; that gap is not explained here.  It is a SECOND run of
      the same seed, not the same trajectory as the quantized one, so the two
      times are not two clocks on one history.  Nothing in this section
      touches what the model does or does not contain: annihilation remains
      absent -- a missing class of mechanism, not a missing term -- and the
      124.49 ps p-Ps lifetime stays in the report as a scale reference only.

109. PHOTON EMISSION OFF IN PRODUCTION, AND HOW FAR THE SELF-PROPULSION
     REACHES: OUT TO ~5 r*, AND NOT INTO THE COLLAPSE TIME
==================================================================

109a. TWO REQUESTS.  Switch photon emission off in production; and check
      whether the self-propulsion of sections 104-106 reaches above
      0.01 a0 = 0.005 a_pair = 2.74 r* and whether it shows in the collapse
      time.

109b. EMISSION OFF.  gRadiationReactionModel now defaults to
      individualLandauLifshitz: the same E1 power leaves as a continuous
      drag instead of photons.  --radiation-reaction stochastic restores the
      cascade.  Consequences, measured on experiment 1, 4 trajectories,
      seed 42, budget 600 s:
      - The collapse time is unchanged, 30.6732 +- 0.0052 ps, because
        section 108 already measured it on a continuous run.
      - The photon-cascade line and the photon panels report nothing, and
        the cascade sample is only collected when a trajectory actually
        emitted (emittedPhotonCount > 0).
      - Cost falls from 388 s to 47 s: the quantized cascade is gone and the
        transit run is the production run itself.
      - The report now prints the closed-form tail as its own line.

109c. HOW FAR THE PROPULSION REACHES (rule A1: |P| > 1e-3 m c after one
      orbit).  Circular start, para, retarded forces, one orbit:
        r        tilt 0      tilt 90     |P|/(m c)
        2.74 r*  6.9e-3      1.8e-1 (*)
        5 r*     1.3e-3      1.7e-3
        10 r*    1.0e-4      1.3e-4
        20 r*    7.0e-6      8.0e-6
        50 r*    1.9e-7      1.9e-7
      So yes: it reaches above 0.01 a0, and the rule is met at 2.74 and
      5 r*.  The fall-off is steep, |P| per orbit ~ r^-3.9 between 5 and
      50 r* (rule A2, a description with no threshold).
      (*) At 2.74 r* with the moments in the orbital plane the circular
      orbit does not survive one turn: it plunges to 0.53 r*, gains
      +7.5 |U_dd| and carries 0.183 m c.  The boundary radius is already
      inside the range where the moment attraction rules the orbit.

109d. DOES IT SHOW IN THE COLLAPSE TIME (rule B: more than 3 sigma of the
      control, 0.016 ps, and more than 0.01 ps).  Experiment 1 as above:
        variant                                 mean collapse time
        control                                 30.6732 +- 0.0052 ps
        CREM_NO_DIPOLE_FORCE=1                  30.6657 +- 0.0037 ps
        CREM_DIPOLE_INSTANTANEOUS_BELOW=20      30.6732 +- 0.0052 ps
      Removing the dipole sector ENTIRELY moves the mean by 0.0075 ps, i.e.
      1.4 sigma and 2.4e-4 relative: rule B is not met.  Replacing the
      retarded dipole sector by its instantaneous form below 1 r* changes
      nothing at all.  So: no, the propulsion does not show in the collapse
      time.

109e. WHY NOT, AND WHERE THE INTEGRATION ACTUALLY ENDS.  With emission off
      every run stops on the retardation margin at a periapsis of 15.9 r*
      (ratio 146.8 against the threshold of 150), not at the boundary: the
      whole region below ~10 r* where the propulsion lives sits inside the
      CLOSED-FORM tail, which carries 0.00070 ps, 2.3e-5 of the total.  And
      t ~ a^3 puts almost all of the time at large radii, where the
      per-orbit momentum is 1e-5 m c and below.  The quantized channel
      reached 4.9 r* (section 108), still above the propulsion's own range.

109f. A CORRECTION TO SECTION 108.  Its text put the tail at ~1e-6 of the
      total from an assumed stop near 0.015 a0.  Measured, the stop is at
      15.9 r* = 0.029 a0 and the tail is 2.3e-5 of the total.  The report
      prints it, so this cannot drift again.

109g. LIMITS.  Measurement A is one orbit from a circular start at each
      radius, para only -- exact ortho carries no momentum at all by the
      symmetry of section 106.  Measurement B is 4 trajectories at one
      seed, all censored-free.  The tail is a closed form, so whatever the
      propulsion does between 15.9 r* and the boundary is excluded by
      construction rather than measured to be absent; with the cascade on
      that stretch is integrated down to 4.9 r*, and section 104f's switch
      is still what removes the sector for deep work.

110. THE COLLAPSE TIME WAS A PROPER TIME OF A DRIFTING FRAME; IT IS NOW A
     LAB TIME
==================================================================

110a. THE OBJECTION.  The pair accelerates itself (sections 104-106), so the
      collapse time of section 108 is measured on a clock that moves with it;
      at the speeds section 107 reached (beta_cm 0.99) the difference is
      enormous.  Correct it to lab time.

110b. THE OBJECTION IS RIGHT IN KIND.  The secular estimator reconstructs
      every checkpoint at zero total momentum, because osculating elements
      carry only the RELATIVE motion, so the drift the dipole sector adds was
      dropped from the clock entirely.  Its lab clock integrated gamma of the
      PHOTON recoil alone -- and with emission off (section 109) that is
      exactly zero, so the reported time was a proper time.

110c. HOW BIG IT IS, MEASURED.  One orbit of a circular start carries
      1.67e-5 m c at 16 r* (section 109), and the gain adds up over orbits
      (rule C: linear within a factor 2 over 16 orbits):
        r, tilt      1 orbit    16 orbits   ratio to linear
        16 r*, 0     1.67e-5    1.91e-4     0.71
        16 r*, 90    1.97e-5    8.19e-6     0.03  (the direction reverses)
        50 r*, 0     1.93e-7    2.30e-6     0.74
        50 r*, 90    1.86e-7    3.71e-6     1.25
      Summing the r^-3.9 law of section 109 coherently over the ~2e5 orbits
      of an inspiral gives an UPPER bound of |P| = 1.07e-2 m c, beta 5.3e-3
      at 16 r*, and a lab-time correction of 1.8e-8 ps (5.9e-10 relative).
      The measured drift is smaller, as the reversing case above implies.

110d. THE CORRECTION, AS IMPLEMENTED.  estimateCremCollapse now reads the
      momentum the measured orbit ends with (not background-subtracted: the
      imbalance sits in the retarded dipole sector, which the background
      carries too), multiplies it by the checkpoint's skipped orbits and
      accumulates it in dipoleDriftMomentum.  Each checkpoint's proper time
      is multiplied by gamma of that drift at the checkpoint MIDPOINT,
      factorized against the photon-recoil gammas (exact to O(beta^2
      beta_drift^2)).  collapseTransitSeconds is now built from
      lifetimeSecondsLab instead of lifetimeSeconds, and the closed-form tail
      carries the same gamma.  centreOfMassDriftBeta and
      collapseTransitDriftBeta report the drift.

110e. WHAT IT CHANGES.  Production, experiment 1, 4 trajectories, seed 42:
        frame drift at the stop      beta = 6.12e-4
        lab-time factor              gamma - 1 = 1.87e-7
        collapse time                30.6732 +- 0.0052 ps, unchanged
      Rule E is met: the correction is six orders below the printed
      precision, so the number stands as reported in sections 108-109 -- but
      it now stands as a LAB time rather than by assumption.
      Cascade times (quantized channel, seed 70001): para 198.974732577 ps
      against 198.974732528 before, i.e. +4.9e-8 ps (2.5e-10 relative);
      ORTHO IS BIT-IDENTICAL at 198.971579221 ps.  That is the symmetry of
      section 106 showing up in the clock: exact ortho carries no momentum,
      so it has no drift to correct.
      Validation 62/62.

110f. A CONSEQUENCE OF SECTION 109 FOUND HERE.  The seven tools that measure
      the photon cascade read the default reaction model, which is no longer
      the quantized one, so they silently measured the continuous inspiral
      instead (para_ortho_lab_pairs printed 30.67 ps where section 107 has
      198.97).  They now select stochasticElectricDipole explicitly.

110g. LIMITS.  The drift is carried on the CLOCK only; it is not fed back
      into the dynamics, because the checkpoint's own elements have no place
      to put it.  The factorization of the two gammas is exact only to
      O(beta^2 beta_drift^2), which at these speeds is below 1e-10.  Below
      ~1 r* the same drift reaches beta_cm 0.99 (section 107) and would
      dominate the clock, but production stops at 15.9 r*.  The accumulated
      momentum is a partial cancellation of rotating per-orbit kicks,
      measured here rather than assumed, and only at two radii.

111. THE 2 GAMMA / 3 GAMMA KNIFE EDGE IS NO LONGER THE p-Ps / o-Ps
     DIVISION, AND SINCE AUDIT 91 IT IS NOT A KNIFE EDGE EITHER
==================================================================

111a. THE QUESTION.  Is the split into two or three photons now equivalent
      to the split into p-Ps and o-Ps?

111b. WHAT THE CODE DOES.  The multiplicity is decided by the two-photon
      weight w = |mu1+mu2|^2/(|mu1|+|mu2|)^2 = (1+cos)/2 of the PREPARED
      moments, drawn as a branching ratio, not thresholded.  The channel
      LABEL is the knife edge cos >= 0.5 on the same moments.  Two different
      functions of one number.

111c. MEASURED, 4000 prepared states, the production ensemble.
        quantity                                    value
        cos: mean / median                          +0.011 / +0.025
        label cos >= 0.5 (p-Ps)                     25.0%
        P(2 gamma) over the ensemble = E[w]         0.506
        P(2 gamma | labelled p-Ps)                  0.878
        P(2 gamma | labelled o-Ps)                  0.383
        |cos| < 0.5, i.e. neither label's interior  50.4%, 2 gamma 0.515
      The pre-registered rule for equivalence (0.99 and 0.01) is missed by
      far in both directions.  Phenomenon 1 and 2 give identical numbers,
      as audit 91 requires.  The analytic prediction for cos uniform on
      [-1,1] -- 0.25, 0.5, 0.875, 0.375 -- is reproduced, so this is the
      free draw's own arithmetic, not a sampling accident.

111d. SO THE ANSWER IS NO, AND THE KNIFE EDGE WENT WITH IT.  Equivalence
      held exactly under the withdrawn imposition: with --spin-quantization
      the prepared cos is +-1 to machine precision, w is exactly 1 or 0, and
      the measurement gives P(2 gamma | p-Ps) = 1.000 over 4000 states and
      P(2 gamma | o-Ps) = 0.000.  That was the knife edge: one number that
      was only ever allowed two values.  With the angle free, the same
      formula becomes a continuous branching ratio, and a labelled o-Ps
      trajectory emits two photons 38% of the time.

111e. WHERE IT LEAVES THE MODEL AGAINST MEASUREMENT.  Spin statistics give
      P(2 gamma) = 1/4: one singlet state out of four.  The model's free
      ensemble gives 0.506, i.e. twice too many two-photon events, because
      an isotropic mutual angle makes E[(1+cos)/2] = 1/2 identically.  The
      ends of the interpolation are forced by the algebra of audit 91
      (aligned moments give w = 1 and zero total spin, anti-aligned w = 0
      and the full hbar); what is not forced, and now visibly wrong by a
      factor of two, is the MEASURE over the angle.  Nothing in the
      classical model selects the 1:3 weighting that spin statistics give.

111f. NOT DONE.  Whether some other measure over the mutual angle -- rather
      than another interpolation between the two forced ends -- would give
      1:3 is not investigated here.  The terminal-moment variant
      (CREM_CHANNEL_AT_ANNIHILATION) was not remeasured; the prepared
      reading remains the default for the reason recorded at it.  The stale
      comment that called the channel deterministic "by default" has been
      corrected.

112. WOULD ANOTHER MEASURE OVER THE MUTUAL ANGLE GIVE 1:3?  ONLY ONES THE
     MODEL DOES NOT PRODUCE
==================================================================

112a. THE QUESTION (from section 111e).  The weight w = (1+cos)/2 is fixed by
      the two forced ends, so the branching ratio is a property of the MEASURE
      over the mutual angle.  Is there a measure that gives 1:3, and does the
      model produce one?

112b. THE CONDITION IS ONE MOMENT.  E[w] = (1+<cos>)/2, so
      E[w] = 1/4 <=> <cos> = -1/2 exactly, and nothing else about the measure
      matters.  Measures that satisfy it:
        two points cos = +-1 with weights 1:3   the withdrawn quantization of
                                                audit 91 plus the imported
                                                statistical weights
        p ~ (1-cos)^2                           E[w] = 1/4 exactly; in spin
                                                variables that is |S_total|^4
        p ~ exp(-1.7965 cos)                    Langevin: coth(l)-1/l = 1/2
      The exponential one names the scale it needs: with the model's own
      U_dd(a_pair) = 4.539e-05 eV, kT would have to be 2.526e-05 eV = 0.29 K,
      i.e. 0.56 of the coupling itself and 3.7e-06 of the binding.

112c. WHAT THE MODEL ACTUALLY PRODUCES.  40 trajectories, seed base 42,
      continuous reaction, terminal weights read with
      CREM_CHANNEL_AT_ANNIHILATION:
        measure                                        <cos>     E[w]
        M1 preparation (isotropic by construction)     -0.059    0.470
           (4000 draws in section 111c)                +0.011    0.506
        M2 terminal, produced by the dynamics          -0.0012   0.4994
        M3 the same, weighted by the collapse time                0.4993
           the same, weighted by 1/t                              0.4994
        M4 Boltzmann on U_dd(a_pair), aligning sign               0.657
           the same, anti-aligning sign                           0.343
           Boltzmann at kT = binding, or at hbar omega_orb        0.5000
        M5 weighted by the coherent M1 rate, p ~ (1+cos)          2/3
        M6 weighted by the resultant spin, p ~ (1-cos)            1/3
      None is inside the pre-registered window [0.24, 0.26].
      M2 is the interesting one: the dynamics does move each angle (the
      terminal cosine regresses on the prepared one with slope 0.81, mean
      |cos| 0.49 -> 0.46, individual trajectories move by up to 0.3), but it
      COMPRESSES the distribution without tilting it -- the mean stays at
      zero to three decimals, which is why the ratio does not budge.  M3 has
      no power to tilt anything either: the 40 collapse times span
      30.62-30.69 ps, a spread of 3.6e-04.

112d. READING.  The measures the model has BRACKET the answer -- 1/3 from the
      resultant spin, 1/2 isotropic, 2/3 from the M1 rate -- and none reaches
      1/4.  Getting there needs a net anti-alignment of <cos> = -1/2, i.e. a
      50% polarization of the mutual angle, while the only angle-dependent
      energy in the model is 7e-06 of the binding and, at the production
      geometry (moments along the orbital normal, U = -2 (mu0/4pi) mu^2
      cos/r^3), tilts toward ALIGNMENT, i.e. the wrong way.  So the answer to
      the question is: yes such measures exist, no the model does not supply
      one, and the two that work are exactly the two imports this project
      has been removing -- quantization of the angle with the 1:3 weights, or
      a thermal reservoir at 0.29 K.

112e. LIMITS.  M2 and M3 are 40 trajectories at one seed base and one
      preparation radius.  The terminal weight comes from
      CREM_CHANNEL_AT_ANNIHILATION, which the code warns against reading as a
      prediction (it carries the singlet-drift defect); it is used here as a
      MEASURE, which is what the question asks about.  Three one-parameter
      families were tried, chosen because they are the only angle-dependent
      rates or energies the model has; this does not exclude some other
      derivation, and 112b says exactly what any candidate has to satisfy.

113. THE TERMINAL-MOMENT VARIANTS, REPEATED: ORTHO REPRODUCES EXACTLY, PARA
     DRIFTS LESS THAN IT DID, AND PHOTONS TILT THE ANGLE DOWN
==================================================================

113a. WHAT WAS REPEATED.  CREM_CHANNEL_AT_ANNIHILATION reads the two-photon
      weight from the moments the trajectory ENDS with instead of the ones it
      was prepared with.  README's record of it ("Kanal wybiera teraz
      dynamika") has para's terminal weight spread over 0.43..0.94 with 8/12
      = 67% two-photon emissions, and ortho at exactly 0 with 0/12.  Those
      numbers predate audit 91 (which withdrew the imposed angle), audits
      96-107 (the clock and field fixes) and audit 109 (which switched the
      default channel off the photon cascade), so the configuration they were
      taken in has to be restored explicitly: imposed angle plus the
      stochastic channel.  Twelve trajectories each, seed base 42, budget
      600 s.

113b. RESULT.
        configuration              terminal weight        emitted 2 gamma
        A1 imposed para, photons   0.576..0.997 (0.845)   11/12 = 92%
        A2 imposed ortho, photons  0.0000 exactly         0/12  = 0%
        B  free draw, photons      0.074..0.763 (0.385)   5/12
        C  free draw, continuous   0.187..0.793 (0.471)   5/12
        D1 imposed para, cont.     0.815..0.982 (0.922)   8/12 = 67%
        D2 imposed ortho, cont.    0.0000 exactly         0/11  = 0%
      (D2's twelfth trajectory ended in a NumericalFailure and carries no
      terminal state; the other eleven are exact.)

113c. AGAINST THE RECORD.  Ortho reproduces exactly, in both channels and
      without a single exception: the anti-aligned configuration is a
      symmetry (section 106), not a fixed point the dynamics has to find.
      Para does NOT reproduce.  Its terminal weight now spans 0.576..0.997
      with the photon cascade against the recorded 0.43..0.94, i.e. the
      singlet drift is SMALLER and the mean weight is 0.845 against 0.758,
      and the emitted share is 92% against 67% -- outside the pre-registered
      67% +- 14% window.  The old number is not reproducible on today's code;
      the fixes between then and now (the magnetization centring of audit
      103 above all, which was pumping the dipole sector) left the aligned
      configuration closer to where it was prepared.

113d. WHAT THE REPEAT ADDS: THE EMISSION CHANNEL TILTS THE MEASURE.  On the
      SAME twelve seeds and the same prepared angles (mean weight 0.4414):
        continuous reaction   terminal mean 0.4709   shift +0.0295
        photon cascade        terminal mean 0.3851   shift -0.0563
      and with the imposed para angle the drift away from weight 1 is 0.078
      continuous against 0.155 with photons.  So the quantized channel pushes
      the mutual angle toward ANTI-alignment, i.e. toward three photons,
      while the continuous drag pushes the other way.  That is a measure the
      model produces on its own and it moves in the direction section 112
      needs -- but by 0.06, where 1:3 needs the mean weight at 0.25.

113e. LIMITS.  Twelve trajectories per configuration, one seed base; the
      free-draw sample happened to contain no prepared para label at all
      (0/12, which has probability 0.75^12 = 3%), so B and C say nothing
      about the label agreement.  The terminal reading remains what the code
      warns it is: it carries the model's singlet drift, so it is a
      measurement of that drift and of the measure it produces, not a
      branching-ratio prediction.  The +0.03/-0.06 tilt is 12 trajectories
      wide and was not tested for convergence in the step or the budget.

114. IS THE MOMENT'S PROJECTION ON THE PARTNER'S FIELD QUANTIZED?  NO -- IT
     IS NOT EVEN CONSERVED
==================================================================

114a. THE QUESTION.  Each particle sits in the net magnetic field of the
      other.  Is the projection of its spin magnetic moment on that axis
      quantized?

114b. WHAT THE MODEL CONTAINS.  The moment is a classical vector of FIXED
      LENGTH -- the measured moment, the one place an hbar enters the moment
      sector -- rotated by Thomas-BMT about the axis
      thomasBmtEffectiveField(v, partner's field, g).  A rotation preserves
      the length and, for a fixed axis, the projection on it; neither
      operation has a discrete spectrum.  So the prediction is "continuous",
      and the two measurements below are what could have refuted it.

114c. THE ENSEMBLE (400 prepared production states, the model's own axis).
        cos(mu1, axis): mean -0.013, median -0.045, min -0.997, max +0.999
        deciles:        33 46 48 38 44 42 32 38 38 41
        within 0.05 of +-1: 4.2%   (a uniform cosine gives 5%; a quantized
                                    projection would give ~100%)
      The spin projection S1.axis it implies has mean -0.007 hbar and fills
      the interval; the quantum answer for a spin-1/2 in a field is exactly
      +-1/2.  (The pre-registered window for this line said "10% +- 5%",
      which was arithmetic on my part: the band 0.05 around +-1 covers 5% of
      a uniform cosine, and 4.2% is that.  The verdict does not turn on it.)
      With --spin-quantization the projection is still spread (8.0% in the
      band, deciles 28..58): that flag imposes the MUTUAL angle between the
      two moments, not either one's projection on the field.

114d. ALONG A TRAJECTORY it is not even an adiabatic invariant.  One full
      collapse, seed 42, 1969 transport substeps traced
      (CREM_DEBUG_PROJECTION):
        cos(mu1, omega1): start +0.885, end +0.753, min -0.419, max +0.901,
                          41 changes of direction, 960 steps up / 1008 down
        cos(mu2, omega2): start +0.424, end +0.047, min -0.901
        cos(mu1, Lhat):   start +0.946, end +0.849, min -0.386, max +1.000
        |omega1| grows by 1e4 from a_pair to the stop
        time spent within 0.05 of +-1: 0.0% of 1969 substeps
      So the projection librates over most of its range.  The reason is that
      the axis is not fixed: it is the orbit-averaged BMT rate, which turns
      with the apsidal precession and the orbital normal while its magnitude
      grows by four decades as the orbit shrinks.  The spin projection in
      hbar runs 0.443 -> 0.376 continuously.

114e. THE ANSWER.  No.  Nothing in the model quantizes that projection, and
      the dynamics does not select the two values either -- it avoids them:
      in 1969 substeps the trajectory never came within 0.05 of +-1.  The
      model's only discrete inputs in this sector are the LENGTH of each
      moment and the starting radius where L = hbar; a projection is neither.
      This is the same boundary audit 91 drew for the mutual angle, now drawn
      for the single-particle projection: it would have to be imposed, and it
      is not.

114f. LIMITS.  One trajectory for the transport history, 400 states for the
      ensemble, one preparation radius, positronium only.  The axis traced is
      the ORBIT-AVERAGED rate the secular layer precesses about; the resolved
      mechanical integrator uses the instantaneous field, whose direction
      moves 3e4 times faster than the precession, so the projection on THAT
      axis is a different (and even less conserved) quantity, not measured
      here.

115. WHAT SHOULD BE QUANTIZED, CHECKED: THE PAIR'S SPIN NORM CANNOT REACH
     THE TRIPLET, AND THE LIBRATION IS A FACTOR 3 FROM THE SPLITTING
==================================================================

115a. WHY THESE TWO.  Section 114 ruled out the single-particle projection on
      the partner's field: in a two-body problem each particle sees a
      different field (|B1|/|B2| = 0.617) and that axis turns 1e4 times
      faster than the precession, so it carries no invariant.  What is
      axis-independent is the PAIR's total spin, and what could carry a
      discrete spectrum out of the model's own dynamics is the libration of
      the mutual angle.  R1 and R2 test exactly those.

115b. R1, THE SPIN-NORM BUDGET.  400 prepared production states:
        |S_i|/hbar             0.500000016 .. 0.500003328
        |S1+S2|/hbar, free     0.060 .. 0.997, mean 0.679
        |S1+S2|/hbar, imposed  0.000000 (para) and 1.000000 (ortho)
        identity residual      1.3e-05 worst
        <|S|^2>/hbar^2, free   0.5135
      The single-particle norm is hbar/2, as it must be from mu/gamma, to
      3.3e-06; the residue is the lab moment's own boost, beta^2/2 = 6.7e-06
      at a_pair, and the same boost is what leaves the identity
      (|mu1+mu2|/2mu)^2 + (|S|/hbar)^2 = 1 at 1.3e-05 rather than at 1e-12,
      which is what the rule asked for.  Along the trajectory (115c's trace)
      |S1+S2|/hbar stays inside 0.367..0.661.
      THE DECISIVE NUMBER: the model's ceiling is |S1+S2| = hbar exactly,
      reached only at the anti-aligned configuration, while the quantum
      triplet needs sqrt(2) hbar = 1.414 hbar.  The quantum ENSEMBLE average
      <|S|^2> = 1.5 hbar^2 is therefore above the model's own MAXIMUM of
      1.0 hbar^2.  No measure over the classical mutual angle can carry the
      quantum spin content: a measure tuned to give 1:3 needs
      <|S|^2> = 0.75 hbar^2 (section 112), which is representable, but it
      then sits at half the quantum value.  So 1:3 obtained that way would be
      one matched moment of the distribution, not the spin algebra, and the
      search for it through the measure is closed.

115c. R2, THE LIBRATION AS A CANDIDATE DOUBLET.  One full collapse, seed 42,
      1969 transport substeps covering all 30.686 ps, L from 1.000 to
      0.170 hbar.  The mutual angle turns 61 times, so the libration is
