      there; its frequency, from consecutive extrema:
        at the prepared state (L = 1 hbar)      68.0 GHz
        median over the inspiral                5.1e4 GHz
        at the end (L = 0.17 hbar)              5.1e6 GHz
      Against the measured o-Ps/p-Ps splitting of 203.3941 GHz the prepared
      value is a factor 3.0 low: rule R2a (within a factor 2) is missed, rule
      R2b (same order, within 10) is met.
      Two things are worth separating here.  The libration beats the STATIC
      dipolar term by six: |U_dd|/h at a_pair is 11.0 GHz, the libration is
      68.0 GHz, because the rate difference that drives it contains the
      charge-field (spin-orbit) part of omega as well as the mutual dipole
      term -- |omega1-omega2| = 1.80e11 rad/s = 28.6 GHz at the start.
      But the frequency is NOT a property of the state: it sweeps five orders
      of magnitude as the orbit shrinks, so reading it as a level splitting
      requires naming a radius, and a hyperfine splitting may not depend on
      one.  That is the objection to this route, and it is structural rather
      than numerical.

115d. WHAT THIS RECOMMENDS NEXT (not done here).  R1 closes the 1:3-through-
      the-measure line.  R2 leaves one testable statement: if the libration
      is to be the doublet, its frequency has to be evaluated where the pair
      actually annihilates rather than where it is prepared, and the
      resulting spread has to be compared with the splitting's own width.
      The action of the libration was not computed -- the trace carries the
      angle but not its conjugate azimuth -- so the semiclassical
      quantization itself is still untested.

115e. LIMITS.  R1 is 400 prepared states and one trajectory, positronium
      only; the 3e-06 and 1.3e-05 residues are the covariant-moment boost,
      not slack in the identity.  R2 is ONE trajectory: 61 extrema is enough
      to name a frequency at the two ends but not to give it an uncertainty,
      and the chirp means the median over the inspiral is a summary of a
      sweep, not a scale.  The comparison uses the measured splitting as a
      number, not as a prediction of this model.

116. WHERE THE QUANTUM DISAGREEMENT COMES FROM: THE TENSOR TERM ON A
     NON-SPHERICAL ORBIT.  MY OWN PREDICTION WAS WRONG
==================================================================

116a. TWO CHECKS, ONE ROOT PROPOSED.  Section 115 left two quantitative
      disagreements with a findable cause: the spin-spin coupling is an order
      of magnitude below the hyperfine splitting, and |S1+S2| drifts where
      quantum mechanics conserves it for an S state.  The proposal was that
      both come from the model's ground state being a circular L = hbar
      orbit rather than an S state.

116b. (b) WHY |S| DRIFTS.  |S1+S2| is conserved exactly when omega1 = omega2,
      since d|S|^2/dt = 2(S1+S2).(omega1 x S1 + omega2 x S2).  A new
      diagnostic switch, CREM_SPIN_RATE_SECTOR, keeps only one source in the
      precession rate.  One full collapse each, seed 42:
        sector kept                |S| start -> end   drift  range  |w1-w2|/|w1|
        both (control)             0.6525 -> 0.5146   0.138  0.294  0.386
        the partner's CHARGE field 0.6525 -> 0.6525   0.000  0.000  0.000
        the partner's DIPOLE field 0.6525 -> 0.4682   0.184  0.679  0.256
      MY PREDICTION WAS WRONG, and cleanly so.  I expected the spin-orbit
      (charge-field) sector to be the cause; it is the one sector that
      conserves |S| EXACTLY.  With only the charge field the two rates are
      equal to every printed digit, the mutual angle does not move at all
      (cos stays +0.1486 over the whole inspiral), and |S| is frozen: the
      spin-orbit sector rotates BOTH moments the same way, so it cannot
      change the angle between them.  The mutual dipole field is what makes
      the rates differ, and it alone reproduces -- in fact exceeds -- the
      control's drift.  Rule b2, not b1.

116c. AND THAT IS EXACTLY THE TERM QUANTUM MECHANICS REMOVES.  The
      dipole-dipole interaction splits into a contact part, which commutes
      with S^2, and an anisotropic TENSOR part, which does not.  In the
      physical ground state the tensor part averages to zero over a spherical
      S state, leaving the contact term, and S^2 is a good quantum number.
      On this model's L = hbar orbit the pair sits at one separation in one
      direction, so the tensor part survives at full strength and drives
      precisely the drift measured above.  The root is therefore not the
      spin-orbit coupling that an L != 0 state brings in, but the tensor term
      that an L = 0 state would have averaged away.

116d. (a) HOW BIG THE MODEL'S OWN SPIN-SPIN COUPLING IS.  Its analogue of the
      splitting is U(aligned) - U(anti-aligned) from azimuthAveragedDipoleEnergy
      (contact term included), at fixed semi-major axis a_pair, against the
      angular momentum:
        L/hbar   periapsis     at periapsis      orbit-averaged [GHz]
        1.00     547.5 r*            21.9              21.9
        0.50      73.4 r*          9 120             175.6
        0.30      25.2 r*        223 128             810.1
        0.10       2.74 r*     97 795 934          15 646.6
        0.03       0.246 r*  6 590 226 992          63 182.4
        0.01       0.027 r*  7 953 185 690          53 667.9
      Rule a1 is met with room to spare: at L = 0.1 hbar the orbit-averaged
      coupling is 15 647 GHz, seventy-seven times the whole measured
      splitting.  The measured 203.3941 GHz corresponds to L = 0.476 hbar and
      the contact part alone (116.8 GHz) to L = 0.573 hbar.
      A correction to what I said before the measurement: I suspected the
      report's dipoleCouplingHz of omitting the model's own contact term.  It
      does use the bare tensor form, but at a_pair the two agree to 1.0000 --
      the contact term is negligible at 547 r*, so the reported 10.98 GHz
      (half the splitting analogue, 21.9 GHz) stands.

116e. WHAT THIS SAYS TOGETHER.  The model's spin-spin scale is a property of
      the ORBIT, sweeping four orders of magnitude between L = hbar and
      L = 0.03 hbar, while the physical splitting is a property of the STATE.
      Nothing in the model picks the L that would reproduce it, and the same
      tensor term that would have to be tuned is the one destroying |S| along
      the way.  Both disagreements are the same missing feature: a spherical
      ground state with density at contact.

116f. WHAT TO MEASURE NEXT (named, not done).  Split the model's own coupling
      into its contact and tensor parts and follow both against L: the
      question is whether any orbit makes the contact part reach 116.8 GHz
      while the tensor part averages small enough to leave |S| nearly
      conserved.  If none does, the model cannot carry the hyperfine
      structure at all, which is a sharper statement than the factor of ten.

116g. LIMITS.  (b) is one trajectory per sector; the ablation changes the
      precession and the moment derivatives that chargeDipoleForces reads, so
      the three runs are not the same trajectory (they end at L = 0.170,
      0.170 and 0.180 hbar).  The exact equality of the two rates in the
      charge-only run is a property of this symmetric preparation and was not
      tested for a drifting pair.  (a) is analytic in the orbit average: a
      Kepler ellipse at fixed a with the model's own softened energy, not an
      integrated trajectory, and the 1/r^3 divergence is cut only by the
      moment radius 0.9668 r*.

117. THE MODEL CANNOT CARRY HYPERFINE STRUCTURE: ITS SCALE AND ITS
     ANISOTROPY ARE THE SAME NUMBER
==================================================================

117a. THE QUESTION (from section 116f).  Split the model's own spin-spin
      coupling into the part that commutes with S^2 and the part that does
      not, follow both against the orbit's angular momentum, and ask whether
      any orbit has the first at the contact value of 116.8 GHz while the
      second is small enough to leave |S| nearly conserved.

117b. THE SPLIT.  After the azimuth average the model's energy is
      U = a(r) (mu1.mu2) + b(r) (mu1.n)(mu2.n).  The first term is the
      isotropic one -- the model's contact/Fermi analogue, which commutes
      with S^2 -- and the second is the tensor term, which does not.  Both
      are read off azimuthAveragedDipoleEnergy itself by evaluating it on two
      moment configurations (both moments in the orbital plane isolates a;
      both along the normal gives a+b), not by copying its formula.

117c. THE SCAN, orbit-averaged, at fixed semi-major axis a_pair, in GHz.
        L/hbar   periapsis    isotropic      tensor     |b/a|
        1.00     547.5 r*        10.975      32.924     3.000
        0.70     156.5 r*        32.004      96.002     3.000
        0.50      73.4 r*        87.841     263.413     2.999
        0.30      25.2 r*       407.281    1217.411     2.989
        0.20      11.1 r*      1382.762    4071.046     2.944
        0.10       2.74 r*    11888.937   27535.564     2.316
        0.05       0.685 r*   70675.938   57704.919     0.816
        0.03       0.246 r*   87105.121   47733.503     0.548
        0.01       0.027 r*   73508.228   42823.174     0.583
      The isotropic part reaches 116.8 GHz at L = 0.4547 hbar, and THERE the
      tensor part is 350.2 GHz -- ratio 3.00.  The ratio is 3 exactly
      wherever the orbit stays outside the moment radius, which is the
      multipole algebra rather than a coincidence, and it only falls below 1
      inside it, where the isotropic part has run up to 7e4 GHz.

117d. THE ABLATION, one full collapse each (CREM_SPIN_RATE_SECTOR).
        sector kept in the precession    |S| drift   |w1-w2|/|w1|
        both (control)                     0.1378       0.386
        the partner's charge field         0.0000       0.000
        its dipole field, whole            0.1843       0.256
        its dipole field, ISOTROPIC part   0.0007       0.000
        its dipole field, TENSOR part      0.2987       0.379
      Rule R117b is met: the isotropic part alone conserves |S| to 7e-4 over
      a whole inspiral, exactly as the quantum contact term does.  The tensor
      part alone is the whole drift and more.

117e. THE ANSWER: NO SUCH ORBIT EXISTS.  Rule R117a asked for an orbit with
      the isotropic part within 20% of 116.8 GHz and |b/a| <= 0.2.  At the
      radius where the magnitude is right the ratio is 3.00, fifteen times
      the bound; at the radius where the ratio finally drops to 0.55 the
      isotropic part is 87105 GHz, 746 times the target.  The two conditions
      move in opposite directions and there is no crossing.
      So this is not a factor-of-ten gap that a better constant could close.
      The model's spin-spin scale and its anisotropy are the SAME quantity
      evaluated at the same radius, locked at a ratio of 3 by the multipole
      structure of a classical dipole field.  Quantum mechanics separates
      them by the state: a spherical S state averages the tensor term to zero
      and leaves a contact term fixed by |psi(0)|^2.  A classical orbit has
      no averaging that does this, because its own anisotropy IS the source
      of the coupling.

117f. WHAT THIS CLOSES AND WHAT IT LEAVES.  Closed: the hyperfine doublet, on
      the same footing as the 1:3 branching of section 115 -- both fail for a
      structural reason, not for a missing term, and both failures are the
      absence of a spherical ground state with density at contact.  Left
      open: the model's own contact term is not useless -- it is what makes
      the isotropic sector conserve |S| to 7e-4 -- so a version of the model
      whose ground state sampled r -> 0 isotropically would have the right
      structure; whether such a state can be built classically with L = 0 and
      a finite inspiral time is a separate question this section does not
      touch.

117g. LIMITS.  The scan is analytic on a Kepler ellipse at fixed semi-major
      axis with the model's own softened energy, not an integrated
      trajectory; the ablations are one trajectory each and use the STATIC
      Plummer split of the partner's field in place of the retarded one, an
      approximation worth ~1e-5 at these speeds.  Both ablations change the
      moment derivatives the mixed magnetic forces read, so the five runs are
      not the same trajectory.  Positronium only.

118. THE S-STATE ANALOGUE, TESTED: THE GEOMETRY WORKS, THE SCALE IS 1238x
     OFF, AND THE STATE DOES NOT SURVIVE ITS FIRST PASSAGE
==================================================================

118a. THE QUESTION (left open by 117f).  Section 117 closed the hyperfine
      structure because the model's coupling and its anisotropy are the same
      number.  The escape it named was a ground state that samples r -> 0
      ISOTROPICALLY, where the tensor term averages away and only the contact
      term survives.  Can that be built classically -- L = 0, isotropic, with
      a finite fall time?

118b. P1, THE GEOMETRY: YES.  Averaged over an isotropic ensemble of orbit
      directions, the tensor structure 3(mu1.n)(mu2.n) - mu1.mu2 vanishes: by
      quadrature it is 5.0e-07 of mu1.mu2 (exactly zero analytically; a
      100000-draw Monte Carlo would only show it to 3.2e-03).  This is the
      one part that works, and it is what a spherical state does that a
      single orbit's azimuth average cannot: audit 117's |b/a| = 3 came from
      averaging around ONE normal, and an isotropic ensemble of normals
      removes it entirely.

118c. P2, THE SCALE: NO, BY THREE ORDERS.  Time-averaged over a radial
      (L = 0) Kepler orbit of semi-major axis a_pair, the model's isotropic
      coupling is 144548 GHz against the 116.8 GHz contact term -- 1238 times
      too large -- because a radial orbit passes through the core, where the
      coupling saturates at 7.97e+09 GHz rather than at a quantum |psi(0)|^2.
      Stated as a length: the moment radius that would put the model's
      contact energy on the Fermi term is 8.74e-11 m = 452 r* = 0.826 a_pair,
      against the 0.9668 r* the model derives from the current loop that
      carries mu at speeds below c (audit 86).  The two differ by 468.  And
      0.83 a_pair is not an adjustable constant: the model DERIVES a_pair
      from the same moments (pairBohrRadius), so a source region that size
      would be the whole orbit.

118d. P3, THE STATE: NO, IT DESTROYS ITSELF.  The radial orbit's free fall to
      the core takes 0.0537 fs, 5.7e+05 times shorter than the 30.67 ps
      collapse time -- that part of "finite fall time" is satisfied.  What is
      not is survival: integrating the model's own E1 power along ONE radial
      passage, with the Coulomb force softened at the same separation floor
      the trajectory uses (0.05 r*), gives 4.91e+04 eV radiated against a
      binding of 6.80 eV, i.e. 7221 times the binding.  The number is an
      upper bound rather than a prediction -- an orbit that radiates
      thousands of times its own binding does not complete the passage it was
      computed on -- and that is exactly the point: a classical L = 0 pair is
      not a state, it is a collision.  The pre-registered rule asked for ten
      core passages; the model does not reach one.

118e. THE ANSWER.  No.  The isotropic L = 0 ensemble has the right symmetry
      and nothing else: its coupling is three orders too strong because a
      classical orbit at the origin has no |psi(0)|^2 to cut it off, only a
      softening length, and the softening length that would work is the size
      of the atom; and the state radiates itself apart before completing one
      fall.  Section 117's closure therefore stands without the escape: the
      hyperfine structure is not reachable by choosing a better orbit.

118f. WHAT WOULD HAVE TO CHANGE.  A stationary, non-radiating, spherically
      symmetric distribution with density at contact is a WAVE FUNCTION, not
      an orbit: it is stationary because it has no accelerating charge to
      radiate, which is the one property a classical trajectory cannot have
      while sampling r -> 0.  That is a missing class of object, in the same
      sense as the annihilation channel of section 108 -- named here so it is
      not looked for again as a missing term.

118g. LIMITS.  P2 and P3 are analytic on the Kepler radial orbit with the
      model's own softened energy and its own separation floor, not
      integrated trajectories: the engine cannot follow a radial passage at
      all (sections 104-107 show it failing below ~0.2 r* on far gentler
      orbits), which is itself part of the answer but was not measured here.
      P3's radiated energy uses the E1 power of the unperturbed orbit, valid
      only while the loss is small -- it is not, by 7221, which is how the
      inconsistency shows.  Positronium only, one semi-major axis.

119. HIDDEN MOMENTUM DOES NOT CLOSE THE BALANCE: IT IS 5-25% OF THE GAP AND
     AT SHORT RANGE IT MAKES IT WORSE
==================================================================

119a. THE HYPOTHESIS.  A magnetic moment in an electric field carries hidden
      momentum (mu x E)/c^2, and section 105 measured that 88% of the pair's
      unbalanced force is the moment sitting in the partner's retarded charge
      field -- the same configuration.  If the model's momentum ledger were
      simply missing that term, the "self-propulsion" of sections 104-106
      would be bookkeeping rather than a broken reaction.

119b. MEASURED.  The trajectory of sections 104-106 (para, retarded forces,
      one orbit) with the hidden momentum of BOTH particles accumulated
      alongside the mechanical one:
        start                 |P_mech| 0        |P_hidden| 0.0285 m c
        1 r*, one orbit       |dP_mech| 0.5992  with hidden 0.4986  (1.20x)
                              charge field only          0.5093  (1.18x)
                              |P_hidden| at the end      0.1518
        0.5 r*, one orbit     |dP_mech| 1.1939  with hidden 1.4168  (0.84x)
                              charge field only          1.4356  (0.83x)
      Rule R119a asked for a tenfold reduction.  At 1 r* the reduction is
      1.20, inside the "does not close it" band, and at 0.5 r* the sum is
      WORSE than the mechanical momentum alone by 1.19.  The hypothesis is
      refuted.

119c. WHY IT CANNOT WORK, WITH THE MEASUREMENT AS THE CHECK.  For a charge
      and a magnetic dipole the interaction field momentum is exactly
      -(mu x E)/c^2, i.e. minus the hidden momentum, which is why a static
      system carries zero total momentum.  Adding one of the pair without the
      other is not a conservation law, and that is what the numbers show:
      the term is real and the right size to matter (0.03 to 0.29 m c along
      these orbits, 5-25% of the gap), but it moves the balance in either
      direction depending on the geometry rather than closing it.

119d. A CONTROL THAT PASSED.  Exact ortho carries zero hidden momentum at
      every step, as it carries zero mechanical momentum: the symmetry of
      section 106 acts on this term too.  So the term could not have broken
      that result, which is what makes the negative answer above a statement
      about the gap rather than about the probe.

119e. SIDE OBSERVATION.  The same trajectory now ends at 0.4515 r* with
      0.5992 m c, against section 104's 0.5331 r* and 0.6875 m c: the
      engine fixes of section 107 (the four-velocity extrapolation and the
      blended stencil) moved the deep orbit slightly and reduced the gained
      momentum by 13%.  The self-propulsion survives them.

119f. WHAT IS NEXT.  The lead of section 105g stands and is now the leading
      candidate: thomasBackReactionForces exists only in the instantaneous
      sum, not in retardedExternalForces.  The test is the one written there:
      add it to the retarded sum and ask whether beta_cm after one orbit at
      1 r* falls from 0.315 below 0.05.

119g. LIMITS.  Two geometries (1 and 0.5 r*, para, tilt 0), one orbit each,
      reaction off.  The hidden momentum is evaluated from the lab moments
      and the partner's field at the particle, with no attempt at the field
      momentum integral itself, which is the other half of the cancellation
      named in 119c.

120. THE THOMAS BACK-REACTION IN THE RETARDED SUM: A LARGE FORCE THAT
     AVERAGES TO 4%, AND IT DOES NOT CLOSE THE BALANCE EITHER
==================================================================

120a. THE LEAD, FROM 105g.  thomasBackReactionForces -- the orbital reaction
      of Thomas precession, derived from its own Lagrangian in section 80 --
      is summed inside chargeDipoleForces, which belongs to the INSTANTANEOUS
      force model.  retardedExternalForces has never carried it.  Since the
      unbalanced 88% of section 105 is the spin-orbit road, a missing
      spin-orbit reaction was the obvious candidate.

120b. WHAT WAS ADDED.  CREM_RETARDED_THOMAS appends the same term to the
      retarded sum, with the moment derivatives taken from the same retarded
      history the rest of that sum uses.  Off by default, so production is
      bit-identical; validation 62/62, header isolation 37/37.

120c. THE TERM IS NOT SMALL, POINTWISE.  Along the control orbit at 1 r*:
        |F_Thomas| / |F_total| on one particle   up to 0.422
        |net Thomas| / |net total| on the pair   up to 0.356
        integral of the net along the orbit      0.026 m c against 0.624
      So it reaches 40% of the force and 36% of the instantaneous imbalance,
      but it oscillates: over one orbit it accumulates 4.2% of the net
      momentum.  That is the whole answer in one line -- a term can be large
      in the force and negligible in the momentum budget.

120d. ON THE TRAJECTORY.  One orbit, para, tilt 0, retarded forces:
        case              control            with the Thomas term
        1 r*    beta_cm   0.2729             0.2974
                |P|       0.5992 m c         0.6248 m c
                dE        +47.3 |U_dd|       +36.1 |U_dd|
                r_min     0.367 r*           0.414 r*
        0.5 r*  beta_cm   0.5054             0.2744
                |P|       1.1939 m c         0.5740 m c
                dE        +16.83 |U_dd|      +3.03 |U_dd|
                r_min     0.270 r*           0.323 r*
      Rule R120a (beta_cm below 0.05) fails at both radii.  Rule R120b (a
      fivefold reduction) fails too: at 1 r* the momentum grows by 4%, at
      0.5 r* it falls by 2.08.  The energy gain does fall, by 1.3 and 5.6,
      and both plunges become shallower, so the term is doing real work --
      just not the work the hypothesis needed.

120e. WHERE THIS LEAVES THE IMBALANCE.  Two candidate closures have now been
      measured and rejected: hidden momentum (section 119, 1.20x at 1 r* and
      worse at 0.5 r*) and the missing Thomas back-reaction (this section,
      0.96x and 2.08x).  Neither is a bookkeeping fix.  What remains
      untested is the field momentum itself, epsilon0 integral of E x B over
      space, which is the only quantity that can carry the difference in
      principle; section 104d's argument against it was scale (the pair
      reaches 1.84 m c while the interaction energy is far smaller), and that
      argument has not been checked by computing the integral.

120f. LIMITS.  Two radii, one orbit each, para and tilt 0 only, reaction off.
      Adding the term changes the trajectory, so the pairs of runs are not
      the same orbit -- the shallower plunges are part of why the momentum
      differs.  The force-share numbers come from the control trajectory with
      the term evaluated but not applied, which is the only way to attribute
      a share to it without changing what it is measured against.

121. THE FIELD MOMENTUM, COMPUTED: 1.6% OF THE GAP.  ALL THREE CANDIDATE
     CARRIERS ARE NOW MEASURED AND NONE CLOSES IT
==================================================================

121a. THE LAST CANDIDATE.  Sections 119 and 120 rejected hidden momentum and
      the missing Thomas back-reaction.  What was left was the field momentum
      itself, eps0 integral of E x B, which section 104d dismissed on a scale
      argument without computing it.  This section computes it.

121b. WHAT IS COMPUTED, AND WHY THAT PART.  The cross terms,
      P_int = eps0 integral (E1 x B2 + E2 x B1) dV: the self terms belong to
      each particle alone, diverge at its own position and renormalize its
      mass, while the cross terms are finite and are the only part that
      depends on the pair's configuration.  Both fields come from the model's
      own functions -- Lienard-Wiechert plus the retarded dipole field of
      each source -- on a spherical grid around the centre of mass, with
      Gauss-Legendre quadrature in cos(theta), uniform azimuth and
      logarithmic shells.

121c. IT CONVERGES, AND THE HORIZON IS THE MODEL'S OWN HISTORY.  The
      cumulative integral at the prepared state, in units of m c:
        R       1.4 r*   2.4 r*   4.1 r*   6.9 r*   11.8 r*
        |P|     8.86e-3  1.01e-2  1.04e-2  1.044e-2 1.045e-2
      i.e. it is flat to 0.1% between 4 and 12 r*, as a near-zone density
      falling like 1/r^4 must be.  Refinement from 48x12x24 to 96x24x48
      moves it by 0.6%, inside the 5% the rule allowed.  Beyond ~12 r* the
      value drifts and then goes non-finite: appendStateHistory keeps
      max(1e-20 s, 4r/c), so a field point further than about 15 r* asks for
      a retarded time the history does not cover.  That is a limit of the
      measurement, not of the integral.

121d. THE ANSWER.  Over one orbit (para, 1 r*, tilt 0, retarded forces):
        |dP_mech|                  0.599213 m c
        |dP_field(cross)|          0.009849 m c
        |d(P_mech + P_field)|      0.589862 m c    ratio 1.0159
      The field momentum is 1.6% of the gap.  Rule R121a asked for a tenfold
      reduction and R121b for twofold; this is neither.  Its magnitude never
      leaves the 1% band: 1.04e-2 m c at the start and 1.02e-2 at the end.
      Section 104d's scale argument is therefore correct, and now measured
      rather than argued.

121e. WHAT THAT SETTLES.  Three carriers have been measured against the same
      0.6 m c: hidden momentum 1.20x (119), the Thomas back-reaction 0.96x
      and 2.08x (120), the interaction field momentum 1.016x (this section).
      The self-propulsion of sections 104-106 is therefore a genuine defect
      of the retarded dipole construction -- the force on each moment in the
      partner's retarded charge field has no reaction anywhere in the model
      or in its field -- and not an omission in the momentum ledger.  The
      remedy has to be in the force sector: either the retarded moment force
      is wrong, or its reaction on the source is missing from the field the
      model builds.

121f. LIMITS.  One orbit, one geometry, reaction off.  The integral covers
      r <= 12 r* and the near-zone tail beyond it is bounded by the plateau
      above; the RADIATION zone is outside the model's history horizon
      entirely and is not measured here, so a radiative momentum flux is not
      excluded by this section -- only by the separate flux bookkeeping the
      engine already carries, which these runs had switched off.

122. THE DEFECT LOCALIZED: FIRST ORDER IN v/c, IN THE MOMENT FORCE, AND ONLY
     46% OF IT IS THE HIDDEN-MOMENTUM RATE
==================================================================

122a. THE CLEAN TEST.  A pair drifting rigidly -- one common velocity, fixed
      separation, no acceleration -- carries a field pattern that only
      translates, so the field momentum is constant and F1 + F2 must vanish
      exactly.  Measured with a synthetic uniform-motion history at
      beta = 0.05, separation 1 r*, moments along the normal:
        |F1| = |F2| = 1.117343e-02 N,  |F1+F2| = 2.9e-13 N,  ratio 2.6e-11.
      The construction is therefore consistent where nothing moves relative
      to anything: the defect needs RELATIVE motion, not merely retardation.

122b. WITH RELATIVE MOTION IT IS FIRST ORDER IN v/c.  Synthetic CIRCULAR
      kinematics at the same separation (both particles on a circle, opposite
      phases, exact velocities and accelerations, no radiation reaction):
        beta     |F1+F2| [N]     |F1+F2|/|F1|
        0.001    5.315e-05       4.74e-03
        0.003    1.594e-04       1.42e-02
        0.01     5.315e-04       4.64e-02
        0.03     1.594e-03       1.33e-01
        0.1      5.293e-03       3.82e-01
        0.2      1.046e-02       6.34e-01
      The net force is exactly linear in beta over two decades (x3.00 per x3),
      so it is a VELOCITY effect, not an acceleration one: at fixed radius the
      acceleration goes as beta^2 and would have shown as a quadratic.

122c. THE CARRIER, CONFIRMED IN THE CLEAN SETTING.  Removing single parts
      (the probe switches of audit 105) at beta = 0.1:
        removed                          |F1+F2| [N]   against 5.293e-03
        the moment force entirely        4.143e-04     12.8x smaller
        the LW field inside it           4.143e-04     12.8x smaller
        the magnetization                3.944e-03
        the dipole field on the charge   4.879e-03
        the poles                        6.229e-03     larger
        the material term                5.293e-03     unchanged
      So 92% of the imbalance is the force on the moments in the partner's
      Lienard-Wiechert charge field, exactly as section 105 found on a real
      trajectory, and the material term contributes nothing to it.

122d. HOW MUCH OF IT IS LEGITIMATE.  A rotating configuration MAY carry a net
      force: what must hold is F1+F2 = d(P_field + P_hidden)/dt.  Both rates
      were computed on the same synthetic history:
        hidden momentum rate d/dt (mu x E)/c^2   2.437e-03 N   46.0% of the net
        interaction field momentum rate          8.377e-04 N   15.8%
      The hidden-momentum share is stable in beta (0.4629 at 0.01, 0.4604 at
      0.1), i.e. it has the same first-order structure as the imbalance.
      Together the two account for at most 62% (they are not collinear, so
      the vector sum accounts for less), leaving about a third of the net
      force with no carrier at all.

122e. WHAT THIS SAYS THE DEFECT IS.  The model's force on a moment is
      grad(mu.B + p.E)/gamma plus a material term.  The classical force on a
      current loop in an external field carries, besides the gradient, the
      rate of its own hidden momentum, -(1/c^2) d(mu x E)/dt -- and that term
      is precisely 46% of what is missing here, with the right scaling.  The
      model has no such term: its material derivative is a different object
      (it differentiates the coupling along the worldline, and the ablation
      above shows it contributes nothing to the imbalance).  So the concrete
      candidate fix is to add the hidden-momentum rate to the moment force
      and re-measure; that is a force-sector change, which is where sections
      119-121 concluded the remedy had to be.

122f. LIMITS.  Synthetic kinematics, one separation (1 r*), moments along the
      orbital normal, one tilt.  The field-momentum rate uses a 48x12x24 grid
      out to 12 r* and a centred difference; its own convergence was checked
      only through the static integral of section 121 (0.6% between two
      resolutions), not for the rate.  The remaining third is quoted as a
      magnitude, not attributed.  Nothing here is a fix: the candidate named
      in 122e has not been implemented or tested.

123. THE HIDDEN-MOMENTUM RATE ADDED TO THE MOMENT FORCE: EXACTLY THE 46% IT
     SHOULD REMOVE, AND THE ORBIT COMES OUT WORSE
==================================================================

123a. WHAT WAS ADDED.  hiddenMomentumRateForce: F = -(1/c^2) d(mu x E)/dt
      along the particle's own worldline, with (dmu/dt) x E from the
      Thomas-BMT precession of the same retarded history and mu x (DE/Dt)
      from the same backward three-point stencil dipoleCouplingMaterialRate
      uses.  Behind CREM_HIDDEN_MOMENTUM_FORCE, off by default.  It is the
      term section 122e named: a current loop carries (mu x E)/c^2, which is
      not in gamma m v, so the mechanical momentum obeys
      dp/dt = F_gradient - d/dt (mu x E)/c^2.

123b. ON THE CLEAN TESTS IT DOES EXACTLY WHAT IT SHOULD.
        uniform motion, beta 0.05    |F1+F2| 4.5e-13 N, ratio 4.0e-11
        circular, beta 0.01          5.315e-04 -> 2.855e-04 N   -46.3%
        circular, beta 0.1           5.293e-03 -> 2.856e-03 N   -46.0%
        circular, beta 0.2           1.046e-02 -> 5.709e-03 N   -45.4%
      Rule R123a is met (rigid drift stays balanced to 1e-11) and R123b is
      met with the reduction flat in beta, which is what a first-order term
      with the right structure must give.  The remaining 54% is the part
      section 122d could not attribute.

123c. ON THE REAL TRAJECTORY IT IS WORSE.  Para, 1 r*, tilt 0, one orbit,
      depth 26, tolerance 1e-8:
                         control        with the term
        |P| after 1 orbit  0.5992 m c     1.1714 m c
        beta_cm            0.2729         0.5015
        dE                 +47.29 |U_dd|  +113.1 |U_dd|
        deepest r          0.3673 r*      0.2855 r*
      Early in the orbit it is better -- at step 48 the momentum is 0.1836
      against 0.3232 -- but the smaller early imbalance leaves the pair on a
      deeper passage, and the deeper passage more than repays it.  At 0.5 r*
      the same pattern: |P| 1.2817 against 1.1939, dE +11.46 against +16.83,
      periapsis 0.2086 against 0.2709.  Rule R123c is not met.
      At depth 20 the term also costs resolution: the same run breaks off at
      step 49 (0.4002 r*) where the control completes, which is the stencil
      cost of one more time derivative in the force.

123d. WHAT THAT MEANS.  The term is right in form and wrong as a fix.  It
      removes precisely the share that was attributable to it, at every speed
      and without disturbing the case that must stay exactly balanced, so
      section 122's attribution is confirmed by construction rather than by
      fitting.  But a partial restoration of a broken balance is not an
      improvement to a trajectory: with 54% of the imbalance still unsourced,
      feeding back a half-corrected force simply moves the orbit somewhere
      else.  The model does not get a better deep passage out of it, and the
      switch therefore stays OFF by default.

123e. UNCHANGED.  Validation 62/62, header isolation 37/37, and with the
      switch off production is bit-identical: seed 70001 gives
      para 198.974732577 ps and ortho 198.971579221 ps, the values of
      section 110.

123f. WHAT IS STILL MISSING.  The 54%.  Sections 119-122 have now measured
      every carrier the classical theory offers -- hidden momentum, field
      momentum, the Thomas back-reaction -- and this section has added the
      one force term that was genuinely absent.  What remains is either a
      second missing force term of the same first order in v/c, or an error
      in the retarded moment force itself that no bookkeeping can absorb.
      The clean synthetic setting of section 122 is the place to look for it:
      it is one geometry, one separation, and the residual is reproducible
      there to three digits.

123g. LIMITS.  One geometry for the trajectory comparison (para, tilt 0, two
      radii), one orbit each.  The new term uses the same backward stencil as
      the material rate, so it inherits its resolution cost and the node-
      crossing sensitivity of section 107e.  The synthetic tests are the same
      as section 122's and share their limits.

124. THE HIDDEN-MOMENTUM RATE TURNED ON IN PRODUCTION, AND A SECOND PIECE OF
     THE IMBALANCE FOUND: THE GRADIENT GROUPING
==================================================================

124a. TURNED ON.  hiddenMomentumRateForce is now part of the retarded sum by
      default (CREM_NO_HIDDEN_MOMENTUM_FORCE removes it).  Audit 123 measured
      what it does and does not do, and the decision was taken on that
      evidence: it is Newton's law for the mechanical momentum of a current
      loop, it leaves a rigidly drifting pair exactly balanced, and it removes
      46% of the sum's imbalance at every speed.

124b. WHAT PRODUCTION DOES WITH IT.  Experiment 1, 4 trajectories, seed 42,
      budget 600 s:
        collapse time          30.6723 +- 0.0045 ps   (was 30.6732 +- 0.0052)
        against the closed form  0.9855                (unchanged)
        frame drift at the stop  beta = 2.72e-06       (was 6.12e-04)
        stop periapsis           15.84 r*              (was 15.91)
      The collapse time moves by 0.0009 ps, a fifth of its own error, and the
      pair's self-acceleration over the whole inspiral falls by 225 times.
      That is the production case for the term: audit 123's worse deep
      passage lives below 1 r*, where production does not go.
      Photon-cascade times move as they must: seed 70001 gives para
      198.975154 ps (was 198.974733) and ortho 198.966547 (was 198.971579),
      i.e. +0.42 fs and -5.0 fs.  Validation 62/62, header isolation 37/37.

124c. THE SECOND PIECE.  The model's moment force is built as a GRADIENT,
      grad(mu.B + p.E).  Written convectively that is
        grad(mu.B) = (mu.grad)B + mu x (curl B),
        grad(p.E)  = (p.grad)E  + p  x (curl E),
      and for the partner's field curl B = (1/c^2) dE/dt and curl E = -dB/dt,
      so the two extra pieces are first order in the frequency -- the order
      the remainder has.  Measured in the synthetic circular setting of
      section 122, against the remainder R = F1+F2 that survives the
      hidden-momentum term:
        beta    |R| [N]      |S| [N]      S.R/|R|     |R-S|/|R|
        0.01    2.8545e-04   1.2208e-04   0.4277      0.5723
        0.1     2.8563e-03   1.1979e-03   0.4194      0.5806
        0.2     5.7087e-03   2.2877e-03   0.4003      0.6000
      S is COLLINEAR with R -- its projection equals its length to four
      digits -- and carries 40-43% of it.  Rule R124b is met, R124a is not.

124d. WHAT THAT IS AND IS NOT.  It is a term of the right size, the right
      order and the right direction, identified without fitting.  It is NOT
      established as an error: for an Amperian dipole the standard force IS
      grad(m.B) - (1/c^2) d(m x E)/dt, which is what the model now has, so
      the convective regrouping is a candidate reading rather than a
      correction.  What the measurement does establish is that the remaining
      imbalance is built from the same curls, i.e. from the time dependence
      of the partner's field at the particle, and not from the pole or
      magnetization construction (their ablations leave it unchanged).
      Accounting so far: 46% the hidden-momentum rate (123), 23% this piece
      (0.54 x 0.42), and about 31% of the original imbalance with no
      identified source.

124e. THE TEST THAT WOULD SETTLE IT (named, not done).  A case with an exact
      closed-form answer: a magnetic moment at rest and a charge in UNIFORM
      relative motion.  There the fields, the Amperian force, the reaction on
      the charge and the field momentum are all known analytically, so the
      model's force can be compared term by term with the exact one rather
      than with its own bookkeeping.  Section 122's rigid-drift test is the
      degenerate case of it (zero relative velocity) and already passes; the
      moving case is where a first-order error must show.

124f. LIMITS.  The synthetic tests are one separation, one tilt, moments
      along the normal.  The curls come from a symmetric stencil at 1e-4 of
      the separation, not from an independent analytic field.  The production
      numbers are four trajectories at one seed.  The ablation lines printed
      by the probe in this run are vacuous: that binary was built from the
      working tree without the probe switches of audit 105.

125. THE EXACT TEST: THE MOMENT FORCE IS RIGHT TO 3e-5, AND THE IMBALANCE IS
     TWO DIFFERENT SOFTENING LENGTHS ON THE TWO SIDES OF ONE INTERACTION
==================================================================

125a. THE SETTING.  A magnetic moment at rest and a charge in UNIFORM
      relative motion past it, at the instant of closest approach.  The
      fields of the charge are the Heaviside ellipsoid in closed form, the
      Amperian force on the moment is grad(m.B) - (1/c^2) m x dE/dt, and the
      reaction is the Lorentz force on the charge in the moment's static
      dipole field.  Everything is analytic, so the model's force can be
      compared term by term instead of against its own bookkeeping.

125b. THE FORCE ON THE MOMENT IS CORRECT.  Separation 20 r*, where the
      model's softening is worth a fraction of a per cent:
        beta    |model total - exact| / |exact|    gradient alone
        0.01    2.8094e-05                         0.99996
        0.05    2.8095e-05                         0.99996
        0.2     2.8099e-05                         0.99996
      The model's gradient and the hidden-momentum rate are each about twice
      the answer and nearly cancel: at beta 0.05 the gradient is
      +7.727e-08 N, the hidden rate -3.864e-08 N, the sum 3.864e-08 N against
      the exact 3.864e-08 N.  So the term added in audits 123-124 is not a
      correction of the model's own making -- without it the force on a
      moment is wrong by 100%, with it correct to 3e-05.  That is an
      independent confirmation from outside the model.

125c. THE REACTION IS OFF BY THE SOFTENING, AND ONLY BY IT.  At 20 r* the
      model's Lorentz force on the charge is 1.0465e-02 of the exact one
      below it, at every beta -- a static, velocity-independent deficit,
      which is what the Plummer softening at 0.9668 r* plus the contact term
      predict at that distance.  At 1 r* with the moment in the orbital plane
      the same comparison gives 0.628, i.e. the model's reaction is a third
      of the exact one.

125d. THAT IS THE IMBALANCE.  The two sides of one interaction are softened
      at DIFFERENT lengths: the moment feels the charge's field, softened at
      separationFloor = 0.05 r*, while the charge feels the moment's field,
      softened at magneticDipoleRadius = 0.9668 r*.  Newton's third law
      cannot survive that, and it does not.  Matching the two lengths --
      CREM_MAGNETIC_RADIUS_SCALE=0.05 -- in the synthetic circular test at
      1 r*, beta 0.1:
        default moment radius   |F1+F2| 2.856e-03 N   ratio 0.2266
        matched to 0.05 r*      |F1+F2| 1.167e-05 N   ratio 0.000966
      a factor 245.  At 20 r*, where neither softening reaches, the imbalance
      is 1.1e-04 either way.  On the real trajectory (para, 1 r*, tilt 0, one
      orbit, depth 26):
        default                 |P| 1.1714 m c   beta_cm 0.5015   dE +113.1
        matched                 |P| 0.0305 m c   beta_cm 0.0151   dE +0.045
      and the orbit no longer plunges at all -- it leaves to 8.13 r*.  This
      reproduces the number section 104e measured for the same switch and
      explains it.

125e. WHAT IS AND IS NOT SETTLED.  Settled: the retarded moment force is
      right, the reaction is right up to the regularization, and the
      self-propulsion of sections 104-124 is the mismatch of the two
      regularization lengths rather than a missing force or a missing
      momentum carrier.  Not settled: what to do about it.  The moment radius
      is not free -- audit 86 derives it from the current loop that carries
      mu at speeds below c, and audits 117-118 showed it sets the whole
      contact sector; matching it to 0.05 r* would multiply the contact
      coupling by (0.9668/0.05)^3 = 7200.  The principled repair is not to
      change the radius but to use ONE length on both sides of the
      interaction: soften the charge's field at the moment radius WHEN it
      acts on a moment, as the moment's field is softened when it acts on a
      charge.  That is a physics change and needs its own audit.

125f. LIMITS.  One geometry per case, one instant (closest approach), uniform
      relative motion only.  The exact Amperian force is differentiated
      numerically on the closed-form fields, with the stencil converged to
      3e-08.  The reaction comparison uses the static dipole field, which is
      exact only because the moment is at rest in this test.  The trajectory
      numbers are one orbit at one radius.

126. ONE SOFTENING LENGTH FOR BOTH SIDES: THE SELF-PROPULSION IS GONE, THE
     PLUNGE IS NOT, AND PRODUCTION DOES NOT MOVE
==================================================================

126a. THE REPAIR SECTION 125e NAMED.  Not a new value for the moment radius,
      but ONE length on both sides of one interaction.  The moment is a loop
      of radius magneticDipoleRadius() = 0.9668 r*, so it samples the
      partner's charge field averaged over its own extent; the charge already
      feels the moment's field softened at that length.  The charge field
      acting on a moment is therefore now softened with the same Plummer
      length (lienardWiechertField gained a floor override;
      matchedMomentSoftening supplies it in the three moment-force paths --
      the gradient stencil, the material rate and the hidden-momentum rate).
      ON by default; CREM_NO_MATCHED_SOFTENING restores the mismatch.

126b. THE SYNTHETIC TESTS.
        rigid drift, beta 0.05, 1 r*     |F1+F2| 1.68e-13 N, ratio 1.5e-11
        circular, 1 r*, beta 0.1         2.856e-03 -> 3.289e-06 N
                                         ratio 0.2266 -> 0.000294, factor 868
      Rule R126a is met (the case that must stay balanced does) and R126b is
      met 87 times over.

126c. THE EXACT TEST IS THE ONE THAT SETTLES IT.  Charge in uniform motion
      past a moment at rest, 20 r*, beta 0.05, against the closed form:
        quantity                       mismatched      matched
        force on the moment, error     2.81e-05        1.0465e-02
        reaction on the charge, error   1.0465e-02     1.0465e-02
        net of the two / |action|       1.1675e-02     1.2508e-03
        the same for the EXACT pair     1.2508e-03     1.2508e-03
      Read the columns rather than the rows: with one length the two sides
      deviate from the point-dipole answer by exactly the SAME 1.05% -- which
      is the loop averaging, a modelling choice, not an error -- and their net
      then reproduces the analytic net to four digits.  With two lengths the
      moment force looked better against the point dipole (2.8e-05) while the
      pair's net force was nine times too large.  Consistency between the two
      sides is what momentum conservation needs; agreement with a point
      dipole is not.  Rule R126c is met.

126d. THE REAL ORBIT.  Para, 1 r*, tilt 0, one orbit, depth 26:
        variant                          |P| [m c]   beta_cm   dE [|U_dd|]  r_min
        before audit 123                 0.5992      0.2729    +47.3        0.367
        with the hidden-momentum rate    1.1714      0.5015    +113.1       0.286
        and with one softening length    0.0061      0.0030    -1.08        0.361
      The self-propulsion is gone -- 192 times smaller than the previous
      default and 98 times smaller than before the hidden-momentum term --
      and the ledger now LOSES 1.08 |U_dd| over the orbit instead of gaining
      113.  What survives is the plunge to 0.36 r*, which is the moments'
      own attraction and was never the defect.

126e. PRODUCTION DOES NOT MOVE.  Experiment 1, 4 trajectories, seed 42:
      collapse time 30.6720 +- 0.0045 ps against 30.6723 before, the drift at
      the stop beta = 7.89e-07 against 2.72e-06, periapsis 15.87 r*.  Cascade
      lifetimes: seed 70001 gives para 198.975153994 ps and ortho
      198.966547464 ps, the second identical and the first moved by 1e-09 ps.
      That is the expected shape: production runs at 15 r* and beyond, where
      a 0.9668 r* softening is nothing.  Validation 62/62, header isolation
      37/37, and the default-on build reproduces the environment-variable run
      bit for bit.

126f. WHAT IS NOW CLOSED.  The self-propulsion thread of sections 104-126:
      the imbalance was 46% a missing force term (the hidden-momentum rate,
      audits 122-124) and the rest a broken action-reaction pair caused by
      two different regularization lengths (audits 125-126).  Neither was a
      missing momentum carrier: hidden momentum, field momentum and the
      Thomas back-reaction were each measured and each too small (119-121).

126g. LIMITS AND WHAT THIS CHANGES ELSEWHERE.  The matched length applies to
      the FORCE on a moment only; the energies (pairDipoleInteractionEnergy,
      azimuthAveragedDipoleEnergy) and the charge-charge force are untouched,
      so the ledger's own consistency below 1 r* has not been re-measured
      here.  Deep-passage trajectories now differ from every number recorded
      in sections 104-125, which were taken with the mismatch; the audit
      trail keeps them as the history of the defect, not as current
      behaviour.  One geometry for the exact test, one orbit for the
      trajectory, positronium only.

127. THE TWO LOOSE ENDS OF AUDIT 126: THE LEDGER'S OWN SOFTENING, AND THE
     DEEP-PASSAGE NUMBERS RE-MEASURED
==================================================================

127a. THE LEDGER'S SOFTENING DOES NOT EXPLAIN THE RESIDUAL.  After audit 126
      the force on a moment reads the partner's charge field softened at
      0.9668 r* while chargeDipoleInteractionEnergy -- that force's own energy
      -- still read it at 0.05 r*, so the force was not minus the gradient of
      the ledger.  Giving the energy the same length and re-running one orbit:
        case                      1 r* dE [|U_dd|]   0.5 r* dE   |P| [m c]
        force matched only        -1.084             -0.230      6.13e-03
        force and energy matched  -1.129             -0.246      6.13e-03
        neither (before 126)      +113.1             +11.46      1.171
      Matching the energy moves the drift 4-7% the WRONG way and leaves the
      momentum untouched, since no force reads that term.  Rule R127a is not
      met, so the ledger keeps its own floor and the switch is opt-in
      (CREM_MATCHED_ENERGY).  What the measurement does settle is that the
      residual drift -- about 1 |U_dd| per orbit at 1 r*, a hundred times
      smaller than before 126 -- is NOT the force/energy softening mismatch.
      The obvious remaining candidate is the one this project has carried
      since section 58: the ledger is instantaneous and the forces are
      retarded.

127b. THE DEEP-PASSAGE NUMBERS, RE-MEASURED ON TODAY'S CODE.  Section 106's
      grid, one orbit, depth 26, |P| after the orbit:
        start, channel, tilt     section 106        now
        1 r*   para 0            0.6875 m c         6.13e-03    r_min 0.361
        1 r*   para 90           fail 42, 0.228     fail 45, 0.187
        1 r*   ortho 0           0 (leaves)         0 (leaves to 2.71)
        1 r*   ortho 90          0 (leaves)         0 (leaves to 6.43)
        0.5 r* para 0            1.189              3.70e-03    r_min 0.266
        0.5 r* para 90           beta_cm 0.943      2.79e-03    r_min 0.205
        0.5 r* ortho 0           fail 94, 0.158     0 (leaves to 2.75)
        0.5 r* ortho 90          0 (leaves)         0 (leaves to 3.18)
        0.35 r* para 0           beta_cm 0.438      2.95e-03    r_min 0.233
        0.35 r* para 90          beta_cm 0.527      9.71e-05    r_min 0.209
        0.35 r* ortho 0          fail 38, 0.132     0 (leaves to 1.76)
        0.35 r* ortho 90         0 (leaves)         0 (leaves to 1.81)
      Para's self-propulsion is down by factors of 100 to 10000 and every
      run completes except para at 1 r* with the moments in the plane, which
      still breaks off at 0.187 r* on the engine defect of section 107e.
      Exact ortho still carries P = 0 bit for bit, as the symmetry of
      section 106 requires.
      A qualitative change worth naming: the ortho runs that used to PLUNGE
      and fail (0.5 and 0.35 r*, tilt 0) now leave the region instead.  The
      plunge was driven through the spin-orbit road -- the charge field acting
      on the moments -- which is exactly the field audit 126 softened.

127c. AND THE RANGE OF WHAT IS LEFT.  Section 109's scan, one orbit, |P|:
        r        section 109 (tilt 0 / 90)    now (tilt 0 / 90)
        2.74 r*  6.9e-03 / 1.8e-01            6.8e-06 / 8.3e-07
        5 r*     1.3e-03 / 1.7e-03            7.1e-06 / 2.4e-06
        10 r*    1.0e-04 / 1.3e-04            7.7e-07 / 3.6e-07
        20 r*    7.0e-06 / 8.0e-06            4.1e-07 / 1.1e-09
        50 r*    1.9e-07 / 1.9e-07            3.7e-11 / 7.8e-12
      The r^-3.9 law of section 109 is gone with the effect that produced it:
      what remains is 1e-6 and below, without a clean power, i.e. at the level
      where the integrator's own residue lives.  The statement of section 109
      -- that the self-propulsion reaches above 0.01 a0 -- no longer holds on
      today's code; it described the defect, and the defect is fixed.

127d. WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE AUDIT TRAIL.  Sections 104-125 measured a
      defect and its causes; their trajectory numbers are the defect's, not
      the model's current behaviour, and 127b-127c are the current ones.  The
      conclusions that survive unchanged are the ones about symmetry (106),
      about what the imbalance was NOT (119-121), and the two force results
      (123-126).

127e. LIMITS.  One orbit per configuration and one seed; the ledger residual
      of 127a was measured at two radii; the retarded-versus-instantaneous
      candidate named there was not tested.  Para at 1 r*, tilt 90 still
      fails, so its line in 127b is a lower bound on the passage rather than
      a completed orbit.

128. THE SPIN-NORM BUDGET, NOW EXACT: THE CEILING IS hbar TO FIFTEEN DIGITS
==================================================================

128a. WHAT WAS LEFT OF SECTION 115.  That section measured the budget and
      drew the conclusion -- the pair's total spin cannot exceed hbar while
      the quantum triplet needs sqrt(2) hbar -- but three things in it were
      approximate or dated: |S_i|/hbar came out 0.500000016..0.500003328
      rather than exactly 0.5, the pair identity closed to 1.3e-05 rather
      than to machine precision, both residues ATTRIBUTED to the lab moment's
      boost without testing that attribution, and the trajectory drift was
      measured before the force changes of sections 123-127.

128b. THE RESIDUES WERE THE BOOST.  The same ensemble with the velocities
      zeroed before the moments are synchronized, so the lab moment IS the
      proper one:
        in motion (as in 115)   |S_i|/hbar 0.500000023..0.500003328,
                                identity worst 1.300e-05
        at rest                 |S_i|/hbar 0.500000000000000 exactly,
                                identity worst 4.441e-16
      So the attribution in section 115 was right, and the algebra behind the
      channel weight is exact, not approximate: at beta = 0 it closes to
      machine precision.

128c. THE CEILING, BUILT RATHER THAN WAITED FOR.  Section 115 reached
      |S1+S2|/hbar = 0.997 in a free draw and 1.000000 in the imposed ortho
      ensemble.  Constructing the extreme directly -- the two MOMENTS
      anti-parallel at rest, which is the spins parallel -- gives
        |S1+S2|/hbar = 1.000000000000000,  identity residual 0.000e+00
      against the sqrt(2) = 1.414214 the triplet requires.  The model's
      maximum S^2 is therefore exactly 1.0 hbar^2 while the quantum ENSEMBLE
      AVERAGE is 1.5 hbar^2: the average of the states the model is supposed
      to describe lies above the largest value it can represent.

128d. THE TRAJECTORY DRIFT, RE-MEASURED.  One full collapse on today's code,
      1842 transport substeps: |S|/hbar runs 0.6525 -> 0.4881, drift 0.1643,
      range 0.3673..0.6609.  Section 115 had 0.6525 -> 0.5146, drift 0.1378,
      range 0.367..0.661 -- the same band, a slightly larger endpoint drift.
      That is what section 116 predicts: the drift is driven by the
      anisotropic dipole TENSOR term, and audit 126 softened the charge-field
      road, not that one.

128e. WHAT IT SETTLES.  The closure of the 1:3-through-the-measure line
      (sections 112, 115) now rests on exact arithmetic rather than on
      numbers good to 1e-05: any measure over the mutual angle that gives the
      1:3 branching needs <|S|^2> = 0.75 hbar^2, which the model can
      represent, and that is half the quantum 1.5 hbar^2, which it cannot
      represent at all -- not on average, and not even at its single most
      favourable configuration.

128f. LIMITS.  The at-rest ensemble is unphysical as an orbit; only the
      algebra is being measured there, which is the point.  The trajectory
      line is one collapse at one seed.  Positronium only: for an unequal
      pair |S_i| is still hbar/2 each, but the gyromagnetic ratios differ and
      the identity's coherent term is no longer symmetric, which was not
      measured.

129. THE LIBRATION'S ACTION IS 0.1 hbar, AND IT CANNOT EXCEED 0.5: THE
     SEMICLASSICAL DOUBLET DOES NOT EXIST
==================================================================

129a. WHAT WAS MISSING.  Section 115 measured the libration's FREQUENCY and
      compared h f_lib with the 203.3941 GHz splitting, but left its ACTION
      uncomputed, because the trace carried the mutual angle and not the
      variable conjugate to it.  For two spins of fixed length s = hbar/2 the
      canonical pair is (s cos theta, psi), psi being the relative azimuth
      about the total spin -- the perpendicular parts of S1 and S2 are exactly
      opposite, so one azimuth describes both.  A new trace
      (CREM_DEBUG_SPINPAIR) prints both moments and the orbital vector, and
      the action follows as J = (1/2 pi) integral s cos(theta) d psi.

129b. MEASURED, one full collapse, 1842 substeps, 53 turning points of the
      angle, psi sweeping 13.17 turns:
        cycle          period      h f_lib        action J
        first          1.104e-11 s   90.55 GHz    0.0985 hbar
        last           2.006e-16 s   4.98e+06 GHz 0.1383 hbar
      The frequency is what section 115 found, sharpened by using whole cycles
      instead of consecutive extrema: 90.55 GHz against 203.3941, a factor
      2.25, so rule R129a (within a factor 2) is narrowly missed.
      The action is nearly an adiabatic invariant: it moves by 1.40 while the
      frequency moves by five orders, which satisfies rule R129c.

129c. AND THE ACTION IS AN ORDER BELOW THE QUANTUM.  J = 0.0985 hbar at the
      start, 0.1383 at the end.  Rule R129b asked for 0.1..10 hbar and the
      measurement sits on its lower edge, which is the answer rather than a
      near miss: an oscillation carrying a tenth of hbar has no level
      structure to quantize.
      The bound is structural, not a property of this trajectory.  The
      variable is s cos theta with |cos theta| <= 1 and s = hbar/2, so over a
      full 2 pi of azimuth the largest action the relative orientation can
      carry is
        J_max = (1/2 pi) * (hbar/2) * 1 * 2 pi = 0.5 hbar,
      below one quantum.  Quantizing this libration with Delta J = hbar is
      therefore impossible in its own phase space, whatever the amplitude.

129d. WHICH IS WHAT QUANTUM MECHANICS SAYS.  Two spin-1/2 particles span a
      four-dimensional Hilbert space, i.e. a finite phase-space volume with
      no ladder in it: the singlet-triplet splitting is the WHOLE space, not a
      spacing between rungs of one.  The semiclassical route asked for a
      ladder and the model's own phase space says there is none -- so the
      answer to R2 is no, and it is no for a reason that would hold for any
      classical two-spin model, not for this one's parameters.

129e. WHAT SURVIVES OF R2.  The libration itself is real, measured, and now
      characterized: frequency 90.6 GHz at the prepared state rising by five
      orders into the cascade, action 0.1 hbar and nearly invariant, amplitude
      |cos theta| swinging within -0.73..-0.13 on this trajectory.  As a
      classical oscillation it is the mechanism behind the channel weight's
      spread (sections 111-113).  What it cannot be is the hyperfine doublet.

129f. LIMITS.  One trajectory, one seed, 53 cycles of which the first and the
      last were integrated; the azimuth frame is built from the orbital
      vector projected perpendicular to the total spin, which is a choice of
      gauge -- the action is gauge-invariant over a closed cycle, and the
      measured |d psi| per cycle (3.14 and 3.22 rad) shows the cycles do
      close to within 3%.  The J_max bound uses |S1| = |S2| = hbar/2 exactly,
      which audit 128 measured to fifteen digits.

130. THE TWO OBSERVABLES ARE NOT IN CONFLICT -- THEY ARE DECOUPLED, WHICH IS
     WORSE: ONE FIXES J, THE OTHER FIXES L
==================================================================

130a. THE NECESSARY CONDITION.  One spin content must give both
      O1  the ratio 2 gamma : 3 gamma = 1:3, i.e. E[w] = (1+<cos>)/2 = 1/4,
      O2  the splitting U(anti-aligned) - U(aligned) = 203.3941 GHz.
      Section 112 answered O1 alone and found measures that satisfy it; the
      question here is whether ONE of them also carries O2, within a factor
      1.5, with no refitting between the two.  A new tool,
      tools/two_observable_consistency.cpp, evaluates both from the model's
      own functions (azimuthAveragedDipoleEnergy and the orbit-averaged BMT
      rates) as a scan over the orbital angular momentum at fixed a_pair.

130b. THE MEASURE'S SCALE NOW COMES FROM INSIDE THE MODEL.  The Boltzmann
      candidate of 112b had a free temperature, which is why 112 could not
      close this.  Section 129 removed it: the mutual angle's own oscillation
      has an action J = 0.0985 hbar at the start of the collapse and
      0.1383 hbar at its end, invariant to a factor 1.40 in between, so
      E_lib = J |omega1 - omega2| is an internal energy for exactly the
      degree of freedom the measure lives on.

130c. THE FIRST RESULT IS AN IDENTITY, NOT A NUMBER.  Measured across the
      scan, hbar |omega1 - omega2| / A = 4.0000 at L = hbar and 3.997-3.999
      down to L = 0.1 hbar.  It is exact: the precession is omega_i =
      gamma_i B and the energy amplitude is A = |mu| B in the SAME field, the
      two gyromagnetic ratios are exactly opposite (-1.76086e11 and
      +1.76086e11 in the trace), so
        hbar |omega1-omega2| / A = 2 hbar |gamma| / |mu| = 4
      because |mu| = |gamma| hbar/2 (audit 128, to fifteen digits).  Hence
        x = A/E_lib = hbar/(4J)      exactly,
      and E[w] = (1 - Langevin(hbar/4J))/2 depends ONLY on the action.  The
      orbit cancels out of O1 completely: over L = 0.1..1.0 hbar, 91 nodes,
      E[w] moves by 1.4e-04.

130d. SO THE TWO OBSERVABLES DO NOT COMPETE -- THEY ARE INDEPENDENT.
        O1 is a function of J alone and of no orbit.
        O2 is a function of the orbit alone: 2A = 21.95 GHz at L = hbar and
           203.3941 GHz at L = 0.4761 hbar (116d).
      At that L the same measure gives E[w] = 0.1907, a factor 1.311 from
      1/4, so rule R130a's evaluable half is MET.  Its other half and rule
      R130b are void as written: they assumed both observables tune with L,
      and O1 does not, so "L(O1)" does not exist (the 0.0567 hbar the
      bisection reports is the sign flip inside the moment radius, not a
      polarization).  My pre-registered prediction was wrong in its
      mechanism: I expected x to rise steeply as r falls; it is constant.
      Parameter-free variants, all L-independent:
        J             secular omega      traced omega (129)
        0.0985 hbar        0.1907             0.3130
        0.1383 hbar        0.2489             0.3609
      All four lie inside the factor-1.5 window [0.1667, 0.3750].  O1 exactly
      needs J = 0.1391 hbar against the 0.1383 measured at the last libration
      cycle -- 0.6% -- but with the traced frequency instead of the secular
      one it needs 0.0675 hbar, so that agreement sits inside the factor-2.06
      ambiguity of rule R130d and is not evidence.

130e. AND THE MODEL DOES NOT PRODUCE THAT MEASURE.  Read straight off the
      section 129 trace, with no Boltzmann assumption and no temperature, the
      time distribution of the mutual moment cosine on the model's own
      trajectory is
        cos(mu1,mu2)  start +0.1486  end +0.5235  min +0.1265  max +0.7303
        time mean +0.4696  ->  E[w] = 0.7348
        last 10% of the time +0.4963  ->  E[w] = 0.7482,
      i.e. the dynamics carries the moments TOWARD alignment, to 1:0.36
      rather than 1:3, a factor 2.94 and on the far side of the isotropic
      0.5.  The Boltzmann measure at the libration scale predicts the
      opposite sign.  The model's ensemble measure, 40 trajectories in 112c,
      is 0.4994: isotropic, factor 2.00 from O1.

130f. A CORRECTION TO 112d.  It said the model's only angle-dependent energy
      "tilts toward ALIGNMENT, i.e. the wrong way", quoting
      U = -2 (mu0/4pi) mu^2 cos/r^3.  That formula is for moments along the
      SEPARATION; at the production geometry (moments along the orbital
      normal) the sign is the opposite, A = +4.5387e-05 eV, so the ENERGY
      favours anti-alignment, which is the direction O1 needs.  The verdict
      of 112d survives, but for the other reason measured in 130e: it is the
      precession, not the energy, that goes the wrong way.

130g. THE ANSWER TO R3.  The necessary condition can be met, but only by an
      imported measure, and the reason is the opposite of the one the test
      anticipated.  It is not that fitting one moment spoils the other: in
      this model the two observables are exactly decoupled, O1 living on the
      libration action and O2 on the orbital angular momentum, so there are
      two knobs for two numbers and matching both is worth nothing.
        candidate                    E[w]     O1    splitting [GHz]    fit
        two points +-1 as 1:3       0.2500   1.00   none, a degeneracy   1
        p ~ (1-cos)^2 = |S|^4       0.2500   1.00   none, scale-free     0*
        Boltzmann at E_lib          0.191    1.31   203.39 at L=0.476    1
        the model's ensemble (112c) 0.4994   2.00   21.95 at L=hbar      0
        this trajectory (130e)      0.7348   2.94   21.95 at L=hbar      0
      (* it fits O1 by its functional form and predicts nothing for O2.)
      Quantum mechanics has no knob at all: 1:3 is the degeneracy of four
      levels and 203.3941 GHz is the gap between the same four.  That is what
      the model cannot imitate, and 130c says why in one line -- its own
      angle carries an action of 0.1 hbar, so the ratio it implies is set by
      J alone and can be tuned without touching any energy.

130h. LIMITS.  The trajectory measure of 130e is ONE trajectory and one
      prepared angle; 112c's ensemble is the ensemble statement, and 112c
      also showed the dynamics compresses the distribution without tilting
      it, so 0.7348 is this libration centre, not an ensemble prediction.
      The Langevin form is an assumption about a single librating degree of
      freedom, which is not a reservoir: its distribution is peaked at the
      turning points, not Boltzmann, and 130e is the direct measurement that
      does not need it.  The scan is analytic on a Kepler ellipse at fixed
      a_pair, as 116d and 117c were, and the secular libration rate is a
      factor 2.06 below the traced one (rule R130d, inside its factor 3).

131. THE ORBITAL ACTION ALONG THE COLLAPSE: THE PREPARED STATE IS EXACTLY h,
     THE PHOTON REMOVES LESS THAN h BY A LAW, AND THE STOP IS A MARGIN
==================================================================

131a. WHAT WAS ADDED.  CREM_ACTION_TRACE in the collapse estimator prints, at
      every checkpoint, the two Bohr-Sommerfeld actions of the osculating
      orbit: J_phi/h = L/hbar, J_r/h = n - L/hbar, with
      n = (K/hbar) sqrt(mu/(2|E|)), plus the eccentricity.  They come from the
      two osculating elements the estimator already carried, so nothing new
      is integrated and nothing feeds back.  The photon-ladder trace gained
      J_phi/h, and tools/orbital_action_ladder.cpp turns the emission rule
      into the ladder it implies.

131b. THE PREPARED STATE IS THE n = 1, k = 1 ORBIT TO NINE DIGITS.
        n = 1.000000000   J_phi/h = 1.000000000   J_r/h = 0.000000000   e = 0
      and the radial action is never excited afterwards: along the whole
      collapse |J_r/h| stays under 0.0054, mean 0.0014, and it goes slightly
      NEGATIVE, which is the over-circular seam (|L| a hair above the
      circular limit for the current energy) and not a radial oscillation.
      So the cascade is the descent of ONE action, J_phi, from exactly h.

131c. Q1: A PHOTON DOES NOT REMOVE h, AND NOW THERE IS A LAW FOR IT.  The
      emission rule is E_photon = hbar omega_orb = 2R/n^3 (audit 29), so
      E' = -R/n^2 - 2R/n^3 and the landing is exact:
        n -> n/sqrt(1+2/n),    Delta n = n [1 - (1+2/n)^(-1/2)].
      Against the two measured ladders:
        from n = 1 (this run)          measured Delta   law      ratio
          1.000000 -> 0.577269           0.422731     0.422650   1.0002
          0.577269 -> 0.272406           0.304863     0.304065   1.0026
          0.272406 -> 0.096794           0.175612     0.178091   0.9861
        from n = 2 (audit 29b)
          1.999995 -> 1.414210           0.585785     0.585786   1.0000
          1.414210 -> 0.910168           0.504042     0.504033   1.0000
          0.910168 -> 0.508943           0.401225     0.401162   1.0002
          0.508943 -> 0.228165           0.280778     0.279720   1.0038
      Median |Delta n| is 0.3049 from n = 1 and 0.5040 from n = 2, so rule
      R131a (0.9..1.1) is answered NO.  But the law says the quantum is
      reached in the correspondence limit and only there: Delta n = 0.8713 at
      n = 10, 0.9852 at n = 100, 0.9985 at n = 1000.  The model's emission
      rule IS the correspondence principle, exactly; what it cannot do is
      remove one quantum of action where n is of order one, which is the only
      place this model lives.

131d. AND THE ACTION LEAVES ONLY IN THOSE STEPS.  Over the whole collapse the
      total drop is 0.903206 h, of which 0.902241 h is the three photon steps
      and 0.000965 h is continuous drift between checkpoints -- 0.11%.  The
      azimuthal part alone gives 0.4226, 0.2993, 0.1813 h, which reproduces
      audit 21b's "demands 1 hbar, surrenders 0.4227, 0.3042, 0.1787" from
      the new trace (rule R131e); six trajectories agree to 0.001-0.03.

131e. Q2: THE STOP IS SHARP AND REPRODUCIBLE, AND IT IS A NUMERICAL MARGIN.
      The retardation safety margin, period/light-crossing >= 150, is on a
      circular orbit the statement
        n >= margin * alpha / (2 pi) = 0.174211,
      so the first rung of the ladder below it is n = 0.094740, after three
      photons.  Thirteen trajectories, seeds 42-54, all stopped by the model
      (no wall-clock censoring), all with three photons:
        n_stop mean 0.096210   sd 0.001406   min 0.094497   max 0.099146
        measured mean / predicted rung = 1.0155
      The stop follows the margin when the margin moves.  At 300 the bound is
      0.348423 and the predicted rung 0.273258; measured 0.273285 and
      0.273322, a ratio of 1.0001, after two photons.

131f. A FLAW IN MY OWN RULE, RECORDED.  R131b asked for the stopping actions
      to fall within 0.1 of an integer, and 13 of 13 do -- because the
      integer is ZERO.  The rule was ill-posed for a stop near the origin.
      The substantive reading is the nearest NONZERO integer, n = 1, which is
      10.4 times away, and the answer to Q2 is no.  R131b's other half, the
      clustering test, passes with a spread of 0.0046 h, but the cluster is
      the ladder's rung, not a floor: it is where the arithmetic of three
      correspondence photons from n = 1 happens to land relative to a
      numerical safety margin, which is not a quantization condition.
      Below the declared domain the closed form fails, although the cause
      does not.  At margin 50 the Kepler estimate predicts n = 0.020148 after
      four photons; the runs stop after THREE, at n = 0.080655 and 0.097152,
      and the recorded stop cause is still the retardation margin -- 4 of 4
      re-runs with the cause printed give RetardationLimit, at margins 50,
      150 and 300 alike.  The estimator's own ratio uses the REGULARIZED
      period and the actual periapsis separation, and at these radii both sit
      far enough below their circular Kepler values to fire the test about
      1.7 times earlier in the ratio than n = margin alpha / 2 pi suggests.
      The closed form is therefore a good guide in the outer region (1.0001
      at margin 300, 1.016 at 150) and not at the deep end.

131g. Q3: --ground-state-floor IS AN ACTION FLOOR AT n = 1, EXACTLY.  Its two
      clamps, measured against the actions:
        energy clamp   E >= -K/(2 a_pair)  ->  n = 1.000000000
        angular clamp  L >= hbar           ->  J_phi/h = 1.000000000
      These are the two halves of J_r + J_phi = h.  So R131c is YES: the flag
      is not a hand-set radius dressed up as physics, it is the
      Bohr-Sommerfeld condition, written twice.

131h. WHAT IT COSTS, WHICH IS THE ANSWER TO R4.  The prepared state already
      HAS n = 1 to nine digits, so an action floor at n = 1 forbids the first
      photon rather than the last: the code says so itself
      ("--ground-state-floor holds the orbit at a_pair, so there is no
      transit from it").  The collapse the model actually measures runs from
      n = 1 to n = 0.070711 at audit 108's endpoint of 0.005 a_pair, i.e. it
      spends 93% of the ground-state action, and the Compton barrier is at
      n = 0.042737.  So quantizing the orbital action does not stop the
      inspiral at a physical radius; it deletes the inspiral, and with it the
      30.672 ps that is this project's headline number.  The floor R4 asked
      for exists, is exact, and is incompatible with the measurement it was
      meant to protect.

131i. UNCHANGED.  Validation 62/62, header isolation 37/37, and the trace
      does not touch the trajectory: seed 42 run with CREM_ACTION_TRACE and
      without it gives lab 199.039646721 ps and proper 199.039646720 ps,
      identical to all printed digits.

131j. LIMITS.  Thirteen trajectories at one preparation (phenomenon 1,
      a_pair, para/free draw) and one pair; the margin test is two seeds per
      margin.  The ladder law assumes the emission rule and a Keplerian
      orbit, which is why its agreement degrades from 1.0000 to 0.986 on the
      last rung, where the dipole term is tens of percent of Coulomb.  The
      margin-50 runs are outside the model's declared domain by the code's
      own statement and are reported as bookkeeping probes, not physics.
      The action is computed from osculating elements, so it is the action of
      the instantaneous Kepler ellipse, not of the retarded two-body system.

132. THE TWO REPAIRS TO 131: THE MARGIN'S OWN RATIO, MEASURED, AND THE KEPLER
     LABEL'S BREAKDOWN, LOCALIZED TO ONE RUNG
==================================================================

132a. WHAT WAS OWED.  Section 131 left two debts.  131f asserted, without
      measuring it, that the estimator's period/light-crossing ratio sits
      about 1.7 times below the circular Kepler estimate 2 pi n / alpha at
      margin 50.  And 131 computed every action from the KEPLER formulas
      while the model's radial potential carries dipole terms, leaving three
      symptoms unexplained: J_r/h drifting to -0.0158, the landing law
      agreeing to 0.9861 on the last rung against 1.0000 earlier, and a 20%
      scatter in the margin-50 stop.

132b. THE MARGIN'S OWN RATIO (repair 1).  CREM_ACTION_TRACE now prints a
      second line with the estimator's own ratio, the Kepler estimate, the
      dipole-aware periapsis and the true separation.  At the stopping
      checkpoint:
        margin  n_stop     own ratio   Kepler estimate   over
          300   0.273285    235.305       235.305        1.000002
          150   0.096794     83.353        83.342        1.000142
           50   0.080655     48.544        69.446        1.4306
           50   0.097152     49.464        83.650        1.6911
      Rule R132a asked for the stop ratio within 10% of 50 and the Kepler
      estimate above it by 1.4 to 2.0: measured 48.54 and 49.46, over by
      1.43 and 1.69.  CONFIRMED, and the 1.7 I guessed in 131f is the upper
      of the two.  The new fact is the other half of the table: at margins
      150 and 300 the two ratios agree to 1e-4 and 2e-6, so the closed form
      is not an approximation there at all -- it is exact until the pair
      reaches the few-r* region, and then it fails by 43-69%.

132c. AND THE 20% SCATTER IS IN THE LABEL, NOT IN THE STOP.  The two
      margin-50 runs stop at ratios 48.54 and 49.46, a 2% spread, at
      separations 5.095 and 8.739 r*.  The stop condition is therefore as
      sharp there as anywhere; what scatters by 20% is n, because the map
      from the ratio to n is the Kepler one and it has broken down by 1.43
      versus 1.69 in those two states.  Section 131's third symptom was an
      artefact of the label.

132d. THE LABEL'S BREAKDOWN, MEASURED AGAINST THE MODEL'S OWN POTENTIAL
      (repair 2).  tools/radial_action_full_potential.cpp puts the model's
      own circular orbit at a radius -- L from dU/dr = 0 in
      U = -K/r + u_dd + u_so + L^2/2r^2, the same assembly
      dipoleAwarePeriapsis solves -- and prints the energy label against the
      angular one, which must agree on a circle:
        a/a_pair     r*      n from E     L/hbar      gap     |u_dd/u_C|
        1.000000  547.509   0.999998332  0.999994996  0.000003  3.34e-06
        0.333333  182.503   0.577341317  0.577323984  0.000017  3.00e-05
        0.074670   40.883   0.273177026  0.273013799  0.000163  5.97e-04
        0.009369    5.130   0.095752904  0.092509441  0.003243  3.24e-02
        0.006505    3.562   0.079956740  0.075226632  0.004730  5.63e-02
      With the dipole terms zeroed the same table gives a gap of 0.000000 at
      every radius, to all printed digits.  Rule R132d asked for the gap to
      fall below 10% of its value under that ablation; it falls to zero
      exactly.  ATTRIBUTED.
      The spin-orbit part contributes nothing in this geometry: u_so goes as
      (mu2 - mu1).(v x r), and with both moments along the orbital normal
      that difference vanishes identically, so the whole gap is u_dd.

132e. A CORRECTION TO MY OWN SIZE ESTIMATE.  I said the dipole term is "tens
      of percent of Coulomb at the last rung".  Measured, it is 3.24% at the
      stop radius of 5.13 r* and 5.63% at 3.56 r*.  The 44.7% quoted in the
      estimator's own comment is at the TERMINAL radius near r*, which these
      trajectories never reach: they stop at 4.3-8.7 r*.  The symptom sizes
      follow that scale, not the 44.7%.
