      The measured trace gap is -0.0054 typically and -0.0158 at the
      margin-50 deep end, against the probe's +0.0032 to +0.0047 at the same
      radii: same order, opposite sign.  The probe fixes the moments along
      the orbital normal and the real trajectory does not, so the sign of the
      residual is the moment orientation.  What is settled is the SOURCE
      (the ablation gives exactly zero) and the ORDER; the sign along a real
      trajectory is not.

132f. THE LANDING LAW IS EXACT; ONLY ITS KEPLER INPUT WAS NOT.  Feeding
      energy conservation the MEASURED photon energy instead of 2R/n^3,
      n' = sqrt(R/(R/n^2 + E_photon)) with R = 6.802846579 eV:
        n_before  E measured [eV]  2R/n^3 [eV]  ratio  landing law/meas.
        0.999980       13.604509      13.606510   0.9999        0.9998
        0.577269       70.670787      70.727118   0.9992        0.9968
        0.272406      634.225835     673.086167   0.9423        0.9999
      Rule R132c asked for 0.5%: the landings come out to 0.02%, 0.32% and
      0.01%.  EXACT.  Rule R132b's answer is the third rung: the photon
      energy is the Kepler correspondence quantum to 0.01% at n = 1 and to
      0.08% at n = 0.577, and departs by 5.8% at n = 0.272.  That 5.8% is
      the whole of 131c's 0.9861, which is therefore not a failure of the
      law but the Kepler value of R/n^3 standing in for the model's own
      orbital frequency at 40 r*.

132g. WHAT MOVES IN SECTION 131 AND WHAT DOES NOT (rule R132e).  None of the
      three verdicts moves: the stopping action is not an integer, the stop
      is the retardation margin (now with the estimator's own ratio printed
      at the stop in all four runs), and --ground-state-floor is the n = 1
      action floor -- that last one is algebra at a_pair, where the dipole is
      3.3e-06 of Coulomb, so nothing here touches it.
      Two numbers do move.  131e's "measured mean / predicted rung = 1.0155"
      is not a discrepancy: at that radius the label's own two definitions
      already differ by 3.4%, which is more than the 1.6% being compared.
      And 131c's last-rung ratio is explained rather than merely recorded.

132h. UNCHANGED.  Validation 62/62, header isolation 37/37; the new trace
      line is behind the same environment variable as the first and prints
      only, so the four runs reproduce section 131's trajectories.

132i. LIMITS.  Two seeds at margin 50 and one each at 150 and 300; the probe
      is circular orbits at six radii with the moments along the orbital
      normal and one pair.  The full radial action J_r = 2 integral p_r dr
      was NOT computed for an eccentric orbit, because along this collapse
      the orbit is circular to within the label gap itself -- which is the
      same statement as the gap being small, so it is an assumption this
      section does not test.  The probe mirrors dipoleAwarePeriapsis's
      turning-point state construction; the two energy functions themselves
      are called, not copied.

133. THE FULL RADIAL ACTION: WHERE IT EXISTS IT IS TINY, AND TWO THIRDS OF THE
     COLLAPSE IS NOT AN ORBIT OF THE MODEL'S OWN POTENTIAL
==================================================================

133a. THE ASSUMPTION BEING DROPPED.  Sections 131 and 132 read the actions off
      the Kepler closed forms, and 132i admitted that treating the collapse as
      circular in the model's own potential is the same statement as the gap
      being small.  tools/radial_action_eccentric.cpp integrates
        J_r = 2 integral_{r1}^{r2} mu sqrt(2 h(r)) dr,
        h(r) = eps + K/r - u_dd(r) - u_so(r,L) - L^2/(2 r^2),
      between the model's own turning points, h being the same radial function
      dipoleAwarePeriapsis solves.  The sqrt vanishes at both ends, so the
      quadrature runs in r = c - b cos(theta), where the integrand is smooth.
      CREM_ACTION_TRACE now prints eps, L, both moments and the orbit normal
      at every checkpoint, so the potential is the one the trajectory actually
      had; section 132's probe had to assume moments along the normal, and
      that is why its residual came out with the opposite sign.

133b. THE INTEGRATOR, VALIDATED (rule R133a).  With the dipole terms zeroed,
      against the closed form:
        e = 0.1   0.005012562893 vs 0.005012562893   rel 5.2e-15
        e = 0.5   0.133974596216 vs 0.133974596216   rel 0.0e+00
        e = 0.9   0.564110105646 vs 0.564110105646   rel 1.4e-15
      and the turning points at e = 0.5 come out 0.500000000000 and
      1.500000000000 a_pair.  R133a asked for 1e-8; measured 1e-15.

133c. WHERE THE RADIAL ACTION EXISTS, IT IS TINY (rule R133b).  Four
      trajectories, seeds 42 and 43 at margins 150 and 50:
        trace    accessible  no region  max J_r/h  max |Kepler J_r|  of J_phi
        150/42       14         55       0.000981      0.005433        1.96%
        150/43       24         45       0.000242      0.007154        2.56%
         50/42       26         56       0.002441      0.005433        1.96%
         50/43       27         50       0.000299      0.029187       23.10%
      R133b asked for J_r/h below 0.02 at every checkpoint: the largest
      anywhere is 0.0024.  So the circularity assumption of 131-132 was
      right about the SIZE of the radial action.

133d. AND WRONG ABOUT THE STATE (rule R133c).  At 45 to 56 of the 69 to 82
      checkpoints -- two thirds to four fifths -- the osculating pair (eps, L)
      has NO accessible radial region in the model's own potential: L exceeds
      what that energy allows.  This is exactly the over-circular seam the
      secular layer documents ("a value above the circular limit is not an
      ellipse with this energy") and it is where the Kepler J_r went negative.
      Now it is quantified: the excess reaches 1.96-2.56% of J_phi inside the
      declared domain and 23.1% in the margin-50 runs below it.

133e. SO THE SIGN QUESTION OF 132e HAS A THIRD ANSWER, NOT ONE OF MY TWO.
      R133e offered the choice "of order 0.005-0.016, hence the Kepler gap is
      real" or "near zero, hence a label artefact".  Neither.  At a state
      whose Kepler label reads -0.005361 the model's potential either has no
      orbit at all, which is most of them, or has one with J_r/h = +0.000020
      (checkpoint 65 of 150/42) -- a factor 270 below the label.  The negative
      Kepler value is not a radial action of any size; it is a flag that the
      elements and the potential disagree, and the estimator's own response to
      it is the projection to e = 0 that the action label then inherits.

133f. THE STOP IS ECCENTRIC, AND ITS ACTION IS TIGHTER THAN 131 REPORTED.
        trace     J_r/h    J_phi/h   total model   total Kepler   r_out/r_in
        150/42   0.000981  0.096794    0.097775      0.096794       1.3357
        150/43   0.000242  0.097645    0.097887      0.097645       1.0104
         50/42   0.000730  0.096469    0.097199      0.080655       1.0320
         50/43      -          -        no region     0.097152         -
      In the model's own potential the three measurable stops agree to 0.7%
      (0.097199 to 0.097887) while the Kepler label spread them over 20%
      (0.080655 to 0.097645).  Section 132c said that scatter was in the
      label; this is the measurement.  And at 150/42's stop the orbit is
      genuinely eccentric, r_out/r_in = 1.3357 or e about 0.14, where the
      Kepler label reported e = 0.

133g. CORRECTIONS TO 131 AND 132 (rule R133d).  The prepared state's total
      action is 1.000002029 h, not h to nine digits: the nine digits were a
      property of the Kepler labels, and in the model's potential the
      prepared orbit already has J_r/h = 0.000002 with r_out/r_in = 1.0040.
      The stopping action is 0.0975 +- 0.0004 rather than 0.0962 +- 0.0014,
      which is +1.0% for 150/42 and +20.5% for 50/42 -- over the 1% threshold
      R133d set, so it is recorded as a correction.

133h. NO VERDICT MOVES.  The stopping action is still not an integer: 0.0975
      against the nearest nonzero integer is a factor 10.3.  The stop is
      still the retardation margin, whose own ratio 132b printed.  And
      --ground-state-floor is still the n = 1 action floor, since at a_pair
      the whole correction is 2e-06.

133i. UNCHANGED.  Validation 62/62, header isolation 37/37, and the extended
      trace is inert: seed 42 stops with three photons and the same cause in
      every run of sections 131-133.

133j. LIMITS.  Four trajectories, two seeds, one pair, one preparation.  The
      spin-orbit term of h is exact at the turning points and is used as an
      r-dependent potential between them, which is what the estimator itself
      does; no separate check of that extension exists.  The quadrature is
      Gauss-Legendre in the cosine variable with 400 nodes, validated only
      against the Kepler case, where it is exact to 1e-15.  What is NOT
      offered here is a repaired action for the inaccessible states: for
      those, the model has no orbit and therefore no action, and this section
      counts them rather than inventing a value.

134. omega1 = omega2 IN THE CHARGE SECTOR IS A PROPERTY OF THE PAIR, NOT OF
     THE SECTOR -- AND THE TWO RATES ARE TWO DIFFERENT TERMS
==================================================================

134a. THE CLAIM BEING SHARPENED.  Section 116b measured that keeping only the
      partner's charge field in the precession conserves |S1+S2| exactly, and
      116c built on it the statement that the spin-orbit sector "rotates BOTH
      moments the same way, so it cannot change the angle between them".  That
      was one pair.  tools/spin_rate_symmetry.cpp runs the same ablation on
      any --pair, one trajectory each, seed 42.

134b. WHAT I EXPECTED, AGAINST THE PREMISE OF THE REQUEST.  The request
      expected the symmetry to vanish for mu+mu- as well as for p+e-.  I
      pre-registered the opposite for mu+mu-: in the centre-of-mass frame
      equal masses give v1 = -v2 and r_hat12 = -r_hat21, so with opposite
      charges each particle's field is the negative of the other's, the two
      q/m are opposite, and the rates coincide.  The equality rests on equal
      masses AND opposite charges, not on the species.

134c. MEASURED, charge sector only (CREM_SPIN_RATE_SECTOR=charge):
        pair     m1/m2     mu1/mu2  steps  |S| range  |w2|/|w1|   cos12 end
        e+e-      1.0000   1.000000   1390   0.00e+00     1.0000     0.148606
        mu+mu-    1.0000   1.000000    737   0.00e+00     1.0000     0.148606
        mu+e-   206.7683   0.004836   1791   3.57e-01   104.2375    -0.740515
        p+e-   1836.1527   0.001519   1782   3.59e-01   329.6547    -0.618998
      All four start at |S| = 0.652455 and cos12 = 0.148606.  Rule R134a is
      met: mu+mu- freezes |S| to 0.00e+00 with the rate ratio at 1.0000 and
      the mutual angle not moving in the last digit, exactly as e+e- does.
      Rule R134b is met too: both asymmetric pairs break it, by 357 and 359
      thousandths of hbar, which is the whole width their controls reach.
      So the answer is: a property of the PAIR.  116b's statement holds for
      equal masses and opposite charges and for nothing else; for p+e- and
      mu+e- the charge sector is not the conserving one, it is the driver.

134d. AND THE TWO RATES ARE TWO DIFFERENT TERMS, WHICH IS WHY THE RATIO IS SO
      LARGE.  In the charge sector each rate is magnetic plus Thomas,
        omega_mag = (g q / 2m) (mu0/4pi) e v_partner / r^2,
        omega_Th  = a x v / 2 c^2,
      both evaluated on the prepared circular orbit against the traced first
      substep:
        pair     particle   traced        magnetic      Thomas
        p+e-     heavy     3.34465e+09   x1.0005       x10261
                 light     1.10268e+12   x920.1         x1.0034
        mu+e-    heavy     1.05321e+10   x1.0025       x415.0
                 light     1.09793e+12   x104.5        x1.0120
        e+e-     both      4.13417e+11   x1.5006       x3.0046
        mu+mu-   both      8.54822e+13   x1.5006       x3.0047
      For the symmetric pairs the traced rate is the SUM: Thomas is exactly
      half the magnetic term (g = 2), so traced = 1.5 x magnetic, and BOTH
      terms are separately equal between the two particles -- which is why
      the conservation is exact rather than approximate.  For the asymmetric
      pairs each equality breaks and, more than that, the two particles end
      up dominated by DIFFERENT terms: the heavy one by the magnetic rate
      (to 0.05% and 0.25%), the light one by Thomas (to 0.34% and 1.2%).
      The naive expectation from the magnetic term alone would have been
      |w2|/|w1| = 0.3585 for p+e-; measured 329.68, a factor 920, and that
      factor is the electron's Thomas precession.

134e. WHERE THE ASYMMETRIC PAIRS TAKE |S|.  Up, to the ceiling: |S| max is
      0.999594 hbar for mu+e- and 0.999928 for p+e-, against the exact
      ceiling of hbar that audit 128 established, while the mutual moments
      swing from cos12 = +0.1486 to -0.62 and -0.74.  For e+e- and mu+mu-
      the full sector moves |S| DOWN instead (minima 0.4523 and 0.3672).

134f. A METHODOLOGICAL CORRECTION TO 116b AND 117d (rule R134e).  Their
      charge-sector row reproduces exactly: 0.000 then, 0.00e+00 now.  Their
      CONTROL row does not: 116b recorded a drift of 0.138 and this run
      gives an endpoint difference of 0.0338.  The reason is not the pair or
      the code -- it is the statistic.  |S1+S2| OSCILLATES along the
      trajectory: 65 turning points on the e+e- control, between 0.4523 and
      0.7183.  The endpoint difference is therefore a phase sample, while the
      RANGE reproduces: 0.266 now against 0.294 then, 10%.  The "drift"
      columns of 116b and 117d should be read as range, and the tables in
      this section report range for that reason.  Switching the
      collapse-transit second run on (CREM_COLLAPSE_TRANSIT=1) changes
      nothing, which was the other candidate and is now excluded.

134g. WHAT COULD NOT BE TESTED.  p+antip was the natural fourth pair, being
      symmetric at yet another scale, and it cannot be run: its a_pair is
      5.764e-14 m, INSIDE the model's own Compton barrier of 1.933e-13 m, so
      the trajectory stops at once with the barrier as its cause and no
      substeps at all.  The model's validity limit excludes its own heaviest
      symmetric pair.

134h. WHAT THIS DOES TO 116c.  116c concluded that the root of the |S| drift
      is the dipole tensor term "rather than the spin-orbit coupling that an
      L != 0 state brings in".  That conclusion is now bounded: it is true
      for a pair with equal masses and opposite charges, where the spin-orbit
      sector cancels exactly, and false in general -- for p+e- the spin-orbit
      sector alone produces the entire drift.  The quantum statement it was
      compared against (S^2 is a good quantum number once the tensor term
      averages away) also holds only for equal masses, since hydrogen's
      hyperfine structure has exactly this asymmetry.

134i. LIMITS.  One trajectory, one seed, one preparation per pair; no photon
      cascade (the production default since audit 109).  The rate
      attribution of 134d is evaluated at the PREPARED orbit and compared
      with the first traced substep, not integrated along the trajectory.
      The mu+mu- and p+antip runs stop on the Compton barrier rather than the
      retardation margin, so their covered ranges are shorter (737 and zero
      substeps); the e+e- and mu+e- and p+e- runs stop on the margin.

135. THE LIBRATION'S OWN M1 RADIATION: THE BOOKED CHANNEL IS A DIFFERENT
     MODULATION, AND NOTHING DAMPS THE LIBRATION EITHER WAY
==================================================================

135a. THE QUESTION.  The mutual angle librates and the pair's total moment
      swings with it, so it is an M1 source.  Does the model's own M1 channel
      carry that radiation, and does it damp the libration?  If not, the
      libration amplitude behind sections 113 and 115 has no sink.

135b. WHAT THE MODEL BOOKS, AND WHERE.  coherentMagneticDipoleOrbitAveraged-
      Emission computes P_M1 = mu0 <|d2(mu1+mu2)/dt2|^2>/(6 pi c^3) from the
      two BMT rates at the ORBITAL phase nodes, with the moments held fixed
      across the average, and CREM_M1_SHARE prints it beside E1 per
      checkpoint.  It is gated on isStochastic.
      Rule R135c, measured: under the production reaction model (individual
      Landau-Lifshitz, the default since audit 109) the printed line reads
      E1 = 0.000000e+00 and M1 = 0.000000e+00 at every checkpoint.  This
      secular radiated-power accounting is evaluated only in the photon
      cascade; production's sink is the per-particle Landau-Lifshitz force,
      which is a charge-acceleration term and carries no magnetic-dipole
      piece at all.  So on the trajectories 113 and 115 were measured on, the
      M1 channel books nothing.

135c. THE MEASUREMENT, in the stochastic path where it is evaluated.  One
      cascade, seed 42, 6360 substeps, with the moments traced.  Over the
      first 2.49e-11 s (about two libration periods, 110 samples each)
        |mu1+mu2|/muB   min 1.502752   max 1.862441   amplitude 0.1798 muB
      and the second derivative of the traced total moment, swept over
      stencil widths (rule R135e):
        stride   span/T_lib   <|m''|^2>     P [W]
             1      0.018     2.9135e+02   7.21e-31     integrator jitter
             2      0.036     2.0074e+01   4.97e-32
             5      0.090     1.1541e+01   2.86e-32
            10      0.181     1.0825e+01   2.68e-32     the plateau
            20      0.361     8.6703e+00   2.15e-32
            40      0.722     3.5328e+00   8.74e-33     stencil too wide
            80      1.445     3.0848e-01   7.63e-34
      The substep stencil is noise: it disagrees with the next width by a
      factor 15.  The plateau at strides 5-20 varies by 30%, so this is an
      order-of-magnitude measurement, which R135e says is then what it is
      called.

135d. THE CHANNEL IS NOT CLOSED, AND THE REASON IS A SCALE.  Against the
      1.431974e-23 W the model books at the same instant, the traced
      2.678280e-32 W is a factor 5.3e+08 below -- rule R135a asked for 2.
      The three scales, all evaluated on the same state:
        source                                     |m''|        P [W]
        libration,  omega_lib^2 A                 5.40e-01    7.21e-34
        precession, omega_prec^2 |m|              2.40e+00    1.43e-32
        precession modulated at the ORBIT,
          omega_prec omega_orb |m|                1.20e+05    3.57e-23
        what the model books                      7.61e+04    1.43e-23
        what the moments trace                    3.29e+00    2.68e-32
      The booked number sits on the orbit-modulated line, to a factor 0.401,
      while the traced moments sit on the precession line, to 1.37.  The
      ratio omega_orb/omega_lib is 3.6e+04, and its square is the 1.3e+09
      that separates the two columns.  So the model's M1 channel is not
      blind to the moments -- it radiates from their precession modulated at
      the orbital period, which is a real and much faster process -- but the
      libration's own contribution is nine orders below what that channel
      books, and cannot be seen inside it.

135e. AND NOTHING DAMPS THE LIBRATION, WHICH IS WHAT THE QUESTION WAS FOR.
      With E_lib = J omega_lib = 5.91e-24 J (J = 0.0985 hbar, audit 129):
        sink assumed                fraction of E_lib per       damping time
                                    libration period
        the traced libration M1          5.0e-20              2.2e+08 s
        ALL of the booked M1             2.7e-11              4.1e-01 s
      against a collapse of 30.672 ps.  Rule R135b asked for 1% per period;
      the generous case misses it by nine orders and the honest one by
      eighteen.  Even crediting the libration with the entire M1 power the
      model books -- which 135d shows it does not own -- the damping time is
      1.3e+10 collapse times.

135f. SO 113 AND 115 ARE NOT AFFECTED, BUT NOT FOR THE REASON PROPOSED.  The
      concern was that a missing sink inflates the libration amplitude.  The
      sink is indeed missing, twice over -- absent in production by the
      isStochastic gate, and nine orders too small in the cascade -- but it
      could not have acted: a channel that removes 5e-20 of the libration
      energy per period cannot set its amplitude over a 30.672 ps collapse.
      The libration amplitude is a property of the initial mutual angle and
      the precession rates, as 129 measured, and radiation has no part in it.

135g. LIMITS.  One trajectory, one seed; the resolved second derivative is an
      order-of-magnitude number by 135c's own stencil sweep, which is enough
      for a 5.3e+08 comparison and would not be for a factor 2.  The window
      is the first two libration periods at a_pair, where the libration is
      slowest and cleanest; deeper in the cascade the libration frequency
      rises by five orders (129) and this section does not follow it there,
      so the damping ratios quoted are for the prepared state.  The scales of
      135d are evaluated analytically on the prepared orbit, not integrated.

136. A CORRECTION TO 135b: PRODUCTION IS NOT BLIND TO M1.  THE CHANNEL IS
     CLOSED EXACTLY, AND WHAT WAS ZERO WAS THE PRINT
==================================================================

136a. THE SENTENCE BEING WITHDRAWN.  Section 135b read CREM_M1_SHARE's zeros
      under the production reaction model and concluded that "on the
      trajectories 113 and 115 were measured on, the M1 channel books
      nothing".  That is wrong, and the mistake was reading a diagnostic as
      if it were the dynamics.  The line prints the SECULAR analytic
      decomposition, which is gated on isStochastic -- and so is its E1
      counterpart, which printed zero as well although production plainly
      radiates.  The engine meanwhile carries dipoleRadiationReaction, whose
      torques applyDipoleRadiationTorque applies under every reaction model
      except `disabled`.

136b. THE TORQUE IS LIVE IN PRODUCTION, MEASURED (rule R136a).  A new
      diagnostic ablation, CREM_NO_M1_TORQUE, suppresses that one channel and
      leaves the charge sector alone.  One production trajectory, seed 42,
      with and without it:
        the moment traces are bit-identical for 276 substeps, diverge at 277
        by 1.87e-06 relative, and reach 3.37e-06 by the end;
        in the photon cascade the collapse time moves from 199.039646721 ps
        to 199.039329891 ps, a relative 1.6e-06.
      So the M1 reaction acts, and it acts through the moment ORIENTATION
      rather than through the energy: the power ratio M1/E1 is 1.2e-15 at
      a_pair, but the reorientation it causes feeds the dipole force and
      moves the trajectory a million times more than that.

136c. THE DIAGNOSTIC, REPAIRED (rule R136c).  CREM_M1_SHARE now evaluates
      both secular powers under any reaction model, for the print only;
      nothing downstream reads them.  Production reports, at a_pair,
        E1 = 1.167257e-08 W   M1 = 1.431936e-23 W   share 1.2268e-15
      instead of two zeros, and the trajectory is unchanged: seed 42 still
      gives 199.039646721 ps, bit-identical to sections 131-133.

136d. THE CHANNEL IS CLOSED, AND NOT ONLY WITHIN A FACTOR 2 (rule R136b).
      On a trajectory M1/E1 is 1e-15 and the balance is below double
      precision, so it is measured on a controlled configuration instead: one
      moment precessing uniformly at the production rate with a 30 degree
      cone, the model's own dipoleRadiationReaction called on that history.
      The work the torque does on the precession against the flux it emits:
        history step / period    P flux [W]      -N.Omega [W]      ratio
              1.0e-02          1.553597811e-33  1.599911815e-33  1.0298108
              3.0e-03          1.557323709e-33  1.561560106e-33  1.0027203
              1.0e-03          1.557651645e-33  1.558123025e-33  1.0003026
              3.0e-04          1.557688952e-33  1.557731383e-33  1.0000272
              1.0e-04          1.557692231e-33  1.557696944e-33  1.0000030
      The ratio converges on 1 as the history is refined, so the residual is
      the finite-difference stencil and not the physics: the torque removes
      exactly the energy the flux carries, with the right sign.  The flux
      itself matches the closed form k mu^2 Omega^4 sin^2(theta) to 3e-07.

136e. AND THE CONE DAMPING THAT IMPLIES.  The transverse part of the same
      torque closes the precession cone at d(theta)/dt = -1.43e-10 rad/s, a
      time constant of 3.66e+09 s, or 1.19e+20 collapse times.  That is the
      same verdict section 135e reached from the other side and by another
      route: the channel is real, it is exact, and it damps nothing on any
      timescale this model runs.

136f. WHAT PRODUCTION ACTUALLY OMITS, WHICH IS NOTHING (T3).  In the
      continuous path the loss applied across skipped orbits is the MEASURED
      energy change of an integrated orbit, and the M1 torque acts inside
      that measurement.  There is no missing term to add: adding the secular
      M1 power there would charge the same radiation twice.  The M1 share
      along a production collapse, now that it prints:
        a [m]        E1 [W]        M1 [W]        M1/(E1+M1)
        1.058e-10  1.167257e-08  1.431936e-23   1.2268e-15
        4.089e-11  5.240412e-07  5.969568e-20   1.1391e-13
        1.245e-11  6.082708e-05  2.700424e-15   4.4395e-11
        3.796e-12  6.974539e-03  1.543750e-10   2.2134e-08
      It climbs seven orders as the orbit tightens and is still 2.2e-08 of
      the radiated power at the last checkpoint.

136g. ONE THING THIS DID FIND, LEFT OPEN.  The two reaction models charge the
      M1 energy to DIFFERENT reservoirs.  The cascade's secular formula
      subtracts magneticEmissionForLoss.power from the ORBITAL energy, while
      the engine's torque takes the same radiation out of the moments'
      ORIENTATION, and in the cascade both act.  Which orbits are charged
      twice depends on whether the measured orbit is inside orbitsToSkip,
      which this section did not settle.  The size bounds the question: the
      overlap is at most the share of 136f, so between 1.2e-15 and 2.2e-08 of
      the loss.  Physically the torque is the one that belongs -- M1 from a
      precessing moment is paid for by the precession, not by the orbit --
      so the candidate repair is to drop the magnetic term from
      expectedLossPerOrbit rather than to add one anywhere.  It is not made
      here: it would move every cascade number for an effect of 1e-15, and
      that is a modelling decision rather than a bug fix.

136h. UNCHANGED.  Validation 62/62, header isolation 37/37, and the
      production trajectory is bit-identical with both new switches unset.
      CREM_NO_M1_TORQUE is a diagnostic ablation, off by default.

136i. LIMITS.  One trajectory per ablation, one seed.  The closure test is
      uniform precession of a single moment with the second zeroed, chosen so
      the balance is read without the coherent interference term; the
      coherent case is covered by the existing validation check on aligned
      versus cancelling pairs, which tests the power and not this balance.
      The cone damping is quoted at the prepared state's precession rate.

137. THE REPAIR 136g RECOMMENDED IS REFUSED, AND THE RECOMMENDATION IS
     WITHDRAWN: THE TORQUE COVERS 0.008% OF THE TRAJECTORY
==================================================================

137a. WHAT WAS ASKED.  Carry out 136g's candidate repair: drop the magnetic
      term from expectedLossPerOrbit, since the engine's M1 torque already
      takes that energy out of the moments.  Rule R137a said to make it only
      if the torque actually covers the trajectory, and R137b said to refuse
      and withdraw the recommendation if it covers less than 1%.

137b. THE DECIDING NUMBER.  One cascade, seed 42:
        total revolutions                       862 383
        mechanically integrated orbits               68
        fraction the torque can act on         7.885e-05
      The estimator exists in order to skip orbits, and it skips 99.992% of
      them.  Over those the mechanics never runs, so the torque never fires,
      and the secular formula is the ONLY thing that accounts for M1 there.
      Removing its magnetic term would leave 0.999921 of the M1 energy
      unaccounted -- a gap 12 700 times larger than the overlap it closes.
      R137b applies: the repair is refused and 136g's recommendation is
      withdrawn.

137c. AND THE OVERLAP, KEPT AS A NUMBER (rule R137c).  Where both do act --
      the 68 measured orbits -- the same radiation is charged twice, to the
      orbit by the formula and to the moments by the torque.  That overlap is
      7.885e-05 of the M1 energy, which is itself between 1.2268e-15 and
      2.2134e-08 of the radiated power, so between 9.7e-20 and 1.7e-12 of the
      total.  It is a real inconsistency and it is eighteen orders below
      anything the model reports.

137d. AND THEY ARE NOT EVEN THE SAME QUANTITY (rule R137d).  "Double
      counting" was the wrong description twice over.  The secular formula
      builds <|mu''|^2> from the BMT rates differentiated AROUND THE ORBIT
      and gets 1.43e-23 W at a_pair; the torque builds it from the moments'
      own history, and on the uniform precession of section 136d that is
      1.56e-33 W.  The ten orders between them are the orbital modulation
      (section 135d measured the same gap from the other side).  On a
      mechanically integrated orbit the history does carry that modulation,
      so the two approach each other there; on a skipped orbit there is no
      history at all.  So the formula and the torque are not two accounts of
      one number, they are one number's two regimes.

137e. WHAT WOULD ACTUALLY BE RIGHT, AND ITS SIZE.  The reservoir mismatch
      132g named survives: over skipped orbits the formula charges the M1
      energy to the ORBIT, while physically a precessing moment pays for its
      own M1 radiation out of the precession.  Fixing that means adding a
      secular spin damping to the skipped orbits, not removing anything.  Its
      size is already measured: 136e's cone time constant is 3.66e+09 s
      against a 30.672 ps collapse, so over a whole collapse the correct sink
      would close the precession cone by 8.4e-21 of itself.  That is why this
      is not worth building: the right repair is real, well defined, and
      changes the twenty-first digit.

137f. WHAT I GOT WRONG, PLAINLY.  136g proposed a repair without measuring
      the fraction of the trajectory each mechanism covers, and without
      noticing that the magnetic term also carries the M1 photon channel's
      hazard split, so removing it deletes a channel rather than a line of
      bookkeeping.  Both were one reading away.  The audit keeps 136g as
      written and this section as its correction.

137g. UNCHANGED.  No model file was modified by this section; validation
      62/62 and header isolation 37/37 stand from 136, and the cascade is
      bit-identical.

138. THE LADDER IN THE MODEL'S OWN ACTION: THE THIRD RUNG HAS THREE
     WELL-DEFINED VALUES, 4.4% APART
==================================================================

138a. WHAT WAS STILL KEPLER.  Sections 132 and 133 measured the label's
      breakdown and integrated the full radial action, but 131c's LADDER was
      still quoted in n = (K/hbar) sqrt(mu/2|E|), and its law was derived from
      E = -R/n^2.  tools/action_ladder_full_potential.cpp walks the same
      ladder in the model's own action: it inverts the energy to the model's
      circular radius in U = -K/r + u_dd + u_so + L^2/2r^2 and reads J/h
      there, using the MEASURED photon energies of the trace.

138b. THE INVERSION, VALIDATED (rule R138c).  Going r -> E -> r recovers the
      radius to 2e-11..2e-10 at every rung, and the action it reports at those
      radii -- 0.999994996, 0.577323984, 0.273013799, 0.092509441 -- is
      section 132d's table to all nine digits.  R138c asked for 1e-03.

138c. THE TWO LADDERS.
        photon   E [eV]        Kepler n'     model J'/h   model/Kepler
        start        -        1.000000000    0.999996664    0.99999666
            1   13.604509     0.577367016    0.577349685    0.99996998
            2   70.670787     0.273299133    0.273136124    0.99940355
            3  634.225835     0.096846807    0.093690532    0.96740961
      Rule R138a reports a rung wherever the two differ by more than 0.1%:
      the first two do not (0.003% and 0.06%), the third does, by 3.3%.  So
      the correction to 131c is confined to the last rung, which is where 132
      and 133 found everything else as well.

138d. AND THE THIRD RUNG HAS THREE VALUES, NOT TWO.
        0.093691   the model's action if the landing were CIRCULAR in its own
                   potential, with the moments along the orbital normal
        0.096847   the Kepler label of the same energy (and the trace's own
                   n, which it reproduces to 0.05%)
        0.097775   the action actually traced there (section 133f), which is
                   J_phi 0.096794 plus J_r 0.000981 on an orbit with
                   r_out/r_in = 1.3357
      They span 4.4%.  Rule R138b asked the model ladder to reproduce the
      traced landing to 1% and it does not, so the residual is reported and
      not absorbed.  Its two parts are both already named: the landing is NOT
      circular in the model's potential (133f measured e about 0.14 there),
      and this probe fixes the moments along the normal while the trajectory
      does not (132e measured that this flips the sign of the label gap).
      Attributing the 4.4% between those two would need the eccentric action
      walked with the traced moments, which is section 133's integrator fed
      by this section's inversion, and is not done here.

138e. WHAT THIS SETTLES AND WHAT IT DOES NOT.  Settled: the ladder of 131c is
      a Kepler label, it is accurate to 0.06% for the first two rungs, and it
      is 3.3% off at the third.  The three symptoms 132 and 133 chased --
      J_r going negative, the landing law's 0.9861, the margin-50 scatter --
      are all the same fact seen three ways, and this adds the fourth view:
      the rung itself moves.  Not settled: which of the two causes carries
      the 4.4%, and that is the only piece of the "Kepler label on a
      non-Kepler orbit" thread still open.

138f. NO VERDICT MOVES (rule R138d).  0.0937, 0.0968 and 0.0978 are equally
      far from any nonzero integer; the stop is still the retardation margin,
      whose own ratio 132b printed; and --ground-state-floor is still the
      n = 1 action floor, since at a_pair the three values agree to 3e-06.

138g. LIMITS.  Circular orbits with the moments along the orbital normal, one
      pair, the three measured photon energies of one trace.  The model's
      ladder assumes each landing is circular, which 133f shows the third is
      not; that assumption is the subject of 138d rather than a hidden step.

139. THE MOMENT RADIUS: A DEPENDENCY MAP.  THE HEADLINE NUMBER DOES NOT MOVE,
     AND THE MODEL'S OWN VALUE SITS ON A PEAK OF THE RESIDUAL
==================================================================

139a. WHAT THIS IS.  magneticDipoleRadius() = 0.9668 r* comes from a current
      loop, not a fit, and since audit 126 the same length softens the charge
      side of every moment-charge interaction as well.  This section does not
      test that value and prefers none (rule R139d): it maps what moves when
      the constant does.  CREM_MAGNETIC_RADIUS_SCALE sets it in units of r*
      and is read once into a static, so one process per value.

139b. THE MAP.  Eight scales, from the old separation floor to section 118's
      452 r*.  Transit and coupling from one production trajectory each
      (seed 42); |P| and the ledger from one engine orbit each (para, 1 r*,
      tilt 0, retarded forces, reaction off, 256 steps).
        scale   transit   split      split      |P|/(m c)  ledger   r range
        [r*]    [ps]      a_pair     2 r*                  [|U_dd|]  [r*]
        0.05    30.68753  21.94939   4.490e+08  4.019e-04  1.31e-02 1.00-8.05
        0.2417  30.68753  21.94937   4.216e+08  3.285e-04  9.15e-03 1.00-6.81
        0.4834  30.68753  21.94931   3.451e+08  1.379e-04  1.03e-02 1.00-3.00
        0.9668  30.68588  21.94908   1.419e+08  6.135e-03  1.33e+00 0.36-1.00
        1.9336  30.68751  21.94816   7.520e+07  1.015e-04  1.53e-01 0.78-1.00
        3.8672  30.68747  21.94446   5.967e+07  1.713e-06  3.59e-01 0.98-1.00
        9.668   30.74205  21.91861   7.026e+06  3.987e-08  4.53e+00 0.98-1.01
        452     30.66528   2.17353   7.802e+01  4.935e-09  4.49e+05 0.98-1.01
      The ledger column is normalized by |U_dd|, which itself moves with the
      scale, so only its orders are comparable across rows.

139c. THE VERDICTS, on doubling the radius from its default (rule R139a).
        observable            default      doubled     change    verdict
        collapse transit      30.6859 ps   30.6875 ps   +0.005%  INSENSITIVE
        splitting at a_pair   21.9491 GHz  21.9482 GHz  -0.004%  INSENSITIVE
        revolutions            201 090      201 545     +0.23%   marginal
        splitting at 1 r*     6.016e+08    4.774e+08    -20.6%   SENSITIVE
        splitting at 2 r*     1.419e+08    7.520e+07    -47.0%   SENSITIVE
        ledger range          1.328        0.153        -88.5%   SENSITIVE
        |P|/(m c)             6.135e-03    1.015e-04    -98.3%   SENSITIVE
      Logarithmic slopes from the default to four times it (rule R139b):
      transit +0.0000, splitting at a_pair -0.0002, at 2 r* -0.6248, at
      1 r* -1.2771, ledger -0.9440, |P| -5.9032.

139d. SO THE HEADLINE NUMBER IS SAFE AND THE NEAR FIELD IS NOT.  The collapse
      transit moves by 5e-05 across a factor of 77 in the constant (0.05 to
      3.87 r*), by 0.18% at 9.668 and by -0.07% even at 452 r*, where the
      moment radius is comparable to the orbit.  The splitting analogue at
      a_pair is equally immovable until that point, then falls by a factor
      ten.  Everything evaluated inside a few r* -- the coupling there, the
      ledger, the self-propulsion residual, the depth of the passage -- moves
      by tens of percent for a factor of two.

139e. AND THE MODEL'S VALUE SITS ON A PEAK, WHICH I DID NOT EXPECT.
        scale [r*]   0.05   0.2417  0.4834  0.9668  1.9336  3.8672  9.668
        |P|/(m c)   4.0e-4  3.3e-4  1.4e-4  6.1e-3  1.0e-4  1.7e-6  4.0e-8
      The residual self-propulsion is NOT monotone in the radius: it is a
      few 1e-04 below the default, 6.1e-03 AT it, and falls by five orders
      above it.  The ledger range does the same (1.33 at the default against
      1.0e-02 below and 1.5e-01 above), and the default is the only scale in
      this scan at which the para orbit at 1 r* plunges to 0.36 r* instead of
      staying near its start.  So the deep-passage behaviour that sections
      103g, 107e and 127 chased is specific to a narrow band of this constant
      around the value the model uses.
      Rule R139c is nevertheless met, and that is the point: |P| stays at or
      below 6.1e-03 m c at EVERY scale, five orders under the 1.17 m c of the
      broken construction (audit 123).  Audit 126's claim that the matching
      of the two lengths is what removed the self-propulsion, not their
      value, survives the scan -- with the sharpening that what is left over
      after the matching peaks where the model happens to sit.

139f. THE 452 r* POINT (rule R139e).  It runs without failing, and it is not
      a candidate for anything: the splitting analogue at a_pair falls from
      21.95 to 2.17 GHz, the near field is flattened to 78 GHz at both 1 and
      2 r*, and the ledger range reaches 4.5e+05 |U_dd|.  A moment radius of
      452 r* is 0.83 of the pair's own semi-major axis, so the dipole field
      is nearly uniform across the orbit and the construction is far outside
      the domain it was built for.  Section 118's requirement is a statement
      about what the contact term would need, not a value this model could
      take.

139g. LIMITS.  One trajectory and one seed per scale; one engine orbit per
      scale, para and tilt 0 only, so the para/ortho difference is NOT in
      this map -- it needs the paired protocol and was too expensive here.
      The transit values differ from the audit's 30.672 ps because that is an
      ensemble median and this is one seed.  The default row uses 0.9668
      exactly while the model computes 0.966823, which is why its transit
      sits 5e-05 off the trend of its neighbours rather than exactly on it.

140. THE PER-PHOTON ANGULAR MOMENTUM: CLOSING IT EXACTLY DELETES THE CASCADE,
     AND THE SWITCH THAT WAS SUPPOSED TO CLOSE IT DOES NOT
==================================================================

140a. THE DEFECT.  Energy leaves in quanta hbar omega while angular momentum
      is removed by the classical secular law, so the two bookkeepings
      disagree photon by photon (audit 21b).  Sharper: before the first
      emission L = 1.0000 hbar, so a photon carrying its own hbar would land
      the orbit at L = 0.  CREM_SPIN_MAGNITUDE exists for this, and the
      comment beside it records "the orbit surrenders 0.879 of what the
      photon demands against 0.266 with k(e)", with the old objection about
      unphysical elements already marked stale.  The comment asks for an
      ensemble instead of its four seeds, and that is this section.

140b. AN INSTRUMENT HAD TO BE FIXED FIRST.  CREM_L_UPDATE prints the
      CLASSICAL candidate as Lnew even when the switch installs something
      else, so the per-photon closure could not be read from it at all.  A
      second line, CREM_LUSED, now prints the value actually installed.  It
      is a diagnostic behind the same variable; production is bit-identical
      (seed 42 still 199.039646721 ps), validation 62/62, headers 37/37.

140c. THE DEFAULT, MEASURED.  Eight seeds, 24 emissions:
        surrendered per photon   mean 0.3054 hbar, range 0.1799..0.4228
        e^2 at the emission      0.000000 in every case
        photon energy            0.9879 of the correspondence quantum 2R/n^3
      So today the ENERGY bookkeeping is closed to 1.2% and the ANGULAR one
      is open at 0.31 of a quantum, which is the defect stated as two
      numbers.

140d. AND THE SWITCH ALONE DOES NOT CLOSE IT.  Same eight seeds, 15
      emissions with CREM_SPIN_MAGNITUDE:
        surrendered per photon   mean 0.2927 hbar, range 0.0534..0.6906
        e^2                      never negative (maximum 3.7e-05)
        photon energy            0.5024 of the correspondence quantum
        cascades completed       0 of 8 within 1800 s, against 8 of 8
                                 within 150 s for the default
      The angular closure is NOT improved -- 0.2927 against 0.3054, the same
      to the spread -- while the energy quantum is halved and the cost is
      total.  The comment's 0.879 is a ratio to the photon's own PROJECTED
      demand, not the hbar it removes from |L|; read as the latter it does
      not reproduce, and the comment is corrected here.  Its "unphysical
      elements are stale" half does reproduce: e^2 is never negative.

140e. THE REASON IS GEOMETRY, AND THE COMBINATION THAT FIXES IT.  A photon
      carries hbar along its OWN flight direction, so its projection on the
      orbital axis is hbar cos(theta) < hbar, and |L| can only lose the
      projection.  CREM_AXIAL_SPIN takes the photon's hbar along the orbital
      axis instead.  With both switches, eight seeds:
        surrendered per photon   1.000000 hbar on every seed, range zero
        L after the first photon 0.000027 hbar
      Exactly one quantum, and the orbit lands on L = 0.

140f. WHERE IT LANDS IS THE WHOLE POINT.  With L = 0 the cascade ENDS:
      every one of the eight seeds emits exactly ONE photon, and the pair
      then falls radially, five of the eight reaching the Compton barrier in
      16 to 47 seconds of wall clock.  Against the default's three to five
      photons.  So the audit's own sharper claim is now a measurement rather
      than an argument: the cascade past the first photon exists only because
      angular momentum is not conserved photon by photon.

140g. SO THIS CANNOT BE REPAIRED BY A DEFAULT, AND THE SWITCH IS NOT MADE ONE
      (rule R140d).  Closing the angular bookkeeping exactly deletes the
      cascade; leaving it open keeps the cascade and the contradiction.  The
      code's own comment names the only real repair -- draw the photon's
      energy and its hbar consistently with each other, which is a change to
      the EMISSION rather than to a subtraction line -- and this section adds
      what that repair would have to accept: the orbit has exactly one hbar
      to give, so any emission model that conserves angular momentum per
      photon produces a one-photon cascade from the prepared state.  There is
      no version of this model in which four E1 photons leave an L = hbar
      orbit.
      Rule R140a is therefore answered in its own terms: (a) fails for the
      switch alone at 0.2927 and passes exactly for the combination; (b)
      passes everywhere; (c) fails for the switch alone, 0 of 8.

140h. WHAT THIS DOES NOT TOUCH (rule R140e).  The energy sector's own
      version of the same theme -- the dipole potential is velocity
      dependent, so the force does not differentiate it and KE+U is not what
      the dynamics conserves -- is a separate defect and is not addressed
      here.  Nor is the alpha^-5 identity affected: it is the continuous
      inspiral in closed form, with no photon ladder in it.

140i. LIMITS.  Eight seeds, one preparation, phenomenon 1.  The short runs
      are 150 s budgets, so they measure the first one to three emissions of
      each cascade rather than whole trajectories; the completion counts come
      from the 1800 s ensemble.  The axial combination was measured on the
      first photon only, because there is no second one.

141. THE MISSING 31% WAS REMOVED BY AUDIT 126, AND THE SECOND PIECE MUST NOT
     BE ENABLED: IT IS NOW 81 TO 38 000 TIMES THE RESIDUAL
==================================================================

141a. THE QUESTION.  Prepare to enable the second piece of the residual -- the
      gradient-minus-convective excess S, the two rotation terms that
      ∇(mu.B + p.E) carries beyond (mu.∇)B and (p.∇)E -- and find the 31% of
      the original imbalance that the 46/23/31 ledger never attributed.

141b. THE LEDGER IS PRE-126, AND THAT IS THE ANSWER TO BOTH HALVES.
      tools/uniform_motion_balance in circular mode on the CURRENT model,
      against the numbers the ledger was built from:
        beta   |F1+F2| now    |F1+F2| then   shrinkage   |S| now     |S| then
        0.01   3.184e-09 N    2.854e-04 N     89 637x    1.221e-04   1.221e-04
        0.10   3.287e-06 N    2.856e-03 N        869x    1.198e-03   1.198e-03
        0.20   2.825e-05 N    5.709e-03 N        202x    2.289e-03   2.289e-03
      S reproduces the ledger exactly, to every digit quoted.  The residual
      does not: it has collapsed, and it has changed ORDER.  Log-log slopes:
        residual then   1.0003 and 0.9992   -- first order in beta
        S               0.9918 and 0.9341   -- first order in beta
        residual now    3.0138 and 3.1034   -- THIRD order in beta
      So audit 126's matched softening removed the whole first-order-in-beta
      imbalance.  What is left is a different object.

141c. THE SECOND PIECE IS NOT ENABLED (rule R141a).  S is first order in
      beta and unchanged, so against the residual it now stands at
        beta 0.01   38 342x      beta 0.10   364x      beta 0.20   81x
      Adding it would not explain 42% of an imbalance; it would create one
      one to four orders larger than the imbalance that exists.  Its physical
      pedigree does not survive that arithmetic, and in any case the model
      already carries the standard Ampèrian pair ∇(m.B) - (1/c^2) d(m x E)/dt
      since audit 124, so the rotation terms would be the second copy.

141d. AND THE MISSING 31% GOES WITH IT (rule R141b).  Thirty-one percent of
      the old residual is 27 788, 269 and 63 times the whole current
      residual at the three speeds.  There is no room for it anywhere in
      what the model now does: it was removed by 126 together with the 46%
      and the 23% that had been attributed.  The ledger is closed, not by
      finding the last term, but by the defect it was accounting for having
      been fixed.

141e. A TOOL HAD BEEN SILENTLY DEAD SINCE 126.  The ablation lines of the
      default build were identical to the control, which rule R141c required
      to be checked rather than believed: tools/build_variant.sh
      --probe-switches patches the source at an anchor that audit 126 itself
      invalidated, when fieldFromOtherParticleAt's call gained
      chargePlummerFloor.  The script asserts and exits, so any ablation
      attempted between 126 and now would have failed loudly -- but the
      six lines printed by a build WITHOUT the switches are silently the
      control value, and that is the trap.  The anchor is repaired here.

141f. WITH THE SWITCHES BACK, THE ABLATIONS SAY THE OPPOSITE OF A DEFECT.
      Residual at beta = 0.1, control 3.287e-06 N:
        term removed                     |F1+F2|        against control
        the partner's poles              9.387e-04 N        285x
        the magnetization term           1.346e-03 N        410x
        the LW field inside the moment   4.143e-04 N        126x
        the material derivative          3.287e-06 N        1.00003x
        the dipole force on the charge   4.111e-04 N        125x
        the moment force entirely        5.077e-04 N        154x
      Every named term except the material derivative is LOAD-BEARING: take
      any one away and the balance breaks by two orders.  The near
      cancellation to 2.9e-04 of |F1| is therefore a property of the five
      terms together, not the accident of a small leftover, and the material
      derivative is inert in it at the 3e-05 level.

141g. WHAT IS STILL UNATTRIBUTED, STATED HONESTLY (rule R141d).  The
      remaining residual is third order in beta, 2.9e-04 of |F1| at beta =
      0.1 and 2.8e-07 at beta = 0.01, and no single-term ablation isolates
      it, because every ablation makes it larger.  It is not the ledger's
      31%: that was a first-order quantity and this is not.  Naming its
      source needs a term-by-term expansion of the retarded fields to third
      order, which this section does not do.

141h. UNCHANGED.  No model file was touched; the only change is the repaired
      anchor in tools/build_variant.sh, which patches a temporary copy of the
      tree and never production.  Validation 62/62 and header isolation 37/37
      stand from section 140.

141i. LIMITS.  One geometry (separation 1 r*, tilt 0, moments as the probe
      prepares them), three speeds, synthetic circular kinematics with nine
      history nodes over two light-crossing times.  The order in beta is read
      from three points, so it is "third order" to within the 3.01 and 3.10
      measured, not a proof of exactly cubic.  |S| is the probe's own
      construction of the excess and is compared against the ledger's
      published values rather than re-derived here.

142. THE THIRD-ORDER RESIDUAL, NAMED: IT IS THE NEXT ODD ORDER OF THE HIDDEN
     MOMENTUM, AND IT IS 1.4e-08 OF THE COULOMB FORCE AT THE PRODUCTION ORBIT
==================================================================

142a. WHAT 141g OWED.  Section 141 left the remaining imbalance unattributed
      and said naming it would need a third-order expansion of the retarded
      fields.  It does not: four measurements name it, and none of them is an
      expansion.

142b. IT IS NOT NUMERICAL.  The history stencil refined 57-fold, from 9 nodes
      over 2 light-crossing times to 512 over 40, moves the residual from
      3.28656e-06 to 3.28940e-06 N at beta = 0.1 -- 0.09%.  Converged.

142c. IT IS ENTIRELY DIPOLE-SECTOR, AND THE CHARGE SECTOR IS RECIPROCAL TO
      THE BIT.  With both moments zeroed (CREM_BALANCE_NO_MOMENTS, added to
      the probe for this) the net force is 0.000000e+00 N at beta = 0.01, 0.1
      and 0.2 -- not small, zero to every printed digit.  No ablation of a
      dipole TERM could have shown this, because each leaves the moment's own
      field in place; only removing the moments does.

142d. AND IT IS EXACTLY LINEAR IN THE MOMENT.  Scaling both moments over a
      factor of sixteen, at beta = 0.1:
        mu x 0.25   8.225007e-07 N        mu x 2   6.580005e-06 N
        mu x 0.50   1.645001e-06 N        mu x 4   1.316001e-05 N
        mu x 1      3.290003e-06 N
      Each step doubles to every digit.  A moment-moment term would be
      quadratic, so this is the CHARGE-MOMENT interaction.

142e. AND THE HIDDEN MOMENTUM SETS ITS ORDER.  With
      CREM_NO_HIDDEN_MOMENTUM_FORCE the residual is 9.178e-05, 9.254e-04 and
      1.901e-03 N at the three speeds -- log-log slopes 1.004 and 1.038,
      first order in beta.  With the term, 3.184e-09, 3.290e-06 and
      2.845e-05 N -- slopes 3.014 and 3.103, third order.  So the term audit
      124 installed cancels the ENTIRE first-order non-reciprocity of the
      charge-moment pair, exactly, and what survives is the next order of the
      same series.  The second order is absent because the series is odd in
      beta.

142f. THE NAMING, AND AN OUT-OF-SAMPLE TEST (rule R142a).  The residual is
      the third-order term of the charge-moment momentum balance whose
      first-order term is the hidden momentum (mu x E)/c^2.  Written as
      |F1+F2|/|F1| = c beta^3, the coefficient measured at three speeds was
      0.2846, 0.2943 and 0.3176, so the rule asked for two new speeds to land
      in 0.28..0.34, predicting 4.0e-07 N at beta = 0.05 and 2.4e-04 N at
      beta = 0.4 before running them.  Measured: 4.014030e-07 and
      2.258791e-04 N, giving c = 0.2871 and 0.2963.  Both inside the band,
      and c now spans 0.2846..0.3176 over a forty-fold range of beta.
      ACCEPTED.

142g. WHAT IT COSTS AT THE ORBIT THE MODEL ACTUALLY RUNS.  At a_pair the
      pair's beta is 0.0036487, so the prediction there is
        |F1+F2|/|F1| = 0.2846 x 4.858e-08 = 1.38e-08.
      Fourteen parts per billion of the Coulomb force.  The repair the naming
      implies -- carrying the (mu x E)/c^2 construction to the next odd order
      in beta, as 124 carried it to the first -- is therefore well defined,
      buildable, and worth nothing numerically.  It is named here so that the
      thread is closed rather than left as a residue.

142h. WHAT THIS CORRECTS.  141g said the source needed a third-order field
      expansion.  That was the wrong instrument: the source is fixed by a
      sector test, a scaling test and an ablation, all of which the existing
      probe could do once it could zero and scale the moments.  Sections 104
      to 141 chased this imbalance through eight named candidates; it ends as
      the second term of the one candidate that turned out to be right.

142i. UNCHANGED, AND LIMITS.  No model file was touched; the moment-zeroing
      and moment-scaling switches are tool-side.  Validation 62/62 and header
      isolation 37/37 stand from section 140.  One geometry (1 r*, tilt 0),
      synthetic circular kinematics, five speeds; c drifts by 12% across them,
      so "third order" is the measured 3.01-3.10 rather than an exact cube,
      and the naming is of the leading behaviour, not a closed form.

143. THE TWO CHANNELS ARE BACK, BY CONDITIONING RATHER THAN IMPOSITION -- AND
     THE M1 RESULT IS STILL NOT
==================================================================

143a. THE DEFECT.  Audit 91 withdrew both impositions on the mutual angle, and
      with them the distinction between menu 1 and menu 2: both sampled the
      identical ensemble, 494/1506 either side of the threshold, mean cosine
      -0.0038, |mu1+mu2|/mu = 1.3293 for both.  The menu entry was a label,
      the paired comparison tool refused to run, and the README still
      described a physics the default run did not produce.

143b. THE REPAIR.  Menu 1 now keeps only draws the model's OWN classifier
      calls para, menu 2 only those it calls ortho, and menu 5 keeps the free
      draw with identification after the fact.  The direction is drawn
      exactly as the free draw draws it; a draw is kept or redrawn on the
      same dipoleAlignment >= 0.5 test the phenomenon label uses.  So the
      accepted states are a SUB-ENSEMBLE of what menu 5 produces -- no cosine
      is set, nothing is aligned by hand, and this is not the cos = +-1
      import 91 removed.  CREM_FREE_CHANNEL_DRAW restores the unconditioned
      behaviour.

143c. MEASURED, 2000 prepared states per channel, seed base 42.
        menu   label share            mean cos    <|mu1+mu2|/mu>
        1      2000/2000 para         +0.7508     1.8697
        2      2000/2000 ortho        -0.2461     1.1563
        5      466/1534, 23.3%/76.7%  -0.0143     1.3220
      Rule R143a asked for 100% and 100%: met exactly.  Rule R143b asked the
      two channels to differ in the quantities that were identical: the mean
      cosine now spans 1.00 between them where it was -0.0038 for both, and
      the coherent moment 1.8697 against 1.1563 where it was 1.3293 for both.
      Rule R143d asked the acceptance rates to match the measured shares:
      menu 5 gives 23.3%/76.7% against the 24.7%/75.3% of audit 91, the
      difference being the seed base and the count.
      My four pre-registered predictions -- +0.75, -0.25, 1.87 and 1.15 --
      all hold.

143d. MENU 5 IS UNTOUCHED (rule R143c).  With and without the conditioning it
      gives the same mean cosine -0.0143, the same median -0.0122 and the
      same 1.3220: the loop is entered only for scenarios 2 and 3, so the
      free draw's stream is unchanged.  And with CREM_FREE_CHANNEL_DRAW both
      menus 1 and 2 return to 23.3%/76.7%, i.e. to being duplicates, which is
      the behaviour earlier sections were measured under.

143e. THE TOOL THAT REFUSED NOW MEASURES.  tools/para_ortho_lifetimes
      refused on the default build because a pair of identical ensembles
      gives 0 +- 0 ps, which reads as a null result and is an instrument with
      no resolving power.  Its refusal is now conditional on
      CREM_FREE_CHANNEL_DRAW.  Run on eight pairs, seed 42, 300 s:
        mean t_para    198.971157 ps
        mean t_ortho   198.955752 ps
        difference     -0.0154048 +- 0.0151902 ps   (1.014 sigma)
        ratio          0.999922557, 95% CI [0.999772946, 1.00007219]
        sign test      ortho longer in 3 of 8, exact p = 0.7266
      A null result, but a real one: the pairs differ, the instrument
      resolves, and it finds no significant para/ortho difference at N = 8.
      That is a different object from the 0 +- 0 it printed before.

143f. WHAT THIS DOES NOT REPAIR, REPEATED RATHER THAN DROPPED (rule R143f).
      Conditioning restores two ensembles; it does not restore cos = -1.  The
      exact cancellation |mu1+mu2| = 0 that the M1 result stood on is reached
      by a uniform draw with probability zero, and the conditioned ortho
      channel carries 1.1563 mu of coherent moment, not zero.  So the
      README's M1 table -- para 1.626..2.000, ortho 0.000..0.000, maxima 32
      orders apart -- remains a statement about the QUANTIZED preparation,
      and a note saying so is now printed beside it in the README itself.
      The hyperfine, contact and M1 sections of the audit are still valid
      only as statements about that preparation, and this section does not
      change that.

143g. UNCHANGED.  Validation 62/62, header isolation 37/37.  Menu 5, menus 3
      and 4, and every continuous-path number are untouched; what moves is
      every seeded number quoted for menus 1 and 2, because the conditioning
      loop consumes draws from the shared stream -- about four per para state
      and 1.3 per ortho one.

143h. LIMITS.  The conditioning is on the model's own threshold, so "p-Ps" in
      menu 1 means exactly "cos >= 0.5", with the band's own consequences:
      audit 111 measured that a cos = 0.5 state still emits three photons a
      quarter of the time, so the channel label and the photon multiplicity
      remain different questions.  The lifetime comparison is eight pairs at
      one seed and one budget; it resolves 0.015 ps and says nothing below
      that.

144. THE KNIFE EDGE WITH BOTH SPIN SWITCHES ON: STILL NONE, AND THE BAND'S OWN
     LIMITS SAY WHY -- BUT THE PAIR OF SWITCHES IS CHEAP, NOT COSTLY
==================================================================

144a. THE SETTING.  Audit 143 gave the two channels back by conditioning, and
      audit 140 showed that CREM_SPIN_MAGNITUDE with CREM_AXIAL_SPIN makes
      the orbit surrender exactly one hbar per photon.  Both are switched on
      here for the measurement only; 140's verdict that they are not defaults
      stands (rule R144e).  The question is whether the channel LABEL and the
      emitted MULTIPLICITY become the same division.

144b. MEASURED, eight trajectories per channel, seed 42, budget 200 s, the
      terminal measure read with CREM_CHANNEL_AT_ANNIHILATION.
        configuration      completed   2 gamma    label agrees   E[w] terminal
        menu 1, switches      8/8       8/8 100%     8/8 100%       0.8711
        menu 2, switches      5/8       3/5  60%     2/5  40%       0.4911
        menu 1, default       0/8         -            -              -
        menu 2, default       1/8       0/1   0%     1/1 100%       0.1252
      Rule R144a asked for P(2 gamma | menu 1) > 0.9 AND
      P(2 gamma | menu 2) < 0.1.  The first holds at 1.00; the second fails
      at 0.60.  NO KNIFE EDGE.  Rule R144b asked for 90% overall agreement:
      measured 10 of 13, 77%.  No partial edge either.

144c. AND THE BAND'S OWN LIMITS SAY WHY IT CANNOT APPEAR.  The label is a
      threshold at cos = 0.5 and the multiplicity is drawn from
      w = (1+cos)/2, so
        para  band  cos in [0.5, 1]   ->  w in [0.750, 1.000]
        ortho band  cos in [-1, 0.5)  ->  w in [0.000, 0.750)
      P(2 gamma | ortho) can only vanish where cos = -1, which a uniform
      draw reaches with probability zero.  Over the large prepared samples of
      143 -- 2000 states per channel, where the statistics are not limited by
      which trajectories completed -- the two conditional probabilities are
        P(2 gamma | menu 1) = 0.8754     P(2 gamma | menu 2) = 0.3770
      and the label agrees with the multiplicity in 0.6818 of the free
      ensemble.  So the edge is not merely unobserved at N = 8: it is outside
      the reach of any conditioning on a threshold, and only the withdrawn
      cos = +-1 preparation can produce it.

144d. WHAT THE SWITCHES DO CHANGE (rule R144d).  They act through the
      trajectory, not through the draw.  With them on, the terminal measure
      tracks the prepared one closely -- menu 1 goes from E[w] 0.8762
      prepared to 0.8711 terminal, 0.6%, and menu 2 from 0.5149 to 0.4911,
      4.6% -- where audit 113 measured the default dynamics tilting it.  The
      reason is in 140: the cascade ends after the first photon, so the
      mutual angle has far less time to drift before annihilation.  So these
      switches make the model's own terminal measure nearly equal to its
      preparation measure, which is the opposite of sharpening a knife edge:
      it removes the one mechanism that was moving the angle at all.

144e. A CORRECTION TO AUDIT 140's COST STATEMENT.  140 measured
      CREM_SPIN_MAGNITUDE ALONE as unaffordable -- 0 of 8 cascades completed
      within 1800 s, against 8 of 8 within 150 s for the default -- and that
      stands.  The PAIR is the opposite: under identical conditions here, the
      same budget, the same parallel load and the same stderr traffic, the
      switched runs completed 13 of 16 trajectories while the default
      completed 1 of 16.  The eccentric slow orbit the single switch produces
      is what costs; adding the axial projection ends the cascade at L = 0
      after one photon and the pair then falls radially, which is fast.  So
      the pair is cheap and the single switch is not, and 140's cost sentence
      must be read as being about the single switch.

144f. WHAT THIS DOES NOT CHANGE.  Neither switch becomes a default: with them
      on the cascade is one photon and the model's collapse time is no longer
      a cascade time at all, which is 140's verdict and is untouched.  Nor
      does the conditioning of 143 become a knife edge; 144c says it cannot.
      The 2 gamma / 3 gamma division and the para/ortho label remain two
      different questions, which is audit 111's result, now measured under
      the two configurations that were supposed to be most favourable to it.

144g. LIMITS.  Eight trajectories per channel at one seed base and a 200 s
      budget, which is why 144c leans on the 2000-state prepared samples for
      the conditional probabilities and uses the trajectories only for the
      terminal measure.  Three of the eight switched menu-2 runs ended on
      outcome 2 rather than completing.  The default rows censored under this
      section's own run conditions and are reported as such rather than as a
      property of the default configuration.

145. WHAT THE DYNAMICS CONSERVES: THE LEDGER CARRIES A TERM THAT DOES NO WORK,
     AND THE OLD ~1 |U_dd| PER ORBIT IS THE RETARDATION
==================================================================

145a. THE DEFECT, RECORDED TWICE AND NEVER MEASURED.  Three terms of the
      ledger depend on velocity: the charge-dipole term is -p.E with
      p = (v x mu)/c^2, linear in v; the Darwin term goes as v1.v2,
      quadratic; and U_dd is evaluated on the LAB moments, which the boost
      makes velocity-dependent.  For L = T - U(r,v) the conserved quantity is
      H = sum v.(dL/dv) - L, and a velocity-LINEAR potential drops out of it
      entirely while a quadratic one enters with the opposite sign.  So the
      ledger may be summing quantities that do not belong to the conserved
      energy.  tools/velocity_dependent_energy reads four ledgers off the
      SAME states of one engine orbit (rule R145c).

145b. WITH THE MODEL'S OWN RETARDED FORCES, NOTHING WORKS.  Para, 1 r*,
      tilt 0, one orbit, 256 steps, tolerance 1e-8:
        ledger                                range/|U_dd|    vs E0
        E0  the model's ledger                  1.328177      1.0000
        E1  minus the charge-dipole term        1.221060      0.9194
        E2  and the Darwin sign flipped         1.896215      1.4277
        E3  and U_dd on the proper moments      1.497418      1.1274
      Rule R145a asked for a variant below 10% of E0's range: none is even
      below 50%, and two are WORSE.  My prediction that E1 would win here was
      wrong, and rule R145b's fallback candidate -- the instantaneous ledger
      against retarded forces, named in 125e -- takes over.

145c. WITH INSTANTANEOUS FORCES IT WORKS, BY A FACTOR OF 44.  The same orbit,
      integrated with instantaneous forces instead, which is the
      configuration the instantaneous ledger could be exact for:
        E0  the model's ledger                  4.068990      1.0000
        E1  minus the charge-dipole term        0.091378      0.0225
        E2  and the Darwin sign flipped         0.660517      0.1623
        E3  and U_dd on the proper moments      0.273438      0.0672
      E1 is 2.25% of E0: rule R145a is met, in the configuration that
      isolates the question.  And it holds across the others:
        configuration                   E0        E1        E1/E0
        r 1, para,  tilt 0            4.069     0.0914     0.0225
        r 2, para,  tilt 0            0.0346    0.000331   0.0096
        r 1, ortho, tilt 0            0.9939    0.0167     0.0168
        r 1, para,  tilt 90           8.031     0.3361     0.0419
      Removing the velocity-linear term reduces the ledger's range by a
      factor of 24 to 104, in every configuration tried.

145d. AND TWO OF MY THREE CANDIDATE CORRECTIONS ARE WRONG.  Flipping the
      Darwin sign makes the range 7.2 times worse than E1 and putting U_dd on
      the proper moments 3.0 times worse, at 1 r*, and both are worse at
      every other configuration too.  So the model is RIGHT about the Darwin
      term's sign and right to evaluate U_dd on the lab moments; only the
      charge-dipole term does not belong in the conserved energy.  That is
      what a velocity-linear potential is: it generates a force -- the
      spin-orbit force, audit 105 measured it as 88% of the moment sector --
      and that force does no work.

145e. WHICH DECOMPOSES THE RESIDUAL THE AUDIT HAS CARRIED SINCE 104
      (rule R145d).  On the same orbit, in |U_dd| per orbit:
        the ledger's spurious velocity-linear term    3.98
        the retarded-versus-instantaneous mismatch    1.13
        what the model reports as its drift           1.33
      The first is E0 minus E1 with instantaneous forces, the second is E1
      retarded minus E1 instantaneous, and the third is E0 retarded.  The two
      errors have opposite signs and PARTIALLY CANCEL: the model's reported
      1.33 is smaller than either of its parts.  So the famous ~1 |U_dd| per
      orbit is the retardation mismatch, and it was sitting on top of a
      larger ledger error that was hiding it.

145f. WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT LICENCE.  It does not licence deleting the
      term from chargeDipoleInteractionEnergy: that same function is the
      radial potential of dipoleAwarePeriapsis, where a velocity-linear term
      enters legitimately at FIXED L, being a function of r and L alone.  The
      distinction is exact and worth stating: the term belongs in the radial
      energy at fixed angular momentum and does not belong in the conserved
      total energy.  Changing conservativeParticleEnergy is therefore a
      separate decision with its own blast radius -- every ledger number in
      sections 102 to 141, the engine's own accuracy gates, and the
      secularLossAbsent test all read it -- and rule R145e keeps this section
      to the measurement.

145g. UNCHANGED, AND LIMITS.  No model file was touched.  Four
      configurations, one orbit each, reaction disabled, 256 steps at
      tolerance 1e-8; the E1 residual of 0.0914 |U_dd| at 1 r* is not
      resolved further here and may be integration error, a fourth
      velocity-dependent path, or both.  The instantaneous-force runs are a
      diagnostic configuration, not the model's physics: the retarded forces
      are what production uses, and 145e is the statement about it.

146. THE TWO ENERGIES SPLIT IN CODE: THE CONSERVED ONE AND THE RADIAL ONE ARE
     NOW TWO FUNCTIONS, AND THE TOTAL IS AGAIN THE SUM OF ITS PARTS
==================================================================

146a. WHY.  Section 145 measured that the charge-dipole term does not belong
      to the conserved energy: dropping it reduces the ledger's range over an
      orbit by a factor of 24 to 104, while the other two candidate
      corrections make it worse.  Two further things were found while doing
      it, both in the code rather than in the physics.
      First, the comment at the Noether energy in crem_trajectory.hpp already
      said "the q-mu term is homogeneous of degree one in both velocities and
      therefore cancels in the Legendre transform" -- and the call under it
      used conservativeParticleEnergy's default weight, which INCLUDES that
      term.  The number contradicted the sentence above it.
      Second, the same frame reports two per-particle energies which have
      always EXCLUDED the term, so the reported total was not the sum of its
      reported parts.
      This section is not a measurement but the split those two facts ask
      for; its acceptance criteria are stated below and each is verified.

146b. WHAT WAS DONE.  conservedParticleEnergy(state) is added beside
      conservativeParticleEnergy: the Legendre-transform energy,
      T + U_Coulomb + U_dd + U_Darwin + U_constraint, with the velocity-linear
      charge-dipole term absent.  The Noether energy now calls it, which is
      the one site whose own comment demanded it.  The charge-dipole energy
      itself is untouched, and so is its use as the radial potential at FIXED
      momentum in dipoleAwarePeriapsis, where a velocity-linear term is a
      function of r and L alone and belongs there.  One function was doing
      two jobs; there are now two functions.

146c. WHAT IT BUYS, MEASURED.  On 200 frames of one trajectory the reported
      total now equals the sum of the two per-particle energies to
        3.533e-16 relative
      i.e. to machine precision, where before it differed by exactly the q-mu
      term.  That term is not small: along one orbit at 1 r*, instantaneous
      forces, 256 steps,
        channel  tilt   max |q-mu|/|E|   max |q-mu|/|U_dd|
        para      0        0.9894            3.991
        para     90        4.0570            7.678
        ortho     0        0.5263            1.021
        ortho    90        0.0051            0.0018
      So the quantity that was being added to the conserved energy reaches
      99% of it at para tilt 0 and four times it at tilt 90.  It is exactly
      zero at the prepared state whenever the two moments are equal, because
      it carries (mu2 - mu1), and it grows only as the precession separates
      them -- which is why this never showed up as an obvious offset.
      The 3.991 at para tilt 0 is the same number section 145 measured as the
      range E0 minus E1, 3.98, from the other direction.

146d. PRODUCTION IS BIT-IDENTICAL.  Seed 42's cascade still gives
      199.039646721 ps, as in sections 131 to 144; validation 62/62; header
      isolation 37/37.  The split changes a REPORTED quantity, not a force:
      nothing in the dynamics reads the Noether energy.

146e. WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED.
        the ledger diagnostics of sections 102-141, which read
          conservativeParticleEnergy -- their numbers are the record of the
          defect and must stay comparable with what was published;
        the ground-state floor test in crem_trajectory.hpp, which compares
          conservativeParticleEnergy against the floor: it is conceptually a
          conserved-energy use, but changing it changes what
          --ground-state-floor does, and that wants its own measurement;
        the bound-field bookkeeping, which subtracts the ledger from a
          balance and would silently move the field energy if the ledger
          moved under it;
        dipoleAwarePeriapsis, which is the radial use and is correct.
      Each is a candidate for the same split and none is made here.

146f. LIMITS.  One call site converted.  The consistency check is 200 frames
      of one trajectory at one seed; the term's size is four configurations at
      1 r*, where it is largest, and it falls with radius as the moments'
      relative motion does.  Whether the conserved energy should also replace
      the ledger in the four places listed in 146e is not decided by this
      section.

147. THE TWO FOURS ARE NOT THE SAME FOUR, AND SECTION 146c NEEDED THE
     QUALIFICATION THIS SECTION ADDS
==================================================================

147a. THE QUESTION.  Section 146c reported the charge-dipole term reaching
      3.991 |U_dd| at para tilt 0, matching the 3.98 section 145 measured
      from the other side.  Separately the audit records an exact factor 4
      between the classical inspiral time and the 2 gamma lifetime,
        t_inspiral = a_pair^3/(3C) = lambda_C/(2 alpha^5 c) = 31.1235 ps,
        tau_2gamma = 2 hbar/(alpha^5 m c^2) = 2 lambda_C/(alpha^5 c)
                   = 124.4942 ps,
      whose ratio is 4 exactly.  Are the two fours the same object?

147b. NO, AND THE FIRST ONE IS NOT A FOUR.  Swept over the separation, para
      tilt 0, one orbit each, instantaneous forces:
        r [r*]      L/hbar     max |q-mu|/|E|     max |q-mu|/|U_dd|
          1.000     0.0427       9.894e-01            3.9910
          2.000     0.0604       6.459e-03            0.0342
          5.000     0.0956       3.017e-04            0.0042
         20.000     0.1911       6.990e-06            0.0014
        100.000     0.4274       5.813e-08            0.0003
        547.509     1.0000       3.548e-10            0.0001
      The ratio falls by four orders of magnitude between 1 r* and a_pair.
      At the production orbit -- the L = hbar state the alpha^-5 identity is
      about -- it is 1e-04, not 4.  And it is not stable against the model's
      own free constant either: sweeping the moment radius at 1 r* gives
        0.4834 r*  0.0214      1.9336 r*  0.4572
        0.9668 r*  3.9916      3.8672 r*  1.0122
      so the near-4 appears only at the default moment radius, which is
      exactly the peak section 139 mapped for the self-propulsion residual
      and the ledger range.  It is a near-field artefact of that band.

147c. WHAT THE SECOND FOUR IS.  A ratio of two prefactors, exact and
      dimensionless: 2 in tau_2gamma against 1/2 in t_inspiral, with
      alpha^5 and lambda_C cancelling between them.  It carries no length
      scale, so there is nothing for a separation to do to it.  Recomputed
      here independently of the code with alpha = 1/137.035999177:
      t_inspiral 31.1235 ps, tau_2gamma 124.4942 ps, ratio 4.000000, and the
      measured 125.164 ps sits +0.54% above the leading order, which is the
      O(alpha) QED correction.
      The fifth power is structural rather than fitted: alpha enters the model
      in one place, as k = alpha hbar c, and a^3/C supplies three powers from
      the radius (a ~ alpha^-1) and two from the Larmor coefficient
      (C ~ alpha^2).  That the same power governs the annihilation width is a
      statement about structure.  The factor 4 itself is NOT credited to the
      model: it is the whole gap between an inspiral time and an annihilation
      rate, and the audit names it as such.

147d. SO THE ANSWER IS NO, WITH A CORRECTION TO MY OWN 146c.  One four is
      exact, dimensionless and independent of everything; the other is a
      measured energy ratio that ranges over 0.0001 to 4 with the separation
      and over 0.02 to 4 with a constant the model is free to choose.  They
      share a digit.
      And 146c, by quoting only 1 r*, could be read as saying the ledger term
      is comparable to the whole conserved energy in general.  It is not: at
      the production orbit it is 3.5e-10 of it.  The term matters where the
      model's deep passages go and nowhere else, which is the same place
      every other near-field quantity matters.

147e. LIMITS.  One orbit per point, instantaneous forces, para tilt 0 for the
      sweep; the moment-radius sweep is at 1 r* only, which is where the
