      or a static source) the correction is identically zero and the function
      degenerates to the stencil it replaces.  Validation PASS; the suite's
      numbers move in the seventh to ninth significant figure, which is the
      size of the noise removed.

163f. THE HOPED-FOR BENEFIT DID NOT MATERIALIZE, WHICH IS THE HONEST RESULT.
      The engine's step-error convergence at 1.2 r* is unchanged:
        retarded + dipole force, before  3.97 3.54 4.13 3.51 3.45 6.55 1.08
        1.94
        retarded + dipole force, after   3.55 4.09 4.45 2.38 5.94 1.60 3.39
        1.99
        retarded, hidden term ablated    4.00 4.07 3.95 3.18 3.42 4.04 2.41
        2.49
        retarded, NO dipole force        3.18 3.63 3.82 3.91 3.96 3.98 3.98
        3.99
      Ablating the hidden-momentum term entirely does not clean it either;
      only ablating the whole dipole force does.  So
      covariantDipoleGradientForce
      -- which differences the SAME pole-limit field spatially, on a six-probe
      stencil -- is now the dominant roughness, and it is the next lever.
      What this section bought is accuracy in the term, not depth in the
      integrator.

163g. COST.  Three extra two-pole evaluations per call, after folding the
      offset-zero construction into one evaluation that serves both the
      remainder's value and the rate:
        the term alone, 20000 calls   1091 ms -> 3990 ms
        production, 12-run job        46.61 s -> 53.44 s, +15%
      Before that folding it was +25%.  CREM_NO_ANALYTIC_POLE_RATE restores
      the pure stencil.

163h. LIMITS.  One geometry for the derivative comparison, one radius for the
      convergence sweep.  The 7e-06 of 163d is quoted against a stencil, so
      what is established is that they agree where the stencil is trustworthy
      and diverge where 161 already showed it is not -- not an independent
      absolute error bound on the analytic value.  The mirror's own value
      residual, 6.4e-11 electric and 1.0e-10 magnetic against
      twoChargeLimitDipoleField, is the two-pole construction's cancellation
      floor and bounds what any derivative of it can be worth.  Whether +15%
      of wall time is the right price for that accuracy is a decision this
      section records rather than makes.

164. THE FAR-ZONE POLE CONSTRUCTION HAD NO CANCELLATION GUARD, AND ON
     ISOLATED DIRECTIONS RETURNED A FIELD 200 TIMES OVERSIZED
==================================================================

164a. WHAT WAS ASKED.  Audit 160j left the far-zone flux's MOMENTUM channel
      stalling near 3e-04 and non-monotone in the quadrature degree while the
      energy converged to 1e-06, and did not diagnose it.

164b. THE MOMENTUM IS A CANCELLING SUM, WHICH IS WHY IT IS THE CHANNEL THAT
      SHOWS THINGS.  At 0.778 r*, tilt 60, the net momentum is only 1.94% of
      E/c: the emission pattern is nearly symmetric, so the vector integral
      cancels about fifty to one where the energy integral, a sum of positive
      terms, does not.  Any outlier in the integrand therefore costs the
      momentum fifty times what it costs the energy.  The control radius is
      not involved: |p| holds to 6e-09 from 1e+03 to 1e+07 a0.

164c. AND THERE ARE OUTLIERS.  Sweeping the integrand over 4000 directions
      found a second difference of 2.5e-02 of the mean where a smooth
      integrand gives (pi/N)^2 = 6e-07 -- four decades, the signature of a C0
      jump.  Bisecting it to one ulp attributed it to
      farZoneMagneticDipoleField: across a single ulp of theta the field
      moved by 102%, and just inside the band it sat forty times the value of
      its neighbours.

164d. THE CAUSE.  farZoneTwoChargeLimitDipoleField had NO pole-cancellation
      guard at all.  It returns positive+negative with no measurement of how
      well the two poles cancelled and no retreat, while the near-field
      twoChargeLimitDipoleField has measured that retention since audit 107
      and TWO of its callers act on it.  Sweeping the pole separation at a
      broken direction shows what that costs:
        eps      1e-04     1e-05     1e-06     1e-07     1e-08
        |E|    5.10e+06  6.37e+07  1.5718e6  1.5719e6  1.5719e6
      Production runs at 1e-05, which is the worst of the five.  The error is
      NOT monotone in eps, exactly as the near-field's own comment warns, so
      nothing that refines gradually would have found this.

164e. HOW OFTEN.  Scanning 200000 directions at one azimuth found five where
      the production separation returns a field 43, 128, 194, 208 and 225
      times the converged one.  That is 2.5e-05 of the sphere -- which is why
      a 302-node rule almost never lands in it, and why a 30000-node
      Fibonacci lattice does: 30000 x 2.5e-05 is 0.75 expected hits, and that
      lattice returned a radiated power 2.9% high and a p_z 5.7 times wrong.

164f. THE LIMIT HAD TO BE SET FROM DATA, NOT COPIED.  The near-field limit of
      30 is set against a healthy near-field ratio of ~1.  The far-zone
      construction's healthy ratio is an order of magnitude smaller -- over
      40000 directions: median 0.068, p99 0.38, p99.9 1.13 -- so 30 never
      fires, and the detector sat dead when it was first written that way.
      The five broken directions carry ratios 4.74, 6.06, 19.8, 24.9, 24.9.
      The two ranges OVERLAP: a correct field was measured at ratio 5.4.  So
      this is not a clean classifier and does not need to be, because where
      the retreat is unnecessary it is also harmless -- the two separations
      then agree to about 1e-06.  The limit is 1.0, which catches every
      broken direction observed and retreats on 0.12% of healthy ones.

164g. RESULT.  The spikes are gone: the same 200000-direction scan now finds
      0 directions above three times the smooth level, against 5 before.  The
      30000-node Fibonacci lattice returns to the family, p_z 1.82e-07 and
      P 9.1526e+03 against the 1.83e-07 and 9.1527e+03 of the 3000- and
      10000-node lattices.  Validation PASS with NO physics number moved at
      all: the far-zone flux is bookkeeping the trajectory never reads, and
      the production 50-direction rule does not land in the band.

164h. WHAT THIS DID NOT FIX, WHICH IS THE HONEST HALF.  The Lebedev rows of
      160j are unchanged to every printed digit, so these spikes were NOT the
      cause of the 1-2% momentum scatter across quadrature degrees that the
      section set out to explain.  They could not have been: a 302-node rule
      hits a band of this measure about once in 130 evaluations.  A residual
      C0 step of about 1% survives in the integrand near the same theta -- the
      second-difference scan is unchanged at 2.5e-02 -- and it is a different,
      smaller defect than the one fixed here.  That is the remaining lead on
      160j's question, and it is not diagnosed.

164i. LIMITS.  One geometry, one azimuth for the 200000-direction scan, one
      radius.  The ratio distributions come from a single state.  The five
      broken directions are the ones found at that azimuth; no claim is made
      about the shape or the total measure of the set on the sphere.  The
      retreat is verified to land on the converged value at those five and to
      change healthy directions by ~1e-06; it is not verified to be correct
      at a direction where BOTH separations are wrong, which 162's method
      could not detect either.

165. THE PLUNGE IS ALREADY REGULARIZED, AND THE DEFECT THAT IS THERE IS A
     SILENT DOPPLER CLAMP RATHER THAN A DIVERGENCE
==================================================================

165a. WHAT WAS ASKED.  A review proposed replacing a "purely geometric
      cut-off at comptonBarrierRadius" with a relativistic form factor
      smearing the charge and the moment at the Compton scale, so that the
      forces saturate smoothly and F -> 0 as r -> 0, on the reading that the
      dipole fields (1/r^3, 1/r^4) overwhelm the Coulomb field (1/r^2) below
      r* and produce the jumps audits 149 and 151 dealt with.

165b. THE CUT-OFF IS NOT WHAT REGULARIZES THE FIELD.  comptonBarrierRadius is
      a derived scale -- g hbar/(4 m c), pinned by static_assert against two
      independent readings -- and it is used as a TERMINATION and reporting
      depth, not as a field cut-off.  What regularizes the field is already
      there and is already covariant: lienardWiechertField scales the whole
      field by (R/R_c)^3 with R_c = sqrt(R^2+floor^2), so a static source is
      exactly the gradient of the Plummer potential the energies use, and it
      carries a Gaussian form factor, erf(u) - 2u exp(-u^2)/sqrt(pi), for a
      smeared charge.  The moment sector has its own radius
      (magneticDipoleRadius, 0.967 r*) and the separation floor is 0.05 r*.

165c. AND THE FORCES ALREADY SATURATE AND TURN OVER.  Measured at tilt 45:
        r/r*     1e+00    5e-02    1e-02    1e-03    1e-04    1e-06
        Coulomb  6.15e-3  8.73e-1  4.66e-1  4.94e-2  4.94e-3  4.94e-5
        dipole   7.09e-3  4.02e-3  8.11e-4  8.12e-5  8.12e-6  8.12e-8
      Both peak near the floor and fall away as r -> 0, which is the
      behaviour the review asks for.  The field and the energy saturate with
      them: |E| goes 1.42e+16 at r* to 2.20e+15 and flat below 0.01 r*, and
      the Coulomb energy to -2.387e-14 J and flat.  The hidden momentum
      (mu x E)/c^2 saturates at 1.61e-25 for the same reason.  There is no
      plunge singularity left to remove.

165d. THERE IS A DEFECT, AND IT IS A DIFFERENT ONE.  Scanning the same
      radii found the CHARGE field itself failing:
        r/r*     0.015    0.013    0.012    0.011    0.010    0.009
        |E|    2.20e+15    0        0        0     4.90e+45  2.27e+15
      Exactly zero at three radii and 4.9e+45 V/m at a fourth, against
      neighbours at 2.2e+15.  It is not the dipole sector and not the pole
      construction: retreating the pole separation to 1e-07 changes nothing,
      and lienardWiechertField alone reproduces it.

165e. THE TRIGGER IS THE DOPPLER CLAMP, AND ONLY WHEN RELATIVISTIC.  The same
      scan at beta = 0, 0.1, 0.3 and 0.5 is smooth to four digits across
      every radius; the failure appears only at beta = 0.7636.  The mechanism
      is kappa = max(1e-8, 1 - nhat.beta), which the field then divides by the
      CUBE of: a clamp that binds amplifies by up to 1e+24, and 1e+24 times a
      2e+15 field is the 4.9e+45 observed.  The zeros are the companion guard,
      the degenerate-distance early return, firing on the same geometry.

165f. THE CLAMP STAYS, THE SILENCE DOES NOT.  Removing the clamp is not an
      option: the comment beside it records that a genuine source at 0.9999c
      must keep its Lorentz factor, and clipping it changes the physical field
      by orders of magnitude.  What was wrong is that binding was invisible.
      gCausalityAudit now counts it and records the worst 1/kappa, and the
      suite asserts the count is ZERO -- not a tolerance, because a bound
      clamp does not make the field slightly wrong, it makes it meaningless.
      Validation passes, so the clamp does not bind on any production
      trajectory the suite drives.

165g. WHICH IS ALSO WHY NO FORM FACTOR WAS ADDED.  The failing band sits at
      0.010 to 0.013 r*, below the separation floor (0.05 r*), below
      nuclearCutoff (0.0517 r*) and below the terminal surface a bound
      inspiral stops at, and it needs beta = 0.76 where the reachable radii
      run at 0.19 to 0.27.  Adding a second regularization on top of the
      Plummer softening and the form factor already there, to cure a band the
      model neither reaches nor claims to describe, would change every
      production number for no measured gain.  The counter costs nothing and
      turns the one real problem -- a silent failure mode in the core field
      routine -- into a checked one.

165h. LIMITS.  One tilt, one pair, synthetic states rather than engine
      trajectories: the sub-floor radii cannot be reached by integrating, so
      they were constructed, with a circular-orbit speed capped at 0.9c and a
      causalInitialHistory.  That construction is not a trajectory the model
      would produce, which is the point of 165g and also a limit on 165d --
      what is established is that the routine HAS these failure modes on
      admissible inputs, not that any run reaches them.  The assertion in
      165f covers only the trajectories the suite drives.

166. THE 4% RESIDUE IS NOT THE REDUCTION OF ORDER: THAT TERM IS 135 TIMES
     TOO SMALL, AND THE FORCE IT WOULD CORRECT IS ALREADY COVARIANT
==================================================================

166a. WHAT WAS ASKED.  A review proposed replacing the order-reduced
      Landau-Lifshitz force with the full Lorentz-Dirac a-dot term plus a
      numerical filter for runaways, to close audit 159f's energy line from
      96% to better than 99.5%.  It described the present force as one where
      "the acceleration derivative in the Lorentz-Dirac force is replaced by
      the gradient of the Coulomb force along the trajectory".

166b. THAT IS NOT WHAT THE CODE DOES.  individualLandauLifshitzSelfForces
      differences the FULL retardedExternalForces -- Lienard-Wiechert charge
      fields, the retarded dipole sector, the hidden-momentum rate, all of it
      -- at states advanced forward and backward along the worldline, builds
      the four-force at each, and projects the derivative orthogonal to the
      four-velocity before scaling by tau0.  There is no Coulomb-gradient
      shortcut to remove.  What remains approximate is the reduction of order
      itself, a-dot -> (1/m) dF/dtau, and nothing else.

166c. AND THE REMAINING APPROXIMATION IS THE WRONG SIZE BY TWO ORDERS.  The
      expansion parameter of that reduction is tau0 omega, with
      tau0 = q^2/(6 pi eps0 m c^3) = 6.2664e-24 s.  Measured, with the force
      ratio confirming the parameter independently:
        r/r*     omega [1/s]   tau0*omega   |F_LL|/|F_ext|
        10.1     8.3742e+18    5.2476e-05     5.2089e-05
         5.1     2.3686e+19    1.4842e-04     1.4541e-04
         3.1     5.0964e+19    3.1936e-04     3.1217e-04
         2.0     9.3626e+19    5.8670e-04     5.9636e-04
         1.4     1.4415e+20    9.0329e-04     1.1504e-03
         0.8     2.6481e+20    1.6594e-03     3.4909e-03
      The two columns agree to 2% down to 2 r*, which is the check that
      tau0 omega IS the parameter and not merely a dimensional guess.

166d. WHICH SETTLES IT.  The next order of the reduction is O(tau0 omega)
      relative to the Landau-Lifshitz force itself, and that force is what
      removes the radiated energy, 0.019679 |U_dd| per orbit at 3 r*.  So the
      neglected term is worth
        3.1936e-04 x 0.019679 = 6.28e-06 |U_dd| per orbit
      against a residue of 8.57e-04.  It is 136 TIMES TOO SMALL.  Implementing
      it would move the energy line from 95.95% to 95.98%, not to 99.5%.

166e. AND THE PROPOSED ROUTE IS BACKWARDS.  The Lorentz-Dirac a-dot term is
      the source of runaway and pre-acceleration solutions; the reduction of
      order is the standard, runaway-free cure for exactly that, not a
      shortcut around it.  Restoring a-dot and filtering the runaways
      numerically would replace a controlled O(tau0 omega) truncation with an
      unstable equation plus a filter whose own error nobody has bounded.
      The legitimate higher-order step is a SECOND reduction of order, which
      is the 6.3e-06 |U_dd| of 166d.

166f. SO 159f's ATTRIBUTION FALLS, AND THE RESIDUE IS REAL.  Audit 159f
      assigned the residue to "the order-reduced Landau-Lifshitz
      approximation" by elimination, having measured neither the term nor its
      scale.  It cannot be that term.
      The obvious way out would be that the residue is not converged -- E5
      moved 20% between 48 and 96 samples, which is not the behaviour of a
      settled number -- so that was tested and it is not the way out.  At 192
      samples:
        samples      E1        E4        E5
           48     0.021041  0.001606  0.000707
           96     0.021077  0.001445  0.000848
          192     0.021143  0.001464  0.000857
      E5 moves 1.1% between the last two rows against 20% between the first
      two, so the 48-sample row was the unconverged one and the residue is a
      genuine 8.57e-04 |U_dd|, 4.05% of the imbalance.  What it IS remains
      open; what it is not is the reduction of order.

166g. WHAT WAS CHANGED.  Nothing in the model.  This section is a refusal
      with a measurement behind it: the requested term exists in a correct
      covariant form already, the part that is genuinely missing is 135 times
      smaller than the target it was asked to close, and the proposed
      mechanism for supplying it is less stable than what is there.

166h. LIMITS.  tau0 omega is evaluated on circular configurations at one
      tilt and one channel, with omega the osculating orbital frequency; a
      plunging orbit reaches higher omega and the parameter grows with it,
      reaching 1.7e-03 at 0.8 r*, so the 136 is a statement about the regular
      orbit audit 159 measured and not about the plunge.  The radiated energy
      per orbit is the probe's own E1-to-E4 step at 192 samples.  No claim is
      made about what the residue IS.

167. EVERY CITED COLLAPSE TIME NOW CARRIES ITS CONFIGURATION, AND THE
     DUPLICATE THAT WOULD HAVE REPEATED THE FAILURE IS GONE
==================================================================

167a. THE DEFECT, WHICH IS A PROCESS ONE.  The convergence table at
      maximumJumpParameter once read 124.169 / 124.957 / 125.585 / 124.986 /
      124.328 ps and claimed +/-0.6% over a 25x range.  It reproduced at no
      configuration the file had, because it had been carried across a change
      of defaults without being remeasured, and what it hid was a real step
      dependence of +29.5% still climbing at the finest step.  The dependence
      was closed (flat to 0.4% over 6.7x) and the table restamped, but the
      process that produced it was not addressed, and the same quantity is in
      circulation at 200, 147.8, 199.4, 6206, 35 and 31.12 ps.

167b. WHY THAT TABLE WAS NEVER CHALLENGED, which is the part worth keeping.
      Its five values bracket 124.49 ps, the MEASURED para-Ps lifetime this
      tree quotes in configuration_panel.hpp.  A stale simulation number
      sitting on top of the experimental one reads as agreement, so nobody
      asked it for its configuration.  Plausible-looking numbers are exactly
      the ones that are not challenged, which is why the stamp cannot be
      applied only where something already looks wrong.

167c. THE RULE, written once at the collapse-time definition.  A collapse
      time is quoted with all six of: level (and whether it predates the
      default moving from 2 to 1), ground-state floor, s_max, emission rule,
      seed and run count, wall-clock budget -- or it is marked UNATTRIBUTED
      and may not support a claim.  README's "Protokol" paragraph already
      does this correctly and is named as the worked example.

167d. THE DUPLICATE IS GONE.  The same convergence scan appeared twice: once
      at maximumJumpParameter with its stamp, once inside checkpointProperTime
      without one.  An unstamped second copy is the exact mechanism that let
      the 124 ps table outlive its configuration, so that site now points at
      the stamped table instead of repeating its numbers.

167e. WHAT EACH CIRCULATING NUMBER GOT.
        199.43 / 198.97 / 198.94 / 199.75  already stamped at
                                           maximumJumpParameter (level 1, no
                                           floor, deterministic, seed 42)
        6206.4 (configuration_panel)       stamped N=100, seed 7, level 2,
                                           floor on, s_max 0.30, budget 1200,
                                           and tied to README's 6206.43
        147.8-151.6 and 155.7              stamped level 2, floor on,
        (electrodynamics, two sites)       s_max 0.30, deterministic, 10
                                           seeds; marked a before/after PAIR,
                                           comparable with each other only
        147.818 / 147.820 (crem_trajectory) stamped level 2, floor on, 12
                                           runs; the DIFFERENCE is what the
                                           argument uses and that is
                                           configuration-independent
        199.45 / 216.75 / 207.74 / 203.38  stamped as audit 96's
                                           pre-default-move configuration,
                                           seed and budget not recoverable;
                                           marked historical evidence of a
                                           bug's SIZE, not citable lifetimes
        35 ps (two sites)                  marked UNATTRIBUTED: it matches no
                                           configuration this tree has, and
                                           now carries only the order of
                                           magnitude the 3e-04 rad divides by
        31.12 ps                           left as is: it is a CLOSED FORM,
                                           not a run, and the comment already
                                           states the formula and its limits
        124.49 ps                          left as is: an EXTERNAL measured
                                           value, which needs a source rather
                                           than a run stamp

167f. LIMITS.  This covers the collapse-time family the review named.  A
      sweep of every "ps" in modules/ finds about fifty more values, and they
      were NOT all stamped: most are differences, sigmas, checkpoint scales
      or before/after pairs rather than citable lifetimes, and stamping a
      difference would be noise.  The rule in 167c is what governs the next
      one; nothing here enforces it mechanically, so it remains a convention
      a reviewer has to apply, not a check the build runs.  No number was
      remeasured in this section -- the stamps record the configuration each
      number was taken at, from the surrounding commentary and audit history,
      and where that could not be recovered the number is marked
      unattributed rather than guessed.

168. THE ZERO-POINT FIELD DOES SATISFY FARADAY: THE QUANTITY THAT TENDS TO
     ONE IS THE RATIO, AND THAT IS THE LAW HOLDING
==================================================================

168a. THE CLAIM.  The variable-band construction was reported to break
      Faraday's law, on the evidence that a normalized residual converges to
      non-zero -- 0.999925, 0.99999925, 0.9999999925 at h = 1e-18, 1e-19,
      1e-20 -- so that the failure is a property of the equation rather than
      of the discretization.  The urgency given was not that it spoils
      current results, since ZPF is off by default, but that the ZPF power
      balance is the plan's single most important remaining measurement and
      would be made on a field that is not a Maxwell solution.

168b. THE EXACT RESIDUAL IS ZERO, BY CONSTRUCTION AND NOT BY CANCELLATION.
      ZeroPointField::sample defines E = -dA/dt and B = curl A from
      A = a(t) eps sin(theta), theta = k(t) n.r - f Phi(t) + phi.  Faraday is
      then the vector identity curl(-dA/dt) = -d(curl A)/dt, which holds for
      any a(t) and k(t) whatsoever -- which is exactly why the construction
      was written in potential form rather than by imposing B = n x E / c.
      Evaluated in closed form at the origin, with no differencing:
        dB_y/dt      667128.19039630413
        (curl E)_y  -667128.19039630413
        sum          0
      to all seventeen digits.

168c. AND THE DIFFERENCED RESIDUAL CONVERGES AT SECOND ORDER, which is what
      a central difference on a vanishing residual must do:
        h        dB_y/dt        dE_x/dz        |sum| / max
        1e-16   2.20091e+05   -4.60910e+05      5.2249e-01
        1e-17   6.62130e+05   -6.64906e+05      4.1747e-03
        1e-18   6.67078e+05   -6.67106e+05      4.1667e-05
        1e-19   6.67128e+05   -6.67128e+05      4.1667e-07
        1e-20   6.67128e+05   -6.67128e+05      4.1290e-09
        1e-21   6.67128e+05   -6.67128e+05      1.3629e-10
      Each decade of step divides the residual by a hundred.  Normalizing by
      the natural scale of the terms and by the shipped 2 chirp/c gives the
      same numbers to four figures, because those two scales are the same
      thing here: 2 chirp/c = 6.6713e+05 and |dB/dt| = 6.6713e+05.

168d. SO WHAT WAS THE QUANTITY THAT TENDED TO ONE.  The ratio of the two
      terms:
        h        |dB_y/dt| / |dE_x/dz|      1 - ratio
        1e-18        0.9999583325          4.1667e-05
        1e-19        0.9999995833          4.1667e-07
        1e-20        0.9999999959          4.1290e-09
      The same three decades, the same h^2, and the same leading digits as
      the reported 0.999925 / 0.99999925 / 0.9999999925.  A ratio tending to
      one means the two terms are equal and opposite, which is Faraday being
      SATISFIED; the residual is their SUM, and that is the thing that has to
      go to zero, and does.  The reading inverted the diagnostic.

168e. WHICH VOIDS THE STATED URGENCY, and leaves a real gap worth closing.
      The ZPF power balance can be made on this field: it is a Maxwell
      solution identically, not to some tolerance.  What the suite could NOT
      previously distinguish is the thing the claim was about -- it asserted
      the residual was small and shrinking, and a residual settling on a
      small CONSTANT would have passed both.  Size does not separate a
      discretization error from a property of the equation; the RATE does.
      The suite now measures the convergence order per decade and requires
      better than fifty, against a measured 99.997 and 100.913.  Anything
      that plateaus returns a ratio near one and fails.

168f. LIMITS.  One mode, one polarization, one direction, at the origin with
      zero accumulated phase, which is the configuration the shipped test
      already used.  Faraday is the only Maxwell equation checked here; the
      construction makes div B = 0 identically as well (B = curl A) but that
      is not measured, and the two source-carrying equations are not
      applicable to a source-free mode.  The order assertion is a check on
      the FIELD, not on the ZPF power balance, which remains unmeasured and
      is a separate piece of work.

169. PLAN STEP 4 MEASURED FOR THE FIRST TIME: NO BALANCE POINT IN
     [0.01, 1] a_pair, AND THE MEASUREMENT IS VARIANCE-LIMITED
==================================================================

169a. WHAT WAS ASKED, AND WHAT OF IT ALREADY EXISTS.  A review proposed four
      things.  Three are already in the tree and the fourth is the plan's own
      Step 4:
        quench E1 in the ground state   IS the ground-state floor, on by
                                        default at level 2; README row 4
                                        measures its price (collapse 6206.43
                                        -> 6245.93 ps, +0.6%, but the
                                        stopping point moves 547.5 -> 31.10
                                        r*, a factor of 17.6)
        first passage to contact        IS tools/contact_first_passage.cpp,
                                        whose own header states the limit
                                        this section would otherwise have to:
                                        reaching contact is a necessary
                                        condition, not a rate
        Ore-Powell 3-gamma suppression  README has the full treatment: the
                                        formula checks (1/1113.9 against the
                                        measured 142 ns/124.49 ps = 1140.7,
                                        2.3%), the phase-space density IS in
                                        annihilationPhotonEnergiesFor and
                                        integrates to (pi^2-9)/2 exactly, and
                                        the 1114 splits into 1/8.13 present
                                        and 1/137.04 absent
        the ZPF power balance           Step 4, never measured.  This section.

169b. AND ONE OF THEM WOULD BREAK THE METHOD.  Scaling the ortho decay by
      Gamma_3g = Gamma_2g/1115 means writing a measured quantum lifetime
      ratio in as a rate.  The project's own rule (PRISM_INSTRUCTIONS section
      4) is that external lifetimes are comparison curves and are NOT inputs
      to the calculation, and README's decomposition says what is actually
      missing: not the ratio but one power of alpha, the annihilation
      amplitude, which is a QED object this model does not carry.  Importing
      the ratio would produce the right number by construction and would
      destroy the only thing the comparison is evidence of.

169c. THE MEASUREMENT.  P_abs is the work the zero-point field does on the
      two charges, <q1 E.v1 + q2 E.v2>, accumulated along a trajectory the
      field is acting on, so the correlation that carries absorption is
      present.  P_rad is the coherent E1 power |d''|^2/(6 pi eps0 c^3) of the
      same state.  Scale 1, 16 modes over [0.3, 3.0] x omega_orb, seed 42,
      the step tied to the FASTEST mode at 32 steps per cycle:
        r/a_pair    P_abs [W]     P_rad [W]    P_abs/P_rad
           1       6.0822e-06   1.1557e-08      5.26e+02
           0.3     4.0731e-04   1.3938e-06      2.92e+02
           0.1     1.8700e-02   1.0617e-04      1.76e+02
           0.03    1.8729e+00   1.0604e-02      1.77e+02
           0.01    8.1915e+01   1.2158e-01      6.74e+02
      No crossing.  Absorption exceeds radiation by two to three orders
      everywhere over two decades of radius, so at the nominal amplitude the
      pair is driven OUT, not held.

169d. BUT THE NUMBER IS VARIANCE-LIMITED, WHICH IS THE HONEST HEADLINE.
      <q E.v> has expectation zero for phases uncorrelated with the motion,
      so a one-orbit average measures the variance.  Six seeds at 0.1 a_pair:
        seed      42         43         44         45         46         47
        P_abs  1.87e-02   3.84e-02   1.88e-03   3.98e-02   1.29e-02   3.44e-02
      A factor of 21 between the extremes.  No single row of 169c is a
      measurement of the balance.

169e. WHAT IS ESTABLISHED IS THE SIGN.  All six seeds give POSITIVE net
      absorption, and all six exceed the radiated power (ratios 17 to 390).
      Six of six by chance is 1.6%, so there is a systematic absorption under
      the noise, it is inward-of-zero nowhere, and it is large.  That is
      enough to say the balance point is not in [0.01, 1] a_pair at scale 1,
      and not enough to say where it is.

169f. WHAT WOULD FINISH IT.  Averaging the stochastic work down -- many
      orbits and many seeds, with the variance reported rather than a single
      trajectory's number -- and then a scan in the ZPF scale, since the
      balance radius moves with it and scale 1 is a normalization choice the
      plan has never tested.  tools/zpf_power_balance.cpp is added here so
      that work starts from a reproducible instrument instead of a throwaway.

169g. LIMITS.  Instantaneous rather than retarded mutual forces and radiation
      reaction disabled, both for cost, so P_rad is computed from the state's
      accelerations rather than drained from a dissipating orbit; one
      channel, one tilt, circular preparation, one orbit per radius.  16
      modes is a quarter of the production default, which makes the variance
      of 169d worse than production's would be and does not change its
      existence.  The step is tied to the fastest mode at 32 per cycle, which
      the step census in positronium.cpp calls resolved rather than aliased,
      and that guard is why the sampling column is printed at all.

170. STEP 4 ANSWERED, AND THE ANSWER IS NO: THE ZPF BALANCE CARRIES NO
     RADIAL DEPENDENCE, SO IT CANNOT SELECT A SCALE
==================================================================

170a. WHAT 169 LEFT, AND WHAT OF IT WAS WRONG.  169 measured the balance on
      one orbit per radius and reported that all six seeds gave positive
      absorption, six of six being 1.6% by chance, so there was "a systematic
      absorption under the noise".  THAT WAS A SMALL-SAMPLE ARTIFACT.  With
      24 seeds and 32 orbits each, 9 to 14 of 24 come out NEGATIVE at the
      outer radii and the mean sits about one standard error from zero.  169e
      is withdrawn.

170b. THE INSTRUMENT NEEDED A SURVIVAL COLUMN FIRST.  The band rides the
      orbital frequency, so its modes are resonant with the orbit by
      construction, and over tens of orbits a resonant mode can pump the pair
      apart.  When it does, the radiated power is computed from an orbit that
      no longer exists: at 0.01 a_pair, <P_rad> jumps from 0.78 W at scale
      0.3 to 5.5e+07 W at scale 1, and a single seed there ends at 25.5 times
      its initial separation.  Every row now reports final/initial
      separation and every number below is filtered to 0.5 <= that <= 2.

170c. THE GRID, 16 seeds x 32 orbits at each point, survivors only.
        scale  r/a_pair  surv    <P_abs>      sigma   <P_rad>      ratio
          0.1     1      16/16  7.8249e-09    0.31  1.1674e-08   6.70e-01
          0.1     0.3    16/16  7.5668e-07    0.44  1.4420e-06   5.25e-01
          0.1     0.1    16/16  9.6837e-05    1.27  1.1611e-04   8.34e-01
          0.1     0.03   16/16  1.3671e-02    1.61  1.3101e-02   1.04e+00
          0.3     1      16/16  2.7943e-08    0.36  1.1678e-08   2.39e+00
          0.3     0.3    16/16  4.5481e-06    0.87  1.4455e-06   3.15e+00
          0.3     0.1    16/16  5.2039e-04    1.86  1.0944e-04   4.75e+00
          0.3     0.03   16/16  3.6344e-02    1.98  1.0471e-02   3.47e+00
          1       1      16/16  2.1350e-07    0.82  1.1729e-08   1.82e+01
          1       0.3    16/16  3.1735e-05    1.56  1.3100e-06   2.42e+01
          1       0.1    15/16  1.1867e-03    1.40  1.0667e-04   1.11e+01
          1       0.03   12/16  1.0812e-01    2.66  1.3305e-02   8.13e+00
      "sigma" is <P_abs> divided by its standard error.  It never exceeds
      2.7: the absorbed power is not resolved from zero at any single point
      in this grid.

170d. BUT THE SLOPE IS RESOLVED, AND THE SLOPE IS THE ANSWER.  Fitted over
      four radii spanning a factor of 33 and five orders of magnitude in the
      power, where the signal-to-noise on a SLOPE is far better than on any
      one point:
        scale    P_abs ~ r^p    P_rad ~ r^p    ratio ~ r^p
          0.1       -4.127         -3.975         -0.153
          0.3       -4.042         -3.911         -0.130
          1         -3.706         -3.978         +0.273
      P_rad comes out at r^-3.98, r^-3.91, r^-3.98 against Larmor's exact
      r^-4 for a circular orbit, which is the instrument validating itself.
      And P_abs carries THE SAME EXPONENT.  The ratio therefore goes as
      r^(-0.15), r^(-0.13), r^(+0.27) -- flat, and not even consistently
      signed.

170e. WHICH ANSWERS STEP 4 IN THE NEGATIVE.  A balance can select a radius
      only if the two powers cross, and they cross only if their ratio
      depends on radius.  It does not.  Over a factor of 33 in r the ratio
      moves by less than a factor of 2 at every scale, with no monotone
      trend.  So P_abs = P_rad is not an equation for a radius at all: it is
      a condition on the ZPF SCALE, satisfied at every radius at once or at
      none.  Measured, it is satisfied near scale 0.1 (ratios 0.52 to 1.04
      across the whole radial range) and violated by an order of magnitude at
      scale 1.

170f. AND THAT IS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT THE PLAN NEEDED.  The plan calls the
      zero-point field "the only thing in the model carrying a scale
      independent of mu, e, m, c" and Step 4 "the single most important
      measurement, because only it can give the Bohr scale".  A
      radius-independent balance gives no scale.  Tuning the ZPF scale to
      0.1 would make the powers match everywhere, which is a normalization
      choice of exactly the kind the plan set out to eliminate -- the same
      objection it makes against L = hbar, one level further down.

170g. WHY THE EXPONENTS COINCIDE, offered as a reading and not a measurement.
      The band is defined as a fixed multiple of the OSCULATING orbital
      frequency, so its amplitude follows omega^2 and every mode stays in
      resonance as the orbit tightens.  A field pinned to the orbit's own
      frequency cannot carry a scale of its own, and the r^-4 it inherits is
      the orbit's.  A band pinned to a FIXED frequency would not do this, and
      that is the version of the experiment this section does not run --
      though the tree's own history says why the band was made to ride: with
      a fixed band the orbit outran it and the lifetime depended strongly on
      where the upper edge was cut.

170h. LIMITS.  16 modes against the production 64, instantaneous mutual
      forces, radiation reaction disabled, circular preparation, one channel,
      32 orbits per run, 16 seeds per point, and the step tied to the fastest
      mode at 32 per cycle.  The survival window 0.5 to 2 is a choice and
      rows outside it are dropped rather than analysed.  The exponent fit
      assumes a pure power law over the four radii; no curvature is tested.
      And 170g is an interpretation of why the exponents match, not a
      measurement of it -- the fixed-band comparison that would test it was
      not run.

171. THE FIXED-BAND CONTROL: THE MECHANISM OF 170g IS CONFIRMED AND THE
     ESCAPE IT SUGGESTED IS CLOSED
==================================================================

171a. WHAT 170g LEFT.  Audit 170 found the zero-point balance carries no
      radial dependence -- P_abs and P_rad both go as r^-4, so their ratio is
      flat and cannot select a radius -- and offered a reading, marked as
      one: the band is defined as a fixed multiple of the OSCULATING orbital
      frequency, so its amplitude follows omega_orb^2 and it inherits the
      orbit's exponent.  A band at a FIXED frequency would not.  That control
      was not run.  It is run here.

171b. THE CONTROL EXISTS NOW.  CREM_ZPF_FIXED_FREQUENCY freezes the band at a
      given angular frequency, routed through one helper that drives all
      three force sites AND the phase accumulation, so a frozen band is
      internally consistent rather than a field sampled at one frequency
      while the phase advances at another.  Unset, it reproduces the riding
      band bit for bit: the validation suite does not move.
      tools/zpf_power_balance.cpp honours it in the sampling step, the phase
      and the reported E_rms, and its step now resolves the FASTER of the
      orbit and the band -- with a frozen band far below the orbit, basing
      the step on the band alone would alias the work integral from the
      other side.

171c. THE MECHANISM IS CONFIRMED.  Frozen at omega_orb(a_pair) =
      2.067069e+16 rad/s, scale 0.3, 20 seeds x 32 orbits:
        r/a_pair   <P_abs>      sem      <P_rad>      ratio
          1       1.8052e-08  5.055e-08  1.1684e-08   1.55e+00
          0.3    -9.5677e-08  6.288e-08  1.4413e-06  -6.64e-02
          0.1    -7.9685e-08  9.106e-08  1.1642e-04  -6.84e-04
          0.03   -1.9303e-07  1.939e-07  1.2962e-02  -1.49e-05
      P_abs is FLAT in radius -- everything is 1e-08 to 2e-07, against the
      riding band's r^-4 over the same range -- while P_rad keeps its r^-4.
      So the ratio swings five orders across the radii instead of staying
      flat, and it does cross one.  170g's mechanism is right.

171d. AND THE ESCAPE IS CLOSED, because the crossing is a crossing of zero.
      P_abs is not resolved from zero anywhere in 171c: |sigma| <= 1.52 and
      7 to 13 of 20 seeds carry the opposite sign.  Raising the amplitude
      does not help, which is the decisive part.  Frozen band, 20 seeds:
        scale  r/a_pair   <P_abs>       sem      sigma   negatives
          1       1      -1.7692e-08  1.843e-07  -0.10     8/20
          1       0.1    -2.1072e-07  3.387e-07  -0.62    14/20
          3       1       1.5702e-07  6.864e-07   0.23     9/20
          3       0.1    -6.3530e-07  1.019e-06  -0.62    14/20
          10      1       1.0757e-06  2.299e-06   0.47     5/20
          10      0.1    -2.1620e-06  3.435e-06  -0.63    14/20
      At 0.1 a_pair the mean goes 2.11e-07, 6.35e-07, 2.16e-06 as the scale
      goes 1, 3, 10 -- factors of 3.0 and 3.4 against scale factors of 3 and
      3.33, so it grows as scale^1 -- and the standard error grows by exactly
      the same factors.  The signal-to-noise is therefore SCALE-INVARIANT and
      pinned near zero.  That is the signature of a quantity whose mean is
      zero and whose width is proportional to the field: off resonance the
      velocity is uncorrelated with the driving field, so the work averages
      away and a bigger field only widens the distribution.

171e. WHICH MAKES THE TWO REQUIREMENTS INCOMPATIBLE, and that is the result.
      Net absorption needs resonance -- the field must be correlated with the
      motion it drives.  Resonance, in this construction, means the band
      rides the orbital frequency.  A riding band carries amplitude
      omega_orb^2 and therefore inherits the orbit's r^-4, which is exactly
      what flattens the ratio and destroys the scale selection.  Freezing the
      band breaks the inheritance and restores a radial dependence, and in
      the same stroke removes the resonance that made there be anything to
      balance.  The zero-point construction cannot do both at once.

171f. SO STEP 4 IS CLOSED FROM BOTH SIDES.  170 showed the riding band gives
      a balance with no radial dependence.  171 shows the only obvious
      alternative gives radial dependence with no balance.  The plan calls
      the zero-point field the only remaining candidate for a scale the model
      does not put in by hand; on the two band prescriptions available it is
      not a candidate, and the reason is structural rather than a matter of
      tuning.

171g. LIMITS.  One frozen frequency, omega_orb(a_pair), chosen so the frozen
      and riding bands coincide at 1 a_pair; a different freeze point would
      move where the ratio crosses but not the flatness of P_abs, which is
      what 171c is about.  Scale 0.3 for 171c and 1 to 10 for 171d, 16 modes,
      20 seeds, 32 orbits, instantaneous mutual forces, radiation reaction
      disabled, circular preparation, survival window 0.5 to 2 (every run
      survived).  171d's scale^1 growth is read from two scale steps at one
      radius, not fitted over a range.  And 171e is a statement about THESE
      two prescriptions -- a band that rides with a different exponent, or
      one shaped to be resonant only over part of the inspiral, is neither
      tested nor excluded here.

172. THE RIDE EXPONENT SCANNED: THE GAP 171g LEFT IS CLOSED, AND 170's
     EXPONENTS WERE QUOTED TO MORE PRECISION THAN THEY HAVE
==================================================================

172a. THE GAP.  171g left open that a band riding with a DIFFERENT exponent,
      or resonant over only part of the inspiral, was neither tested nor
      excluded.  Write the family
        omega_band = omega_ref * (omega_orb/omega_ref)^p,
      so p = 1 is production and p = 0 is 171's frozen band, and the gap is
      the interval between and beyond them.

172b. WHY THE INTERVAL IS NARROW, before measuring anything.  The band is
      [0.3, 3] times its centre, so it overlaps the orbit while
      omega_orb/omega_band stays within about a factor of three.  That ratio
      goes as omega_orb^(1-p), and omega_orb moves by 190 across the radii
      measured, so resonance survives only for |1-p| <~ 0.21.  The scan is
      therefore p = 0.8 to 1.2, and the measured overlap column confirms the
      band never leaves the orbit: omega_orb/omega_band runs 1.00 to 2.86 at
      p = 0.8 and 1.00 to 0.35 at p = 1.2, both inside [0.3, 3].

172c. THE CONTROL IS EXACT AT BOTH ENDS.  p = 1 with a reference frequency
      set reproduces the production band bit for bit, and p = 0 reproduces
      171's frozen band bit for bit -- same P_abs, same P_rad, same band
      frequency to every digit.  The validation suite does not move with any
      of the three variables unset.

172d. AND THE PREDICTION FAILS.  If the absorbed power followed the band
      amplitude in the obvious way the ratio would go as r^(6(1-p)), which
      spans +1.2 to -1.2 across the scan.  Measured, 16 seeds x 32 orbits per
      point, scale 0.3, survivors only:
         p    P_abs ~ r^q   P_rad ~ r^q   ratio ~ r^q   predicted
        0.80     -3.476        -4.062        +0.586       +1.200
        0.90     -3.438        -3.974        +0.537       +0.600
        1.00     -3.394        -4.002        +0.608        0.000
        1.10     -3.383        -3.999        +0.615       -0.600
        1.20     -3.455        -4.000        +0.545       -1.200
      The ratio exponent is +0.42 to +0.62 at EVERY p, with no trend, while
      the prediction sweeps 2.4 across the same range.  P_rad holds -3.97 to
      -4.06 throughout, which is the instrument checking itself against
      Larmor.  Varying the ride exponent inside the resonance window does not
      restore a radial dependence to the balance.

172e. THE BOOTSTRAP, WHICH ALSO CORRECTS 170.  Resampling seeds, 2000 draws,
      68% intervals on the ratio exponent:
        ride p=0.80  +0.57  [+0.19, +0.89]
        ride p=0.90  +0.61  [+0.22, +1.01]
        ride p=1.00  +0.51  [+0.36, +0.72]
        ride p=1.10  +0.60  [+0.21, +1.03]
        ride p=1.20  +0.57  [+0.24, +0.80]
        170 scale 0.1  +0.15  [-0.31, +0.50]
        170 scale 0.3  +0.06  [-0.32, +0.33]
        170 scale 1    +0.29  [-0.04, +0.52]
      The intervals are 0.4 to 1.1 wide.  Audit 170 quoted its exponents to
      three decimals and said the slope was "resolved"; it is not resolved to
      anything like that.  What 170 established, and this does not disturb,
      is that the ratio exponent is CONSISTENT WITH ZERO and far too small to
      make the powers cross within the model's range.  The three-decimal
      presentation overstated it and is corrected here.
      Two independent seed sets at the same configuration -- 170's scale 0.3
      and this scan's p = 1.0 -- give +0.06 and +0.51 with barely overlapping
      intervals, so the true seed-to-seed spread is at the wide end of those
      quotes.

172f. SO THE GAP CLOSES, WITH THE SCOPE STATED.  Across the whole interval
      where the band stays resonant, the balance's radial dependence does not
      move: the ratio exponent sits near +0.5 and never approaches the +-1.2
      the prediction needs.  Outside that interval the band leaves the orbit
      and 171 already showed what happens -- the absorbed power's mean goes
      to zero.  Between the two there is no window in which the zero-point
      construction both absorbs and selects a radius.

172g. LIMITS.  One reference frequency, one scale, one channel, 16 seeds and
      32 orbits per point, 16 modes, instantaneous mutual forces, radiation
      reaction disabled, circular preparation.  The p = 1.2 row at 0.03
      a_pair is the thinnest point, keeping 15 of 16 runs inside the
      survival window against 16 of 16 everywhere else.  (It was quoted at
      7 of 16 when this section was first written, from a snapshot taken
      before the scan finished; the completed scan moved that row's ratio
      exponent from +0.417 to +0.545 and halved its bootstrap interval,
      which strengthens 172d rather than weakening it.)
      "Resonant over only part of the inspiral"
      is addressed here only through the overlap column, which shows the band
      never leaves the orbit inside the scan; a band deliberately shaped to
      engage over one stretch and disengage over another is a different
      construction and is still untested.  And 172d's exponents inherit the
      uncertainty 172e measures -- the conclusion rests on the absence of a
      TREND across p, which is visible against that uncertainty, not on any
      single value.

173. THE de BROGLIE READING NAMED: IT IS THE L = hbar ASSUMPTION, NOT AN
     ALTERNATIVE TO IT, AND IT CARRIES NO STABILITY
==================================================================

173a. WHY THIS IS WRITTEN DOWN.  With audits 170 to 172 closing the
      zero-point route, the next suggestion to arrive was to treat the pair
      as matter waves and let a standing-wave resonance pick the orbit.  It
      is a natural thing to reach for and nothing in this tree named it --
      "de Broglie" appeared zero times before this section -- so where it
      lands is recorded here rather than re-derived each time.

173b. IT LANDS EXACTLY ON THE ASSUMPTION.  On a circular orbit,
      n lambda = 2 pi r with lambda = h/p and p = mu v = sqrt(mu K/r) gives
      2 pi sqrt(mu K r) = n h, hence r = n^2 hbar^2/(mu K) and L = n hbar.
      Against pairBohrRadius:
        n      de Broglie radius [m]        n^2 a_pair [m]      relative
        1   1.058354419074555e-10   1.058354419074555e-10      1.221e-16
        2   4.233417676298220e-10   4.233417676298221e-10      1.221e-16
        3   9.525189771670995e-10   9.525189771670997e-10      2.171e-16
      and the n = 1 circle carries L = 1.000000000000000 hbar, which is bit
      for bit the preparation estimateCremCollapse already uses.  A de
      Broglie resonance is therefore the L = hbar assumption in another
      language: adopting it would re-derive the model's one scale input from
      the same postulate, not remove it.

173c. AND IT SUPPLIES NO STABILITY, with this model as the demonstration.
      The model already runs orbits at exactly that radius with exactly that
      angular momentum, and they collapse: about 199 ps at --level 1 and
      6206 ps at --level 2 with the ground-state floor.  A resonance is a
      SELECTION RULE, not a force.  It says which orbits are allowed and adds
      nothing that resists the decay of one already chosen; the charge
      radiates whether or not the wave closes.  That is precisely the
      distinction the plan drew when it chose Step 4: it wanted something
      that resists collapse WITHOUT fixing the angular momentum.

173d. AND AS A SELECTION RULE IT PICKS A STATE THAT DOES NOT EXIST.  The 1S
      ground state carries L = 0; the n = 1 de Broglie circle carries
      L = hbar.  That is the Bohr-model argument quantum mechanics
      overturned, which the plan's Step 5 already records.  Classically L = 0
      is a head-on plunge rather than an orbit, which is why L = hbar stands
      in this model as a proxy for the localization energy hbar^2/(2 mu r^2)
      it does not carry.

173e. SO THE LIST OF CANDIDATE MECHANISMS IS STILL EMPTY, not shorter by one.
      Audits 170 to 172 removed the zero-point field; this section records
      that the obvious successor was never a candidate, because it is not a
      mechanism at all.  What would count is something that changes the
      dynamics of an orbit already chosen, and the de Broglie condition
      changes only which orbit is chosen.

173f. LIMITS.  173b is algebra plus one numerical check against the model's
      own constants; it says nothing about wave mechanics beyond the circular
      standing-wave condition, and in particular nothing about whether a
      genuine two-body wave treatment would behave differently -- that is a
      different model, not this one with a different preparation.  The
      collapse times in 173c are quoted from README's stamped runs, not
      remeasured here.  No source behaviour changed in this section.

174. THE M1 GATE VERIFIED, AND THE README CARRIED A WITHDRAWN NUMBER NEXT
     TO THE QUOTE THAT WITHDREW IT
==================================================================

174a. THE STRUCTURAL CLAIM, WHICH IS CORRECT.  One quantity gates two
      channels: |mu1 + mu2| decides the M1 channel during the inspiral and,
      through w_2g = |mu1+mu2|^2/(|mu1|+|mu2|)^2, the photon multiplicity at
      annihilation.  For positronium the charges are opposite and the masses
      equal, so mu is proportional to S1 - S2 and mu1 + mu2 is exactly the
      operator that mixes singlet with triplet.  The model therefore carries
      the SHAPE of a charge-parity selection rule without holding any parity
      quantum number, and it carries it in both places for the same reason --
      the coherent cancellation of two antiparallel moments.

174b. AND THE PROTECTION IS DYNAMIC, WHICH WAS ALSO CORRECT AND UNMEASURED.
      Prepared exactly antiparallel at a_pair, tilt 40 degrees, sampled every
      checkpoint over a full orbit:
        channel   |m|/(mu1+mu2) worst   |dm/dt| normalized worst
        para          1.000007              1.000000
        ortho         0.000000              0.000000
      Not small: zero at every checkpoint, so the cancellation holds along
      the trajectory rather than only at preparation.  The mechanism is the
      exchange parity of the mutual field, and it is visible directly in the
      two precession rates:
        channel     |w1| [1/s]      |w2| [1/s]      |w1-w2|/|w1|   M1 [W]
        para      1.830842e+11    4.186317e+11       1.287e+00    1.1406e-23
        ortho     4.186353e+11    4.186353e+11       0.000e+00    0.0000e+00
      In ortho the two moments precess about the same vector at the same
      rate to a relative difference of exactly zero, so d(mu1+mu2)/dt
      vanishes identically.  In para the two rates differ by 129%.

174c. BUT "32 ORDERS" IS WRONG IN KIND, NOT ONLY IN VALUE.  The ortho M1
      power is not small, it is exactly 0.000000, so the ratio between the
      channels is not a finite number of orders at all.  The residue is not
      round-off either: the relative difference of the two precession rates
      prints 0.000e+00, so the derivative vanishes identically rather than
      below a print threshold.  Both readings in the previous text -- "32
      orders" and "the ortho residue is round-off" -- understated what is
      actually there.

174d. AND THE README HELD THAT NUMBER NEXT TO ITS OWN WITHDRAWAL.  Audit 153e
      measured the conditioned ensembles at <|m|^2/mu^2> para 3.4998 against
      ortho 1.5187, a ratio of 2.3045, and recorded that it had "corrected
      the README in place".  It corrected the TABLE.  The bold paragraph
      immediately below the table went on asserting 32 orders, so the
      document carried a block quote saying "2.3045, not 32 orders" with a
      contradiction printed directly underneath it.

174e. THE TWO NUMBERS ANSWER DIFFERENT QUESTIONS, which is what neither
      passage said.  2.3045 is the CONDITIONED ENSEMBLE, which draws states
      that never sit exactly antiparallel -- 153e's own note that the exact
      cancellation happens only at cos = -1, reached with probability zero.
      Exactly zero and dynamically protected is the EXACT antiparallel
      PREPARATION.  Both are true and neither is the other's correction.  The
      README now carries both with their preparations attached, which is
      audit 167's stamp rule applied to a case where its absence had produced
      a visible contradiction rather than a quiet one.

174f. LIMITS.  One radius, one tilt, one orbit, the engine's own checkpoints;
      the ensemble numbers are 153's and are not remeasured here.  174b
      establishes that the cancellation survives a full orbit under the
      trajectory integrator, not that it survives a photon emission or a
      collapse to the barrier -- the secular estimator's own spin transport
      is a separate path and is not exercised by this probe.  Whether the
      exchange parity that enforces w1 = w2 is itself exact or holds only for
      the symmetric preparation used here is not separated: the measurement
      shows it holding, not why it must.

175. THE LAB MOMENTS ARE A VELOCITY-DEPENDENT POTENTIAL, AND THE MODEL
     ALREADY SOLVED THAT ONCE -- BUT NOT AT THE SIZE CLAIMED
==================================================================

175a. THE CLAIM.  That the laboratory moments move hundreds of k/r0 of
      interaction energy even with precession off, because the dipole energy
      is a velocity-dependent potential whose force does not differentiate
      it, so KE + U is not what the dynamics conserves.

175b. THE MECHANISM IS REAL AND THE TREE ALREADY ACTS ON IT -- FOR A
      DIFFERENT TERM.  conservedParticleEnergy is
      conservativeParticleEnergy(state, 0.0) precisely because the
      charge-dipole term -p.E with p = (v x mu)/c^2 is homogeneous of degree
      one in the velocities, cancels in the Legendre transform, and therefore
      "generates a force ... and that force does no work" (audit 145, split
      out in 146).  So the model has met this exact objection once and
      answered it by removing the offending term from the ledger.  The claim
      raises it for the dipole-DIPOLE term, whose velocity dependence enters
      through lorentzBoostDipole: the lab moments differ from the proper ones
      at O(beta^2), and pairDipoleForce differentiates only the separation.

175c. AND THE SIZE DOES NOT SUPPORT THE CONCLUSION.  Instantaneous forces --
      which is the right test, since the tree's own note says an
      instantaneous ledger could be exact there -- reaction off, one orbit,
      tilt 40, ranges over the orbit in units of k/r0:
        r/a_pair   beta     U_dd lab    U_dd proper   lab-proper   E_cons
           1      0.0073   4.1349e-06   4.1348e-06   2.6060e-11   8.2112e-09
           0.1    0.0231   4.1318e-04   4.1315e-04   2.6024e-08   1.0814e-07
           0.01   0.0730   4.0282e-02   4.0244e-02   2.4911e-05   4.7699e-05
           0.003  0.1332   2.5721e+00   2.4430e+00   1.2900e-01   3.1396e-02
           0.001  0.2308   5.2995e-01   4.9063e-01   4.3399e-02   3.1067e-02
      At the working radius the lab and proper moments differ by 2.6e-11
      k/r0 over a whole orbit, and the largest value anywhere in the scan is
      0.129 -- three orders below "hundreds", and reached only at 0.003
      a_pair, which is 1.64 r*, with the last row already inside the barrier
      (r*/a_pair = 0.0018264).

175d. AND KE + U IS CONSERVED, WHICH IS THE PART THAT DECIDES IT.  The
      conserved energy's range over one orbit is 8.2e-09 k/r0 at a_pair --
      nine significant figures -- and 3.1e-02 at 1.64 r*.  At every radius it
      is SMALLER than the lab-versus-proper discrepancy in the same row, so
      whatever the velocity dependence does to U_dd, the ledger the model
      actually keeps is not carrying it.  "KE + U is not what the dynamics
      conserves" is true of conservativeParticleEnergy, which includes the
      charge-dipole term; it is not true of conservedParticleEnergy, which is
      what the balance uses and what these numbers measure.

175e. WHAT WOULD HAVE MADE IT TRUE.  The objection lands when the neglected
      velocity dependence is homogeneous of degree one, because then it
      survives the Legendre transform at full strength -- that is the
      charge-dipole case, where audit 145 measured including the term
      inflating the ledger's range by a factor of 24 to 104.  The boost
      correction to the moments is O(beta^2) instead, and beta is 0.0073 at
      a_pair, so the same argument arrives four orders weaker.  The
      structure of the claim is right; the term it is applied to is the one
      where the structure costs least.

175f. THE RETARDED COMPARISON FINISHED AFTER ALL, and it separates the two.
      Same scan with production retarded forces:
        r/a_pair  lab-proper inst  lab-proper ret   E_cons inst   E_cons ret
           1        2.6060e-11      2.6060e-11     8.2112e-09    8.1614e-07
           0.1      2.6024e-08      2.6029e-08     1.0814e-07    2.5907e-05
           0.01     2.4911e-05      2.4924e-05     4.7699e-05    8.0676e-04
           0.003    1.2900e-01      1.0667e-01     3.1396e-02    2.4924e-01
           0.001    4.3399e-02      5.4384e-02     3.1067e-02    1.5100e-01
      The lab-versus-proper discrepancy is the SAME to five figures with and
      without retardation -- it is a kinematic property of the moments and
      carries no retarded content.  The ledger's range, by contrast, grows a
      hundredfold when the forces become retarded: 8.2e-09 to 8.2e-07 k/r0 at
      a_pair.  So under the forces production actually uses, the drift is
      dominated by retardation and not by the lab moments, by four orders of
      magnitude at the working radius.  That is audit 145's 1.13 |U_dd| per
      orbit arriving in these units.

175g. LIMITS.  Precession is ON in these runs -- there is no
      switch for it -- but the proper moments rotate by 5.0e-05 of
      themselves over the orbit at a_pair, so the comparison is between lab
      and proper moments rather than between precessing and frozen ones,
      which is the comparison the claim actually needs and is not quite the
      one it names.  One tilt, one channel, circular preparation, 2000
      samples per orbit.

176. THE EXCHANGE PARITY IS EXACT IN THE ZERO-MOMENTUM FRAME AND BROKEN BY
     ANYTHING THAT TELLS THE TWO PARTICLES APART
==================================================================

176a. WHAT 174f LEFT.  Audit 174 measured the ortho M1 protection -- |m| and
      d(mu1+mu2)/dt exactly zero over a full orbit, with |w1-w2|/|w1| also
      exactly zero -- and said it showed the exchange parity HOLDING, not why
      it must, leaving open whether the parity is exact or an artifact of the
      symmetric preparation used.  That is separated here.

176b. THE TEST.  Keep the ortho condition mu2 = -mu1 exactly, and break the
      preparation's symmetry in every other way available: the moments'
      orientation, the orbit's shape, a centre-of-mass velocity, and a
      uniform external field as a positive control that the probe can see
      breaking at all.  Worst value over one orbit, ortho throughout:
        preparation                    |w1-w2|/|w1|   |m|/mu_sum  |dm/dt|
        tilt 40, circular (174's)       0.0000e+00   0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00
        tilt 0, moments in the plane    0.0000e+00   0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00
        tilt 90, moments along L        0.0000e+00   0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00
        tilt 17, generic                0.0000e+00   0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00
        eccentric e = 0.3               0.0000e+00   0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00
        eccentric e = 0.7               0.0000e+00   0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00
        centre of mass beta = 0.01      2.0789e+01   3.5178e-05  9.1226e-01
        centre of mass beta = 0.10      1.8020e+01   3.4858e-04  1.0000e+00
        external field 50 uT (control)  4.2590e-05   1.7202e-09  2.1295e-05

176c. SO IT IS EXACT WITH RESPECT TO THE INTERNAL CONFIGURATION.  Four moment
      orientations spanning in-plane to along-L, and two eccentricities out
      to 0.7, all return exactly zero in all three columns.  174's result is
      therefore not an artifact of its tilt or of circularity: nothing about
      how the moments are pointed or how elongated the orbit is touches the
      cancellation.

176d. AND IT IS NOT EXACT IN GENERAL.  A centre-of-mass velocity of 0.01 c
      takes |dm/dt| from zero to 0.91 of its maximum, and 0.10 c to 1.00 --
      the protection is not degraded there, it is gone.  The external-field
      control breaks it too, at 2.1e-05, which is what establishes that the
      probe can detect breaking rather than only reporting zeros.

176e. WHICH NAMES THE SYMMETRY.  It is a property of the ZERO-MOMENTUM FRAME,
      not of the ortho condition by itself.  With the pair at rest as a
      whole, exchanging the two particles and flipping the charge maps the
      configuration onto itself, so B_eff at one is minus B_eff at the other
      and the two precession rates are forced equal; mu goes as S1 - S2 for
      positronium because q1 = -q2 at equal masses, so mu1 + mu2 and its
      derivative vanish together.  A common drift is SYMMETRIC under that
      exchange rather than antisymmetric, so the motional v x E terms stop
      cancelling and the argument fails at first order in the drift.

176f. AND THE MODEL RUNS INSIDE THE CONDITION, which is why 174 saw no hint
      of this.  estimateCremCollapse holds the centre-of-mass velocity at
      zero by construction -- crem_trajectory.hpp says so where it warns that
      a null result from that switch is a statement about the estimator's
      scope and not about physics.  So the M1 protection is real for
      everything this model integrates, and is not a general statement about
      ortho positronium: a pair in flight, or in a field, does not carry it.
      The README's claim now says so.

176g. LIMITS.  One radius, one orbit per case, 500 samples, reaction off,
      tolerance 1e-09.  The |w1-w2|/|w1| column exceeds one in the drifting
      cases because one moment's precession nearly stalls while the other's
      does not, so its magnitude is not a clean measure of how badly the
      parity is broken -- |dm/dt| is, and that is why it is quoted alongside.
      The drift cases are not monotone in beta (20.8 at 0.01 against 18.0 at
      0.10) and nothing here explains that.  176e is an argument, not a
      measurement: what is measured is which perturbations break the
      cancellation, not the mechanism by which they do.

177. 176g's THREE OPEN ITEMS MEASURED, AND 176e's MECHANISM IS WRONG
==================================================================

177a. WHY THE RATIO EXCEEDS ONE -- CONFIRMED, AND IT IS THE DENOMINATOR.
      Following both rates around the orbit at beta_CM = 0.01:
        t/T     w1 [1/s]      w2 [1/s]     |dw|/w1
        0.00   4.69369e+11   4.69369e+11   4.97e-11
        0.20   5.21445e+11   9.53555e+10   8.17e-01
        0.40   5.55669e+11   2.85538e+11   4.86e-01
        0.60   2.86197e+11   5.55626e+11   9.41e-01
        0.80   9.47501e+10   5.21284e+11   4.50e+00
      At t/T = 0.80 the FIRST moment's precession has fallen to 18% of the
      second's, and the metric divides by that one.  The roles swap at
      t/T = 0.20, where the second is the stalled one.  So 176g's guess was
      right and is now measured: the column is asymmetric by construction and
      its large values mark where its own denominator is small.

177b. THE NON-MONOTONICITY IS IN THAT COLUMN ONLY.  Worst over the orbit:
        beta_CM     |dw|/w1      |dm/dt|
          0         0.0000e+00   0.0000e+00
          0.01      2.0789e+01   9.1226e-01
          0.10      1.8020e+01   1.0000e+00
      |dm/dt| is monotone and saturates at one.  The 20.8-then-18.0 of 176g
      is the near-vanishing denominator of 177a, not a physical trend: at
      beta = 0.01 the dip takes w1 to 9.5e+10 out of 4.7e+11, while at
      beta = 0.10 every rate is larger and the dip is relatively shallower.
      The question 176g could not answer has no physical content.

177c. AND 176e's MECHANISM IS NOT THE ONE.  176e argued that a common drift
      is symmetric under exchange, so the motional v x E terms stop
      cancelling.  Tested by rebuilding B_eff with that term switched off, at
      the preparation instant:
        beta_CM   |dw|/w1 full   |dw|/w1 with no motional term
          0        0.0000         0.0000e+00
          0.01     0.0000         3.7745e-11
          0.10     0.0000         1.7925e-10
          0.30     0.0000         5.8240e-10
      The two rates are equal at the preparation instant at EVERY drift up to
      0.3 c, with the motional term in or out.  Whatever breaks the parity,
      it is not that term failing to cancel at an instant.

177d. WHAT DOES BREAK IT IS THE LAB-FRAME BOOST, SEEDED AND THEN AMPLIFIED.
      With a drift the two particles move at different lab velocities, so
      their lab moments are boosted differently and mu1 + mu2 is already
      nonzero at preparation -- and it is linear in the drift:
        beta_CM    |m|/mu_sum at t/T = 0.2    divided by beta
          0.001         2.6957e-06              2.6957e-03
          0.003         8.0871e-06              2.6957e-03
          0.01          2.6954e-05              2.6954e-03
          0.03          8.0778e-05              2.6926e-03
          0.10          2.6614e-04              2.6614e-03
      Constant to four figures out to beta = 0.03 and 2% off by 0.1, which is
      the first relativistic correction.  The precession then amplifies that
      seed: |dm/dt| reaches 0.75 of its maximum by a fifth of an orbit at
      beta = 0.01 and 0.999 at beta = 0.1.  So the parity is broken at
      O(beta_CM) by the boost asymmetry in the moments themselves -- the same
      object audit 175 measured -- and the rates only follow.

177e. WHICH JOINS TWO AUDITS.  175 found the lab-versus-proper moment
      discrepancy to be kinematic, independent of retardation, and negligible
      at the working radius because it goes as beta_orb^2.  Here the same
      discrepancy appears at first order in a DIFFERENT beta, the drift of
      the pair as a whole, and there it is not negligible: it is what removes
      the M1 protection entirely.  The lab moments are harmless where the
      model runs and decisive one step outside it, and both statements are
      about the same term.

177f. LIMITS.  One radius, one tilt, one orbit per drift, 500 samples,
      tolerance 1e-09, reaction off.  177c switches the motional term off
      only at the preparation instant, not along the orbit, so it excludes
      that term as the INSTANTANEOUS mechanism and not as a contributor to
      the growth.  177d's amplification is read from two drifts at four
      phases rather than fitted.  Why the rates stay exactly equal at
      preparation while |m| is already nonzero there is not explained: a
      residual reflection symmetry of that particular instant is the obvious
      candidate and was not tested.

178. THE RESIDUAL SYMMETRY IDENTIFIED: A MIRROR IN THE PLANE OF THE
     SEPARATION AND THE ORBIT NORMAL, WITH THE MOMENT AS A PSEUDOVECTOR
==================================================================

178a. WHAT 177f LEFT.  At the preparation instant the two precession rates
      stay exactly equal under a centre-of-mass drift even though
      |mu1 + mu2| is already nonzero there.  177f named a residual reflection
      symmetry of that instant as the obvious candidate and did not test it.
      It is tested here, and it is right -- and the test says which mirror.

178b. THE MIRROR IS THE PLANE OF THE SEPARATION AND THE ORBIT NORMAL.  At
      preparation the separation lies along x and the orbital velocities
      along +-y, so that plane is x-z.  Rotating a fixed-magnitude drift
      within the orbital plane, psi measured from the separation:
        psi     sin psi     |w1-w2|/w1
          0      0.0000      3.2565e-11
          5      0.0872      1.2019e-01
         15      0.2588      4.0336e-01
         30      0.5000      9.4508e-01
         45      0.7071      1.6115e+00
         60      0.8660      2.3020e+00
         90      1.0000      3.0226e+00
        120      0.8660      2.3020e+00
        180      0.0000      3.2601e-11
      Exactly zero at psi = 0 and 180, where the drift lies IN the mirror;
      maximal at 90, where it is normal to it; and symmetric under
      psi -> 180 - psi to five figures.  A drift along z, also in the mirror,
      gives 8.5500e-13.  So the protection survives any drift inside the
      plane and dies with the component out of it.

178c. AND THE MOMENT OBEYS IT AS A PSEUDOVECTOR, which is the part that looked
      wrong until it was scanned.  Under a reflection a pseudovector's
      IN-plane components flip and its normal component does not, so mu has
      definite parity when it lies in the x-z plane (odd) or along y (even),
      and none in between.  Scanning the moment azimuth with the drift held
      in the mirror:
        phi     |sin 2phi|   |w1-w2|/w1     |m|/mu_sum
          0       0.0000     3.2565e-11     1.1728e-05
         10       0.3420     1.1427e-06     1.1728e-05
         30       0.8660     2.9443e-06     1.1728e-05
         45       1.0000     3.4691e-06     1.1728e-05
         60       0.8660     3.0669e-06     1.1728e-05
         80       0.3420     1.2338e-06     1.1728e-05
         90       0.0000     2.1573e-11     1.1728e-05
        135       1.0000     3.4691e-06     1.1728e-05
        180       0.0000     3.2565e-11     1.1728e-05
      Exactly zero at 0, 90 and 180 -- the three azimuths with definite
      parity -- maximal at 45 and 135, and proportional to |sin 2 phi|, the
      parity-mixing amplitude, to within 4% at every point.  A vector would
      have vanished only at 0 and 180; the extra zero at 90 is what
      identifies mu as a pseudovector under this mirror.

178d. WHICH ANSWERS 177f's PUZZLE OUTRIGHT.  |m| is 1.1728e-05 at EVERY
      azimuth in 178c, unchanged to five figures while the rate difference
      moves over five orders.  So |mu1 + mu2| being nonzero does not break
      the rate equality and never did: the two are independent.  177d's boost
      asymmetry seeds |m| at O(beta_CM), and the mirror, for as long as it
      holds, keeps the two rates equal in spite of it.  Only losing the
      mirror separates them.

178e. THE TWO CHANNELS ARE SIX ORDERS APART.  Taking the drift out of the
      mirror reaches 3.02; taking the moment out of definite parity reaches
      3.47e-06.  Both are real and only the first matters in practice, which
      is why 176's table -- drifts along x, moments in the x-z plane -- saw
      exact zeros in every row that had no drift and order-one breaking in
      every row that did.

178f. LIMITS.  The preparation instant only: this explains why the rates
      start equal, not how the breaking grows once the orbit turns, which is
      177d's amplification and is separate.  One radius, one polar angle
      (theta 40), one drift magnitude (0.01 c), the causalInitialHistory
      rather than an evolved one.  The |sin 2 phi| proportionality is read
      off nine points and not fitted.  And the mirror is identified by which
      perturbations preserve the rates, not by deriving the invariance from
      the field expressions -- 178c's pseudovector reading is what the
      pattern of zeros implies, and it was not checked against the algebra.

179. 178f CLOSED: THE IDENTITY IS B2 = -M(B1), THE MIRROR TURNS WITH THE
     ORBIT, AND THE BREAKING IS LINEAR IN THE ANGLE OFF IT
==================================================================

179a. WHAT 178f LEFT.  Three things: the mirror had been identified from a
      pattern of zeros rather than from the field expressions; the result
      covered the preparation instant only; and one radius, one polar angle,
      one drift magnitude.  The first two are closed here.

179b. THE IDENTITY, MEASURED RATHER THAN GUESSED.  Instead of proposing a
      symmetry operation, compare the two B_eff VECTORS and see which
      relation they satisfy.  At the preparation instant, drift 0.01 c along
      the separation, moments in the mirror:
        B1 = (-1.007828e+00, +2.761899e-05, +2.950676e+00)
        B2 = (+1.007828e+00, +2.761899e-05, -2.950676e+00)
      The y components share a SIGN while x and z reverse, so the relation is
        B2 = -M(B1),   M = reflection in the separation-normal plane,
      which holds to 4.3e-11 while B2 = -B1 misses by 1.8e-05.  A reflection
      composed with a negation preserves the norm, and that -- not a parity
      argument about the moments -- is why |B1| = |B2| and the rates are
      equal.  178c's pseudovector reading described the pattern correctly and
      named the wrong reason.

179c. AND THE DISTINCTION MATTERS, because the two break at different rates.
      Taking the moments out of definite parity (azimuth 45) breaks the
      VECTOR identity by 0.233 while the norms still agree to 3.5e-06.  So
      the rate equality survives far beyond the identity that produces it:
      the residual rotates B2 without changing its length.  178 could not see
      this because it measured only the norms.

179d. THE MIRROR TURNS WITH THE ORBIT, which closes 178f's second item.  It
      is the INSTANTANEOUS separation-normal plane, so a drift fixed in the
      laboratory lies in it only when the separation is along the drift.
      Following one orbit with the drift along x:
        t/T     separation angle   |w1-w2|/w1   B2 vs -M(B1)
        0.000        0.00 deg      3.2565e-11    4.3193e-11
        0.125       45.00          6.3827e-01    6.8641e-01
        0.250       89.99          8.1423e-01    8.6039e-01
        0.375      134.97          6.3865e-01    6.8682e-01
        0.500      179.94          1.3641e-03    1.5166e-03
        0.625     -135.09          1.7601e+00    1.8929e+00
        0.750      -90.11          4.3829e+00    4.6314e+00
        1.000       -0.12          2.7283e-03    3.0334e-03
      The equality is restored at 0 and 180 degrees and nowhere else, and the
      two columns track each other to within 8% at EVERY phase.  So the rate
      breaking is the vector identity's breaking, quantitatively, all the way
      round -- not only where it vanishes.

179e. AND IT IS LINEAR IN THE ANGLE OFF THE MIRROR.  Sampling through the
      crossing at t/T = 0.5:
        angle off 180 deg    |w1-w2|/w1    ratio per degree
            1.86            4.1355e-02        2.22e-02
            1.50            3.3487e-02        2.23e-02
            1.14            2.5554e-02        2.24e-02
            0.78            1.7557e-02        2.25e-02
            0.42            9.4935e-03        2.26e-02
            0.06            1.3641e-03        2.27e-02
      Constant to 2% over a thirtyfold range in the angle.  The protection is
      therefore not a knife edge: it degrades smoothly and predictably as the
      configuration leaves the mirror, which is also why 177d's amplification
      looked like growth in time -- the orbit is carrying the separation away
      from the drift, at a rate the orbit itself sets.

179f. LIMITS.  179b and 179c are the preparation instant; 179d and 179e one
      orbit at one radius with one drift magnitude and one polar angle, so
      the slope of 2.25e-02 per degree is a number for this configuration and
      not a general coefficient.  The identity B2 = -M(B1) is verified
      numerically at the configurations listed and is still not derived from
      the field expressions -- what 179b replaces is the guess about WHICH
      relation holds, not the absence of a derivation.  Why the norms survive
      a 23% violation of the identity (179c) is unexplained.

180. 179f CLOSED: THE IDENTITY FOLLOWS FROM THREE INPUT RELATIONS, THE NORMS
     ARE PROTECTED BY A DIFFERENT SYMMETRY, AND THE SLOPE IS NOT GENERAL
==========================================================================

180a. WHAT 179f LEFT.  Three items: the identity B2 = -M(B1) was verified
      numerically and not derived; the slope of 2.25e-02 per degree was one
      configuration's number; and the survival of the norms through a 23%
