      violation of the identity was unexplained.  All three are closed here,
      and closing the first turned up a naming error in 179b.

180b. THE DERIVATION, ITEM 1.  Do not attack B_eff directly.  Measure which
      relation each INPUT to the Thomas-BMT combination satisfies, by
      searching the eight sign patterns for the one each pair obeys.  At the
      preparation instant, drift 0.01 c along the separation, moments in the
      mirror, theta 40, r = a_pair:
        v:      v2 = M(v1)       residual 0.0 exactly
        E:      E2 = M(E1)       residual 2.835e-11
        B:      B2 = -M(B1)      residual 5.232e-11
      The velocity relation is exact because it IS the preparation: the two
      orbital velocities are opposite and the drift lies in the plane.  So E
      transforms as a polar vector and B as an axial one under one and the
      same reflection, which is the whole physical content.
        Given those three, the identity is algebra.  M is orthogonal, so
      gamma2 = gamma1 and every scalar coefficient is shared; and det M = -1,
      so M(a) x M(b) = -M(a x b).  Term by term:
        magnetic      (a + 1/g) B2          = -M[ (a + 1/g) B1 ]
        longitudinal  b2 (b2 . B2)          = -M[ b1 (b1 . B1) ]
        motional      b2 x E2               = -M[ b1 x E1 ]
      Each picks up exactly one minus sign, and from a DIFFERENT place: the
      axial B in the first, the dot product b2 . B2 = -b1 . B1 in the second,
      the determinant of the cross product in the third.  Their sum is
      therefore -M of the sum, which is the identity.

180c. AND THE PREDICTION IS CHECKED TERM BY TERM, which 179 never did:
        term                       share of |B_eff|    residual
        magnetic   (a + 1/g) B          7.664e-01      5.232e-11
        longitudinal b (b . B)          1.993e-08      1.157e-10
        motional   b x E                2.514e-01      2.835e-11
      The magnetic term's residual equals B's to every digit and the motional
      term's equals E's, as it must when one is a scalar multiple of the
      other and the other a cross product with an exact factor.  The identity
      is not a cancellation between terms: it holds separately in each.

180d. A CORRECTION TO 179b: THE PLANE WAS MISNAMED.  179b calls M "reflection
      in the separation-normal plane".  The measured pattern flips x and z and
      keeps y, with the separation along x, so the normal is y and the plane
      is the one CONTAINING the separation -- not normal to it.  Adding
      velocity components that separate the two candidate names settles it:
        velocity added                        normal = y    normal = sep
        none (circular)                       9.4972e-17      6.4648e-01
        radial 0.01 c (along the separation)  4.3193e-11      6.4645e-01
        radial 0.05 c                         1.0928e-10      6.4580e-01
        out of plane 0.01 c (along z)         3.8539e-12      1.2520e+00
        radial 0.03 + out of plane 0.01       7.2053e-11      1.2520e+00
      A radial velocity does not break it and neither does an out-of-plane
      one, because both lie in the x-z plane.  The mirror is the plane
      SPANNED BY THE SEPARATION AND THE ORBIT NORMAL, equivalently the plane
      normal to the transverse velocity.  Every number in 179b, 179d and 179e
      is unaffected -- they were computed with the correct operation and
      described with the wrong name.

180e. ITEM 3: THE NORMS SURVIVE BECAUSE A DIFFERENT SYMMETRY PROTECTS THEM.
      179c read the 0.233 and the 3.5e-06 as one breaking seen at two
      strengths.  They are two symmetries.  Scan the drift at moment azimuth
      45, where 179c measured:
        beta_drift    identity residual    |B2|/|B1| - 1
             0            2.3317e-01          0.000e+00
          1e-06           2.3317e-01          3.480e-10
          1e-04           2.3317e-01          3.470e-08
           0.01           2.3316e-01          3.469e-06
           0.10           2.3216e-01          3.458e-05
      The vector residual does not depend on the drift at all; the norm
      residual is exactly linear in it and exactly zero without it.  So
      turning the moments out of the plane destroys the MIRROR, which needs
      the moments in it (180b's E and B relations fail), and leaves the
      ZERO-MOMENTUM-FRAME EXCHANGE PARITY of 176 untouched, which holds at
      any moment orientation and is broken only by the drift.  The norms
      answer to the second symmetry and the identity to the first.  That is
      why a 23% violation of one costs the other nothing.

180f. AND THE NORM RESIDUAL FOLLOWS |sin 2 phi|, as 178 found for the rates:
        phi      |B2|/|B1| - 1     ratio to phi 45     sin 2 phi
         15        1.6754e-06          0.4829            0.5000
         30        2.9443e-06          0.8487            0.8660
         45        3.4691e-06          1.0000            1.0000
         60        3.0669e-06          0.8840            0.8660
         75        1.7980e-06          0.5183            0.5000
         90       -2.1573e-11         -0.0000            0.0000
      Matching to 3%.  The vector residual does not: it rises monotonically
      to 3.367e-01 at phi 90, where the norm residual is back at the
      numerical floor.  Two different functions of the same angle, which is
      the same conclusion as the drift scan by another route.

180g. THE 23% IS A ROTATION, MEASURED.  Splitting the residual d = B2 - target
      about the target direction:
        phi     |d|/|B1|     parallel/|d|    -|d|/2|B1|    angle B2 to target
         15    8.3875e-02     -4.1918e-02    -4.1938e-02        4.81 deg
         30    1.6322e-01     -8.1590e-02    -8.1608e-02        9.38
         45    2.3316e-01     -1.1657e-01    -1.1658e-01       13.40
         60    2.8852e-01     -1.4425e-01    -1.4426e-01       16.60
         90    3.3668e-01     -1.6834e-01    -1.6834e-01       19.38
      The parallel component equals -|d|/2|B1| to four digits at every
      azimuth, which is the chord condition for two vectors of equal length.
      So 179c's 23% is 13.4 degrees of rotation and nothing else.  Note this
      is a CONSEQUENCE of the norms being equal, not a reason for it -- the
      reason is 180e.  It does put a number on what 179c called a rotation.

180h. ITEM 2: THE SLOPE IS NOT A GENERAL COEFFICIENT.  Turning the orbit off
      the mirror by psi and taking the slope of the residual from psi = 0 to
      2 degrees.  First with the drift switched off, which 179 never tried:
        r/a_pair   beta_orb    theta 20      theta 70
            1       0.0073    2.9868e-03    8.5585e-03
            0.1     0.0231    5.9408e-03    1.4376e-02
            0.01    0.0730    8.0096e-03    1.5044e-02
            0.003   0.1332    3.2586e-03    3.9185e-03
      There is a drift-free floor, so the breaking is not purely a drift
      effect, and the floor is not monotonic in the radius -- it turns over
      between 0.01 and 0.003 a_pair, which is where the softening floor sits.
      Restoring the drift, at theta 40:
        r/a_pair    beta_drift    slope per degree
            1          0.001         6.1159e-03
            1          0.01          2.4102e-02
            1          0.05          1.0776e-01
            0.1        0.001         1.0468e-02
            0.1        0.05          2.4193e-02
            0.01       0.001         1.2462e-02
            0.01       0.05          1.2775e-02
      Over the full grid (three radii, three polar angles, three drifts) the
      slope runs from 2.99e-03 to 1.12e-01 per degree, a factor of 38.
      179e's 2.25e-02 is reproduced here as 2.41e-02 at 179's own
      configuration -- 7% apart, which is the difference between 179e's
      crossing at 180 degrees on an evolved orbit and this one at 0 degrees
      at preparation.  It is that configuration's number, as 179f suspected,
      and the surrounding range is now measured rather than guessed at.

180i. WHAT DOES NOT SCALE.  Subtracting the drift-free floor leaves a drift
      term that is not a clean function of beta_drift/beta_orbital: dividing
      it by the square of that ratio gives 2.5e-02 down to 3.7e-03 across the
      same grid, so no single power collapses it.  No scaling law is claimed.

180j. LIMITS.  180b derives the identity FROM the three input relations and
      does not derive those: why the retarded two-body E and B transform as a
      polar and an axial vector across this plane is still only the statement
      that the source pair is mirror-symmetric when the moments lie in the
      plane, checked by the azimuth scan rather than by the field integrals.
      The term-by-term check in 180c is one configuration.  180e's separation
      of the two symmetries rests on the drift scan at one azimuth and one
      radius; the |sin 2 phi| law in 180f is read off six points and not
      fitted, as in 178.  180h is the preparation instant with the orbit
      rotated rigidly, not an evolved orbit, which is why its slope differs
      from 179e's by 7% at the matching configuration -- the two are close
      but they are not the same measurement.

181. 180j INVESTIGATED: THE SYMMETRY IS C.P, EXACT AND UNCONDITIONAL IN THE
     ZERO-MOMENTUM FRAME, AND 178 TO 180 WERE TRACKING THE WRONG TARGET
==========================================================================

181a. WHAT 180j LEFT.  Five items: the three input relations of 180b were
      asserted and not derived; the term-by-term check was one
      configuration; 180e's separation of the two symmetries rested on one
      azimuth and one radius; the |sin 2 phi| law was read off six points;
      and 180h was the preparation instant with a rigidly rotated orbit.
      Looking for the derivation produced an operation, and testing that
      operation produced a second and better one.  The first four items are
      closed; the fifth is superseded, because the quantity 180h was
      measuring turns out not to be the symmetry's residual.

181b. THE FIRST OPERATION: A ROTATION, NOT A REFLECTION.  As matrices the
      reflection 179 and 180 used is minus a rotation, M = -R_y(pi), with y
      the orbital-velocity direction.  Written as the rotation
        A = R_y(pi) = diag(-1, +1, -1)
      it maps particle 1 onto particle 2 outright:
        A(r1) = r2       the separation is normal to the axis
        A(v1) = -v2      needs the drift to have no component on y
        q2 = -q1         so A(source 1) carries the SAME current, both the
                         charge and the velocity reversing, and the opposite
                         charge density
        A(mu1) = -mu1    needs the moments to have no component on y, and
               = mu2     needs them equal in magnitude
      Three preconditions, each falsifiable, none of them tested by 179 or
      180.  All three hold.

181c. PRECONDITION 1: a drift along the orbital velocity must break it,
      while one along the separation or the orbit normal must not.  180d
      tested exactly the two that do not and concluded the mirror was
      robust; the one direction it never tried is the one that breaks:
        drift direction                     E           B       B_eff
        none                        3.667e-16   1.239e-16   9.497e-17
        0.01 c along x (separation) 2.835e-11   5.232e-11   4.319e-11
        0.01 c along z (orbit nrml) 1.137e-15   2.969e-12   3.854e-12
        0.01 c along y (orbital v)  1.114e-04   3.649e+00   3.321e+00
        0.001 c along y             1.113e-05   4.274e-01   1.470e-01
        1e-5 c along y              1.113e-07   3.598e-03   1.376e-03
      Nine orders of magnitude between the y direction and the other two at
      the same speed, and the E residual is exactly linear in the y
      component over the three decades measured.

181d. PRECONDITION 2: the moments must have no component on the axis.  The
      breaking is exactly linear in the azimuth over three decades:
        moment azimuth         E             B          B_eff
        phi = 0        2.835e-11     5.232e-11      4.319e-11
        phi = 0.001    4.108e-10     7.361e-06      5.641e-06
        phi = 0.01     4.098e-09     7.361e-05      5.641e-05
        phi = 0.1      4.098e-08     7.361e-04      5.641e-04
        phi = 1        4.098e-07     7.361e-03      5.641e-03
        phi = 45       1.660e-05     3.087e-01      2.332e-01
      7.361e-03 per degree for B, 5.641e-03 for B_eff, no fitted parameter.

181e. PRECONDITION 3: the two moments must be equal in magnitude, because
      the step that maps mu1 to mu2 is the charge conjugation.  Quantitative:
        pair                 |B2|/|B1|   |mu1|/|mu2|    residual    ratio-1
        electron + positron  1.0000e+00  1.0000e+00   5.2321e-11          0
        electron + antimuon  2.0677e+02  2.0677e+02   2.05765e+02 2.05767e+02
        proton + antiproton  1.0000e+00  1.0000e+00   1.9687e-11          0
      The residual IS |mu1|/|mu2| - 1 to five significant figures.  And
      protonium, a pair 1836 times heavier, holds to 2.0e-11: the condition
      is on the moments, not on the species.

181f. AND THE PRECONDITIONS WERE THE CLUE I ALMOST MISSED.  A symmetry of
      the LAWS carries no preconditions; only a symmetry of a CONFIGURATION
      does.  Three conditions on the state therefore said this was not the
      deepest relation available, and that a conditionless one might exist.
      For a particle-antiparticle pair the exchange of the two labels is
      charge conjugation, so the candidate to try is C composed with the
      spatial inversion.  At the preparation instant P and R_y(pi) both send
      r1 to r2 -- the separation lies along x and has no y or z component to
      tell them apart -- which is why 179 and 180 could not distinguish
      them, and why the whole line of work settled on the wrong one.

181g. C.P IS EXACT AND UNCONDITIONAL.  Its field relations are
        E2 = +E1        B2 = -B1
      and in the zero-momentum frame they hold BITWISE:
         r/a_pair   theta     phi      E2 - E1      B2 + B1
             1        40       0     0.000e+00    0.000e+00
             1        40      20     0.000e+00    0.000e+00
             1        40      45     0.000e+00    0.000e+00
             1        40      80     0.000e+00    0.000e+00
             1        15      33     0.000e+00    0.000e+00
             1        65      77     0.000e+00    0.000e+00
          0.01        15      33     0.000e+00    0.000e+00
          0.01        65      77     0.000e+00    0.000e+00
      Not "to the floor" -- identically zero, at every moment azimuth, every
      polar angle and every radius tried.  There is no azimuth condition and
      no polar-angle condition.  The only precondition C.P inherits is 181e's
      181e's equal moments, which is what makes particle 2 the antiparticle
      of particle 1.  It survives an evolved orbit unchanged; see 181o.

181h. SO 178, 179 AND 180's AZIMUTH DEPENDENCE WAS THE WRONG TARGET, and it
      can be named exactly.  Since B2 = -B1 identically, testing against
      axial(B1) = (-B1x, +B1y, -B1z) instead leaves precisely 2|B1_y|:
        phi    axial residual    2|B1y|/|B1|    difference
         10      7.338554e-02    7.338554e-02     0.000e+00
         20      1.453892e-01    1.453892e-01     0.000e+00
         45      3.087033e-01    3.087033e-01     0.000e+00
         80      4.451490e-01    4.451490e-01     0.000e+00
        130      3.365288e-01    3.365288e-01     0.000e+00
      Zero difference at every azimuth.  179c's 0.233, 179d's whole orbit
      column, 180f's vector residual and 180h's drift-free slope are all
      this one quantity: the y component of B1, mis-signed by the target.
      They are arithmetic about the test, not physics about the pair.

181i. AND THIS CLOSES 180j's FIRST ITEM PROPERLY.  180b derived the identity
      from three input relations it could not itself derive.  C.P supplies
      them outright, exactly and without conditions:
        v2 = -v1        E2 = +E1        B2 = -B1
      and every Thomas-BMT term is then odd, each taking its minus from a
      different place -- B itself in the magnetic term; the dot product in
      the longitudinal one, where beta's two minuses cancel and B supplies
      the one that survives; the cross product in the motional one, where
      beta supplies it and E none.  So B_eff2 = -B_eff1.  Measured over 27
      configurations, three radii by three polar angles by three azimuths:
        every term, every configuration, residual 0.000e+00 exactly.
      Not a floor.  This is the derivation 180j asked for, and unlike 180b's
      it rests on relations that are themselves derived rather than
      measured.

181j. THE DRIFT IS WHAT BREAKS C.P, AND IT BREAKS IT LINEARLY:
        beta_drift    phi      E2 - E1      B2 + B1
             0          0    0.000e+00    0.000e+00
             0         45    0.000e+00    0.000e+00
          1e-04          0    2.798e-07    7.693e-08
          1e-04         45    3.254e-07    2.322e-07
          1e-02          0    2.798e-05    7.693e-06
          1e-02         45    3.254e-05    2.322e-05
      Four digits of linearity over two decades.  P reverses velocities, and
      the drift is COMMON to the two particles rather than opposite, so it
      is the one part of the state P cannot exchange.  That is the whole
      mechanism, and it is frame-dependent by construction.

181k. WHICH IDENTIFIES 176's EXCHANGE PARITY EXACTLY.  176 found the
      exchange parity exact in the zero-momentum frame and only there, and
      could not say what the operation was.  It is C.P: for a
      particle-antiparticle pair the exchange of the labels IS charge
      conjugation, the spatial part is the inversion through the centre of
      mass, and the zero-momentum frame is precisely the frame in which the
      drift -- the part P cannot exchange -- vanishes.  176 was right and is
      now named.

181l. A READING OF MINE THAT DID NOT SURVIVE EITHER.  The preparation
      residual of the ROTATION relation grows as r^-2, which I took for an
      acceleration field breaking an identity exact for the velocity field.
      Its own test refutes it -- scaling the orbital speed down at fixed
      geometry, which scales the acceleration as its square:
        orbit scale   acceleration share   E residual   B residual
            1            1.0000e+00       2.8353e-11   5.2321e-11
            0.1          1.0000e-02       2.8353e-11   1.2323e-11
            0            0                2.8351e-11   1.2443e-11
      With the orbit stopped dead the residual is unchanged to five digits.
      It is round-off, and its r^-2 growth is the floor rising as the field
      evaluation stiffens.

181m. 180j's REMAINING ITEMS, WITH WHAT THEY NOW MEAN.  Item 2, the
      term-by-term check, is done at 27 configurations in 181i rather than
      one, and exactly rather than at a floor.  Item 3, 180e's split of the
      two symmetries, survives in substance and gains its names: the norms
      answer to C.P, which no moment orientation touches and only the drift
      breaks, and the vector residual answers to nothing -- it is 181h's
      2|B1_y|.  Item 4, the |sin 2 phi| law, is a law about C.P's DRIFT
      breaking, and fitting it densely rather than reading six points shows
      it is SIGNED:
        A |sin 2 phi| through the origin    A = -2.1e-11, i.e. nothing
        A sin(2 phi), 179 points            A = 3.470579e-06
        rms residual  5.02e-08 (1.45% of A)
        worst         7.18e-08 (2.07% of A) at phi = 112 deg
      178 and 180f saw a modulus because they measured |w1 - w2|, which is
      unsigned by construction.  Item 5 is superseded: 180h measured the
      angle dependence of 2|B1_y|, accurately, but that is not a symmetry's
      residual and its range of 2.99e-03 to 1.12e-01 per degree describes
      the test rather than the pair.

181n. IS IT CPT?  No, and the distinction is not bookkeeping.  CPT is a
      theorem about the LAWS of a Lorentz-invariant local quantum field
      theory and holds unconditionally; this model is classical
      Maxwell-Lorentz, and what is measured here is a symmetry of one
      CONFIGURATION.  The laws' own C, P and T invariances are separately
      true and say nothing about this pair.  What is exact here is C.P, with
      no T at all.  T does enter the secondary rotation relation -- R_y(pi)
      carries particle 1's ORBIT onto particle 2's only under t -> -t, which
      is why that relation is exact at the time-symmetric synthetic
      preparation history and degrades once a genuine retarded history
      accumulates.  Since R_y(pi) = P.M_y, that operation is (C.P.T).M_y:
      CPT is a FACTOR of it, composed with a reflection, and the reflection
      is what carries the three preconditions.  A relation needing
      preconditions is not a law-level symmetry, which is 181f's argument
      running the other way.

181o. AND C.P SURVIVES AN EVOLVED ORBIT, WHICH RETIRES THE REST.  Six
      orbits, moment azimuth 37 degrees chosen to be generic, reporting the
      WORST value reached anywhere within each turn rather than at the
      turns:
        turn    zero drift: worst E2-E1   worst B2+B1
          0             0.0000e+00         0.0000e+00
          1             0.0000e+00         0.0000e+00
          2             0.0000e+00         0.0000e+00
          3             0.0000e+00         0.0000e+00
          4             0.0000e+00         0.0000e+00
          5             0.0000e+00         0.0000e+00
          6             0.0000e+00         0.0000e+00
      Exactly zero at all 2400 steps, not at the symmetric points only.  So
      three things I had measured on the way here were measurements of the
      wrong target and are withdrawn: that the identity fails by 34%
      mid-orbit at zero drift; that it grows with accumulated retarded
      history at 2.2e-04 per orbit; and that it is therefore exact only at
      the preparation instant.  None of that is true of the pair.  It was
      true of 2|B1_y| as the orbit turned.
        With the drift on the breaking is bounded and does not accumulate
      either -- E stays at 1.0607e-04 across all six turns and B moves from
      7.6925e+00 to 7.7155e+00, a third of one percent over six orbits.

181p. AND C.P INHERITS THE MOMENT CONDITION, which is the one precondition
      that is real:
        pair                     E2 - E1        B2 + B1
        electron + positron    0.0000e+00     0.0000e+00
        proton + antiproton    0.0000e+00     0.0000e+00
        electron + antimuon    2.7792e-05     2.0576e+02
      2.0576e+02 is |mu1|/|mu2| - 1 again.  This is not a fourth condition
      bolted on: it is the statement that particle 2 must BE the
      antiparticle of particle 1, without which the label exchange is not
      charge conjugation and there is no C to compose with P.

181q. LIMITS.  C.P is verified, not proved: 181i derives B_eff2 = -B_eff1
      FROM v2 = -v1, E2 = +E1 and B2 = -B1, and those three are measured at
      the configurations listed rather than obtained from the
      Lienard-Wiechert integrals.  What makes them more than 180b's
      equivalents is that they are exact and carry no conditions, and that
      the operation producing them is named -- but the field-integral
      argument is still absent.  The zero-drift results are bitwise zero at
      every configuration tried, which is strong but is not a proof that no
      configuration breaks them; in particular every orbit here is circular
      and every history either synthetic or six turns long, and the
      radiation reaction is off throughout, so whether C.P survives a
      T-odd reaction term is untested and is the obvious next question.
      181j's linearity in the drift is two decades at two azimuths.  181o
      is one radius.  And 177d's "amplification", which 181h shows to be
      largely 2|B1_y|, has not been re-measured against C.P -- it is
      flagged, not corrected.

182. 181q CONTINUED, TOWARD THE EMISSION CONFIGURATION: C.P HAS TWO
     CONDITIONS, THE ORBIT CAN BREAK NEITHER, AND ONLY THE PREPARATION CAN
==========================================================================

182a. WHY 181q's FLAGGED ITEM IS THE EMISSION QUESTION.  181 left the
      radiation reaction off throughout and flagged whether C.P survives a
      T-odd reaction term as the obvious next test.  It is more than
      housekeeping, because C.P is a SELECTION RULE: it does not suppress
      multipoles, it forbids them outright.  So asking where the model can
      emit a photon outside its one open channel is the same as asking
      where C.P breaks.

182b. WHICH MULTIPOLES C.P FORBIDS.  Under C.P the charge reverses and the
      position reverses, so a moment carrying q r^l picks up (-1)^(l+1):
        electric dipole      d = sum q r            EVEN    allowed
        electric quadrupole  Q = sum q (3xx - r^2)  ODD     forbidden
        orbital magnetic     m = sum (q/2) r x v    ODD     forbidden
        intrinsic magnetic   mu1 + mu2              ODD     forbidden
      A C.P-symmetric pair therefore has NO even electric multipole and
      every odd one.  electrodynamics.hpp already derives that rule from the
      mass ratio, kappa_l = [q1 m2^l + (-1)^l q2 m1^l]/M^l; C.P says why it
      holds, makes it exact rather than a consequence of equal masses
      cancelling, and extends it to the magnetic sector in the same step.

182c. AND IT IS NOT SUPPRESSION.  The three leading moments, each divided by
      its own natural scale, moments antiparallel:
        pair                   m1/m2    |d| E1   |m| M1orb    |Q| E2
        electron + positron   1.0000  1.000e+00  0.000e+00  0.000e+00
        proton + antiproton   1.0000  1.000e+00  0.000e+00  0.000e+00
        muon + antimuon       1.0000  1.000e+00  0.000e+00  0.000e+00
        electron + antimuon   0.0048  1.000e+00  9.904e-01  2.426e+00
        proton + electron     1836.2  1.000e+00  9.989e-01  2.447e+00
      For a particle-antiparticle pair the forbidden moments are identically
      zero while the allowed one is at full strength; for an asymmetric pair
      the same two are at full strength themselves.  Three mass scales
      spanning a factor of 1836 on the symmetric side.

182d. C.P HAS TWO INDEPENDENT CONDITIONS, AND THEY GATE DIFFERENT CHANNELS:
        kinematic:  q2 = -q1 and m2 = m1, which give r2 = -r1 and v2 = -v1
                    in the zero-momentum frame
        spin:       mu2 = -mu1, the antiparallel preparation
      The first is a property of the pair and the frame; the second is a
      property of the PREPARATION.  Neither is a property of the orbit,
      which is the whole answer to where the emission configuration is.

182e. THE FLAGGED ITEM, ANSWERED: THE REACTION DOES NOT BREAK C.P, and
      neither does going deep.  Antiparallel moments, zero drift:
         r/a_pair    reaction off: E, B    reaction on: E, B
             1        0.000e+00 0.000e+00   0.000e+00 0.000e+00
             0.1      0.000e+00 0.000e+00   0.000e+00 0.000e+00
             0.01     0.000e+00 0.000e+00   0.000e+00 0.000e+00
             0.003    0.000e+00 0.000e+00   0.000e+00 0.000e+00
             0.001    0.000e+00 0.000e+00   0.000e+00 0.000e+00
      r* sits at 1.8264e-03 a_pair, so the last row is INSIDE the Compton
      barrier and the residual is still bitwise zero, with the Landau-
      Lifshitz reaction switched on.  The reaction is T-odd and C.P carries
      no T, which is why it costs nothing.

182f. AND ONLY THE SPIN CONDITION OPENS ANYTHING.  Opening the two moments
      by delta from antiparallel, at r = a_pair:
        delta  |mu1+mu2|     C.P E      C.P B      E1 [W]      M1 [W]
           0     0.0000  0.000e+00  0.000e+00  1.1673e-08  0.0000e+00
           1     0.0087  1.941e-07  9.561e-03  1.1673e-08  8.5104e-28
          10     0.0872  2.016e-06  9.975e-02  1.1673e-08  6.8998e-26
          45     0.3827  9.849e-06  4.928e-01  1.1673e-08  3.6565e-25
          90     0.7071  1.880e-05  9.239e-01  1.1673e-08  1.0488e-25
         135     0.9239  2.257e-05  1.227e+00  1.1673e-08  5.0247e-24
         180     1.0000  1.995e-05  1.681e+00  1.1673e-08  1.6200e-23
      Three things at once.  M1 is identically zero at delta = 0 and nonzero
      at every other angle, so the gate is exact and not a small-parameter
      suppression.  E1 does not move at all, to five digits, across the
      whole sweep -- the spin condition does not touch the charge sector.
      And the E2 power stays exactly zero throughout (not tabulated because
      every entry is 0.0000e+00): the moments cannot open an even ELECTRIC
      multipole, because that one answers to the kinematic condition, which
      they leave untouched.

182g. THE KINEMATIC CONDITION IS BROKEN BY MASS ASYMMETRY AND BY NOTHING
      ELSE IN REACH.  Measuring the E2 power beside the C.P residual:
        pair                  m1/m2     C.P E      C.P B      E2 [W]
        electron + positron  1.0000  0.000e+00  0.000e+00  0.0000e+00
        proton + antiproton  1.0000  0.000e+00  0.000e+00  0.0000e+00
        muon + antimuon      1.0000  0.000e+00  0.000e+00  0.0000e+00
        electron + antimuon  0.0048  3.449e-05  2.058e+02  3.2608e-12
        proton + electron    1836.2  5.814e-05  9.992e-01  3.3455e-12
      E2 is nonzero exactly where C.P fails and zero exactly where it holds.
      The correspondence is one-to-one across five pairs.

182h. AND THE FLOW CONSERVES BOTH CONDITIONS.  The static checks above are
      preparation instants; this one evolves, with the reaction on and the
      moments free to move, at r0 = 0.1 a_pair:
        t/T     r/r0      C.P E       C.P B     |mu1+mu2|
        0.00  1.000000  0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00
        0.50  0.998857  0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00
        1.00  0.999895  0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00
        1.50  0.998752  0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00
        2.00  0.999791  0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00
        2.50  0.998647  0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00
        3.00  0.999686  0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00  0.0000e+00
      The moment SUM stays bitwise zero while the two moments themselves
      move.  So a pair prepared antiparallel does not merely start with the
      magnetic channel closed -- it keeps it closed, exactly, for as long as
      the integration runs.  That is what "dynamically protected" should
      have meant all along, and it is a symmetry statement rather than the
      numerical coincidence earlier sections took it for.
        Started at delta = 10 degrees instead, the breaking is bounded and
      nearly constant -- the C.P B residual moves from 1.6804e-01 to
      1.6811e-01 over the same three orbits and |mu1+mu2| from 8.7156e-02
      to 8.7158e-02.  Note the asymmetry: the ZERO is exact because a
      symmetry holds it there, while a nonzero value is merely slowly
      varying, drifting in the sixth digit.  Only the closed channel is
      protected; the open one is not conserved, just slow.

182i. SO THERE IS NO EMISSION CONFIGURATION ALONG THE INSPIRAL.  This is a
      negative result and a sharp one.  The orbit has nothing that can break
      either condition: the kinematic one is fixed by the pair's identity
      and the frame, and the spin one by the preparation.  Falling inward
      does not break C.P, going inside r* does not break it, and switching
      on the radiation reaction does not break it.  A model of this pair
      therefore cannot find a radius, a phase or a depth at which a channel
      beyond E1 opens, because no such radius exists.  Every search of that
      kind -- including the ones this audit series has been running since
      177 -- was looking along an axis on which the answer is constant.
        What DOES open a channel is one number fixed before the inspiral
      starts: the angle between the two moments.  It is not a configuration
      the dynamics reaches; it is a configuration the pair is born in.

182j. WHAT THIS IS THE CLASSICAL SHADOW OF, stated as a correspondence and
      not as a derivation.  In the real system the photon multiplicity is
      fixed by C-parity: para-positronium, spins antiparallel, C = +1,
      decays to an EVEN number of photons; ortho, spins parallel, C = -1, to
      an ODD number.  The model's exact symmetry is C.P, its spin condition
      is exactly "antiparallel", and what that condition gates is exactly
      the magnetic channel.  The correspondence is close enough to be worth
      recording and is not more than a correspondence: this model has no
      photon number, C.P is a symmetry of one configuration rather than a
      quantum number of a state, and nothing here derives 2 or 3.

182k. LIMITS.  Everything in 182c, 182f and 182g is the preparation
      instant; only 182e and 182h evolve.  The M1 column of 182f is the
      model's coherent intrinsic channel, built on the moment SUM, so "M1 is
      zero when the sum is zero" is in part a statement about how the
      channel is defined and not only about the pair -- what is not
      definitional is that the sum is exactly zero and stays so, which is
      182h.  The M1 power in 182f is not monotonic in |mu1+mu2| (it falls
      from 3.66e-25 at delta 45 to 1.05e-25 at delta 90), so no scaling law
      is claimed and the second derivative of the moment, not its size,
      evidently sets the rate; that is the precession question and is
      deliberately not pursued here.  182g's one-to-one correspondence is
      five pairs, three symmetric and two not, at one radius.  And 182b's
      parity argument is stated for the moments, not carried through the
      retarded field integrals, which remains 181q's standing gap.

183. 182j CHECKED AT THE BOHR n=2 -> n=1 TRANSITION: C.P IS BLIND TO L, SO
     THE CORRESPONDENCE IS WITH (-1)^S AND NOT WITH C-PARITY
==========================================================================

183a. WHY A RADIATIVE TRANSITION IS THE TEST.  182j recorded, as a
      correspondence and not a derivation, that the model's C.P lines up
      with the C-parity rule for annihilation: antiparallel spins give
      C = +1 and an even photon count, parallel give C = -1 and an odd one.
      Annihilation is a bad discriminator, because both states there carry
      L = 0 and C-parity, which is (-1)^(L+S), reduces to (-1)^S.  A
      radiative transition changes L by one and therefore FLIPS the state's
      C-parity, so it separates the two readings.  Bohr positronium's
      n = 2 -> n = 1 jump is the cleanest such case the model can be put in.

183b. THE CONSTRUCTION, VERIFIED FIRST.  Circular orbits at r = n^2 a_pair:
        n    r/a_pair    L/hbar
        1        1       1.0000
        2        4       2.0000
        3        9       3.0000
      and the binding at n = 1 is 6.8028 eV, so the n = 2 -> n = 1 photon
      carries 0.75 x 6.8028 = 5.1021 eV.  Both are the textbook values for
      positronium, so the model's a_pair is the Bohr radius it should be.

183c. C.P IS EXACTLY ZERO AT BOTH ENDS OF THE TRANSITION, moments
      antiparallel:
        n    r/a_pair    C.P E       C.P B       E1 [W]      M1 [W]
        1        1     0.000e+00   0.000e+00   1.1673e-08  0.0000e+00
        2        4     0.000e+00   0.000e+00   4.5598e-11  0.0000e+00
        3        9     0.000e+00   0.000e+00   1.7792e-12  0.0000e+00
      Identical before and after.  Whatever the model's symmetry is, it does
      not change across the jump.

183d. AND IT IS BLIND TO L, which is the decisive measurement.  Holding the
      ENERGY at n = 2 (semi-major axis 4 a_pair) and varying the
      eccentricity sweeps L without touching anything else:
        eccentricity    L/hbar      C.P E       C.P B
            0.00        2.0000    0.000e+00   0.000e+00
            0.30        1.9079    0.000e+00   0.000e+00
            0.60        1.6000    0.000e+00   0.000e+00
            0.90        0.8718    0.000e+00   0.000e+00
            0.99        0.2821    0.000e+00   0.000e+00
      A sevenfold range in L, bitwise zero throughout.  C.P does not see the
      orbital angular momentum at all, and the algebra says why: under C.P
      both r and v reverse, so r x v is even and L is C.P-even for any
      value it takes.

183e. SO 182j's CORRESPONDENCE IS WITH (-1)^S, NOT WITH C-PARITY, and it is
      narrowed here.  In the real 2P -> 1S transition the state's C-parity
      flips, from (-1)^(1+S) to (-1)^(0+S), and the single photon's C = -1
      balances the books.  The model's C.P does not move: it is exactly zero
      at L = 2 hbar, at L = 1 hbar and at every L between.  So what the
      model carries is the classical shadow of the SPIN factor alone.  182j
      read it as the full C-parity because annihilation, the only case it
      looked at, cannot tell the two apart.  The annihilation statement
      stands; the general one does not.

183f. AND THE MODEL'S SELECTION RULE DOES NOT DISCRIMINATE.  It gets this
      transition's channel right -- 2P -> 1S is E1, and E1 is exactly what
      C.P leaves open -- but it would give the same answer for any
      transition, since it cannot see the quantum number that decides which
      transitions are allowed.  A rule that is right everywhere because it
      is constant everywhere is not evidence for the rule.

183g. THERE IS A CLASSICAL EMISSION CONFIGURATION AFTER ALL, and it is
      kinematic rather than symmetric.  A classical orbit radiates at its
      ORBITAL frequency, which sweeps through the Bohr photon's during the
      inspiral and equals it at exactly one radius.  With omega ~ r^-3/2 and
        omega(a)/omega_photon = [hbar/(mu a^2)] / [3k/(8 a hbar)] = 8/3
      exactly, once mu a k = hbar^2 is used, the crossing sits at
      (8/3)^(2/3) = 1.92300 a_pair.  Measured:
        r/a_pair   L/hbar     omega      om/photon    E1 [W]     C.P E
          1.0000   1.0000   2.0671e+16     2.6667   1.1673e-08  0.000e+00
          1.5000   1.2247   1.1252e+16     1.4515   2.3058e-09  0.000e+00
          1.9230   1.3867   7.7515e+15     1.0000   8.5363e-10  0.000e+00
          2.5000   1.5811   5.2293e+15     0.6746   2.9883e-10  0.000e+00
          4.0000   2.0000   2.5838e+15     0.3333   4.5598e-11  0.000e+00
      So the radius at which the model radiates the right COLOUR carries
      L = 1.3867 hbar and is not a Bohr orbit at all.  It is a genuine
      emission configuration in the frequency-matching sense, which 182i
      said did not exist -- 182i's claim was about C.P breaking, and this is
      not that.  Note also that C.P is bitwise zero at this radius like
      every other, so the one place the model's emission looks quantally
      right is a place where its symmetry says nothing.

183h. A CORRECTION TO 182f: E2 IS NOT EXACTLY ZERO WHEN THE MOMENTS OPEN.
      182f reported the quadrupole column as identically zero across the
      whole azimuth sweep and concluded that the spin condition "cannot
      reach the charge sector".  That column was computed with the reaction
      model DISABLED, and needsQuadrupolePower in electrodynamics.hpp is
      true only for the automatic and stochasticElectricDipole models, so
      the quadrupole power was never evaluated.  I had caught exactly this
      gating one measurement earlier, for 182g, and did not go back.  Rerun
      with the automatic model:
        delta   |mu1+mu2|     E1 [W]       E2 [W]       M1 [W]
            0     0.0000   1.1673e-08   0.0000e+00   0.0000e+00
            1     0.0087   1.1673e-08   4.6922e-31   8.5104e-28
           10     0.0872   1.1673e-08   6.7005e-31   6.8998e-26
           45     0.3827   1.1673e-08   2.5534e-31   3.6565e-25
           90     0.7071   1.1673e-08   1.9170e-31   1.0488e-25
          135     0.9239   1.1673e-08   2.7959e-31   5.0247e-24
          180     1.0000   1.1673e-08   1.0805e-30   1.6200e-23
      E2 is exactly zero at delta = 0 and nonzero at every other angle, so
      the GATE is real and C.P still holds it shut.  What is wrong is the
      reason: the moments do reach the charge sector, indirectly.  The
      quadrupole power depends on time derivatives of Q, and with the
      moments opened the two particles feel different dipole-dipole forces,
      so a2 is no longer -a1 and Q-dot-dot-dot is nonzero even where Q
      itself vanishes.  The share is about 1e-22 of E1, negligible in any
      accounting but not zero as a matter of principle, and 182f's sentence
      is withdrawn.

183i. LIMITS.  183c, 183d, 183g and 183h are preparation instants; nothing
      here evolves, and in particular the n = 2 -> n = 1 inspiral is NOT
      integrated -- at roughly 1e5 orbits between the two radii it is out of
      reach of the direct integrator, so the transition is characterised by
      its endpoints and by the frequency crossing rather than followed.
      No radiated energy or transition time is compared against 5.1021 eV
      and 3.19 ns here.  183d's sweep is one semi-major axis.  183e is an
      argument about what the model's symmetry can represent, not a
      measurement of C-parity, which this model does not carry: it has no
      photon number and no discrete L, and 183f is the price of that.

184. THE 8/3 IS NOT A g-FACTOR: g CANCELS OUT OF a_pair ENTIRELY, AND THE
     GAP IS THE CORRESPONDENCE PRINCIPLE
==========================================================================

184a. THE SUSPICION, WHICH IS A REASONABLE ONE.  183g put the
      frequency crossing at 1.92300 a_pair carrying L = 1.3867 hbar, not a
      Bohr orbit.  pairBohrRadius in particle_species.hpp is written through
      the two magnetic MOMENTS and then divided by g1*g2, so an anomalous g
      leaking into the orbital sector would be exactly the kind of thing
      that produces an off-integer L.  It is worth checking rather than
      assuming, because the definition genuinely does mention g.

184b. IT DOES NOT LEAK: g CANCELS EXACTLY.  With mu = g|q|hbar/(4m),
        16 M mu1 mu2 / (g1 g2 |q1q2| k |q1q2|)
          = 16 M [g1|q1|hbar/(4m1)][g2|q2|hbar/(4m2)]
            / (g1 g2 |q1q2|^2 k)
          = M hbar^2 / (m1 m2 |q1q2| k)
          = hbar^2 / (mu_red * k|q1q2|),
      which is the Bohr radius with no g in it.  Forcing g by hand over a
      tenfold range confirms it to every digit printed:
            g      a_pair [m]        L/hbar      bind [eV]   om1/om_gamma
        1.000000  1.0583544191e-10  1.000000000  6.802846579  2.666666667
        2.000000  1.0583544191e-10  1.000000000  6.802846579  2.666666667
        2.002319  1.0583544191e-10  1.000000000  6.802846579  2.666666667
        5.585695  1.0583544191e-10  1.000000000  6.802846579  2.666666667
       10.000000  1.0583544191e-10  1.000000000  6.802846579  2.666666667
      1.0583544191e-10 m is 2 a_0, the positronium Bohr radius, and L is
      exactly one hbar.  The 8/3 is g-free.

184c. AND HERE IS WHERE g ACTUALLY ACTS, for contrast -- the magnetic
      sector and nothing else.  Moments parallel so the channel is open:
            g     a_pair [m]      E1 [W]       M1 [W]     |B1| [T]   C.P B
        1.0000  1.058354e-10  1.167308e-08  4.0406e-24  1.3612e+00  8.60e-01
        2.0000  1.058354e-10  1.167312e-08  1.6163e-23  1.3940e+00  1.68e+00
        2.0023  1.058354e-10  1.167312e-08  1.6200e-23  1.3944e+00  1.68e+00
        4.0000  1.058354e-10  1.167329e-08  6.4656e-23  2.0444e+00  2.29e+00
        8.0000  1.058354e-10  1.167396e-08  2.5871e-22  4.1076e+00  2.28e+00
      M1 scales as g^2 to four digits across a factor of eight, |B1| grows
      with it, a_pair does not move at all and E1 moves only in its sixth
      digit, through the dipole forces' small effect on the accelerations.
      With the moments ANTIPARALLEL instead, M1 is 0.0000e+00 and the C.P
      residual is 0.000e+00 at every one of those g values: the symmetry
      does not care about g either.

184d. SO WHAT THE 8/3 IS: THE CORRESPONDENCE PRINCIPLE, at the small n where
      it is expected to fail.  For the n -> n-1 jump, the orbital frequency
      at n against the emitted photon's:
            n     omega_orb(n)     omega_photon      ratio
            2     2.583836e+15     7.751508e+15    0.333333
            3     7.655810e+14     1.435464e+15    0.533333
            5     1.653655e+14     2.325452e+14    0.711111
           10     2.067069e+13     2.424340e+13    0.852632
           50     1.653655e+11     1.704621e+11    0.970101
          200     2.583836e+09     2.603345e+09    0.992506
         1000     2.067069e+07     2.070173e+07    0.998500
      Exactly 1/3 at n = 2 and tending to 1 as n grows.  This is Bohr's own
      statement that the classical and quantum frequencies agree only in the
      large-n limit, and the n = 2 -> n = 1 jump is the worst case in the
      whole series.  The model reproduces the failure correctly; it is not
      a defect in the model and it is not a parameter choice.

184e. WHICH DEMOTES 183g.  I called 1.92300 a_pair "a genuine emission
      configuration in the frequency-matching sense".  That was too
      generous.  The orbital frequency omega ~ r^-3/2 falls monotonically
      from infinity to zero, so it crosses ANY positive target exactly once
      -- a crossing radius exists for every transition one might name, and
      its existence carries no information.  Nothing happens there: C.P is
      bitwise zero at 1.92300 a_pair as it is everywhere, the radiated power
      is unremarkable, and the curve being crossed is wrong by a factor of
      three on one side and 8/3 on the other.  183g's table stands as
      arithmetic; its billing as an emission configuration is withdrawn.
      182i's claim -- that no configuration along the inspiral opens a
      channel -- was not in fact qualified by it.

184f. LIMITS.  184b and 184c force g through ParticleSpecies directly and
      keep the masses and charges at the electron's, so they test the
      cancellation in pairBohrRadius and not whatever else a physically
      different g would imply.  184c is one radius and the preparation
      instant.  184d is the Bohr formula evaluated in the model's own
      constants, not an integration: no inspiral is followed here either,
      for the reason 183i gives.

185. THE g-FACTORS THE MODEL USES, WHAT THEIR EQUALITY IS WORTH, AND A
     NARROWING OF 184b
==========================================================================

185a. THE VALUES.  particle_species.hpp carries CODATA 2022 g-factors, the
      anomalous ones rather than the Dirac 2:
        species       g                  q               m [kg]
        electron      2.00231930436092   -e              9.10938371e-31
        positron      2.00231930436092   +e              9.10938371e-31
        muon          2.0023318412300002 -e              1.88353163e-28
        antimuon      2.0023318412300002 +e              1.88353163e-28
        proton        5.5856946893000003 +e              1.67262193e-27
        antiproton    5.5856946893000003 -e              1.67262193e-27
      The electron anomaly it implies is a = g/2 - 1 = 1.159652180460e-03,
      1159.65 ppm.  Electron and positron share the g-factor and the mass as
      the SAME LITERAL, so the equality is bitwise and not merely numeric,
      and the charges are exactly opposite.  What that produces:
        |mu| first  = |mu| second = 9.2847646860434566e-24 J/T, same bits
        gamma first + gamma second = 0.000e+00 exactly
      The sign lives in the charge, not in g: magneticMoment returns a
      magnitude through |q|, and gamma = g q / (2m) carries the direction,
      which is why the two gyromagnetic ratios cancel exactly.

185b. AND THAT EQUALITY IS AN ASSUMPTION, WHICH QUALIFIES 181e AND 182p.
      The comment above the definitions says it plainly -- the antiparticle
      takes the particle's mass and g "which is CPT rather than an
      independent measurement".  181e and 182p measured C.P's breaking as
      |mu1|/|mu2| - 1 and found it zero for e+e-, p+pbar and mu+mubar.  That
      zero is, in its MAGNITUDE part, a property of the parameter file: the
      two moments are bitwise equal because they are computed from the same
      two literals.  So the model cannot test C.P breaking sourced by CPT
      violation, because CPT is built in upstream of the dynamics.
        What remains a dynamical result is everything else: the kinematic
      condition r2 = -r1 and v2 = -v1, verified over evolved orbits in 181o
      and 182h, and the conservation of the antiparallel condition by the
      flow.  The magnitude equality is given, not earned, and the audits
      should have said so.

185c. A NARROWING OF 184b.  I wrote that forcing g "confirms it to every
      digit printed".  True for the eleven digits printed, and false under
      the stronger reading: g cancels ALGEBRAICALLY, and the arithmetic is
      floating point, so 1 to 2 units in the last place survive.  At full
      precision, g = 1 against CODATA:
      quantity   g = 1                   g = CODATA              verdict
      a_pair     1.0583544190745555e-10  1.0583544190745552e-10  differs
      omega      7751504060950774        7751504060950777        differs
      L/hbar     1.386722755275906       1.386722755275906       same bits
      C.P B      0                       0                       same bits
      The cancellation is exact as algebra and good to 1-2 ULP as code.

185d. THE CONFIRMED RESULT IS UNAFFECTED.  The crossing radius solved from
      the model's own constants:
        g = 1      r_cross/a_pair = 1.9229994270765445
        g = CODATA r_cross/a_pair = 1.9229994270765443
        analytic   (8/3)^(2/3)    = 1.9229994270765445
      One ULP apart, and g = 1 happens to land on the analytic value while
      CODATA sits one bit below.  L/hbar at the crossing is 1.386722755275906
      with the SAME BIT PATTERN at both g.  So "1.92300 a_pair, L = 1.3867
      hbar, not a Bohr orbit" stands unchanged at g = 1, to all sixteen
      digits, and 184's conclusion is not disturbed.

185e. BUT E1 DOES DEPEND ON g, AND THE SIGN IS THE PREPARATION.  184c saw
      this in a sixth digit without naming it.  Taking g = 1e-4 as a
      moments-off baseline and differencing:
        g        antiparallel: dE1/g^2    parallel: dE1/g^2
        1.0000       -1.39588423e-14          +1.39589917e-14
        2.0000       -1.39589358e-14          +1.39595335e-14
        2.0023       -1.39589361e-14          +1.39595352e-14
        4.0000       -1.39593094e-14          +1.39617002e-14
        8.0000       -1.39608034e-14          +1.39703651e-14
      Constant to about one part in 1e4 over a factor of 64 in g^2, so
        dE1 = +- 1.3959e-14 g^2 W at r = a_pair,
      the sign set by whether the moments are parallel or antiparallel.  The
      mechanism is the dipole-dipole force adding to or subtracting from the
      Coulomb attraction, changing the acceleration that Larmor squares.
        Which means there is a para/ortho split in the ALLOWED channel:
        E1 parallel     1.1673121109918508e-08 W
        E1 antiparallel 1.1673009176877052e-08 W
        difference      1.11933e-13 W, i.e. 9.59 ppm
      Small, but it is a spin-gated difference in E1 itself, independent of
      the M1 channel that 182 was about, and it was not previously recorded.

185f. NEXT STEP, PRE-REGISTERED.  185b says the model cannot currently test
      CPT-sourced C.P breaking.  The way to resolve that is to put the
      violation in deliberately and measure the response.  Break the
      parameter-level CPT by one number, epsilon, in two independent ways --
      g2 = g1(1+eps), and separately m2 = m1(1+eps) -- with the moments
      ANTIPARALLEL throughout, and sweep eps from 1e-15 to 1e-3.
        Rule R185a.  A g-mismatch leaves the kinematic condition intact and
        breaks only the spin one, so |mu1+mu2| = eps |mu1| and the C.P
        residual should be LINEAR in eps with slope 1, matching 181e's
        |mu1|/|mu2| - 1 law.  The sweep also fixes the detection floor: the
        eps below which the residual is round-off.
        Rule R185b.  M1 should then open as eps^2, since M1 goes as the
        square of the moment sum.  E2 should stay exactly zero, because a
        g-mismatch cannot reach the charge sector -- except through the
        indirect force route 183h found, which predicts a nonzero E2 of
        order 1e-22 of E1 rather than a bitwise zero.  This is the sharper
        half of the prediction, since it is where 182f was wrong.
        Rule R185c.  A MASS mismatch breaks the KINEMATIC condition instead,
        because r2 = -r1 fails in the centre-of-mass frame, so it should
        open E2 at full strength the way 182g's asymmetric pairs do, while a
        g-mismatch of the same eps should not.  If both mismatches open E2
        the same way, R185c is refuted and the two conditions are not as
        independent as 182d claims.
      Then evaluate at the experimental bounds.  The e+/e- g-factor ratio is
      tested to roughly 1e-12 and the mass ratio to roughly 1e-8; BOTH
      NUMBERS NEED A STAMPED SOURCE before anything is quoted from them, per
      the configuration-stamp rule.  At those epsilon the model would give a
      predicted residual M1 share for para-positronium -- a CPT-allowed odd
      channel in the even-channel state.  That is the number worth having,
      and it is the first thing in this series that would be falsifiable
      against an experiment rather than against another audit.

185g. LIMITS.  185a is the parameter file, not a measurement.  185c and 185d
      are one radius and one quantity each.  185e is the preparation instant
      at r = a_pair only, so the 9.59 ppm is not shown to hold at other
      radii and its r-dependence is unmeasured; the dipole-dipole force goes
      as r^-3 against Coulomb's r^-2, so the split should grow inward and
      that is untested.  185f is a proposal, not a result: nothing in it has
      been run.

186. 185f RUN: R185c CONFIRMED, R185a's SLOPE REFUTED, R185b SPLIT -- AND
     M1 IS NOT GATED BY THE MOMENT SUM AT ALL
==========================================================================

186a. THE DESIGN, WITH ONE CASE 185f DID NOT ANTICIPATE.  Writing the rules
      I missed that a MASS mismatch necessarily drags the moments with it,
      since mu = g|q|hbar/(4m).  So mode B below breaks both conditions at
      once and cannot discriminate.  The fix is a third mode that
      compensates: setting g2 = g1(1+eps) TOGETHER with m2 = m1(1+eps)
      leaves mu2 = mu1 exactly while the masses still differ, isolating the
      kinematic condition.  Three modes, moments antiparallel throughout,
      eps swept over six decades at r = a_pair:
        A   g2 = g1(1+eps)                     spin condition only
        B   m2 = m1(1+eps)                     both
        C   m2 and g2 both (1+eps)             kinematic condition only
      Mode C's |mu1+mu2| reads 0.0000e+00 at every eps, so the compensation
      works, and |r1+r2|/a reads exactly eps/2 in modes B and C, which is
      the centre-of-mass asymmetry the kinematic condition forbids.

186b. RULE R185a: LINEARITY MET, SLOPE REFUTED.  The C.P residual against
      eps, with the decade-to-decade slope in logs:
        eps      mode A       slope    mode B      slope    mode C     slope
        1e-07  4.9096e-08      --    1.0001e-07     --    6.5537e-08     --
        1e-06  4.9097e-07    1.0000  9.9999e-07   1.0000  6.5537e-07   1.0000
        1e-05  4.9096e-06    1.0000  9.9999e-06   1.0000  6.5537e-06   1.0000
        1e-04  4.9095e-05    1.0000  9.9999e-05   1.0000  6.5535e-05   1.0000
        1e-03  4.9077e-04    0.9998  9.9999e-04   1.0000  6.5511e-04   0.9998
        1e-02  4.8898e-03    0.9984  9.9999e-03   1.0000  6.5272e-03   0.9984
      Exactly linear over five decades in all three modes, so the linearity
      half of R185a is met.  The predicted SLOPE of one is not: mode A gives
      0.49096, mode C gives 0.65537, and only mode B gives 1.0000.  What the
      rule got right is the mechanism -- |mu1+mu2| does come out exactly
      eps |mu1| -- and what it got wrong is carrying that to the B residual,
      which is normalised by the FULL |B1|, of which the dipole part is only
      a share.  The three coefficients do not add or cancel in any way I can
      account for (0.491, 1.000, 0.655) and I am not going to invent a
      decomposition for them; they are reported as measured.

186c. AND THE DETECTION FLOOR.  At eps = 1e-15 mode A gives a residual of
      1.4882e-15 against a moment sum of 1.0646e-15, so the model still
      resolves the breaking there; at eps = 1e-12 the residual is 2.3588e-13
      where linearity would want 4.9e-13, so the floor is biting by then but
      the signal is not lost.  The model therefore resolves a g-mismatch
      down to roughly 1e-15, which is below any bound an experiment would
      impose.

186d. RULE R185c: CONFIRMED, AND DECISIVELY.  The quadrupole power:
        eps      mode A  (g only)      mode B and mode C    slope
        1e-07    2.5100e-32           2.0983e-27             --
        1e-06    9.4808e-35           2.0980e-25           2.0000
        1e-05    2.7806e-32           2.0980e-23           2.0000
        1e-04    3.7609e-32           2.0980e-21           2.0000
        1e-03    7.0933e-36           2.0980e-19           2.0000
        1e-02    4.2929e-32           2.0979e-17           2.0000
      A mass mismatch opens E2 as eps^2, slope 2.0000 to four decimals over
      five decades, which is what kappa_2 = q1 eps/2 predicts analytically.
      A g-mismatch does not open it at all: mode A's column does not scale,
      does not stay monotonic, and scatters over four orders of magnitude
      with slopes running from -3.72 to +3.78.  That is round-off, not a
      channel.
        And modes B and C give the SAME E2 to five digits at every eps,
      although mode C has mu2 = mu1 exactly.  So the quadrupole is gated by
      the kinematic condition alone and the moments do not reach it.  182d's
      claim that the two conditions are independent survives its first real
      test.

186e. RULE R185b: MET FOR M1, REFUTED FOR E2.  M1 opens as eps^2 once it is
      above the floor -- mode A's slopes run 2.0434, 2.0113, 2.0061 over the
      last three decades, mode B's 1.8788, 2.0434, 2.0022, mode C's 1.8073,
      2.0544, 2.0090.  The E2 half is wrong: I predicted a nonzero
      quadrupole of order 1e-22 of E1 from 183h's indirect force route, and
      mode A shows nothing above round-off.  183h's 1e-30 W was measured at
      FULL moment opening; scaled by eps^2 to eps = 1e-2 it would be 1e-34 W,
      below what the arithmetic here can carry.  So the prediction is not
      contradicted by physics, it was simply made at the wrong amplitude,
      and as a rule it fails.

186f. AND SOMETHING NOT PRE-REGISTERED: M1 IS NOT GATED BY THE MOMENT SUM.
      Mode C carries |mu1+mu2| = 0.0000e+00 at every eps and still radiates
      M1 -- 1.6344e-27 W at eps = 1e-2 against mode A's 4.0510e-28, a factor
      of 4.03 MORE than the mode that actually has a nonzero moment sum.
      So the magnitude of the sum is not what opens the channel: unequal
      masses make the two moments precess at different rates, so the sum's
      time derivative is nonzero even where the sum itself vanishes
      identically, and it is the derivative the radiation formula squares.
      182k flagged exactly this suspicion from the non-monotonic delta scan
      and could not separate it; here it is separated, because mode C holds
      the sum at exactly zero by construction.
        This weakens 182f's reading.  The M1 gate is real -- at eps = 0
      everything is exactly zero -- but "the spin condition gates M1" is too
      narrow: the KINEMATIC condition gates it too, and more strongly.

186g. THE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS, WHICH ARE THE POINT OF THE EXERCISE.  At
      r = a_pair, normalising by E1 = 1.1673e-08 W and writing e = eps/1e-2:
        g-mismatch      M1/E1 = 3.47e-20 e^2      E2/E1 = not opened
        mass mismatch   M1/E1 = 1.40e-19 e^2      E2/E1 = 1.797e-09 e^2
      These are the model's response to a CPT-violating parameter, and they
      are what an experimental bound would be fed into.  NO BOUND IS QUOTED
      HERE: the e+/e- g-factor and mass ratios need a stamped source before
      any number is taken from them, per the configuration-stamp rule, and
      supplying one is the remaining step.  What can be said without it is
      the shape -- both channels are quadratic in the violation, so a bound
      of 1e-12 on eps suppresses the M1 share by 1e-20 relative to the
      eps = 1e-2 figures above, which puts it far below anything this model
      could be held to.

186h. LIMITS.  One radius, the preparation instant, antiparallel moments
      only; nothing here is evolved, so whether these channels persist or
      accumulate over an orbit is untested and 182h's conservation result
      was measured at eps = 0 only.  The floor in 186c is read off two
      points rather than fitted.  186b reports three coefficients it does
      not explain.  186g's transfer functions are single-radius
      normalisations and the r-dependence of both is unmeasured, which
      matters because E1 itself runs as r^-4 while the quadrupole runs
      steeper.  And the whole exercise breaks CPT in the PARAMETERS, which
      is not the same as a Lagrangian that violates it -- the model has no
      mechanism that would distinguish the two, which is 185b's point
      restated rather than resolved.

187. 186h TAKEN UP: THE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS RUN AS r^-4 AND r^-1, 186b's
     COEFFICIENT IS THE DIPOLE SHARE, AND THE MASS MODES SILENTLY LEAVE
     THE POSITRONIUM REGIME
==========================================================================

187a. WHICH OF 186h's LIMITS, AND WHY THAT ONE.  The transfer functions are
      186's deliverable and quoting them at a single radius is their weakest
      point, because the pair spends its whole life falling from a_pair
      toward r*.  If the channel shares run with radius, the a_pair figures
      describe the one place the pair is least interesting.  Two cheaper
      items from the same list are taken with it.

187b. 186b's UNEXPLAINED COEFFICIENT, CLOSED.  186b measured the C.P
      residual for a g-mismatch as 0.49096 eps and said only that the
      residual is normalised by the full |B1| while the mismatch rides the
      dipole part.  That is testable: scale the moments down by 1e-6 to
      isolate the charge-motion field and difference.
        r/a_pair   |B| full     |B| charge   |B| dipole   share   measured
            1     2.387252e+00 1.564572e+00 1.172059e+00 0.490966 0.49077
            0.1   1.486281e+03 4.946721e+02 1.171162e+03 0.787982 0.78739
            0.01  1.175655e+06 1.561296e+05 1.093245e+06 0.929903 0.92906
      The coefficient IS the dipole share, to 0.1% at three radii spanning
      two decades.  And it is not a constant: the dipole field grows as r^-3
      against the charge field's r^-2, so the share climbs toward one as the
      pair falls, which the measured column follows.

187c. THE TRANSFER FUNCTIONS ALONG THE RADIUS, eps = 1e-3, with local
      power-law slopes in log r:
        MODE A (g-mismatch, spin condition)
        r/a_pair     E1 [W]      M1/E1     slope    E2/E1     C.P/eps
            1     1.1673e-08  3.4220e-22    --    noise      0.49077
            0.3   1.4410e-06  4.2836e-20  -4.0115 noise      0.66141
            0.1   1.1666e-04  3.4647e-18  -3.9987 noise      0.78739
            0.03  1.4327e-02  4.2014e-16  -3.9851 noise      0.87938
            0.01  1.1052e+00  2.8243e-14  -3.8303 noise      0.92906
        MODE C (kinematic condition only)
        r/a_pair     E1 [W]      M1/E1     slope    E2/E1     slope
            1     1.1685e-08  1.3702e-21    --    1.7955e-11   --
            0.3   1.4424e-06  1.7137e-19  -4.0108 5.9860e-11 -1.0001
            0.1   1.1678e-04  1.3864e-17  -3.9989 1.7975e-10 -1.0009
            0.03  1.4344e-02  1.6832e-15  -3.9861 6.0305e-10 -1.0054
            0.01  1.1229e+00  1.1457e-13  -3.8416 1.8826e-09 -1.0362
      Two clean laws.  M1/E1 goes as r^-4 in BOTH modes, and since E1 itself
      goes as r^-4 (1.1673e-08 to 1.1666e-04 over one decade is 1e4), the M1
      power runs as r^-8.  E2/E1 goes as r^-1 for the kinematic mode, slope
      1.000 to three decimals over two decades.  A g-mismatch still never
      opens E2 at any radius -- that column neither scales nor stays
      monotonic, exactly as 186d found at a_pair.

187d. SO 186g's NUMBERS ARE a_pair NUMBERS AND UNDERSTATE THE CHANNELS.
      M1/E1 climbs from 3.42e-22 at a_pair to 2.82e-14 at 0.01 a_pair, eight
      orders of magnitude for the same eps; E2/E1 climbs from 1.80e-11 to
      1.88e-09, two orders.  186g's transfer functions should be read as
        g-mismatch     M1/E1 = 3.47e-20 e^2 (r/a_pair)^-4
        mass mismatch  M1/E1 = 1.40e-19 e^2 (r/a_pair)^-4
                       E2/E1 = 1.80e-09 e^2 (r/a_pair)^-1
      over 1 down to 0.01 a_pair, and 186g's bare figures are the r = a_pair
      case of these.  The qualitative conclusion of 186g is unchanged --
      both channels are quadratic in the violation, so a small bound
      suppresses them hard -- but the radius enters at the fourth power and
      any bound applied at a_pair alone would be eight orders too generous
      by 0.01 a_pair.

187e. AND A TRAP THE SWEEP WALKED INTO.  isPositronium() tests the two
      masses for EXACT equality with electron.mass, so a mass-perturbed pair
      is not positronium and separationFloor() returns zero, while
      pointParticleBoundaryOf switches from comptonBarrierRadius to the
      generic collision boundary:
        pair                          isPositronium   floor [a_pair]
        stock electron + positron          yes         9.132269e-05
        mode A: g2 = g1(1+1e-3)            yes         9.132269e-05
        mode B: m2 = m1(1+1e-3)            NO          0
        mode C: m2 and g2 both             NO          0
      So mode A keeps the positronium regularization and modes B and C do
      not, which is an asymmetry between the modes that nothing in 185f or
      186 controlled for.  It is not the floor itself biting -- the stock
      floor sits at 9.13e-05 a_pair, below every radius quoted -- it is the
      dipole sector running unregularized, which diverges below about 0.003
      a_pair in mode C and reaches NaN at 0.001.  The gating is deliberate
      and its comment says why, so it is recorded here rather than changed:
      a tolerance-based isPositronium would be a production behaviour change
      needing its own justification.

187f. WHICH BOUNDS WHERE 186 AND 187 HOLD.  Everything in 186 is at
      r = a_pair, four orders above the floor and far outside the divergent
      region, so 186 is untouched.  187c is quoted only over 1 down to 0.01
      a_pair; the 0.01 rows already show the M1 slope drifting off -4 to
      -3.83 and -3.84, which is the same departure setting in, so even they
      are the edge rather than the middle of the valid range.  Nothing at or
      inside r* is quoted from the mass modes at all.

187g. LIMITS.  187b's identification is three radii and holds to 0.1%, not
      exactly; the residual is not claimed to BE the dipole share times eps,
      only to track it.  187c is one eps and the preparation instant, with
      no evolution, so 186h's first item -- whether these channels persist
      or accumulate over an orbit -- is still open and is the obvious next
      thing.  The mode A E2 column is called noise on the strength of its
      non-monotonicity, not on a floor estimate.  And 187e documents an
      asymmetry it does not remove, so any future comparison of the mass
      modes against the g mode below 0.01 a_pair is invalid as the code
      stands.

188. 187e FIXED: THE POSITRONIUM MASS TEST IS BANDED, WHICH REMOVES THE
     DIVERGENCE AND SUPERSEDES TWO NUMBERS IN 187c
==========================================================================

188a. WHAT WAS WRONG.  187e recorded that isPositronium() tested the two
      masses for EXACT equality with electron.mass, so a pair perturbed in
      the fifteenth digit was a different species: separationFloor() dropped
      to zero and pointParticleBoundaryOf switched from comptonBarrierRadius
      to the generic collision boundary.  187e declined to change it,
      calling a tolerance-based version a production behaviour change
      needing its own justification.  That justification is below, and the
      change is made.

188b. THE CHANGE.  particle_species.hpp now reads
        constexpr double positroniumMassBand=1.0e-2;
      and tests |m - m_e| <= band * m_e on each role instead of ==.  The
      charge tests are untouched.  Nine static assertions pin the
      classification of every pair --pair can name, plus the band's own two
      edges, so the predicate cannot drift silently again:
        electron+positron and positron+electron   true
        muon+antimuon, proton+antiproton          false
        electron+antimuon, proton+electron        false
        a pair perturbed by 1e-3                  true
        a pair perturbed by 1e-1                  false

188c. WHY THE BAND IS SAFE.  The nearest other pair the CLI can select is
      the muon at 206.8 electron masses, four orders outside the band, so no
      selectable pair changes classification and this is a no-op for every
      run the CLI can start.  It changes behaviour only for pairs built in
      code, which is the case that was broken.  The cost inside the band is
      bounded by the band: comptonBarrierRadius is a fixed electron-mass
      constant rather than a pair-dependent one, so a pair admitted at the
      edge carries a barrier wrong by at most 1% -- against the alternative
      of losing the floor altogether, which was wrong by whatever the
      unregularized field happened to produce.
        Checked: the build carries the assertions, validation is 62/62 PASS
      and header isolation 38/37, unchanged.

188d. AND IT REMOVES THE DIVERGENCE.  Mode C of the 186 sweep, which is the
      mass-perturbed one, near and inside r* = 1.8264e-3 a_pair:
        r/a_pair   E1 before     E1 after    M1 before    M1 after
          0.01     1.1229e+00   1.1063e+00  1.2865e-13  1.2549e-13
          0.005    1.8149e+01   1.4475e+01  1.4684e-11  1.1084e-11
          0.003    2.5111e+02   7.1029e+01  3.4446e-10  6.0621e-12
          0.002    4.5848e+03   2.5559e+02  1.7317e-06  1.1489e-08
          0.0015   4.4616e+04   5.7745e+02  2.6605e-04  1.0756e-07
          0.001    4.3801e+06   1.7656e+03     NaN      1.0688e-07
      The NaN is gone and E1 at 0.001 a_pair falls by a factor of 2480 onto
      1.7656e+03, against mode A's 1.7602e+03 at the same radius.  The two
      modes now agree to four digits everywhere, which they must at
      eps = 1e-3 and did not before.

188e. AND MODE A IS UNTOUCHED, WHICH IS THE CHECK THAT MATTERS.  Mode A
      perturbs only g, so it satisfied the old predicate already; re-running
      187c's scan reproduces its entire column to every digit printed --
      3.4220e-22, 4.2836e-20, 3.4647e-18, 4.2014e-16, 2.8243e-14 for M1/E1
      and 0.49077 through 0.92906 for C.P/eps.  So the change moved exactly
      what it was meant to move and nothing else.

188f. WHICH SUPERSEDES TWO NUMBERS IN 187c.  Mode C's row at 0.01 a_pair was
      computed in the wrong regime:
        quantity        187c        corrected     shift
        E2/E1        1.8826e-09    1.8717e-09     0.6%
        C.P/eps      0.12387       0.13259        7.0%
      The power laws are unaffected -- over 1 down to 0.03 a_pair the M1/E1
      slopes now read -4.0143, -3.9993, -3.9859 against 187c's -4.0108,
      -3.9989, -3.9861, and the E2/E1 slopes -1.0001, -1.0009, -1.0053
      against -1.0001, -1.0009, -1.0054.  187c's conclusions stand; its two
      innermost mode C entries are replaced by the ones above.

188g. AND THE INNER DEPARTURE IS NOW THE MODEL'S OWN REGULARIZATION.  Below
      about 0.01 a_pair both modes leave the r^-4 law together -- the M1
      slope reads +0.2186 in mode A and +0.2363 in mode C at 0.003, then
      -5.9787 and -5.9750 at 0.001 -- and E1 rises by 64 and 24.8 across
      those steps where r^-4 would give 123 and 81.  That is the short-range
      field weight softening the field, which is what it is there for, and
      it now applies identically to both modes.  It is not a power law and
      187c's range of 1 down to 0.01 a_pair remains the range in which the
      transfer functions are quoted.

188h. LIMITS.  The band is 1e-2 because that is comfortably wider than any
      parameter study this series has run and four orders narrower than the
      nearest selectable pair; it is not derived from anything physical, and
      nothing measures what a pair at the edge of it actually does beyond
      the 1% barrier bound argued in 188c.  188d and 188e are one eps and
      the preparation instant.  The change does not touch
      pointParticleBoundaryOf's OTHER branch, so what a band-admitted pair
      should use as a collision boundary is decided by the same gate and has
      not been examined separately.
